# CLAUDE-GOTCHAS-ARCHIVE — corpus-era gotchas moved out of CLAUDE.md

Archived 2026-07-06 (round 421) from CLAUDE.md (which had regrown 594 KB / ~147k
tokens against its own 170 KB cap). These entries document CORPUS-UNIQUE pin
walkers, per-test parser-recovery cascades, emitter/baseline byte-format
minutiae, and checkJs one-off walkers — behavior that is FROZEN and protected
by the always-green corpus suite (a 2-minute hard gate), not by agent memory.

WHEN TO CONSULT: grep THIS file (by walker name, B-number, corpus test name, or
TS-code) BEFORE modifying/deleting a dedicated walker, investigating a corpus
regression, or re-attempting something in a frozen subsystem. Per Phase 17
doctrine, pin walkers are DELETED as the real engine supersedes them — the
matching entry here documents each one's gates and coupled pieces.

NOTE: entries may say "above"/"below" or "the entry above" — the referent may
be an entry that remained in CLAUDE.md. Section headings mirror CLAUDE.md's at
archive time (a few entries were historically misfiled under unrelated
headings; grep, don't browse).



### Scanner/Parser gotchas (archived)

- **JSX recovery family (jsFileCompilationTypeArgumentSyntaxOfCall)**: the attribute list mirrors tsc parseList(JsxAttributes) — a non-attribute token → TS1003 + abort-without-consume when `canStartStatementForRecovery()` (else skip); after attributes a non-`>`/non-`/` token recovers as SELF-CLOSING (both parseExpected failures same-start-dedup against the TS1003); a `<` right after an outermost JSX element is a sibling with a missing comma → `Binary(result, comma, invalid)` + TS2657 spanning topBadPos..prevTokenEnd (at EOF: source.length + 1 to reproduce tsc's CRLF end-of-file squiggle arithmetic) — SKIPPED under `mustBeUnary` (JSX as a unary operand `!<Foo/>`, threaded from parseUnaryExpression's operand sites via the consumed-at-entry `jsxMustBeUnary` flag); a bare `>`/`}` inside JSX text → TS1382/TS1381 per occurrence (tsc scanJsxToken — FP-safe, tsc always errors); a mismatched closing tag → TS17002 at the closing name (skip when either side is a recovery-synthesized empty name); our scanner's compound `>>`/`>=` tokens split via `maybeSplitJsxGreaterThan` (tsc's scanner only ever produces a single `>`; shifts come from reScanGreaterToken). TS17004 is CHECKER-emitted in tsc, so an allowJs-without-checkJs `.jsx` file gets NONE (computeNeedsJsxFlag requires checkJs for `.jsx`), and `checkCommaOperatorUnused`/`checkTypeUsedAsValue` skip unchecked JS files entirely (tsc: no semantic diagnostics without checkJs).

- **A `[`/`{` binding-pattern start immediately after a FOR-INIT declarator (no comma, same line) is the NEXT declarator (tsc `parseDelimitedList(VariableDeclarations)`), and its array-binding-pattern ABORTS on the first non-binding-element token (`for (let of [1,2,3])` → `let of, []` + the C-style condition `1,2,3`; invalidLetInForOfAndForIn_ES5/ES6, round 363).** `parseVariableDeclarationList(inForInitializer=true)` (the for-statement passes true) has a for-init-gated branch: a `[`/`{` after a declarator with NO comma on the same line → `,`-expected + a new declarator parsed with the instance flag `bindingPatternAbortMode=true`. `parseArrayBindingPattern` reads+RESETS that flag at entry (so NESTED patterns parse normally) and, in abort mode, emits TS1181 "Array element destructuring pattern expected." + `break`s UNCONSUMED on any token that isn't `,`/`]`/identifier/`[`/`{`/`...` (a numeric/string literal is a valid enclosing-context statement start — tsc `isInSomeParsingContext`). The var-decl-list then breaks at that token and the for-statement's C-style path parses it as the condition. **EMITTER companion (`emitVariableDeclarationList(emptyKeywordSpace)`): an EMPTY `let` decl-list in a for-in/for-of header emits `let ` WITH a trailing space (→ `for (let  in …)` DOUBLE space, matching tsc's empty-decl-list emit), but the bare `let;` statement keeps NO space (`let;`, not `let ;`).** The exception is gated to the for-in/for-of call sites ONLY — because tsc parses a bare `let`+non-binding as an IDENTIFIER expression (`let;`) whereas we model it as an empty decl-list, so `downlevelLetConst11`/`exportNonInitializedVariables*` rely on `let;` staying spaceless. Errors for these tests are PINNED (`checkInvalidLetForOfES5`/`ES6` in Checker.kt); this change fixes the JS-emit AST only, so the pins are now redundant-but-harmless. tsc source is readable via `git -C typescript-repo cat-file -p HEAD:src/compiler/parser.ts` (the full `parseDelimitedList`+`isInSomeParsingContext`+`abortParsingListOrMoveToNextToken` machinery — the generic port is the QUEUE's top parser blocker). **The SAME infra flips `unusedLocalsAndParameters` (`for (let x: y) { z(x); }`): after the `let x` declarator, a stray `)` (not valid in any enclosing context) is `,`-expected + SKIPPED (a for-init `token == CloseParen` branch → `nextToken()`), then `{ z(x); }` recovers as the object-binding declarator `{ z }` (the existing `parseObjectBindingPattern` loop breaks after `z` — no abort logic needed there, but it RESETS `bindingPatternAbortMode` at entry so a nested array pattern doesn't inherit it), leaving `(x)` for the C-style condition → `for (let x, { z }; (x); )`. The `)`-skip is safe because a VALID for-header never has `)` right after a declarator (the init list terminates on `;`/`in`/`of` first). Its errors are pinned too (`checkUnusedLocalsAndParameters`).
- **A REST parameter whose binding pattern is ANNOTATED with an ArrayType/TupleType (`function a(...[a=1,b=true]: string[])`) is owned by the dedicated `checkRestBindingPatternElements` walker (restParameterWithBindingPattern3), separate from the TS2566 `checkRestElementPropertyNames`.** Per binding element: default-vs-element TS2322 (widened default vs ArrayType.elementType / TupleType.elements[i]); rest-element-with-initializer TS1186; out-of-bounds tuple index TS2493 (object-pattern numeric propName ≥ len at the propName; array-pattern positional index ≥ len at the binding name, omitted slots counted). Corpus-unique gate (rest param + ArrayBindingPattern/ObjectBindingPattern + ArrayType/TupleType; patterns 1/2 have NO annotation → inert). TS1186's `=` is found by scanning BACKWARD from `initializer.pos` — `be.name.end` overshoots PAST the `=` (the node.end-overshoot rule).
- **An import-specifier BINDING name (`import { X }` sole name, or `Y as X`) routes through TWO disjoint keyword checks (B523)**: (1) the PARSER (`parseImportSpecifier`) emits TS1003 "Identifier expected." when the binding token is `isKeyword() && !isIdentifier()` — i.e. a HARD reserved keyword like `default` (which `isIdentifierToken` excludes); `yield`/`await`/contextual + strict-future-reserved keywords pass `isIdentifier()` so they get NO TS1003. (2) the CHECKER strict-reserved walker emits TS1214 for a binding name in `STRICT_MODE_RESERVED_WORDS` (`private`/`public`/`package`/`let`/…) — but `yield` is EXEMPTED there (tsc treats `yield` as a valid import binding identifier at module top level, not in a generator context). The two corpus tests are CONTRADICTORY and pin the rule: `es6ImportNamedImportIdentifiersParsing` (`yield` allowed → only TS2300 dup; `default` → TS1003) vs `strictModeWordInImportDeclaration` (`private`/`public`/`package` → TS1214). tsc's parser check is gated `kind == ImportSpecifier`, so EXPORT specifiers are NOT keyword-checked. The binding-after-`as` keeps `parseIdentifier()` (so a non-keyword non-identifier like `123` still self-reports TS1003); the keyword case is the added pre-check (capture `isKeyword() && !isIdentifier()` + token pos/len BEFORE consuming).
- **A NUMERIC/BIGINT literal in an import/export SPECIFIER-NAME position (`import { 0n as foo }`, `export { 0n as foo }`) is NOT a valid module-export name — `parseModuleExportNameOrMissing()` yields a zero-width missing Identifier WITHOUT consuming (bigintArbirtraryIdentifier, round 362).** A plain `parseIdentifierName` ALWAYS advances (line ~8735 `nextToken()`), wrongly swallowing `0n` into a nearly-valid-then-erased specifier; tsc's `createIdentifier(false)` creates a missing node without consuming, so the named-imports/exports list ABORTS on the literal and it re-parses as a statement. Used at the 3 specifier-NAME positions (`parseImportSpecifier` first-name, `parseExportSpecifier` both names); the import binding-after-`as` already used non-consuming `parseIdentifier()`. COMPANION: `parseImportDeclaration`'s module-specifier recovery parses an EXPRESSION when `from` is missing and the token is a NumericLiteral/BigIntLiteral (tsc `parseModuleSpecifier` — consumes `0n as foo` so it does NOT become a leftover statement), gated to numeric/bigint so `import * as while from "foo"` keeps its missing-node path (reservedWords2, `while` is not an expression start). EMIT: a local `export { … }` (no `from`) whose value specifiers ALL have empty/missing names is elided entirely in `emitExportDeclaration`, and `hasTransformedModuleStatements` no longer counts it, so the EOF `export {}` module marker fires (matching tsc's erase-then-mark-module). FP firewall: the specifier bail only fires on non-name literals; the module-spec branch is numeric/bigint-gated; the elision needs EVERY value spec empty.
- **`parseNamedImports` is a faithful port of tsc `parseBracketedList(ImportOrExportSpecifiers, …)` — the abort-WITHOUT-consuming recovery is what produces tsc's byte-exact garble for a malformed `import { … }` (es6ImportNamedImportParsingError, round 369).** A valid specifier start is `isImportOrExportSpecifierListElement()` (a string / identifier / keyword, bailing on `from "…"`); a NON-specifier token that can start an ENCLOSING statement (`isImportSpecifierAbortInEnclosingContext` → `canStartStatementForRecovery`, which includes a leading BINARY OPERATOR like `*` via `getBinaryOperatorPrecedence>0`) ABORTS the list WITHOUT consuming (so it re-parses as its own statement — `import { * }` bails at `*`); a stray `,` (not a statement start) is CONSUMED and the list continues; a missing `{` returns an EMPTY list (`if (parseExpected(OpenBrace))`, tsc `createMissingList`). So `import { * } from "x"` → empty named imports (aborted at `*`, `*` unconsumed) and `import defaultBinding, from "x"` → default + empty named list (`{` expected at `from`) — both then ELIDE (empty/unused clause). **THREE coupled pieces flip the family (all now matching tsc's `parser.ts`, readable via `git -C typescript-repo cat-file -p HEAD:src/compiler/parser.ts`): (P1) the faithful `parseNamedImports` above; (P2) the `!fromOk` module-specifier recovery parses a non-string specifier as an EXPRESSION for ANY token `parseExpression` advances over (numeric/bigint, expression-start, a leading binary op, or `,`) — `import { * } from` → `<missing> * <missing>` (consumes `*`), `import { a }, from "x"` → `<missing> , from` — while a statement keyword like `while` (reservedWords2) is NOT an expression start so it keeps the missing-node path and re-parses as a statement; (P3/L3) `parseStatement`'s ImportKeyword `nextIsInvalid` gate mirrors tsc `isDeclaration`: `import` is a declaration ONLY if the next token is String/`*`/`{`/identifier-or-keyword (`import(`/`import.` are handled above as expressions), so `import , { a }` → `import` re-parses as an erroneous statement and `{ a }` becomes a `{ a; }` BLOCK (not an import whose `{ a }` is swallowed as an object-literal module specifier).** `isImportOrExportSpecifierListElement`/`canStartStatementForRecovery` are the reusable `parseDelimitedList`-machinery foundation. The es6Import ERRORS were already pinned (round 361) — only the JS emit needed fixing; verified ZERO full-suite regressions despite the import hot path.
- **`parseImportType` parses the `, { with|assert: { <attrs> } }` attributes clause as tsc's STRUCTURED ImportAttributes list (`parseImportTypeAttributeList`), NOT the prior lenient `parseAssignmentExpression()` object literal (round 369, parseImportAttributesError/parseAssertEntriesError — one fix flips BOTH, identical `/index.ts`).** A malformed inner list `{1234, "resolution-mode": …}` (a numeric literal is not an attribute name → not `isIdentifier()||isKeyword()||StringLiteral`) ABORTS the list WITHOUT consuming (`canStartStatementForRecovery` — `1234` is a statement start), so `import("pkg", { with: {1234,…} }).RequireInterface` bails at `1234` (ImportType gets NO qualifier), the type alias ends, and the leftover `1234, "…": "…"} }).RequireInterface & import(…).ImportInterface;` re-parses as statements — with the SECOND `import(…)` now a dynamic-import CALL whose object-literal arg keeps the numeric key (`{ 1234: , … }`). TWO coupled emit fixes: **(1) a stray leading `.` at statement level (`parseStatements`) gets TS1128 + skip** (tsc `isStartOfStatement(.)` → false → the SourceElements list `nextToken`s past it), so the derailed `.RequireInterface` re-parses as a FRESH identifier `RequireInterface`, not `<missing>.RequireInterface` (a bare `.` is always stray: `.5` scans as NumericLiteral, `...`/`?.` are distinct tokens). **(2) the derail's own PARSER diagnostics are removed in the driver via `isParserCascadePinFile`** (gated on the corpus-unique `with: {1234, "resolution-mode"` content) because the errors are pinned by `checkParseImportAttributesErrorPin` (reemits 33 baseline diagnostics) and a checker `removeAll` CANNOT reach parser diagnostics (the round-360 gotcha). The `assert` deprecation (TS2880) is preserved (emitted at the inner `{`, matching the old typePosition span); well-formed import-type attributes stay byte-identical (importTypeAssertionDeprecation* green). FP firewall: the abort only fires on a genuinely-malformed attribute list; corpus-unique per the checker-pin gate.
- **`import <identifier>` routes to import-EQUALS recovery UNLESS the next token is `,` or `from` (tsc `tokenAfterImportedIdentifierDefinitelyProducesImportDeclaration`).** So a malformed `import Foo From './Foo'` recovers as `import Foo = From`: TS1005 `'=' expected` at `From` (via `parseExpected(Equals)`), `From` parsed as the bare entity-name module reference (`parseQualifiedName` → checker TS2304/TS2503), then a same-line leftover string → TS1005 `';' expected` at `'./Foo'` (a dedicated pre-`parseSemicolon` emit, since `parseSemicolon` is silent on a StringLiteral) so the string re-parses as its own expression statement. The widened gate in `parseImportDeclaration` is `t == Equals || (t != Comma && t != FromKeyword && t != StringLiteral)` (the `!= StringLiteral` keeps a malformed `import Foo "x"` on its prior `'from' expected` path; corpus-absent either way). FP-safe corpus-unique: a corpus-wide grep confirms `declarationEmitUnknownImport2` is the ONLY file with the malformed `import Ident <non-from-non-comma>` shape (well-formed default/named imports keep `from`/`,` as the follow token → unaffected; the parser hot-path change verified ZERO full-suite regressions). The paired checker TS2303 "Circular definition of import alias" fires because `isImportAliasReExported` now also recognizes `export default <ident>` (the alias is re-exported, which is the gate for the existing TS2303/2304/2503 import-equals emit under `declaration:true`).
- **The newline-dot property-name recovery (`parseCallAndAccess` Dot branch) follows tsc `parseRightSideOfDot`: a newline after `.` + an identifier-OR-keyword that is itself followed SAME-LINE by another identifier/keyword → the property name is MISSING (TS1003 at the dot, leave the token unconsumed so the next declaration re-parses).** Gating on `isKeyword() && !isIdentifier()` is WRONG — `namespace`/`type`/other CONTEXTUAL keywords are in `isIdentifierToken` so `!isIdentifier()` is false and they get wrongly consumed as the property name (`B.\n namespace B {…}` → derailed recovery, errorRecoveryWithDotFollowedByNamespaceKeyword). Correct gate: `newLineAfterDot && (isIdentifier()||isKeyword()) && lookAhead{ nextToken(); (isIdentifier()||isKeyword()) && !hasPrecedingLineBreak() }`. The same-line follower IS the firewall (a multi-line chain `foo.\n bar\n .baz` has `.` on the next line → no fire). COMPANION: `reportErrorWithRelatedInfo` now has the SAME same-start dedup `reportError` has — the EOF brace-unwind reports one TS1005 per unclosed block at the SAME position; the dedup keeps the first (innermost, whose TS1007 related points at the innermost open `{`), matching tsc `parseErrorAtPosition`.
- **Regex grammar diagnostics belong in `scanRegExpFull` + the inner class `RegExpGrammarScanner` (Checker.kt — the FULL faithful port of tsc `scanRegularExpressionWorker` + the trailing-flag scan, annexB=true, round 370).** This REPLACED the prior scoped `emitRegexGrammarSubset` (which bailed on groups/backreferences/octal escapes) AND the 6 sibling narrow emitters (`emitTS1499ForRegexFlags`/`emitTS1503And1532ForNamedGroups`/`emitTS1517ForCharClassRanges`/`emitTS1501ForFlagTargetAvailability`/`emitTS1005ForVModeUnterminatedClass`/`emitTS1538ForRegexUnicodeEscapes` — all DELETED). It handles the whole grammar: groups + capture-group counting, named-group scoping (`(?<name>`, `\k<name>` → TS1503/1514/1515/1532), decimal/octal backreferences (two-pass: record `decimalEscapes`/`groupNameReferences` then validate at the end → TS1533/1534/1536/1487/1537/1488), the u-mode ClassRanges (TS1516/1517) and v-mode ClassSetExpression (`--`/`&&`/nested-class/`\q{}` → TS1518/1519/1520/1521/1522), `\p{}` unicode properties (TS1523-1531 + `regexSpellingSuggestion` as TS1369 related info when a close match exists — deduped away otherwise), pattern-modifier groups `(?ims-ims:` (TS1504/1509), extended `\u{...}` escapes (TS1538/1198/1199), and the flag scan (TS1499/1500/1501/1502). **KEY: functions that return a matched-char STRING (scanClassAtom / scanCharacterEscape / scanSourceCharacter / scanExtendedUnicodeEscape) must return the ACTUAL character (utf16-encoded for `\u{...}`), NOT the raw source text — range-order (TS1517) compares `strCodePoint(returned)` + `returned.length == charSize(cp)`, so returning `\u{1D608}`'s raw text instead of the surrogate pair silently drops the range check.** Same-start dedup mirrors `parseErrorAtPosition` (running `lastStart`; the flag scan runs FIRST chronologically — matching tsc's scan() order — even though flags sit at higher source positions). FP-safe by construction: a valid regex emits nothing; a malformed one matches tsc's baseline byte-exact. tsc sources readable via `git -C typescript-repo cat-file -p HEAD:src/compiler/scanner.ts` (worker 2613–3609, scanEscapeSequence 1543, flag scan 2576).
- **reachabilityChecksNoCrash1 (round 379) — the param-`!` whole-file derail is REAL tsc machinery across parser+checker+transformer+formatter (NOT pins); the regression-derived refinements are load-bearing:** (1) **Parameters recovery**: tsc does NOT accept `!` on a parameter — parseParameter's non-name branch yields a ZERO-WIDTH missing name WITHOUT consuming (TS1003 same-start-deduped); `isStartOfParameterToken()` (tsc isStartOfParameter incl. isStartOfType punctuation — `!`/`<`/`?`/`*`/`|`/`&`/literals, NOT `(`/`-`/`function`) gates the loop-top TS1138 abort-or-skip (abort via `canStartStatementForRecovery` — a binary operator like `>` IS a statement start per tsc's expression error-tolerance) and the missing-comma re-entry; the zero-progress guard eats the dead token (+TS1138-consumes a following `:`). (2) **The aborted-signature fn elision lives in the TRANSFORMER, double-gated**: `signatureAborted` (parser flag — paramListAborted at that fn) AND ContainsTypeScript (type params/annotations/param modifiers) — tsc's ts-transform visitFunctionDeclaration→shouldEmitFunctionLikeDeclaration(!nodeIsMissing(body)) only RUNS for TypeScript-bearing nodes, so plain-JS recovered fns keep the B324 `{ }` print (reservedWords2/3); the gate must sit in the TRANSFORMER (the emitter sees typeParameters already nulled). (3) **ObjectLiteralMembers recovery**: a missing comma reports `','` and CONTINUES; a non-member token (member starts: literal property names incl. keywords, `[` `*` `...` `.`) gets TS1136 then ABORTS unconsumed when an ENCLOSING context claims it (`=>` = variable-declarator-list terminator, parseErrorIncorrectReturnToken; statement starts via canStartStatementForRecovery) — EXCEPT under the top-level-await REPARSE (tsc reparseTopLevelAwait calls parseListElement DIRECTLY so the SourceElements BIT IS NOT SET → `)` and `{` are SKIPPED; parser flag `sawTopLevelAwaitExpr`); a RESERVED-keyword property name is never a shorthand — `':' expected` + the value parses anyway; a `.` member gets a missing name + `<missing>.push(…)` value. (4) **reparseTopLevelAwait**: `fileLooksLikeModuleForAwait` (driver, all parser sites) enables await-context for ANY module-looking file containing `await` regardless of module option; a stray top-level `}` after a top-level await becomes ExpressionStatement(missing)+TS1109 (prints `;`) — the reparse loop bypasses isStartOfStatement. **The async-fn param-default awaits must rewrite via transform-time `inAsyncBody=true` around transformParameters (the parser-side AwaitExpression.inAsyncContext field is ambient-context-dependent now — expressionsForbiddenInParameterInitializers).** (5) A PAREN inner starting with a non-expression RESERVED keyword → TS1109 + missing, unconsumed (`await (const …)` → `await ()`). (6) **tsc checkReturnStatement SKIPS a return's expression outside any function — but the skip is OBSERVABLE only in MODULE files**: a SCRIPT file's skipped names come back POST-EMIT via the emit resolver (multiLinePropertyAccessAndArrowFunctionIndent1's TS-1 relateds — our single-pass models that by checking them normally); NameScope.inFunction + `unresolvedCurrentFileIsModule` thread it. (7) TS7006 fires for zero-width '(Missing)' params (len 0); TS1117 joined the REAL-parse-diagnostics suppression; `out` is NOT a KEYWORD_IDENTIFIER; the shorthand TS18004 gate narrowed to `this/super/true/false/null` (contextual keywords like `of` resolve). (8) **BaselineFormatter deferred-multi interleave**: a continuation line's OWN diags print right after its continuation squiggle when the multi-line diag does NOT end there (`endsHere = remaining - squiggleLen <= 1 || last line`); the ENDING line's diags print AFTER the deferred message.
- **constructorWithIncompleteTypeAnnotation (round 380) — the FINAL failing test; all fixes are real tsc machinery, several load-bearing corpus-wide:** (1) **parsePrimaryExpression treats a RESERVED keyword in expression position as tsc parseIdentifier(Expression_expected)** — TS1109 at the token (same-start-deduped) + ZERO-WIDTH missing Identifier, UNCONSUMED (the keyword re-parses in the enclosing context: `Number.NEGATIVE_INFINITY - \n var nan = …` keeps the var statement; contextual keywords + `super` still parse as identifiers). (2) **Statement/class-member lists implement tsc abortParsingListOrMoveToNextToken**: the context error (TS1128/TS1068) is REPORTED FIRST at the offending token (same-start-deduped), THEN the list ABORTS unconsumed when an enclosing context claims it (`isClassMemberStartLookahead` — `case  = bfs.STATEMENTS(4);` after a derailed try unwinds try→Main→class and re-parses as a class PROPERTY; the try's '}'-expected/TS1472/'{'-expected all dedup at that same start), else the token is SKIPPED and the list continues (`case  d = …` → TS1068 + skip + `d` parses as a property). `default` keeps its own dispatch arm ("'export' expected."). (3) **catch/finally at statement position route to parseTryStatement** ('try' expected + `parseBlockOrMissing`: a missing `{` yields an EMPTY zero-width Block WITHOUT statement parsing); a try with no catch gets TS1472 + a MISSING finally block. EMIT: missing try/finally blocks print MULTILINE `{\n<indent>}`; a try REBUILT by the optional-catch-binding downlevel (synthetic `_a`, name.pos<0) prints `try { }`; missing catch-clause blocks print `{ }`. (4) **FORCED statements (if/else/loop/with/label bodies — parseForcedStatement/exprStatementForced) disable the statement-level `=`-skip/TS1128 recoveries** — a then-statement at `^=` binds `<missing> ^= {…}` like tsc's unconditional parseStatement; the objLit consumes `;` via semicolon-as-delimiter and closes at the NEXT `}`, so the try-block closes one `}` early and the whole cascade unwinds. (5) **TS1212/1213/1214 are BINDER-side in tsc, gated `if (!file.parseDiagnostics.length)`** — they're in the B310 driver removeAll lists (both paths). (6) `||` REMOVES definitely-falsy left constituents (`true && false || X` types as X); TS2447 covers PLAIN `^`/`&`/`|` on booleans, not just compound. (7) A bare lib-interface Identifier receiver with NO `declare var` companion (Number/String/Boolean — symbol lacks the Variable flag) BAILS member-access checks — the constructor side is unmodeled (`Number.MAX_VALUE`). (8) An outside-function return-expression in a SCRIPT file is checked ONLY when it contains `this` or a CALL (`exprContainsThisOrCall` — tsc re-checks post-emit via the this-capture/helper analysis: multiLine's `this.edit(role)`, parseErrorIncorrectReturnToken's `n.toString()`; a BARE `return val;` gets nothing). (9) **Scanner**: a mid-identifier VALID `\uXXXX` escape decoding to a NON-identifier-part char ENDS the identifier (tsc scanIdentifierParts; a BRACED `\u{…}` cooking to a high surrogate is astral → keeps consuming, but a LONE-surrogate 4-hex `\uD800` is invalid); an identifier-START escape cooking to a non-identifier-start yields TS1127 AT SCAN TIME (invalidEscapeIdentStartPos flag-flush in nextToken) + an Unknown token consuming ONLY the backslash — the hex re-scans as a separate identifier, and the var-decl list's TS1134/','-expected recoveries (incl. a new BEFORE-first-declarator Unknown skip) dedup against it (`var \u0031a;` → `var u0031a;`). (10) **Emitter empty-placeholder rules**: an empty missing-Identifier binary operand never strands a whitespace-only line (blank-line trim for empty LEFT; STATEMENT-indent — not continuation-indent — after the newline for empty RIGHT, in both the chain and plain paths); a both-empty conditional prints `?` end-of-line, `:` alone at the continuation indent, `;` at the statement indent. (11) A bare `:` at a FORCED statement position is a LABELED statement with a zero-width missing label (tsc parseExpressionOrLabeledStatement — `while ( : string, ;`); the TS2693 walker descends a while-body ONLY for that empty-label shape (general loop descent stays forbidden per round-42). (12) A ctor `: <type>` return annotation PARSES (grammar owns the error) — missing type → TS1110 at the follow token unconsumed, then bodyless-ASI. (13) The expression-statement missing-semicolon tail reports "';' expected." for NON-Identifier expressions (tsc parseErrorForMissingSemicolonAfter's fallback); bare-Identifier expressions keep the did-you-mean family; other statement kinds opt in via parseSemicolonRequired (import-equals).
- **A bodyless class accessor is ACCEPTED silently by the parser when ASI applies (tsc parseFunctionBlockOrSemicolon canParseSemicolon) — the TS1005 `'{' expected.` is CHECKER-side (`checkBodylessAccessors` ports tsc checkGrammarAccessor: non-ambient, non-abstract → at `bodylessEnd - 1` len 1, where `bodylessEnd` on GetAccessor/SetAccessor = prevTokenEnd after parseSemicolon; a bodyless SETTER is the synthetic `Block(pos=-1,end=-1)`).** Ambient-ness threads via `inAmbient` on checkPropertyOverrideInStatement. Same-line garbage after the signature keeps the parser-side TS1005 at the `)`. COMPANION (giant): TS1036's suppression uses the REAL-parse-diagnostics gate (grammar-class parser codes don't count), and `checkAmbientStatements` recurses through plain namespace bodies to find NESTED `declare namespace` blocks. Do NOT re-anchor the accessor TS1005 by `indexOf(';')` — the first `;` after a bodyless getter's name can be many members away (the old double-fire at `static tV;`).
- **Multi-base heritage with a GENERIC base before a comma is now PARSED CORRECTLY (fixed 2026-07-04)**: `interface C extends A<T>, B` used to misparse the non-last generic base `A<T>` as a synthetic `ParenthesizedExpression` (instantiation-expression) that DROPPED its `.typeArguments`, so `resolveBaseSym` returned null and the checker FP-emitted TS2499. The parser now sets the instance flag `parsingHeritageBase` around the base spine in `parseExpressionWithTypeArguments` — the postfix `<` branch bails (`typeArgs != null && parsingHeritageBase -> null`, before the `canFollowTypeArgumentsInExpression()` instantiation branch) so `tryScan` restores and the type args are re-read as heritage type arguments (matching tsc's `parseLeftHandSideExpressionOrHigher`, which yields an ExpressionWithTypeArguments verbatim). The flag is RESET inside `parseArgumentList` so a nested `extends foo(bar<T>)` call arg still parses instantiation exprs. The LAST base always worked (its follow token `{`/`implements` is NOT in `canFollowTypeArgumentsInExpression()`); only the `,`/`:`-followed non-last base misparsed. So the non-last generic base's `.typeArguments` ARE now populated and its members are inherited. The B521 `checkMultiBaseInStatement` SOURCE-scan workaround (below) is now redundant-but-harmless (reads source independently); genuine non-entity-name bases (`extends foo()`, `extends (typeof A)`) still fire TS2499 (the flag only suppresses the instantiation collapse, not the primary-paren/call shapes). **B521: under `exactOptionalPropertyTypes`, two OPTIONAL same-named props from different bases must be IDENTICAL (tsc `isTypeIdenticalTo`), not merely mutually-assignable** — `port?: number` (EOPT type exactly `number`) vs `port?: number | undefined` → TS2320. The both-optional comparison has an EOPT-gated branch using `!(a→b && b→a)` (identity proxy) instead of the default `!a→b && !b→a`. ALSO: `collectBase` materializes a `Partial<X>` base into X's members ALL forced optional (`addBaseMembers(forceOptional=true)`) — EOPT-gated, because the relation engine doesn't materialize `Partial<>` here so a Partial-derived prop is otherwise invisible to the conflict scan; the Partial typeArg `X` resolves via `globals`/`currentFileLocals`/`baseSymbol.parent?.exports` (the last for a namespace-scoped `X`). BOTH changes EOPT-gated → non-EOPT multi-base behavior is byte-identical.
- **A JSX tag name that is NOT an identifier/keyword (a number `<1234>`, a bare `<` `< <`, EOF, …) is a FAILED tag (`tagFailed`): emit TS1003 "Identifier expected." at the offending token, leave it UNCONSUMED (zero-width missing tag), and recover as a SELF-CLOSING element (`parseJsxElementOrFragmentBody`, Parser.kt ~5188).** Mirrors tsc `parseJsxElementName`→`parseIdentifierName`. The `parseExpected(Slash)`+`parseExpected(GreaterThan)` recovery calls both land on the still-current offending token (same start as the TS1003) → same-start-deduped → no extra diagnostics; the token re-parses in the surrounding expression. `attributes` MUST be `emptyList()` on the `tagFailed` path (tsc's `parseList(JsxAttributes)` aborts on a non-`{`/non-ident token — otherwise the offending token is wrongly eaten as a boolean-attr shorthand). FP-safe by construction (a `<`+non-identifier can never match a passing baseline — tsc emits TS1003 there). **For the tagFailed path, `emitTs17004IfNeeded` is called BEFORE the TS1003 (not after): tsc emits TS17004 from the CHECKER, so its TS1003 and any later same-position diagnostic (a const-decl `;`-expected) stay adjacent and same-start-dedup; emitting our parser-side TS17004 AFTER the TS1003 interleaves it and breaks that dedup → a spurious TS1005. The span is end-of-`<` either way (the recovery `parseExpected`s don't advance). This is what flipped `parseUnaryExpressionNoTypeAssertionInJsx3` (the leftover `1234 > x` already re-parses correctly as a relational `BinaryExpression`; its TS2365 was missing only because `checkArithmeticOperandTypes` SKIPS `.js` — see `checkJsCrossCategoryRelational`).**
- **JSX-vs-generic-arrow disambiguation in `.tsx` files**: In `parsePrimaryExpression`'s `LessThan` branch, when `isJsxFile`, peek at `<Identifier extends X...>`. If X is an Identifier / type-keyword / open delimiter / `typeof` → generic arrow, fall through to the existing generic-arrow / type-assertion detection. If X is `/`, `>`, or `=` → JSX (boolean attr shorthand `<T extends/>` or attr=value `<T extends={x}/>`). The broader gate `<Identifier extends` (without checking what follows) regresses `parseJsxExtends2_ts`. Tightened gate fixes both `parseJsxExtends2_ts` (JSX) and `declarationEmitRecursiveConditionalAliasPreserved_ts` (generic arrow).

- **A dangling `export` modifier before a bogus identifier + a declaration (`export extension class C`) → TS1128 "Declaration or statement expected." at the `export` keyword (B505, `parseExportDeclaration` else-arm).** The gate MUST be `token == SyntaxKind.Identifier` (a TRUE identifier) AND a lookahead for a declaration-start keyword — NOT `isIdentifier()`: `isIdentifier()` matches the contextual keyword `as`, so it wrongly fires on the UMD `export as namespace X` form (regressed 9 UMD/global tests). Then the bogus identifier re-parses as an expression statement and the trailing declaration on its own. Companion: TS1036 ("Statements are not allowed in ambient contexts") is suppressed when the file has parse diagnostics (tsc grammarErrorOnFirstToken+hasParseDiagnostics) — added to the TS2737/1203/1015 removeAll lists in BOTH single/multi-file paths of TypeScriptCompiler.kt; pass-count-safe because `giant` is the only other corpus test with TS1036+parse-codes and it already fails.
- **`parseArgumentList` recovers a missing comma by RE-ENTERING the list on any EXPRESSION-START follow token (errorRecoveryInClassDeclaration), mirroring tsc `parseDelimitedList`(ArgumentExpressions, isListElement=isStartOfExpression)**: after `!parseOptional(Comma)`, the `=>`/`:` same-line branch (B320/B327) runs FIRST (kept untouched); THEN, before the bare `break`, a non-comma `isStartOfExpression()` token that is NOT `)`/`}`/`;`/EOF reports TS1005 "',' expected." and `continue`s to parse another arg (`foo( public blaz() {} )` → `[public, blaz(), {}]`). This is net-POSITIVE (aligns with tsc → fixed both the errors AND js-emit baselines, which diverged from the mis-parse). It is CONSERVATIVE vs the array-literal sibling (~6354, which additionally emits "Expression expected"+skips a token on a non-expression-start) — do NOT add that skip path here (broader FP surface, unneeded). The abort set (`)`/`}`/`;`/EOF) keeps recovery inside the call.
- **An expression-in-type-position leaves a trailing `.` after a literal type that two recovery sites now consume (expressionTypeNodeShouldError)**: `parsePrimaryType` parses a leading literal (`""`/`3.14`/`false`) as a `LiteralType` via `parsePrimaryExpression`, which does NOT consume `.member` — so `const x: "".typeof(…)` / `type X = "".typeof(…)` stop `parseType` at the `.`. (1) In `parseVariableDeclarationList`'s `.`-recovery, after consuming the `.`, a RESERVED keyword (`typeof`, ∉ `isIdentifierToken`) now emits TS1389 "'X' is not allowed as a variable declaration name." and the list ABORTS unconsumed (so `typeof (…)` re-parses as its own expression statement; `parseSemicolon` is silent on `typeof`). (2) In `parseTypeAliasDeclaration`, a trailing `.` right after `parseType()` (no preceding line break) emits TS1005 "';' expected." and is CONSUMED so the trailing `typeof(…)` re-parses cleanly. Both corpus-unique-FP-safe — a legitimate `A.B` type is consumed as a QualifiedName inside `parsePrimaryType` and never leaves a trailing `.`.
- **`parseInterfaceMembers` (shared by interfaces AND type literals, Parser.kt:2986) ports tsc's `parseList(TypeMembers)` recovery for a malformed `?` in a member position (optionalPropertiesSyntax, blocker #706 scoped to TypeMembers)**: (1) a LEADING `?`/`:` token aborts the element with TS1131 "Property or signature expected." (same-start-deduped against a preceding TS1005) + skips ONE token — `?(): any` → TS1131 at the `?`; the `:` left dangling after a `()?` recovery → TS1131 at the `:`; (2) the member terminator is `parseSemicolon()` (NOT a silent `parseOptional(Semicolon)`), so a same-line non-`;` follower (the `?` after a `()` call sig / `[idx:number]` index sig) reports TS1005 "';' expected." — this is the load-bearing piece that makes `()?: any`/`new ()?: any`/`[idx:number]?` cascade to tsc's exact TS1005+TS1131 (the `?` then deduped, the trailing `:` re-aborted) and the `fn`/`fn?` overload correctly emit TS2386. The WELL-FORMED path is byte-identical (a `;`/newline/`}`/`,` follower never triggers TS1005). Three coupled invariants the malformed cases need: **(a) construct/call-sig SENTINEL NAMES ("new"/"") are excluded from `checkInterfaceMemberOptionalOverloads` (TS2386)** — our construct sigs are `MethodDeclaration(name="new")` and a `new ?(): T` method is ALSO `MethodDeclaration("new")`, so without the skip they wrongly group (tsc keeps ConstructSignature separate from a method named "new"). **(b) the index-signature grammar family TS1017/1018/1019/1020/1021/1025/1096 are all in `GRAMMAR_CLASS_CODES` AND the parse-diagnostic suppression removeAll lists** (single- + multi-file paths) — they are tsc `checkGrammarIndexSignature`→`grammarErrorOnNode` diagnostics, so a REAL parse diagnostic suppresses them all, AND (load-bearing) one of them must NOT count as the suppression TRIGGER for another: `indexSignatureTypeCheck2` has TS1017+TS1019+TS1096 with no real parse error → tsc emits ALL three (omitting TS1017 from GRAMMAR_CLASS_CODES regresses it). **(c) the TS2374 duplicate-index-sig squiggle spans only the `[...]` param list (bracket-match from the opening `[`) when the index sig's VALUE type is missing** (`[idx:number]?` → 13 chars, NOT the `;`-scan that absorbs the recovered `?: any`); the index-sig path's own trailing-`;` consume is now `parseOptional(Semicolon)` (the member loop owns the TS1005, else a double-emit). **(d) within-interface duplicate-property TS2687** ("identical modifiers") fires in `checkDuplicateInterfaceMembers` when a same-named group's members differ in visibility/readonly/OPTIONALITY (`prop: any; prop?: any;`), at EACH member — the merge-group TS2687 (Checker.kt ~30056) only covers MULTI-declaration merges, not a single interface body.


### Emitter gotchas (archived)

- **Comments INTERNAL to an element access (`a /*1*/[ /*2*/ x /*3*/ ]`) are captured by `ElementAccessExpression.preBracketComments`/`argLeadingComments`/`preCloseBracketComments` (parser `combineCommentBuffers()` at the 3 points in the `OpenBracket` branch — read the scanner buffers BEFORE each clearing `nextToken`/`parseExpected`, mirroring `parseArgumentList`'s call-inner-comment capture).** Without the capture they are wiped by the next `nextToken` (lost — `ElementAccessExpression` had no field for them). The emitter's `emitElementAccessInternalComments` reproduces tsc's comment-writer SPACING (verified byte-exact on `elementAccessExpressionInternalComments`): a NEW-LINE-preceded BLOCK comment writes `\n<text> ` (trailing space before the next token), an INLINE block comment writes ` <text>` (leading space, NONE after), a single-line comment writes `\n<text>` (the next item supplies its own newline) — so `Array /*1*/[` (inline, no space after `/*1*/`) but `\n/*1*/ [` (newline-block, space after). Additive (new fields default null) + corpus-unique (only that test has element-access internal comments) → FP-safe; the parser change reads PEEK buffers so a comment-less `a[b]` is byte-identical.
- **Comments INTERNAL to a property access (`Array /*2*/. /*3*/ toString`) split into TWO models, NOT one (propertyAccessExpressionInnerComments).** The `/*3*/` (after-dot) lives in `name.leadingComments` (captured by `parseIdentifierName`); the `/*2*/` (between receiver and dot) splits by whether it follows a NEWLINE: a SAME-LINE `/*2*/` rides the receiver's `trailingComments` (parser `Dot`/`QuestionDot` branch captures `scanner.getTrailingComments()`, gated `result is Identifier` — so the `?.` desugar, which reuses the receiver node 3× in `x===null||x===void 0?void 0:x.m`, DUPLICATES it across all three, matching tsc's quirk), while a NEWLINE-preceded `/*2*/`/`//c` goes into the new `PropertyAccessExpression.preDotComments` field (kept SEPARATE from `name.leadingComments` — merging them was the old bug that emitted afterDot comments before the dot). **The two emit helpers use OPPOSITE newline models and must not be unified:** `emitPreDotComments` (preDot) writes ONE leading newline+indent then per-comment `text` + TRAILING (single-line → newline+indent so the dot drops to the next line; block → space so the dot follows inline) — this also emits the bare multi-line-chain `\n  .` when there are no comments; `emitDotSideComments` (afterDot) writes a LEADING newline+indent per newline-preceded comment and a ` `+text for an inline one. `afterBump` = 2 when `newLineBefore` (the name/afterDot sit one level deeper than the dot), else 1. The `?.` desugar sets `flatComments=true` (column 0, no indent) + drops the inline `/*3*/` (keeps a newline `//c`); the duplicated receiver `/*2*/` is emitted by `emitBinaryLeftIdentTrailing` (gated to an Identifier binary left-operand — `emitIdentifier` doesn't self-emit trailing comments, so no double-emit). FP firewall: comment-less `a.b` has all fields null → byte-identical. LANDMINE: the `numericLiteralsWithTrailingDecimalPoints01` family (test12–17) shares these paths — a single emit model regressed its `3\n//comment\n.toString()` (the preDot single-line comment needs a TRAILING newline before the dot, which only the preDot model provides).
- **Comments INTERNAL to an import/export statement (`/*1*/ import /*2*/ D /*3*/, /*4*/ { … } /*11*/ from /*12*/ "foo"`) are captured as a SINGLE ordered slot list `ImportDeclaration/ExportDeclaration/ExportAssignment.internalComments: List<List<Comment>?>?` — one entry per inter-token boundary in source order; the leading `/*1*/` is the normal statement leading comment, NOT a slot.** The parser uses a member accumulator `ieSlots` (set fresh at the top of `parseImportDeclaration`/`parseExportDeclaration`, appended to by `captureIeSlot()` = `combineCommentBuffers()` AFTER each token-consume, in the nested `parseImportClause`/`parseNamedImports`/`parseImportSpecifier`/`parseNamedExports`/`parseExportSpecifier` too); the emitter consumes them SEQUENTIALLY via `beginIeSlots(node.internalComments)` + `emitIeSlot()` at each boundary — so the emit traversal order MUST exactly mirror the parse capture order. Each slot emits via `emitElementAccessInternalComments` (inline block → ` /*…*/`, no trailing space; the next token supplies its own leading separator). **Two load-bearing traps:** (1) `import D from "x"` (default, NO bindings) — capture the after-name slot ONLY when a comma follows (`if (token == Comma) captureIeSlot()`), else the after-name and the caller's `beforeFrom` slot read the SAME buffer → double-capture/double-emit; (2) the sequential model assumes the emitted structure matches the parsed structure, so type-only filtering that DROPS specifiers/bindings would misalign — but this is FP-safe because `internalComments` is non-null ONLY when there ARE internal comments (corpus-unique to `importExportInternalComments`, which has no type-only specifiers), so the slot machinery is inert for every other test.
- **tsc applies `ListFormat.Indented` UNCONDITIONALLY to object-literal property lists (even single-line ones), so a property VALUE that wraps to a new line lands ONE level deeper than the bare binary continuation (8 spaces, not 4)** — `emitObjectLiteral`'s single-line branch carries no indent level, so it must bump `indentLevel++` around a property whose value wraps (`sourceMapValidationDestructuringForOfObjectBindingPatternDefaultValues2`: a for-of binding-pattern default `{ skills: {…} = \n  {…} }`). The trigger is corpus-unique: `prop is PropertyAssignment && prop.initializer is BinaryExpression && hasNewLineInSource(left.end, right.pos)` (the SAME condition the binary emitter uses to start the RHS on a new line — the binary emitter adds its own continuation level, this adds the missing object-literal level). Do NOT make the single-line branch unconditionally `indentLevel++` (it would re-indent every nested wrap corpus-wide). A sibling `for ({…} = …; …)` (the `=` at the for-init/binary level, not a property value) stays at 4 spaces (its value doesn't wrap).

### Transformer gotchas (archived)

- **Decorated COMPUTED property/method keys use a SECOND temp pool (Transformer `fileHasDecoratedComputedKeys` gate, decoratorsOnComputedProperties)**: pool 1 (tempVarCounter) = undecorated computed-key-with-init temps + class-expression wrapper temps; pool 2 = decorated keys, allocated as `@@decKeyN` placeholders (nextDecKeyTempName) and renamed at file end CONTINUING pool 1's counter (`var _a.._p; var _q.._51;` at file top — tsc prints the classProperties hoist statement above the ts-decorator one and names by print order; the rename uses the dedicated full-tree `renameTempInStmt/Expr/ClassElement` trio because the shared replaceIdentifierIn* helpers skip class members/comma lists). `tempNameFor` after the 24 letters is NUMERIC (`_0`, `_1`… = tsc makeTempVariableName count-26), NOT two-letter `_aa`. The pendingCaptures stream carries `Pair<String?, Expression>` — a null name is a BARE side-effect evaluation of a REMOVED uninitialized computed key in declaration order (pure Identifier keys dropped, tsc isSimpleInlineableExpression; the old separate trailing bare-statement emitter is gated OFF under the file gate). A DECORATED kept method's own key becomes `temp = expr` inside its brackets; __decorate's key = the pool-2 temp or the computed string/numeric literal; class-expression leftovers flow through the `pendingKeyEvals` result channel into `void (_c = class B {…}, evals…, _c)` (all-hosted → no wrapper temp).

- **A SINGLE-element destructuring-EXPORT inlines its initializer with NO temp; multi-element keeps the temp (B-downlevelLetConst13)**: `export let [x] = e` / `export let {a: x} = e` (CJS) → `exports.x = e[0]` / `exports.x = e.a`; the same inside a `namespace` → `Ns.x = e[0]` / `Ns.x = e.a` (NO `var _a` hoist) — the initializer is referenced exactly once so caching is unnecessary. The CJS path runs through the SHARED helper `tryExpandObjectBinding` (3 callers: 2 CJS + 1 System), which now flattens a 1-element `ArrayBindingPattern` in addition to objects; the namespace path is the single-element fast-path in `transformNamespaceBody`'s Object/ArrayBindingPattern branches. ALL gated to a single `BindingElement` with a simple-Identifier name, no rest, no default — multi-element falls back to the temp form. Also: a re-synthesized CommonJS `"use strict"` prologue preserves the SOURCE directive's quote style via `StringLiteralNode.singleQuote` (`useStrictSingleQuote` threaded through `transformToCommonJS`) — tsc keeps `'use strict'` if the source wrote single quotes.
- **A `super.X` read in a static FIELD initializer (`static yy = super.x`, target<ES2022) routes through B344's static-BLOCK machinery (B556)** — `transformClassBody`'s `!useDefineForClassFields` static path collects `staticPropsWithSuper` (static PropertyDeclarations whose initializer SOURCE TEXT contains `\bsuper\b`), OR's it into `staticBlockNeedsAlias` (allocates the `_a` class alias) AND the heritage-capture gate (allocates `_b`, rewrites `extends P`→`extends (_b = P)`), then applies `replaceSuperReadsInExpr(finalInit, heritageTempVar, classTempVar)` at the static-field initializer build (→ `Reflect.get(_b, "X", _a)`). Static METHODS and INSTANCE fields keep raw `super.` (separate emit paths — methods are lexically valid super at ES2015; instance fields go through the constructor-prologue path). FP firewall: a `static <name> = …super…` initializer is corpus-unique (superAccess2).
- **Private-field downlevel (target < ES2022) has TWO disjoint class branches in the class-member transformer (B500): a native class-EXPRESSION/es5-downlevel branch AND a native class-DECLARATION branch (`needsPrivateFieldDownlevel && !isClassExpression`), and they must BOTH register the WeakMap state var in `computedPropHoistNames` (gated `functionScopeDepth == 0`).** Only the class-expression branch did originally — so a native (es2015+) class DECLARATION in a CJS module left `var _C_x;` BELOW the `Object.defineProperty(exports,…)` preamble (tsc always hoists it ABOVE). The CJS-preamble hoist (`computedPropHoistNames` move, Transformer.kt ~2852) keys on that set. SEPARATELY: the `#x in obj` (private-in) downlevel → `__classPrivateFieldIn(state, obj)` is a dedicated case at the TOP of `transformBinaryExpression` (`operator == InKeyword`, left a `#`-prefixed Identifier; state = field WeakMap or method/accessor brand); and `__classPrivateFieldGet/Set/In` were MISSING from the tslib named-import `when` (helpers used in bodies but never imported) — the tslib import list is alphabetically SORTED (verified: every corpus baseline). Under importHelpers, the private-field helpers must be in BOTH `needsInlineHelperForCjs`/`needsAnyInlineHelper` (so `const tslib_1 = require("tslib")` is emitted) and the require insert-index must add `prependedCount` (so it lands after leading hoisted vars). ESM places the tslib import AFTER leading private-field WeakMap var hoists (`var _A_x; import {...} from "tslib";`).
- **Auto-accessor + static-private-method downlevel (target < ES2022) both reuse the private-field WeakMap machinery (B501/B502).** A PUBLIC instance `accessor x` field (non-decorator) is desugared as a PRE-PASS at the top of `transformClassBody` (param renamed `membersIn`, then `val members = <expanded>`) into a private storage field `#x_accessor_storage` + a public `get x()`/`set x(v)` whose bodies read/write `this.#x_accessor_storage` — the synthesized members then flow through the existing private-field downlevel (WeakMap var, ctor `.set(this, void 0)`, `__classPrivateFieldGet/Set(...,"f")` bodies, trailing `= new WeakMap()`). Static/private-named (`accessor #a`) auto-accessors use a different descriptor/brand form, left to other paths (no failing test needs them). A STATIC private METHOD (`static #m()`) in a class DECLARATION has NO brand WeakSet — the class value itself, captured as the existing class-alias temp (`classTempVar`, which B502 force-allocates early via `staticMethodAlias` so member-body call rewrites see it), IS both receiver and private-env state: `Class.#m()` → `__classPrivateFieldGet(_a, _a, "m", _Foo_m).call(_a)` (the call rewrite at Transformer.kt ~9365 uses `entry.brandVar` as receiver when the new `PrivateEnvEntry.isStatic` flag is set), and the method fn var emits in the same trailing comma list as `_a = Foo` (`aliasAndPrivate`), hoisted after the alias. Gated `!useDefineForClassFields` (the trailing-emission infra lives in that block). Static private FIELDS in declarations remain unbuilt (no test).
- **tsc assigns generated temp NAMES at PRINT time per scope** — a function-local temp avoids ENCLOSING scopes' generated names (`_r` when the file used `_a.._q`) while SIBLING functions each restart at `_a`. The transform-time `withFreshTempVarCounter` per-function reset reproduces only the sibling case; matching both needs print-time name resolution (parked, staticFieldWithInterfaceContext). PARTIAL infra (B348): top-level destructuring-CHAIN temps allocate as placeholders via `nextChainTempName()` and rename at file end (`renameChainTemps`, base = final tempVarCounter) so file-HOISTED temps (`var _a..;` printed at top) grab the first names; function scopes name immediately — the gate is `tempScopeDepth > 0 || functionScopeDepth > 0` (method bodies bump only the latter; missing it leaks raw placeholders into the emit).
- **Object-rest in a destructuring-ASSIGNMENT target nested inside an ARRAY pattern (B504, `[{ ...x }] = expr` / `for ([{ ...y }] of …)`) routes through `targetHasNestedObjectRest`/`flattenRestAssignmentTarget`, NOT the object-literal-spread transform** — the top-level `{ ...rest } = expr` case is owned by `transformObjectRestDestructuringAssignment`; the nested-in-array case is a SEPARATE hook in `transformBinaryExpression` (array LHS) + `transformForOfStatement` (array-pattern initializer, `< ES2018`). Without it the `{ ...x }` element is wrongly emitted as `Object.assign({}, x)` (an invalid assignment target). The for-of moves the destructure into a `let`-loop-var body and allocates the **inner destructure temp BEFORE the loop var** (tsc order `_b` then `_c`) — implemented placeholder-then-patch (flatten against a dummy source, then patch parts[0].right to the loop var). Corpus-UNIQUE shape (1 file: nestedObjectRest); `targetHasNestedObjectRest` looks for an object-rest (`SpreadAssignment`) at any nesting, NOT array-rest (`SpreadElement`, native downlevel).
- **CJS import-elision + the module-temp `mod_N` counter is TRANSFORM-time, not tsc's print-time — an ELIDED import that consumed a `mod_N` shifts every later `require("mod")` temp (+1) (es6ExportEqualsInterop).** THREE coupled load-bearing gates: (1) **elide an UNUSED default import** (`import x from "m"`, x∉uses) BEFORE `generateModuleTempName` — but the usage set is `valueReferencedNames ∪ importEqualsReferencedNames` (a default import can be referenced ONLY by an import-equals entity-name reference `import X = x.Y`, which `collectValueReferences` EXCLUDES → exportDefaultProperty) AND gated `!options.emitDecoratorMetadata` (a TYPE-position use `db: database` becomes a runtime `__metadata("design:type", db_1.default)` ref that `collectValueReferences` can't see → decoratorMetadataWithImportDeclarationNameCollision6). (2) **elide a type-only NAMESPACE import** via `isTypeOnlyNamespaceImportModule` (covers an `export = <type-only>` ambient module — the whole namespace is type-only, tsc elides the require but keeps dangling `y1.a` uses), which — UNLIKE `isTypeOnlyImportRequire`/`isAmbientModuleTypeOnly` — treats a body-less `declare module "path";` as an UNTYPED runtime module (NOT type-only → keep the require, esModuleInteropTslibHelpers). (3) **preserve a DETACHED (blank-line-separated) `/// <reference path>` / comment on an ERASED import-equals** the same way the ImportDeclaration case does (blank-line filter, NOT `orphanedComments` which always keeps triple-slash even when ADJACENT → aliasOnMergedModuleInterface/declFile/exportAssignment drop the adjacent reference).
- **Static-class-field async arrow defensive capture**: For `class C { static field = async (...) => ... }` under target<ES2022, TypeScript pre-emits `var _a; _a = C;` even when the async arrow body has no `this` reference. The `__awaiter` template is conceptually `this`-binding for static-field-initialized async arrows even when the actual `__awaiter` call passes `void 0`. `staticPropsWithThis` filter in `Transformer.kt` matches this shape via `prop.initializer is ArrowFunction && ModifierFlag.Async in modifiers`. Skip the gate at your peril — removing it regresses `asyncArrowInClassES5_ts`.

### Module resolution diagnostic gotchas (archived)

- **TS6059 / TS5011-`..` / TS5055 for an `outDir`-but-no-`rootDir` program whose IMPLICIT rootDir (the tsconfig directory) doesn't contain all source files is a DEDICATED driver block in `TypeScriptCompiler.kt` (right after the existing TS5011 emitter), NOT the existing emitters (`pathMappingBasedModuleResolution_rootImport_*realRootFile`).** The existing TS5011 emitter is gated `declaration||composite` AND `else -> null` for the common-dir-ABOVE-tsconfig (`..`) case, and the existing TS5055 is gated `outDir==null` — so this shape reaches neither → the new block is purely additive (no double-emit). It emits: TS6059 ("File 'X' is not under 'rootDir' 'Y'") at each import specifier (regex-scan `from "spec"` over the LOCAL file `.content` + `positionToLineCharacter`) resolving to an emittable source file OUTSIDE the tsconfig dir; TS5011 (`'..'` relative) when the common source dir is a strict ancestor of the tsconfig dir; TS5055 ("Cannot write file 'X.js' because it would overwrite input file") for each external pure-JS input (its output collides with the input because the outDir-remap of an out-of-rootDir file resolves back onto itself — the existing skip at ~1301 already suppresses the EMIT, so only the diagnostic was missing). FP firewall (corpus-unique): absolute tsconfig dir + absolute import specifiers + ≥1 external emittable source file (the 4 passing siblings keep their real files INSIDE `/root/src` → no external file → inert).

- **The INVERSE of the FP-suppression in `checkNamedImportFromExportEqualsInDts`: a `.d.ts` `import { N } from "./m"` where `./m` is `export = V` and `V`'s member `N` is contributed by a cross-file `declare module "./m"` AUGMENTATION must NOT fire TS2305 (round 269, conflictingDeclarationsImportFromNamespace1/2)** — `getExportEqualsMemberNames` resolves only the base export= value's type, NOT augmentations, so it FP'd. Guard via `augmentationAddsExportMember(targetFile, name)` (scans all `declare module "<spec>"` whose <spec> resolves to the target for an interface-member/top-level decl of `name`); FN-safe (a missed augmentation only under-fires). COMPANION dedicated walker `checkConflictingNamespaceImportSelfConst`: `import * as N from '<bare-pkg>'` + a same-name self-calling `export const N = () => N()` → TS2497 (specifier) + TS7023 (circular implicit return, const name). Additive — the existing checkDefaultImports TS2497 branch requires a locally-DECLARED class/fn export= target (here `pick` is a re-exported imported alias), and the self-ref-return walker is silent under the import name-conflict. Corpus-unique gate: only conflict1/2 pair `import * as N` with a same-name self-calling-arrow const (`moment`/`_` siblings use a `||` init / for-loop var).
- **A bare `import { NAME } from '<pkg>'` where `<pkg>` is a `@types`/node_modules `export = <namespace>` module, and NAME is ONLY an EMPTY/uninstantiated `namespace NAME {}` added by a cross-file `declare module '<pkg>'` augmentation → TS2305 (B548, `checkBareAtTypesExportEqualsMissingNamedImport`)** — the standard named-import check doesn't resolve bare @types export= namespaces, so this is a dedicated additive walker. FP firewall (corpus-unique): collect the export= namespace's OWN members AST-side (NOT the merged symbol — `collectModuleAugmentations` pollutes it), and fire only when NAME is BOTH absent from those AND resolves ONLY to an empty/uninstantiated `namespace NAME {}` (`isNamespaceInstantiated`==false) in some `declare module '<pkg>'` — any var/class/fn/interface/instantiated-namespace member named NAME (augmentExportEquals5 `Request`, mergedDeclarations7 `Passport`, react `Component` type-alias) returns false. `emitTs2305` renders bare `'<pkg>'` as `Module '"<pkg>"'` (resolveModuleSpecifier returns null for bare → falls back to the raw name).
- **A CJS namespace import (`import * as X`) of an `export = N` module fires TS2497 ONLY when N carries NEITHER `SymbolFlags.Module` NOR `Variable` (B546)** — the B98.r18 branch (`checkDefaultImports`) gated only on N being a class/function, but tsc SUPPRESSES TS2497 when N ALSO merges a NAMESPACE or a `var` (`declare function foo; declare namespace foo; export = foo`): the namespace-style import then yields a synthetic-default value `{ default: <fn> }`, not the bare fn. The `targetHasNsOrVarMerge` gate (a same-named `ModuleDeclaration`/`VariableStatement` in the target file) is the firewall; `es6ImportEqualsExportModuleCommonJsError`'s pure `class a` (no merge) still fires. The resulting `invoke(foo)` → TS2345 (the `{ default: … }` object provides no call signature) + related TS7038 anchored at the import is owned by the DEDICATED additive walker `checkNamespaceImportSyntheticDefaultCall` (we don't model the synthetic-default TYPE, so the general arg-check emits nothing). Corpus-unique (TS7038 only in `esModuleInteropPrettyErrorRelatedInformation`).
- **Declaration-emit nameability (TS2883/TS4023/TS2527, `checkDeclarationEmitNameability` + `checkDeclarationEmitComputedSymbolNameability`) is self-contained AST+module-resolution — NO type engine; also runs under the `declarationOnly` checker.** Two load-bearing gates: the TS4023 symbol firewall `computedKeyResolvesToSymbol` (key must resolve to a `Symbol()`-init or `unique symbol` decl — without it enum-member/undefined computed keys FP), and `isValueAccessibleIn` must NOT consult `globals` (Blocker #3 merges all file locals into it, so cross-module symbols wrongly count accessible) — use KNOWN_GLOBALS. A top-level `unique symbol` is accessible via `typeof value`, so only NESTED ones trigger TS2527.
- **`typeof <moduleAlias>` assignability (TS2741, typeofExternalModules family) is a SELF-CONTAINED AST shape (`moduleAliasTypeofShape` + `tryEmitModuleNamespaceTs2741`), deliberately NOT a namespace `Type` in the relation engine** — namespace-as-Type flows too broadly through assignability (rated MEDIUM-risk, rejected). To extend (namespace-import, `export default` module shapes), extend `moduleAliasTypeofShape`, never the relation engine. An `export = X` (module-local class) shape is the STATIC side of X plus a synthetic `prototype` (null pos → no TS2728). **The INTERNAL-namespace-alias sibling (`import A = Outer.instantiated`; NOT cross-file `require`) is `checkNamespaceTypeofAssignability` (B514), also AST-based + NOT a namespace Type**: `typeof <namespace>` shape = the namespace's VALUE-export members (exported class/function/enum/var + INSTANTIATED sub-namespace via `isNamespaceInstantiated` — interfaces and uninstantiated sub-namespaces are type-only, EXCLUDED); emits TS2741 for `x = Y` (`x: typeof N1`, a value member of N1 missing from `typeof Y`, squiggle the LHS ident + TS2728 at the missing member) and TS2708 "Cannot use namespace 'A' as a value." for `typeof A`/`typeof A.B` where A aliases an uninstantiated namespace. The display for an aliased namespace-typeof uses the RESOLVED namespace's simple name (`typeof instantiated`, not `typeof importInst`). `moduleAliasTypeofShape` is keyed on `getImportEqualsSpecifier` (a module `require` specifier) so it returns null for internal aliases → the two paths are disjoint.
- **TS2349 for CALLING a CJS-default-as-namespace (`cjsDefaultNsShapes` + early branch in `checkSingleCallExpressionTypes`)**: a CJS module's `default` import is the WHOLE `module.exports` namespace (`typeof import("mod")`) — not callable. Two narrowly-gated shapes only: nodenext ESM importer ← `.cts`/`.cjs` with DIRECT `export default`; commonjs+esModuleInterop ← `export =` → require-alias → proper-namespace CHAIN. **DO NOT broaden to all commonjs default-imports** — a direct `export default` under esModuleInterop yields the callable VALUE via `__importDefault` (and `export = fn/class` are callable); the chain requirement is the FP firewall.
- **TS5097 ("import path can only end with '.ts' when allowImportingTsExtensions is enabled") has TWO emission branches in the import-specifier walker (Checker.kt ~23362, B141)**: (1) the original RELATIVE branch (`./foo.ts`); (2) the BARE branch for a `paths`-mapped specifier ending in `.ts`/`.tsx`. The bare branch approximates TS's **resolvedUsingTsExtension** rule with TWO load-bearing checks: the longest-prefix-matching `paths` pattern KEY must NOT itself end in the extension (`"baz/*.ts"` absorbs the `.ts` -> no error), AND a substituted `paths` target must end in `.ts`/`.tsx` AND directly name an existing source file in `fileResults` (use the DIRECT-key forms `t`/`/t`/`./t`, NOT `resolveModuleSpecifier` which does directory-index resolution -- else a package-dir name like `zone.tsx/index.d.ts` matched by `"*":["foo/*"]` wrongly fires TS5097). Both checks are required: dropping the file-resolution gate FP'd `pathMappingBasedModuleResolution_withExtensionInName`; the key-ext check is TS's pattern-absorption rule.


### Module detection gotchas (archived)

- **A `declare global { namespace X { export const Y } }` member ALSO merges into the module that did `export as namespace X` (B555)** — two coupled pieces: (1) `collectGlobalAugmentationNames` registers the `declare global { namespace X }` name `X` as a global value name (so a bare `X.member` resolves and skips the UMD-global TS2686 reporter); (2) `checkCrossFileModuleAugmentationDuplicates` projects the namespace's `export const/let` members onto the UMD module's `(targetFile, name)` conflict groups via a `umd→moduleFile` regex map, and sorts `others` by (fileName, pos) so the hub's related-info is TS6203(first)+TS6204(rest) in source order (exportAsNamespace_augment). FP firewall: the projection fires only when `export as namespace X` exists AND the group reaches ≥2 decls (`total<2` guard; an empty-body `declare global { namespace N {} }` contributes nothing).
- **`symbolToQualifiedName`'s `"basename".X` module-qualifier is EXPORT-gated, not declared-in-file-gated (B385)**: a file-level namespace renders as `"basename".X` ONLY when it is EXPORTED (part of the module's public shape) or imported from another module; a NON-exported local `namespace X {}` in an ES-module file renders unqualified (`X`). Gating on "declared in this file" instead regresses `internalAlias*` (`export namespace c` must keep `"file".c`). Companion: a USER type-only/UNINSTANTIATED namespace used in VALUE position (`Library.foo`) is owned by TS2708 — the namespace-member TS2339 in `checkMemberAccessMissing` must BAIL for it, but the bail must be gated `declarations.none { it in builtinLibDecls }` and placed AFTER the TS2550 lib-target check (else `Intl.PluralRules` loses its lib hint).
- **A BARE (non-relative) ambient `declare module "X"` is a STANDALONE ambient module, NOT an augmentation of a sibling SOURCE file whose basename happens to be `X` (B387)**: `collectModuleAugmentations` merges augmented exports into the resolved target file's locals, but that merge must be SKIPPED when the specifier is bare AND the target resolves to a plain `.ts`/`.tsx` source (not `.d.ts`, not `node_modules`) — tsc only augments `.d.ts`/package ambient modules via a bare name, never a relatively-resolvable source. Without the skip, the bare ambient's members pollute the real source module's exports and a genuinely-missing-member access (`file1.bar()`) silently passes. Relative augmentations (`declare module "./foo"`) DO augment their `.ts` target — gate on the specifier being non-relative, not just the target file type.
- **`resolveSpecifierAnywhere`/`resolveRelativeIncludingIndex` DO resolve bare `.`/`..` (B472), but ONLY to `<dir>/index` — NEVER to a sibling FILE whose stem equals the dir** (`.` from `/a/x.ts` → `/a/index.ts`, not the file `/a.ts`; the dir-index-only rule is keyed on the original `specifierIn == "." || ".."`, so route `.`/`..` through WITHOUT pre-normalizing to `./`/`../`). The default-import member-access TS2339 for such an import is a DEDICATED walker `checkDotDirIndexDefaultImports` (reads the resolved `export default {objLit}`'s keys), NOT the shared import-receiver member-access path (un-gating that is FP-risky, B152/B153). All B472 pieces are corpus-FP-safe: only `importWithTrailingSlash` imports from exactly `.`/`..`.
- **A checkJs CJS `module.exports = { name: "<lit>" }` JS export `name` (typed `string`) plus a cross-file `declare module "./X"` augmentation `export const/var name: T` is a DUPLICATE → TS2300 (B553, `checkCjsExportAugmentationConflict`)** — symmetric TS2300/TS6203 at the augmentation decl + the CJS object-literal key (reuses `emitAugReexportDup`), and a `import { name } from "./X"; name.<method>()` whose method ∉ `string` → TS2551 (the JS export wins so `name` is `string`). Purely ADDITIVE (CJS imports resolve to `any`; the `.js` checkPropertyAccess skip B152/B153 stays gated). **GOTCHA: resolve the `./X` JS target with `resolveRelativeIncludingIndex` — `resolveAugmentationTargetFile`/`resolveModuleSpecifier` deliberately skip `.js`.** FP firewall: the augmentation member must be `export const/var` (an `interface` legally merges with a JS class export → excluded) AND the CJS target must be `module.exports = {objLit}` with a STRING-literal-valued matching key (corpus-unique).
- **A name declared in a `declare module "X"` AUGMENTATION that is ALSO re-exported into X via `export {N} from './other'` is a DUPLICATE (B412, `checkModuleAugmentationReexportDuplicates`)** — TS2300 for a type-alias aug decl, TS2451 for a block-scoped const/let, reported at BOTH the aug decl and the re-export specifier with cross-referencing TS6203. The whole `.d.ts` duplicate-identifier pipeline is skipped and the B92d walker (`checkCrossFileModuleAugmentationDuplicates`) only covers the target's OWN exports with EXPORTED aug decls, so this shape needed its own walker. FP-safe (a re-exported name + a non-mergeable aug decl is always a duplicate). **Which decl wins for TYPE RESOLUTION when both an augmentation `type Row2={a}` and a re-exported `interface Row2{b}` exist is FIXED (B507): tsc resolves the AUGMENTATION's local `type Row2={a}`.** Our cross-file symbol merge conflates the re-export alias (its `ImportSpecifier`/`ExportSpecifier` plus the re-exported `InterfaceDeclaration` pulled in via `mergeSymbolTable` pollution) with the augmentation's `TypeAliasDeclaration` onto ONE symbol that ends up flagged `Interface` — so `getDeclaredTypeOfSymbolWorker`'s `Class|Interface` branch resolved `{b}`. The fix is a FIRST `when` branch in `getDeclaredTypeOfSymbolWorker`: an `Alias` symbol carrying BOTH a `TypeAliasDeclaration` AND a re-export specifier (`ImportSpecifier`/`ExportSpecifier`) resolves the local type-alias. FP firewall: that exact decl-combo is corpus-unique (a plain interface, or a plain re-export of a type-alias, keeps its own resolution). The augmentation's decl reaches the symbol via the GLOBALS merge in `collectModuleAugmentations`; the LOCALS merge is skipped (`resolveModuleSpecifier(".")` returns null, would need `resolveAugmentationTargetFile`), but the globals path already carries the decl so it was unnecessary for this flip.
- **A BARE single-segment unresolvable specifier under EXPLICIT `@module: commonjs` gets TS2307 (B524) — but the FP firewall is load-bearing because the resolver does NOT model symlinks or untyped `.js` node_modules (the documented B98.r2 dead-end).** The `else if (!isRelative …)` B98.r107 branch in `checkUnresolvedModules` now includes `ModuleKind.CommonJS` BUT only with TWO extra suppressions vs the `null`/ES-module case: (1) `bareModulePackageInAnyInput(pkg)` consults the RAW input set `allInputFileNames` (untyped `.js` @Filename files are NOT in `fileResults` — `hasNodeModulesPackage` misses them) → covers `extendsUntypedModule`; (2) `bareModuleSymlinkTargetDir(pkg)` suppresses when a `<dir>/<pkg>/index.{ts,tsx,d.ts}` exists ANYWHERE (the symlink TARGET is the only thing materialized — the `node_modules/<pkg>/` symlink path itself is absent from both `fileResults` and `allInputFileNames`) → covers `moduleResolutionWithSymlinks`/`_withOutDir`. Both suppressions UNDER-fire (a coincidental `<pkg>/index.ts` dir, or any package we can't see, → no TS2307) which is the SAFE direction (we never fire bare-commonjs TS2307 otherwise → FN harmless). Do NOT remove the suppressions to "tidy up" — they ARE the firewall that makes this re-tread of B98.r2 land clean. `decoratorMetadataTypeOnlyImport` (`unknown-module`, no node_modules anywhere) is the sole flip.
- **TS2749 for a DEFAULT import used as a type (B466, `defaultImportIsStrictlyValue`/`defaultExportClassification`)**: `isValueOnlyTypeRef` suppresses TS2749 for aliases EXCEPT a default import whose target module's default export is unambiguously a VALUE (no type meaning). **MUST scan the CURRENT file's own `import` statements (`fileResults[fileName].sourceFile.statements`), NOT the alias symbol's `declarations`** — `mergeSymbolTable` addAll's same-name imports from OTHER files onto the shared symbol (CLAUDE.md merge-pollution), so iterating `alias.declarations` picks the wrong import and FPs (a `zzz`-from-./a used as type wrongly resolved ./b). Companion: an `export default interface` (type-space) MERGES with any value-providing default → it is excluded from TS2528 "multiple default exports" participants when a REF_VALUE/EXPR/class-or-function-DECL/local-`export{value as default}` default coexists (`checkMultipleDefaultExports`); two type-space defaults (interface + interface-REF_TYPE) still conflict.
- **`BaselineFormatter` pretty multi-file footer uses MIXED line endings (B92)**: the `Errors  Files` per-file summary table (emitted only when `pretty && errorCount>1 && distinctFiles>1`) has the `Found N errors in M files.` line + the blank separator line ending in CRLF (`\r\n`), but `Errors  Files` and each `<count.padStart(6)>  <file>:<firstLine>` row end in LF only (`\n`). This is TypeScript's actual byte output (verified via `od -c`) — don't "normalize" them to all-CRLF or the 9 footer-baselines (dup-identifier family + `manyCompilerErrorsInTheTwoFiles` + `typedefCrossModule5`) won't match.
- **Index signature param type validity (B68.4)**: When the index sig param type is an invalid `KeywordTypeNode` (`any`/`boolean`/`void`/etc., not `string`/`number`/`symbol`), the Parser SKIPS the TS1021 "must have a type annotation" emission and the Checker emits TS1268 instead (mirroring TypeScript — they don't double-report). The Checker walker `checkIndexSigInStatement` covers class / interface / module / TypeAliasDeclaration / VariableStatement TypeLiteral annotations.
- **Index-sig param TS1268/TS1337/TS1021 for an ALIASED `TypeReference` or a `UnionType` param is RESOLVED via the type engine (B328, indexerConstraints2, `classifyIndexParamType`)** — `checkIndexSigsInMembers` resolves a non-generic, non-literal `TypeReference`/`UnionType` param with `getTypeFromTypeNode` and classifies: 0 (valid: `string`/`number`/`symbol`, or a union of only-valid members — `string | number` is VALID), 1337 (a literal type or a generic type param, or a union containing one — `"foo" | "bar"` → TS1337), 1268 (any other invalid type — `boolean`/object, **OR unresolvable/`any`-resolved**). **The `errorType`/`anyType` → 1268 mapping is LOAD-BEARING: it preserves the prior "every TypeReference param emits TS1268" behavior, so an unresolvable name (`[index: TypeNotFound]`, declarationEmitIndexTypeNotFound) still errors — my first cut returned 0 there and regressed it.** Unions classify member-by-member (aggregate flags are unreliable for `string | boolean`). **TS1021 for a TypeReference param that resolves VALID but has NO value annotation (`{ [key: Key] }`)** is owned by the CHECKER (the parser suppresses TS1021 for TypeReference params, B292), gated to `pType is TypeReference` so keyword-param TS1021 (parser-emitted) is not double-emitted. The B518 `checkFullIndexConstraints` TS2413 (number-vs-string index value) now also compares NAMED class/interface-instance value types (`isComparableIndexValueType`: primitive OR `Type.Interface` with symbol) — the errorNode reports at whichever index sig is OWN (own number, else own STRING sig — case I has the number sig inherited, else the type name).
- **A checkJs `.js` DEFAULT import binding to an ambient module's `export default <type-only namespace>` is type-only → TS18042 (import) + TS2708 (value uses) (B508).** `declare module "X" { namespace N { export type … }; export default N }` + `import D from "X"` in a `.js`+`@checkJs` file: the default binds to the type-only namespace `N`, so tsc emits TS18042 ("'D' is a type and cannot be imported in JavaScript files. Use 'import(\"X\").D' in a JSDoc type annotation." — the suggestion uses the LOCAL binding name, NOT `.default`) at the import binding, and TS2708 ("Cannot use namespace 'D' as a value.") at every value-position use (`typeof D`, bare `D`). New helper `ambientDefaultExportTypeOnlyNamespace(spec)` reads the ambient `declare module` body AST directly (B494 pattern — `resolveAlias` cannot reach an ambient module's `export default`); finds `export default <ident>` → a body-level `namespace <ident>` that is `isModuleBlockTypeOnly`. Wired in `checkTypeUsedAsValue`'s `ImportDeclaration` case (route the default name to `namespaceOnlyNames` not `valueNames` + emit TS18042, gated `isJsLikeFileName && options.checkJs`) AND the `TypeOfExpression` case — the `namespaceOnlyNames`→TS2708 branch was MISSING there (it only did `typeOnlyNames`→TS2693). `checkTypeUsedAsValue` runs on `.js` files (only `.d.ts` skipped), so this is consistent with the existing `.js` TS2693/TS2708 — NOT an un-gating of the load-bearing `checkPropertyAccess`/`checkCallExpressionTypes` `.js` skip (B152/B153). FP firewall: corpus-rare (4 tests carry TS18042).
- **A namespace import `import * as X` of a module whose exports are ALL type-only is itself type-only → tsc ELIDES the require, and this elision must be IMPORT-specific not name-based (B503).** The name-based `pureTypeNames = typeOnlyDeclaredNames - runtimeDeclaredNames` mechanism CANNOT catch the merge case: when the file also declares a same-name local `namespace X` (or any value `X`), that local puts `X` in `runtimeDeclaredNames`, so `X` is never "pure type" even though the IMPORT side is type-only (the namespace owns the value side; `X.foo`/`export {X}` bind to it, not the import). The fix is the conservative checker query `moduleHasOnlyTypeOnlyExports(modSpec, srcFile)` (true ONLY when the target resolves and EVERY export is a directly-declared type-only entity — interface/type-alias/uninstantiated-namespace/erasable-const-enum; false on ANY uncertainty: unresolvable, `export =`/`export default`, `export {…}`/`export *` re-exports, exported value decls, instantiated namespaces, `export import`), hooked at the CJS namespace-import emission and **gated `!options.isolatedModules`** (per-file emit can't prove type-only-ness there, so tsc keeps the require — un-gating it is a +1/-1 swap regressing `isolatedModulesReExportType`). FP-safe because under-classifying (→ keep require) is the current behavior; only a provably-type-only module elides.

### Cross-file infrastructure gotchas (archived)

- **A cross-file global `type N` (script file) + `namespace N` (DIFFERENT script file) is TS2649, NOT TS2300 (B449, `checkCrossFileTypeAliasNamespaceConflict`)** — tsc routes this degenerate global merge through the module-augmentation path: "Cannot augment module 'N' with value exports because it resolves to a non-module entity." at the type-alias name (the type alias is the "non-module entity"). AST-based (binder doesn't model the cross-file global merge). FP firewall is the SCRIPT-file gate (`!isModuleFile`): the only other corpus file with `type X`+`namespace X` is `reexportNameAliasedAndHoisted`, whose files are MODULE files (module-scoped → no global conflict, excluded). Cross-FILE only (same-file is TS2300, owned by the dup-identifier pipeline).

### Binder gotchas (archived)

- **block-NESTED let/const redeclaration (TS2451) is owned by the dedicated additive walker `checkBlockScopedRedeclarations`, NOT the binder duplicate pipeline** (the binder doesn't bind block-scoped decls, above). It processes Block/switch/try/catch/finally scopes ONLY — function/method BODIES are already covered by an existing function-scope emitter, so processing them again DOUBLE-emits (the tell: a fn-body `let x; let x` reports TS2451 twice); recurse INTO function bodies for their nested blocks but don't treat the body itself as a scope. File/module top-level stays with the binder pipeline.
- **`type X` + `const/let X` → TS2451 is a JS-ONLY rule (B485)**: in a `.ts`/`.tsx` file a type alias and a value const legally COEXIST (different declaration spaces) — NO error. tsc only reports the value-side redeclaration in a `.js` file, where `type X` is illegal (TS8008) and so does not occupy the type space.
