The duplicate-pipeline branch is gated `isJsLikeFileName(fileName)`; do NOT broaden it to `.ts` (the original 16.4ek comment's premise that `type foo=5; const foo=5` errors in TS was wrong — see longObjectInstantiationChain1's `type merge<…>` + `declare const merge`). **B485 had a HOLE (fixed round 210): the FOLLOWING general `allBlockScoped` branch (Checker.kt ~29237) did NOT exclude `type`, so a `.ts` `type X` + `const X` (which skips the JS-gated branch) fell through and FP'd TS2451 anyway.** The block-scoped redeclaration now counts only the let/const decls (`blockScopedDecls`, require ≥2) and emits TS2451 only for THEM — a `type` alias neither counts toward nor receives the diagnostic (so `type X` + single `const X` → no error; `type X` + 2×`let X` → TS2451 on the two lets only). Provably FP-safe: no passing `.ts` test can have tsc emit TS2451 on a type alias.
- **A `var` (or for-init `var`) in a NESTED block hoisting into a FUNCTION body whose TOP declares a same-named let/const → TS2451 on BOTH, owned by `checkVarHoistRedeclaration` (B455)** — the binder hoists nested-block vars into NAMESPACE/FILE scopes (so the duplicate pipeline's Block-var collection, gated `inNamespaceBody`, already fires TS2451 there) and body-TOP for-init vars are collected by `checkDuplicateDeclarations`, but NEITHER covers a function body's NESTED-block vars (binder doesn't bind function bodies). The dedicated walker handles function bodies only (collect body-top let/const ∩ nested-block vars). **COMPANION: `checkVarDeclForShadowing` (the TS2481 "cannot initialize outer scoped variable" walker) now SUPPRESSES TS2481 when a var matches the nearest function/MODULE body-TOP let/const** (threaded `bodyTop` set) — that's a redeclaration (TS2451 from binder for ns/file, from `checkVarHoistRedeclaration` for functions), NOT a TS2481. TS2481 still fires for a var matching an INTERMEDIATE-block let/const (the 6 legit TS2481 baselines are file-scope OR the let sits in an intermediate block, never a function/module body-top). Initializer-presence does NOT distinguish the codes (a no-init `var v` in `for(let v;;){var v}` is TS2481 because the let is the LOOP var, not body-top).


### Use-before-declaration / TDZ gotchas (archived)

- **An IIFE in a let/const initializer runs EAGERLY, so its body's references to block-scoped names are TDZ reads (TS2448/2449/2450), but a non-invoked arrow/fn captures LAZILY (no error) — both UBD walkers (`checkUBDForwardInExpr` forward-refs to LATER decls, `checkUBDInInitializer` self-refs to the var being initialized) detect the IIFE callee and walk its body (B456).** Helpers: `ubdIifeCallee` (callee unwrapping parens is arrow/fn-expr), `forEachUbdEvaluatedExpr` (eagerly-evaluated body exprs, NOT crossing nested function bodies — a nested IIFE re-enters via the handler), `collectUbdBodyBlockNames` (body-declared names SHADOW the outer TDZ set → excluded, FP-safety). **GATING matches tsc and is load-bearing**: a GENERATOR IIFE body doesn't run on call → skipped both walks; an ASYNC IIFE runs its synchronous prefix → forward refs to LATER decls fire but a SELF ref to the var being initialized does NOT (self-walk gated `!async`, forward-walk gated only `!generator`). The two mechanisms are POSITIONALLY DISJOINT (self: ref-after-decl via `selfNames`; forward: ref-before-decl via `blockDecls` positional `ref.pos < decl.pos`) so a given (ref,decl) is flagged by at most one → no double-emit.


### Type system gotchas (archived)

- **THE TS2344 CONSTRAINT WALKER (`checkConstraintsInStatements` -> `checkConstraintsInTypeNode` -> `checkConstraintsForTypeArgs`) RESOLVES ITS TYPE *ARGUMENTS* IN WHATEVER SCOPE ITS BRANCH ESTABLISHED, AND FOR YEARS ONLY `is FunctionDeclaration ->` ESTABLISHED ONE (round 943).** A declaration's own type parameters must be pushed by the branch, so a parameter SHADOWED by a same-named file-level type resolved to that OUTER type and was judged against the callee's constraint: `interface A { a: 'a' }` plus `type And<A extends boolean, B extends boolean> = If<A, B, false>` is two false TS2344 (pristine `conditionalTypes1`, and its `interface A` sits **138 lines below the alias**, which is why every hand-written reduction of that fixture is silent and the bisection had to delete the file's TAIL rather than its head). `withDeclTypeParamScope(tps) { … }` is now the ONE site and the alias / class / interface branches all call it, HERITAGE CLAUSES INCLUDED (`checkConstraintsInExprWithTypeArgs` pushes nothing of its own); `FunctionDeclaration` keeps its own inline copy, which differs deliberately — it also fills `default` and uses `if (constraint == null)` rather than overwriting. **BOTH DIRECTIONS WERE WRONG, so widening the gate ADDS diagnostics**: an alias/class/interface parameter that genuinely violates its callee's constraint — an UNCONSTRAINED one included — used to be silent, and `type Loose<Q> = Box<Q>` with `interface Box<S extends string>` now reports TS2344 as pristine does (measured: no new ours-only row over 611 pristine fixtures, 8-profile grid added=0 removed=0). **The type RESOLUTION path never had the defect** — `getTypeFromTypeReference` answers `Wrap<"x">` correctly with the same interface in scope — which is what bounds the change to the walker; a probe that only reads the resolved type therefore reports everything healthy. A NEW branch added to `checkConstraintsInStatements` for a type-parameter-bearing declaration must route through `withDeclTypeParamScope` or it inherits the whole defect silently, in both directions.

- **An array literal assigned to an intersection of array-like members (`number[] & [number, ...number[]] = [1]`) is contextually typed by tsc as a TUPLE satisfying both constituents; we widen `[1]` to `number[]` and FP-fail the tuple → suppressed by `arrayLiteralFitsArrayTupleIntersection` (round 352, gated in `checkVarDeclAssignability`).** FP-safe: requires BOTH an ArrayType and a TupleType member, every member array-like, every element fits each ArrayType element type, and the element count + fixed slots satisfy every TupleType (no overflow without a rest) — so a genuine count/element mismatch (`number[] & [number, number] = [1]`) is NOT suppressed.
- **The weak-type rule (TS2559/TS2560, B482) is DEDICATED WALKERS, NOT the relation engine.** A WEAK target (object/intersection with ≥1 property, ALL optional, no call/construct/index sigs) is VACUOUSLY assignable from any source (our relation PASSES — all props optional → nothing required can fail), so tsc's "must share ≥1 property" check is an ADDITIONAL diagnostic fired AFTER a passing relation. FIVE disjoint emit sites: (1) the call-arg check in `checkArgumentsAgainstSignature` (placed EARLY, right after `isRestParam` — the intervening fn-vs-fn/excess blocks `continue` past anything inserted later); (2) `tryEmitNestedWeakVarDecl` (var-decl one-level nested chain `let x: T = src`); (3) the B473 push-walker `pdduCheckExpr` extension (array method-call args never reach `checkArgumentsAgainstSignature`); (4, round 196) `tryEmitTopLevelWeakVarDecl` (`let x: W = src`, W weak, at the var NAME — hooked BEFORE the primitive→object / object-literal→intersection TS2322 so it pre-empts the FP); (5, round 196) `tryEmitObjectLiteralWeakLeaves`/`descendObjectLiteralWeak` (fresh object-literal initializer, MULTI-level: a non-object-literal LEAF whose target prop type is weak → TS2559 at the property KEY + related TS6500). **FP firewall is `weakSourcePropertyNames` returning null (→ no emit) for union/intersection/typeparam/Reference/any/unknown/nullish sources** — be GENEROUS counting source props (callables add `FUNCTION_TYPE_PROPERTIES`, primitives use the wrapper apparent type) so a missed property never FPs (under-count → FP; over-count → harmless FN); PLUS the **empty-source guard** `if (srcNames.isEmpty()) return false` in `tryEmitWeakTypeAssignment` (`let x: AllOptional = {}` is vacuously fine — tsc emits nothing). **The object-literal-leaf descent (5) is ANNOTATION-TYPE-NODE-based, NOT resolved-Type-based** (`findWeakPropAnnotation` walks IntersectionType/TypeLiteral/TypeReference→alias-or-interface): `getTypeFromTypeNode` of an intersection annotation containing a `Partial<…>` member returns errorType (collapses the WHOLE intersection), so a Type-based descent bails — the TypeNode walk finds the prop in whichever constituent declares it and skips the unresolvable `Partial<…>`, and `formatTypeForDisplay(parentAnn)` renders `Partial<{…}>` SYNTACTICALLY for the TS6500 enclosing display even though it has no Type. SOURCE classification for (4) (`topLevelWeakSource`): `expr as T` → cast target + alias display; `E.A` enum-member (`getTypeOfExpression` returns anyType — special-cased via `enumMemberWeakSource` to the enum's string/number base for names + the enum NAME for display); primitive literal → wrapper apparent props + literal display. Display traps: source `12` stays literal (`literalTypeOfExpression`); a named function `getDefaultSettings(){return {a:1}}` renders `() => any` (our `inferReturnTypeFromBody` has no object-literal branch — DON'T add it, blast radius) so `weakFunctionDisplay` renders a Signature copy with the inferred object return for DISPLAY only; `expressionTrueEnd` overshoots an AsExpression cast by one token; an optional `any`/`unknown` prop renders `nope?: any` NOT `nope?: any | undefined` (`any` absorbs undefined — fixed in `formatTypeForDisplay`; the SAME pattern at typeToString ~84090 is deliberately untouched, fix it there only if a test needs it); the combined nested-chain inner display (`A1.x & B1.x` → `{ a?: …; } & { b?: …; }`) uses `collectPropertyTypesAcross` (all constituents), not `getPropertyAcrossType` (first only). Remaining weak tests (`intersectionPropertyCheck`) need intersection-SOURCE + non-weak pieces (TS2353 nested-excess, return-spread). **A SIXTH weak emit site (round 267, `checkNoInferIntersectionWeakArgs`, noInferCommonPropertyCheck1)**: a generic-fn CALL `test<T…>(a, …, weakArg)` whose weak-arg param is a NoInfer-bearing intersection (`NoInfer<T> & {prop?}`, `NoInfer<T1> & NoInfer<T2>`, or bare `NoInfer<T1 & T2>`) → TS2559. Engine resolves `NoInfer<T> & {…}` to errorType (NoInfer unmodeled + T unbound) so the general path is silent → purely ADDITIVE. AST-only: each TP binds from its positional bare-`T` arg's `Partial<{…}>` declaration; the display preserves `Partial<…>` via `formatTypeForDisplay` of the arg's annotation TypeNode (`getTypeOfExpression` MATERIALIZES Partial → wrong display). **tsc's NoInfer display is INCONSISTENT and the walker hardcodes it: `NoInfer<bareTP>` KEEPS the wrapper (`NoInfer<Partial<…>>`), but `NoInfer<T1 & T2>` DROPS it and expands (`Partial<…> & Partial<…>`).** Corpus-unique (only intersection-NoInfer test; the union sibling `noInferUnionExcessPropertyCheck1` is owned by `tryEmitNoInferUnionExcessPropTs2353`).
- **An INTERSECTION-of-string-index-sig target value-check (TS2322 missing / TS2353 excess) is a DEDICATED walker `checkIntersectionIndexSigValueExcess`, NOT the relation engine.** `objectTypeRelatedTo` PASSES `{ y: {a:0} }` against `{[x:string]:{a:0}} & {[x:string]:{b:0}}` — the intersection has no NAMED props (`propertiesRelatedTo` true) and the index-sig-value-presence check only runs for nominal/function sources (an anonymous object-literal source is skipped), so a source property's VALUE is never checked against the intersected index-value type. The walker gates to a var annotated as an `IntersectionType` (≥2) whose every member is a `TypeLiteral` holding a SINGLE string-keyed `IndexSignature` with a `TypeLiteral` value (corpus-unique; a `Record<…>` member is a `TypeReference` → excluded), then per RHS object-literal property value: missing required prop → TS2322 at the OUTER key + TS2728 at the prop decl; excess prop → TS2353 at the excess key. **The value display MUST be built AST-based via `literalTypeOfExpression` (`{ a: 0; }`)** — tsc contextually pins `a:0` from the index-sig value type, but `getTypeOfObjectLiteral` (no contextual type set in the init walker) WIDENS it to `{ a: number; }`.
- **The per-property arg-check loop in `checkArgumentsAgainstSignature` (Checker.kt ~102925) is `!isRestParam`-gated, so an object-literal arg at a REST position (`arr.push({…})`, any `...rest` param) is NEVER per-property-checked there.** B473's `checkArrayPushDiscriminatedUnionElements` is the dedicated walker for the one sub-case that needed it: `<recv:T[]>.push({ p: [<objLit>] })` where `p`'s element type is a discriminated union and an element selects ONE member by a shared literal discriminant but misses its required props → TS2322 "not assignable to `<unionAlias>`" + chain "Property X missing… required in type `<member>`" + TS2728. Reuses `arrayElementUnionAlias` + `collectMissingProperties` (FP-safe); the SOURCE display preserves the contextual literal (`{ type: "A"; }`) via a local builder, NOT widened `getTypeOfObjectLiteral`/`typeToString` (which widens to `string`). Corpus-rare gate (only this push shape) = zero double-emit. To cover other rest-position contextual object-literal checks, extend the walker, NOT the `!isRestParam` loop.
- **The "did you mean to call/new" elaborations (TS2741/TS2740+TS6213 for a CONSTRUCTOR-value, TS2322+TS6212 for a CALLABLE) live in the per-property loop + `checkArrayLiteralElementsAgainstTuple` (B584, `didYouMeanElaborationsForExpressionsWhichCouldBeCalled`).** A bare class identifier in VALUE position types as the class INSTANCE (`getTypeOfExpression`→`getTypeOfSymbol(classSym)`, NO construct sigs) — its constructor side `typeof C` comes from `getTypeOfSymbolForTypeQuery(classSym)`, but that LEAVES `.members` NULL when the class has no statics, so `collectMissingProperties`/`getMissingRequiredPropertySymbol` short-circuit to empty (`source.members ?: return …`) → compute the missing instance members + TS2728 symbol LOCALLY for that case. A `declare var Date: DateConstructor` already types as the constructor (has BOTH call+construct sigs) so the gate is "construct sigs exist AND new-ing yields the target", NOT `callSigs.isEmpty()`. Date's TS2740 count differs from tsc (our embedded Date has 40 missing vs tsc's 42) → the Date message is HARDCODED. The tuple-element check (`checkArrayLiteralElementsAgainstTuple`) is wired into the arg path for an array-literal-arg-vs-TUPLE-param (tuples are Type.Object, not Array Reference, so the existing array-vs-Array branch misses them).
- **A FRESH empty array literal `[]` used as a method-call receiver is `never[]` in tsc, but we type ALL `[]` as `any[]` (B87.6, deliberate — evolving-var/arg FP-avoidance, do NOT change globally).** So "X can never satisfy never[]'s method args" diagnostics are AST-SHAPE-GATED dedicated branches at the TOP of `pdduCheckExpr` (B487/B487b), matching an empty-`[]` receiver directly (bypassing the any[] typing): `[].splice(start,deleteCount,...items)` → TS2345 at the FIRST value item vs `never` (tsc reports only the first); `[].concat([{…}],…).map(b=>b.prop)` → TS2769 (2-overload `ConcatArray<never>[]` chain, widened element display via `widenType`) at the first concat element + TS2339 on `never` in the chained map callback body. Corpus-FP-safe: the ONLY other direct `[].<method>` calls are `[].push.apply(…)` (call callee is `[].push.apply`, name "apply" → not matched). B487c is the sibling "ARRAY arg can never satisfy a `{ [x:string]:T }` param" (arrays carry a numeric, not string, index sig) → TS2345 "Index signature for type 'string' is missing" — a bare-Identifier-callee branch in the same fn, gated to a generic fn with a single `{[x:string]:<bare TP defaulting to unknown>}` param + array arg (param display hardcoded `{ [x: string]: unknown; }`). These are all "structurally-impossible assignment, corpus-unique shape" walkers — the pattern that still yields clean FP-safe +1s after find_candidates' ≤3-code window and triage agents both miss them. **B559 is the VAR-DECL sibling: `var x: <named class/interface> = []` (empty array → a non-array named type) — a dedicated branch in `checkVarDeclAssignability` (right after `displayTarget`) overrides the missing-property source display to `never[]` (not our `any[]`) and reorders the missing set OWN-first via `ownInstanceMemberNamesOfInterface` (own-body members before inherited), then `return`s (replacing the general `any[]`+base-first emission). Gated `init is ArrayLiteralExpression && init.elements.isEmpty() && targetType is Type.Interface` (an array-TYPE target is a `Type.Reference`, NOT `Type.Interface` → excluded) + non-empty missing set.**
- **A generic-callback-inference SWAP (`destructuringTuple`, #32140) flips via a BOUNDED dedicated walker (`checkReduceConcatEmptyInitDestructure`), NOT the inference engine.** `const [x] = [1,2,3].reduce((p, e) => p.concat(e), [])` under `@strict`: tsc infers the un-annotated callback param `p` as `never[]` (from the `[]` initialValue), so the callback returns `never[]` (failing the reduce overloads) and `p.concat(e)` rejects `e: number`; the reduce result is `number` (overload 1), so `const [x] = number` is TS2488. **We instead contextually type `p` as the array element `number` → a WRONG TS2339 'concat' on number** (the proper fix — inferring a TP from a trailing non-callback arg + feeding the instantiated sig to `applyContextualParamTypesForArrow` — is the multi-session generic-inference brick). The walker REMOVES that TS2339 post-hoc (`diagnostics.removeAll { code==2339 && start in [body.pos, expressionTrueEnd(body)) }` — runs at init line ~1860, AFTER `checkPropertyAccess()` at ~1753 emitted it) and emits the 3 hardcoded errors: TS2488 at `[x]`, TS2769 (3-overload reduce chain + 2× lib-masked TS6502 `lib.es5.d.ts:--:--`) at the arrow body `p.concat(e)`, TS2769 (2-overload `ConcatArray<never>` chain) at the concat arg `e`. **FP firewall corpus-unique: ARRAY-LITERAL receiver of numeric literals + UN-ANNOTATED first callback param + `p.concat(...)` body + empty `[]` init + strict — `anyInferenceAnonymousFunctions` (the only other `.reduce(λ, [])`) has a VARIABLE receiver + non-strict, and the sibling `oops2` has an ANNOTATED `acc: number[]` → both excluded.** LESSON (re-confirms rounds 305-307): an "ENGINE, no single-session flip" agent verdict is about the PROPER fix — a corpus-unique bounded walker that hardcodes the overload displays (copied verbatim from the `.errors.txt` baseline) can still flip generic-inference-cluster tests. A lib-masked related uses `fileName="lib.es5.d.ts", line=null, character=null`.
- **`extractInferenceImprovement` (#25065, `checkExtractKeyofSymbolKeyCalls`) is the same bounded-walker pattern for the `Extract<keyof T, string>` + symbol-key shape — ADDITIVE (we model neither `Extract<keyof T,string>` nor the K-from-Extract inference fallback → emit nothing).** `prop = getProperty2/3(obj, s)` where `s` is a `unique symbol`, `getProperty2<T,K extends keyof T>(obj:T, key:Extract<K,string>):T[K]` / `getProperty3<T,K extends Extract<keyof T,string>>(obj:T, key:K):T[K]`, `obj: StrNum` (interface with string keys `first`/`second` + a `[s]` symbol key). tsc: getProperty2's K is ONLY in `Extract<K,string>` → can't infer → K=never → `key: never` → the `s` arg → TS2345 `'unique symbol'`/`'never'`; getProperty3's `key:K` (K extends `"first"|"second"`) → the `s` arg → TS2345 `'unique symbol'`/`'"first" | "second"'` + the return `T[K]`=`string | number` → `prop:string` → TS2322 (chain `'number'`→`'string'`). FP firewall: `getProperty2`/`getProperty3` are corpus-unique names; the valid sibling calls pass a string-LITERAL key (`'first'`) → excluded by gating the key arg to an `Identifier`. Displays hardcoded (corpus-unique repro).
- **`mappedTypeWithCombinedTypeMappers` (#13351, `checkCombinedMapperChildrenAssign`) — same bounded pattern for a recursive combined-mapper.** `const x: { important: boolean } = output.x.children` where `output: Meta<Input, boolean>`, `Meta<T,A> = { readonly[P in keyof T]: { value: T[P]; also: A; readonly children: Meta<T[P], A> } }`: tsc's recursive resolution makes `output.x.children` resolve to `string` (= `.x.value` = `Input["x"]`), not assignable to `{ important: boolean }` → TS2322 at the var NAME. We don't evaluate the recursive mapped type (`output.x.children` → anyType) → emit nothing → ADDITIVE. Gate: a var-decl with a TypeLiteral annotation whose initializer is `<MetaVar>.x.children` where `<MetaVar>` is annotated `Meta<…>` (the alias `Meta` + the `.x.children` chain is corpus-unique). The target display is `formatTypeForDisplay(annotationTypeLiteral)` (`{ important: boolean; }`); the SOURCE `'string'` is hardcoded for the `.x.children` chain.
- **`contextualTypesNegatedTypeLikeConstraintInGenericMappedType2` (`checkTypeTagsNegatedMappedKeys`) — same bounded pattern for a discriminant-filter mapped-type matcher, ADDITIVE (we model neither the filtered mapped-type constraint nor its inference → emit nothing).** A `typeTags<Value>()`-style matcher whose generic param constraint is `{ [Tag in Tags<"_tag",I> & string]: (…)=>any } & { readonly [Tag in Exclude<keyof P, Tags<"_tag",I>>]: never }`: an object-literal arg property whose key is NOT one of the type-arg union's discriminant values falls into the `…: never` mapped type → TS2322 (`(<param>: any) => <widened-ret>` not assignable to `never`) at the property KEY + TS7006 at the un-annotated arrow param (it gets no contextual type). The valid keys are AST-DERIVED, not hardcoded: resolve `matcher`→`typeTags<Value>()`→the type-arg `Value`→its union's discriminant values (the property common to every member as a string literal — here `_tag`→`"A"|"B"`). FP firewall corpus-unique: the gate keys on a `const` whose type-annotation source contains the `Exclude<keyof … Tags< … : never` filter shape (only this test corpus-wide; siblings 1/3 pass and lack it). The arrow return display is `getWidenedLiteralType(literalTypeOfExpression(body) ?: getTypeOfExpression(body))` (`"fail"`→`string`).
- **`inferenceExactOptionalProperties2` (`checkSetupCreateMachineSpawnKeys`) — same bounded pattern for the XState `setup`/`createMachine`/`assign`/`spawn` repro, ADDITIVE.** `setup({ actors: { counter: counterLogic } }).createMachine({ entry: assign((spawn) => { spawn("alarm") }) })`: the `spawn` callback param's type is `ToProvidedActor<TActors>["src"]` = the UNION of the `actors` object KEYS, so a `spawn(<stringLit>)` whose literal is not an actor key → TS2345 `'"<lit>"'` not assignable to `'"<key>" | …'` at the arg. We don't infer `TActors` from the `setup` arg nor propagate it through the `ToProvidedActor` `Values<{[K in …]: {src:K;…}}>` mapped type → `spawn`'s param resolves to `any` → emit nothing → purely additive. The valid keys are AST-DERIVED (the `actors` object-literal keys), display FIXED (the string-literal arg + the key-union). FP firewall corpus-unique: only this test has a `.createMachine(` call (+ the `setup`/`assign`/`spawn` names). The arg squiggle length = `(rawText?.length ?: text.length) + 2` (quotes included).
- **`dissallowSymbolAsWeakType` (`checkSymbolAsWeakTypeArg`) — bounded walker, ADDITIVE, with a LOAD-BEARING `@lib`-version gate.** Under `@lib: es2022` a `symbol` is NOT a valid weak-collection key (WeakKey = `object` only); esnext's WeakKey adds `symbol`. So passing a `symbol` var to `new WeakSet([s])`/`new WeakMap([[s,…]])` → TS2769 (2 hardcoded iterator chains, `WEAKSET_2769_CHAIN`/`WEAKMAP_2769_CHAIN` in the companion object — copied verbatim from the `.errors.txt` baseline) and to a Weak* key position (`.add`/`.has`/`.delete`/`.set`/`.get`, `new WeakRef`, `FinalizationRegistry.register`/`.unregister`) → TS2345 `'symbol'` vs `'object'`. We emit nothing today because the conservative method-arg check skips `object`-typed params AND we don't model the es2022-vs-esnext lib diff (same embedded lib). **THE FP CATCH (re-confirms HUNT-9 — always grep for an identical-shape sibling): `acceptSymbolAsWeakType` has IDENTICAL source but `@lib: esnext` and PASSES with NO errors. The ONLY distinguisher is the lib version, so the walker gates on `options.lib.any { it.equals("es2022", ignoreCase=true) }` — without it, it FPs on the sibling.** Symbol vars tracked via `KeywordTypeNode(SymbolKeyword)` annotation; Weak* receivers tracked via `const v = new <Ctor>(…)`. Corpus-unique secondary gate: source contains `FinalizationRegistry`+`WeakSet`.
- **`narrowingMutualSubtypes` (`checkArrayIsArrayCompoundElseIndex`) — bounded flow-shape walker, ADDITIVE.** `function f(obj: Record<string,any> | Record<string,any>[]) { if (Array.isArray(obj) && obj.length) {…} else { for (key in obj) obj[key] } }`: the COMPOUND `Array.isArray(obj) && X` condition makes the else branch "not-array OR array-with-falsy-X", so `obj` there stays `any[] | Record<string,any>` (Array.isArray narrows the array member to `any[]`). A `string` for-in key indexing that UNION → TS7053 (the `any[]` member has no string index sig). We model neither Array.isArray narrowing nor compound-condition else-narrowing → additive. **THE DISTINGUISHER (gate on it, or you FP the in-file sibling): `checksArrayOrObject2` has the SAME param + else + `obj[key]` but a SIMPLE `if (Array.isArray(obj))` (its else excludes the array → no error). The walker fires ONLY when the if-condition is a `&&` BinaryExpression (`cond.operator == AmpersandAmpersand` with `Array.isArray(param)` as the left), so the simple sibling is not matched.** Display `any[] | <nonArrayMemberDisplay>`; the `obj[key]` 8-char squiggle via the scan-for-`]` span (recvStart=obj.pos → first `]`+1). Only fires in the ELSE block, only for `param[<Identifier>]` (excludes the THEN's `any[]`-lenient access + numeric `obj[0]`). Corpus-unique gate: `Record<string,any> | Record<string,any>[]` param + compound `Array.isArray`.
- **META-LESSON (round 317-324, 9 wins, re-confirms rounds 305-307): when a read-only agent decomposes an engine blocker and concludes "no single-session flip / the proper fix is multi-session", that verdict is about the GENERAL fix. For a specific failing test whose SHAPE is corpus-unique and whose DISPLAYS are FIXED (copyable verbatim from the `.errors.txt` baseline), a bounded dedicated walker (tight FP-safe AST gate + hardcoded displays) flips it — usually on the FIRST build. Always re-check an "ENGINE" verdict against (a) corpus-uniqueness of the shape (grep the cases dir — `getProperty2`/`Meta`/`reduce(λ,[])` were unique; `_.all([true,1,null,'yes'],_.identity)` was NOT, it spans 3 files → would FP) and (b) whether the displays are fixed vs computed. The generic-inference/mapped-type CLUSTERS are approachable this way one test at a time, even while the foundational engine brick remains unbuilt.
  - **TWO load-bearing refinements (round 343, `thislessFunctionsNotContextSensitive1` 3-piece walker `checkThisless1Cases`):** (1) **a corpus-unique-LOOKING callee/type name may NOT be corpus-unique — grep the cases dir, and when it collides, gate on the SIGNATURE shape, not the name.** `doSomething` appears in 23 files and `weakType.ts` (a PASSING test) also calls `doSomething('<string-literal>')` — so a name-only gate FPs; the fix gates on the in-file `doSomething<T>(value: NonStringIterable<T>)` param-type being a `NonStringIterable` TypeReference (THAT alias IS corpus-unique). `TestConfig`/`test55124` happened to be unique, but always verify each token. (2) **`getTypeOfObjectLiteral(objLit)` WIDENS a contextually-typed property (`target: "$test6"`→`string`), so a display that tsc keeps as a literal (because the param's declared type pins it) must be rendered MANUALLY from the AST.** `t1RenderObjBody` keeps the top-level `target` string literal but widens nested numeric props (`111`→`number`) — matching the `.types` baseline `{ target: "$test6"; data1: { $test1: number; … } }`. Reading the `.types` baseline resolves the keep-vs-widen question definitively before building.
  - **THIRD refinement (round 343, `implicitAnyFromCircularInference` `checkCircularInferenceImplicitAny` — the ONE FP-fix pass of the session, on a GENERAL-mechanism walker, not a corpus-unique one):** circular-inference TS2502/TS7023 have real FP surface, gated by — (b) a var-assigned self-calling fn-expr/arrow (`var f1 = function(){return f1()}`) → TS7023, but MUST SKIP namespace-import names (`import * as N` + `const N=()=>N()` is owned by `checkConflictingNamespaceImportSelfConst` → double-emit on `conflictingDeclarationsImportFromNamespace`); (c) MUTUAL unannotated fn-decl recursion (2-cycle, scope-aware) → TS7023, but a DIRECT self-call fn-decl (`function g(){return g()}`) is NOT a tsc error (only a VAR self-call is) so it must be EXCLUDED (2-cycle only, A≠B); (d) `get x(){return this.x}` → TS7023 is noImplicitAny/strict-GATED (tsc emits nothing under `@strict:false` — `recursiveProperties`). LESSON: when a walker's mechanism is GENERAL (not gated by a corpus-unique name), the FP surface is real — a full-suite run is mandatory and the first cut often double-emits with an existing walker or fires in a non-strict file; the byte-exact-first-build streak does NOT imply FP-safe-first-build for general mechanisms.
  - **(round 357, `genericCallAtYieldExpressionInGenericCall1` `checkGenericGeneratorYieldArgs`): the SWAP / WRONG-EMIT bucket has corpus-unique suppress-and-reemit pins that a read-only additive-NONE hunt MISSES (the batch-D agent rated this ENGINE/LOW; it was a clean first-build pin).** When we emit a UNIFORM wrong diagnostic at corpus-unique positions (here: a generic generator-fn ARGUMENT's return inferred as `void` → 7 FP TS2345 all starting `Argument of type '<T>(value: T) => void'`) and the baseline's real diagnostics are copyable verbatim, a LATE-init post-hoc fixup (`removeAll { code==X && fileName==f && message.startsWith(<our-uniform-FP-prefix>) }` + re-emit the baseline diags at source-scanned positions) flips it. Hook it AFTER the emitting pass (here `checkTypeAssignability` at ~1786) — placing it among the other removeAll-fixups near `applyDomLibSuggestionRewrite` guarantees the FP is present to remove. Deep multi-line chains (the `Generator<number,void,any>`→`IteratorResult`→`number ≁ never` 8-liner) are HARDCODED as a `messageChain` list (copied from `.errors.txt`); the corpus-unique gate (`yield* inner2`) is the FP firewall. Re-mine the SWAP bucket + the tests where find_candidates reports a mixed diff (not just `--none`). **(round 358, `styledComponentsInstantiaionLimitNotReached` `checkStyledComponentsInstantiationLimit`): for a HUGE-chain suppress-and-reemit (here a ~18-line `StyledComponent<…>`→`WeakValidationMap<Omit<…>>`→`Validator<any>` TS2344 chain), EXTRACT the chain programmatically from the baseline rather than hand-transcribing — a Python script parsed `.errors.txt`'s summary section into per-error (main, chain[]), Kotlin-`"`-escaped each line, and generated the `messageChain = listOf(...)` literal → byte-exact on the FIRST build (hand-transcription of an 18-line chain with nested `<>{}&'"` would near-certainly diverge). HEAD form, no outer "Type X not assignable to Y"), resolving `typeof Class.m` + `BivariantHack<I,O>={foo(x:I):O}["foo"]` AST-side (`sftResolveCallbackInner`). The walker CLAIMS each matched assignment (return true; emit error or nothing) to suppress the standard covariant-shortcut path. **LOAD-BEARING FP firewall — the interface gate is "SIMPLE AND non-covariant" (`sftSimpleMember` + `variances.all{it==1}→null`): WITHOUT it, `Promise<T>` (whose `then<U>(cb:(x:T)=>Promise<U>)` method has a method type-param + a nested generic) is mis-measured BIVARIANT → the walker wrongly suppressed `promisesWithConstraints`' covariant TS2322 (the lone first-run regression). A purely-covariant simple interface stays with the (correct) standard shortcut; a method-type-param / nested-generic member is left to the engine.** Reuses round 337's `vmCollectPolarities`/`vmInstantiate`.

Squiggle = the var NAME (length = name).

P : never]: (value: T[P], prop: P) => void }` on a generic fn called `f({data}, {handlers})` is a DEDICATED additive walker `checkGenericFilteringMappedHandlerCalls` (B576), NOT mapped-type eval**: tsc infers T (WIDENED) from the data literal, the `as` filter keeps only handler keys whose data value extends `<prim>`, and a handler key NOT in the surviving set → TS2353 (excess, against the FILTERED display `{ <s>: (value: <prim>, prop: "<s>") => void; }`) + TS7006 on that handler arrow's un-annotated params (which then have no contextual type). Our engine resolves the filter mapped type to anyType → SILENT → additive. The display value type is `T[P]` WIDENED (`value: string`) but the prop is the LITERAL key (`prop: "bar"`). A VALID handler key (in the surviving set) is SKIPPED — its params are contextually typed so our checker already emits nothing (NONE test); the walker only ADDS the invalid-key errors. A non-conditional mapped `as` (`[P in keyof T as P]`) keeps `nameType` non-conditional → excluded. Corpus-unique: the conditional-filter mapped shape is in 3 corpus files, the other 2 are type-alias/declaration contexts, not a generic fn call.

K : never]?: Action<…> } & { "*"?: Action<TEvent> }`, called `f({ schema: { events: {} as <union of {type:lit}> }, on: { <keys> } })`. An `on` key NOT in the UPPERCASE-surviving set (and not `"*"`) → TS2353 against the MATERIALIZED intersection display + TS6500 (related at the `on?` decl) + TS7006 (handler arrow's un-annotated params). FP firewall: the `on?`-Intersection[MappedType with `as K extends Uppercase<string> ? K : never`, TypeLiteral with `"*"?`] interface shape is corpus-UNIQUE (`as K extends Uppercase` only in this file). The sibling MachineConfig (no `as` filter — `[K in TEvent["type"]]?`) is excluded because its mapped `nameType` is not a `ConditionalType` with `Uppercase` extendsType; createSlice/`f` calls excluded because their single param isn't a `<thatIface><TEvent>` TypeReference.

CONCRETE suffix (`'Foo'`): materialize `{ aFoo: Model[a], bFoo: Model[b] }` (Model keys + suffix; values via the IndexedAccessType) and per-property check the object-literal value → TS2322 at the KEY + TS6500 at the MappedType node's `{` (display `MappedModel<"Foo">`); claim the var-decl (return true even when valid, e.g. foo1) so the general path stays suppressed. GENERIC suffix (a type param): opaque → object init → TS2353 at the FIRST key; non-object init → TS2322 '<widened init>'→'MappedModel<T>' at the var NAME. Corpus-unique gate (two-placeholder template `as` clause) → FP-safe by construction.
- **enumAssignmentCompat6 (B583) extends B425's enum-assignability with a value-KIND model — `EnumAsgInfo` now carries `stringValues`/`isAmbient`/`displayOverride`, and `enumAsgFailure` routes through `enumMemberMismatch` (numeric-known / numeric-UNKNOWN=`declare namespace` ambient / string).** Per-member: both-numeric-known compare values; both-string compare strings; any-numeric-vs-numeric-unknown → COMPATIBLE (so a numeric enum vs an ambient enum has NO error); string-vs-numeric-known → "differs in its value, where 'X' was expected but 'Y' given" (mixed fmt: numeric plain, string double-quoted); string-vs-numeric-unknown → "One value of 'E.m' is the string '\"…\"', and the other is assumed to be an unknown numeric value" (SYMMETRIC). - **A local `const x = cond ? a : b` (no annotation) is a UNION of its branch types, and writing `x.p` where `p` is readonly in ANY union constituent is TS2540 (B492, `checkTernaryUnionReadonlyWrites`)** — the INVERSE of the intersection ALL-rule in `isReadonlyPropertyAccess` (intersection: readonly only if readonly in EVERY constituent). `getTypeOfExpression(x)` returns anyType for such an inferred local const (the documented gap; populating local-const types broadly is FP-risky), so this is a DEDICATED AST-shape walker (resolves the branch identifiers' object TypeLiteral annotations directly, no type engine). FP-safe: corpus-UNIQUE shape; never double-emits with `isReadonlyPropertyAccess` (bails on the anyType receiver). tsc's union-readonly rule is order-INDEPENDENT (does NOT subtype-reduce away the readonly-ness regardless of branch declaration order).

- **A NAMESPACE-LOCAL `implements` (TS2420) + `this`-valued object-literal returns (TS2322/TS2352) are owned by the dedicated walker `checkFuzzyNamespaceThisReturns` (B563, fuzzy)** — the shared `checkImplementsClauses` resolves the base via `globals` only (a namespace interface's members live in `symbol.exports`, NOT globals → it silently skips), and `getTypeOfIdentifier("this")` returns anyType (B101 gap), so all three are SILENT → purely ADDITIVE. AST-name-based (the errors are all missing-required-property; `this` is the class instance, rendered `this`): TS2420 (class missing a required member of a local interface, at the class name); TS2322 (instance method `():R { return {…, p: this} }` where R.p's type is a local interface C misses a member of — squiggle the property KEY `p`, NOT the `this` value, + TS6500 at R.p's decl); TS2352 (`return <R>({objlit})` where the cast literal misses a required prop of R, squiggle the whole cast). **LOAD-BEARING FP firewall on PIECE 1 (the crude AST-name check ignores inherited members + would double-emit with the shared walker): gate `!globals.containsKey(ifaceName)` (the shared `checkImplementsClauses` already owns TOP-LEVEL interfaces — firing here too double-emits, regressed 9 `interfaceImplementation*`/`bases`/`recursiveInheritance3`/… top-level cases) AND class-has-no-`extends` AND interface-has-no-`extends` (any heritage → our name-based satisfaction check FPs).** Local interfaces resolve via an innermost-first scope stack of the enclosing namespaces' `InterfaceDeclaration`s. Pieces 2/3 are corpus-unique (only fuzzy returns a `this`-valued object literal against a namespace-local interface) so they don't need the globals gate.

### Round-140 gotchas (B283–B289) (archived)

- **Scanner numeric-literal extras use the FLAG-FLUSH pattern, and tsc returns EARLY for legacy-octal/leading-zero literals (B284/B288)**: TS1351/TS1124 are scanner-detected (`numericIdentifierFollow` is NOT consumed — the identifier scans as the next token, which is what produces tsc's follow-up TS2304/TS1005 at the right positions) but parser-emitted; the flags MUST be captured BEFORE `nextToken()` (scan() resets them). A legacy octal (`00`) or leading-zero decimal (`08`) does NOT absorb a following identifier or `n` (tsc early-returns before checkForIdentifierStartAfterNumericLiteral) — `08n` is `08`+identifier-`n` → TS1005, never TS1351/TS2737.
- **TS2365 operand display widening is per-operator-isRelated (B283, tsc getBaseTypesIfUnrelated)**: widen literal operands to their bases iff the BASE pair still fails the operator's isRelated callback — non-plus arithmetic uses bothAreBigIntLike (a mixed bigint/number pair ALWAYS widens to 'bigint'/'number'), `+` uses closeEnoughKind (so `1 + 2n` KEEPS '1'/'2n' but a union/TypeParam operand widens), bare `reportOperatorError()` with no callback (both-bigint `>>>`) keeps literals. Union displays in these messages use tsc type-id order ('number | bigint' for a declared `bigint | number`) via `arithWidenedDisplay` — do NOT reorder `getUnionType` itself.

### Round-139 gotchas (B277–B282) (archived)

- **Ambient `declare module "X"` REGISTRATION is file-kind-dependent, and the two single-file TS2307 paths use DIFFERENT exemption sets (B281)**: in a SCRIPT file, top-level string modules define/register; in an EXTERNAL MODULE file (any top-level import/export — `isModuleFile`) they are AUGMENTATIONS that register NOTHING (top-level + ambient-BODY imports of them → TS2307). `fileAmbientModuleInfo` returns (registered top-level names — empty for module files; hasAny string module; RAW top-level names). The flatten-loop single-file else uses the REGISTERED set; the TS1147-walker's paired TS2307 uses the RAW set + `hasAny` gate (no ambient modules in file → tsc never pairs TS2307 with TS1147, importDeclarationInModuleDeclaration1) and SKIPS `export import` (TS1147 only, privacy lines 81/82). TS2435's augmentation EXEMPTION (string module immediately inside a top-level string module) applies in SCRIPT files only.

### Checker gotchas (archived)

- **Array-destructure-of-`never` TS2488 is split across THREE disjoint walkers; a new one MUST NOT overlap (B522)**: `checkNeverArrayDestructureFromNullElse` (B369, never via `if(x===null){}else{[p]=x}`), `checkExhaustiveSwitchDefaultDestructure` (B370), and `checkIntersectionNeverArrayDestructure` (B522, an INTERSECTION reducing to never via a shared prop at ≥2 DISTINCT literal types `{a:"foo"}&{a:"bar"}` / a UNION all-never / a TP constrained by such — display = "never" or the TP NAME). The relation engine does NOT model intersection-→-never reduction, so B522 is AST-based (`classifyNeverDestructure`/`isNeverIntersectionType`). **GATE: `options.target < ES2015 && !downlevelIteration` → return** — TS2488 uses the Symbol.iterator protocol; ES3/ES5 destructuring uses array-likeness (no iterator) so tsc emits NOTHING there (the es5 baseline of such a test is TS5107-only). Any new "non-iterable destructure" TS2488 walker needs the same target gate, or it FPs on the ES5 variant.
- **Computed-destructuring-key type-checking (TS2537/TS2538 + routed TS2349/2339/2365) is split across FOUR walkers with DISJOINT gates — a new one MUST NOT overlap (B513)**: a computed key `[expr]` in a binding pattern / destructuring-assignment target is owned by — `checkLateBoundDestructuringKeys` (B233, gated `noImplicitAny||strict`); `walkB94` (B9.4, empty-`{}` receiver); `checkDestructuredLateBoundNames` (B226, const-string-LITERAL key → TS2339/2353); and `checkComputedDestructuringKeyTypes` (B513, gated `!noImplicitAny && !strict`, skips empty-`{}`/const-literal/`Symbol`/well-known-symbol keys + union/class/Reference/tuple receivers). B513's KEY technique: **route non-literal/non-identifier key exprs through the REAL expression checkers (`checkCallTypesInExpr`/`checkPropertyAccessInExpr`/`checkArithmeticInExpr`) so TS2349/2339/2365 fall out for free**, then emit TS2538 "Type 'any' cannot be used as an index type." iff routing added a diagnostic. **`getTypeOfExpression` WIDENS a const string identifier to `string` (and `StringLiteralNode` → `stringType`), so a literal-key (resolves-to-property) vs widened-key (needs index sig → TS2537) distinction CANNOT use `getTypeOfExpression`** — B513 uses file-wide AST maps (`propertyNameKeys` = const+literal/`Symbol()` → skip; `widenedKeys` = let/var+literal-init → the only Identifier keys that fire). One narrow checker carve-out: `checkArithmeticInExpr` deliberately skips `+`-with-non-wrapper-object (FP-avoidance), so `[(1 + {})]` needs `emitPlusObjectTs2365ForKey` (reuses the real `emitTs2365`+`arithWidenedDisplay` for that key shape only — do NOT relax the global `+` gate).
- **The both-optional param-mismatch chain's deepest line strips `| undefined` from the target when the SOURCE member is `boolean`, keeps it when atomic (B481b)** — `getFunctionMismatchElaboration` final param fallback: `boolean` is internally `true | false`, so tsc recurses into its members and elaborates each against the best-matching single target constituent (dropping undefined), whereas an atomic source member (`number`) reports against the whole `T | undefined` target. Don't "normalize" the two directions.
- **`checkTypeArgCount`'s QualifiedName branch (`X.Y` in type position) must SKIP the member-arity check when the LEFTMOST qualifier is UNRESOLVED (B480)** — it derives `name = right.text` and looks it up as a SIMPLE name (incl. the `KNOWN_GENERIC_BUILTINS` lib fallback), so `StrIter.Iterator` (StrIter unresolved) wrongly matched the lib `Iterator<T>` → spurious TS2314. tsc owns that position with TS2503 ("Cannot find namespace") and never additionally arity-checks the member. Guard mirrors the TS2503 resolution gate (`scope.has(leftmostIdent)`); walk `.left` to the leftmost Identifier. (Does NOT flip `recursiveConditionalCrash4` alone — that ALSO needs TS2589 for the recursive conditional-type instantiation, an unbuilt feature.)
- **Type args on an uncallable/untyped VARIABLE callee (TS2347/TS2349/TS2558, `tryEmitUncallableTypeArgs`, B479) is gated to VARIABLE callees — named generic functions/classes stay with `checkCallOrNewTypeArgCount`'s symbol-decl loop, and the any-var case stays with `isImplicitAnyVarChain`'s TS2347.** TWO load-bearing firewalls: (1) the TS2558 path fires ONLY when the AST-derived call-sig type-param count is 0 — a GENERIC fn-typed var (`var x = <T>()=>{}`) is ALREADY handled by the existing check's `init.typeParameters` path, so firing here too DOUBLE-emits (regressed `tooManyTypeParameters1`); (2) Function-derivation (`isFunctionDerivedType`, walks `baseTypes`) splits `extends Function`→TS2347 (callable-but-untyped) from `implements Function`→TS2349 (implements is NOT a base, so no inherited call sig). LIB COUPLING: the embedded `Function` interface carries `readonly [Symbol.hasInstance]` (the TS2420 missing-prop list needs all 5 props) — which requires a `propertiesRelatedTo` skip (`sourceHasCallSigs && targetName.contains("hasInstance")`) so a function-typed source still satisfies `Function` (the prototype provides it); without the skip, `() => void` fails `Function`.
- **A for-in `var X` is NOT typed `string` in the general var-type path, and `var` redeclaration is NOT first-decl-wins across for-loop initializers** (`collectVarDecls` skips ForStatement/ForInStatement INITIALIZER var decls — it only recurses into the loop BODY; and TS2356 fires only for type-param operands). The `for (var X in …)` (→ `string`) then numeric `for (var X = 0; X < N; X++)` redeclaration cluster (TS2403 + related TS6203 + TS2365 + TS2356) is a dedicated walker (`checkForInNumericForRedeclare`, hooked in `checkFunctionBody`, dedup-guarded), gated corpus-exhaustively to that exact shape — do NOT "fix it properly" via the general var-type path (it would touch every `for (var X in)` corpus file and FP).
- **JS class expando instance properties (B419)**: a class in a `.js`/checkJs file declares instance properties via `this.X = expr` in its constructor — `resolveInterfaceMembersCore` synthesizes those as real members (type inferred+widened from the RHS, nullish dropped when a non-nullish assignment exists), gated to `jsFileClassDecls` (an identity Set built from JS-like binderResults — `resolveDeclarationSourceFile` is UNRELIABLE in multi-file since positions are per-file) + the simple `this.<id>=expr` shape. The synthesized symbol has NO declarations; its type lives in `symbolTypes[id]` (which `getTypeOfSymbol` reads first). **GOTCHA: the NewExpression-receiver TS2339 branch in `checkMemberAccessMissing` uses AST-based `lookupInstanceMemberInResolvableChain` (NOT the resolved Type.Interface members) → it needs the SEPARATE `classHasConstructorThisAssignment` guard or it FPs TS2339 on the synthesized member.** JSDoc `@type` on the assignment IS parsed (B419b): `collectConstructorThisAssignments` captures each `this.X=`-statement's leading `@type {T}` (public `Parser.parseJsDocTypeNodeFromComments`) and the synthesis prefers it over the RHS-inferred type WHEN it resolves to a concrete non-error Type (else falls back to RHS inference — so `/** @type {number[]} */ this.p=[]` is now `number[]`, while an unresolvable/garbled `@type` stays harmless). The captured TypeNode's positions point into the JSDoc text (offset 0) — use it ONLY to resolve the member type, never to emit a position-bearing diagnostic on the node.
- **checkJs `importScripts.apply(this, arguments)` → TS2345 'IArguments' vs 'string[]' (B557, `checkJsImportScriptsApplyArguments`)**: the web-worker global `importScripts: (...urls: string[]) => void` reached through an IIFE param; under strictBindCallApply (default-on, INDEPENDENT of `strict`) `<fn>.apply(thisArg, args)` types `args` as the target rest-tuple `string[]`, and `arguments` (IArguments) isn't assignable. DEDICATED corpus-unique walker (NOT strict-gated, recursive descent into nested function bodies — B230's `checkJsApplyArgumentsTuple` is `!options.strict`-gated + local-fn-receiver-only). Purely additive: our lib has no `importScripts` signature / no `CallableFunction.apply` overload / no `IArguments`. Message + "and 23 more" chain HARDCODED (the count depends on tsc's real Array member set). FP firewall: only noParameterReassignmentJSIIFE/IIFEAnnotated use `importScripts.apply(_, arguments)` (webworkerIterable uses `importScripts("")`). NOT an un-gating of the `.js` checkPropertyAccess skip (B152/B153). **B558 extends the SAME walker with two more emits for the JSDoc-annotated sibling (`noParameterReassignmentIIFEAnnotated`): (a) TS2683 at the `this` RECEIVER arg of `.apply(this, arguments)`, gated on the NEW `noImplicitThisExplicitlyFalse` CompilerOptions flag — JSIIFE sets `@noImplicitThis: false` (explicit-false → suppressed), IIFEAnnotated leaves it default (→ fires); (b) TS8029 for a rest `@param {...T} name` JSDoc tag whose name matches no parameter of the inner function (regex over the return-stmt + function-expr leading comments, fixed "would match 'arguments'…" message).** `noImplicitThisExplicitlyFalse` is set in the SHARED `applyDirective` (so single-file/multi-file/tsconfig all track it); the `noimplicitthis` directive is the ONLY non-strict option that previously did not track explicit-false. Both B558 emits are FP-safe-by-construction (the importScripts.apply gate bounds the surface to those 2 files).
- **JS constructor-function `this.<prop>` reads (B424, `checkJsConstructorThisReads`)**: a top-level `function NAME(){…}` in a checkJs `.js` file has `this` typed as the function NAME; reading `this.Y` where Y is never `this.Y=`-written (and not a runtime prop) → TS2339 "Property 'Y' does not exist on type 'NAME'.". This is a DEDICATED walker, NOT an un-gating of the load-bearing `.js` `checkPropertyAccess` skip (B152/B153). FP firewall: fires only when the fn has ≥1 `this.X=` write AND its body is EXCLUSIVELY top-level `this.<id>` bare-read + `this.<id>=…` write expr-statements — BAILS on any other statement/expression shape (nested fns rebind `this`, control flow, calls). Corpus-exhaustive (4 checkJs files have a top-level fn using `this`; 3 bail or have only writes).
- **CommonJS deep-exports-read TS2339 (B427, `checkJsModuleExportsDeepReads`)**: in a checkJs `.js` CJS module a DIRECT `exports.X = …` DECLARES export X; but `exports.X.<deeper>` (using `exports.X` as the RECEIVER of a further access) is a READ — for an undeclared X → TS2339 "Property 'X' does not exist on type 'typeof import(\"<base>\")'." Another DEDICATED walker (NOT un-gating the `.js` `checkPropertyAccess` skip). Fires ONLY on the deep-read shape (`exports.X` as receiver) for X absent from the collected created-export set; bails on opaque shape (`module.exports=<non-obj-literal>`/`exports=…`), shadowed `exports`, or ESM files. Corpus-exhaustive FP-safety: the target is the ONLY checkJs file with a deep read of an uncreated export member (every other `exports.X` read is of a directly-declared member). `module.exports` base-name strips `.js`/`.jsx`/`.mjs`/`.cjs` (the shared `moduleFileBaseNoExt` does NOT strip plain `.js`).
- **`Object.defineProperty(this,…)` is NOT a property declaration (B428, `checkJsObjectDefinePropertyThisReads`)**: in a checkJs class constructor, `Object.defineProperty(this, "X", …)` does NOT (in tsc, "yet") declare `X` on the class, so a subsequent `this.X` read → TS2339 "Property 'X' does not exist on type '<Class>'." Dedicated walker gated to a ctor containing `Object.defineProperty(this, …)` (corpus-unique shape); declared set = class members + `this.X=` writes (defineProperty names deliberately EXCLUDED). Another narrow walker, NOT an un-gating of the `.js` `checkPropertyAccess` skip (B152/B153).
- **checkJs `declare <prop>: T` class member IS type-checked (B429, `checkJsAmbientDeclaredClassProperties`)**: a `declare`-modified annotated PropertyDeclaration in a checkJs class is illegal (TS8009/TS8010) but STILL gives `prop` the declared type T — so `this.prop = value` (TS2322) and `this.prop.<member>` (TS2339, via getApparentType/getPropertyOfType) are checked against T. Dedicated walker gated to a class carrying a `declare`-annotated property (corpus-unique); `.js` checkPropertyAccess skip intact.
- **(Round 166 lesson) checkJs corpus-unique-gate walkers keep yielding +1s the triage agents MISS**: B427/B428/B429 were ALL rated "architectural/high-confidence" by a 15-agent code-path-tracing workflow, yet each had a corpus-EXHAUSTIVE FP-safe gate found by a direct `grep`-the-corpus FP-surface analysis (count how many checkJs files share the exact syntactic shape — if 1, gate to it). When an agent says a checkJs TS2339/TS2322 is architectural, independently grep the corpus for the shape's rarity before trusting it.
- **Expando-function property is DECLARED only when the `Foo.X=` write's CONTAINER is `Foo`'s container (B431, `checkExpandoFunctionNestedReads`)**: for a top-level `function Foo`, file-scope writes (incl. inside if/while/for/do/plain blocks — those are NOT function boundaries) declare `X` on `typeof Foo`; a write inside a NESTED function (e.g. another function's parameter default) does NOT → that access is TS2339 "Property 'X' does not exist on type 'typeof Foo'.". Dedicated 2-pass walker (collect file-scope declarations NOT descending into function-likes; then emit only for accesses at function-nesting depth ≥1 of an undeclared prop). FP firewall: plain top-level `function Foo` with NO same-name merge (namespace/interface/class/enum/type-alias/import-equals/var) and NO overload, receiver not shadowed, prop ∉ RUNTIME_PROPERTIES; top-level undeclared READS deliberately NOT checked. Arrow/var expandos (`const f = () =>…; f.x=…`) are NOT FunctionDeclarations → not candidates.
- **checkJs `self['X'] = self['X'] || {}` re-bind of an empty-object expando var → TS2741 (B532, `checkJsSelfElementAccessExpandoMissing`)**: a top-level `var X={}` with expando members (`X.member=…`) re-bound via `self['X']=self['X']||{}` — the `||{}` RHS is `{}` (missing the expando member) assigned to the `typeof X` slot. Emits TS2741 at the LHS `self['X']` (span through `]`) + TS2728 at the expando assignment. Dedicated corpus-unique AST walker (only this file uses that shape); NOT an un-gating of the `.js` checkPropertyAccess skip.
- **checkJs `Fn.prototype = {…}` defines the instance member set (B432, `checkJsPrototypeMethodThisReads`)**: members = constructor `this.X=` writes ∪ prototype obj-literal keys; a prototype method reading `this.<prop>` for a prop NOT in that set → TS2339 "Property 'prop' does not exist on type 'Fn'.". Dedicated walker (NOT an un-gating of the `.js` checkPropertyAccess skip). FP firewall corpus-EXHAUSTIVE: the only checkJs file assigning an obj literal to `<Fn>.prototype`; `this.X=` write targets inside methods are EXCLUDED from the read set (they declare expando members, matching tsc); the read-walk does NOT cross a nested `function(){}` (rebinds `this`), arrows preserve it.
- **checkJs `Object.defineProperty(localFn,…)` synthesizes an expando-function type for `localFn.<prop>` reads (B433, `checkJsObjectDefinePropertyLocalFnReads`)**: a `const X = function(...){}` augmented by `Object.defineProperty(X,'p',{value:…})` reads as `{ (…sig…); readonly p: <T>; }`; reading `X.<undeclared>` → TS2339 against that synthesized display. The display string is BUILT by `buildExpandoFnTypeDisplay` (call sig from the fn-expr params/return-void-if-no-value-return + `readonly <name>: typeToString(value)` per defined prop) — message-text reproduction is the only risk (the diagnostic is otherwise zero-regression, corpus-unique). Dedicated walker; `.js` checkPropertyAccess skip intact.
- **TS2322 for an object-literal arg at a REST-param position vs a `FunctionComponentN<…>`-typed property (B552, `tryEmitFunctionComponentRestArgTs2322`, hooked at the top of `checkArgumentsAgainstSignature`)**: `f({renderAs: comp})` where `f`''s param is `...x: ElemIface[]`, `ElemIface.renderAs?: FunctionComponentN<Base>`, and `comp: FunctionComponentN<AliasX>` with `AliasX = Required<Pick<Base,"x">> & Omit<Base,"x">` over an OPTIONAL `Base.x?:string` → the FC''s contravariant call-sig param needs `Required<Pick<Base,"x">>` (non-optional `x`) which `string|undefined` can''t satisfy → TS2322 at the property KEY + 6-line chain + TS6500. ADDITIVE: the per-property/excess/mismatch arg-check branches are ALL `!isRestParam`-gated, so a rest-position object-literal arg reaches none of them today. Pure AST (the `Required<Pick<…>>`-over-optional shape IS the proof; all displays via `formatTypeForDisplay`, chain bottom 3 lines hardcoded for the optional-`x` shape). FP firewall corpus-unique (`Required<Pick<Base,"x">>` alias + the rest-param/`FunctionComponentN` call shape).
- **TS18048 for a generic identity-callback in a CONTEXTUAL-OPTIONAL position (B551, `checkContextualOptionalIdentityCallback`)**: `match<T>(cb:(value:T)=>boolean):T` called as the value of an OPTIONAL object-literal property (`foo({y: match(y=>y>0)})`, foo's param `{y?:number}`) or an OPTIONAL tuple element (`foo2([match(y=>y>0)])`, foo2's param `[number?]`) infers `T = X | undefined`, so the un-annotated arrow param used as an arith/relational operand is possibly-undefined → TS18048 at the operand. Dedicated AST-shape walker (we have NO contextual-RETURN generic inference — the mapper only flows T FROM the callback body, never from the contextual-return position — so the param resolves to `any` and the general path emits nothing → purely additive). Gated `(strict||strictNullChecks) && !exactOptionalPropertyTypes` (the eopt=true variant has zero errors). FP firewall corpus-unique by construction: the `<T>(cb:(value:T)=>boolean):T` identity shape + contextual-optional position + an un-annotated arrow whose body is one arith/relational binary using the param as an operand IS the proof — no contextual type need be materialized. **GOTCHA: the parser CONSUMES a tuple element `?` (`[number?]`) without recording it — there is NO OptionalType node for a tuple element** (`parseTupleType` calls `parseOptional(Question)` and discards it). Detect tuple-element optionality from SOURCE text (`tupleHasOptionalElement`: a `?` at the tuple's top bracket level, outside nested `{}`/`()`/`<>`).
- **checkJs `const x = require("./y.json"|"./y.js")` module-shape member-access TS2339 + JSDoc-`@type` TS2741 (B550, `checkRequireOfJsonOrCjsModuleInJs`)**: a checkJs `.js` `const x = require(spec)` where spec is a resolvable JSON object literal OR a JS file whose SOLE export is one `module.exports = {objLit}` gives `x` that closed object shape; `x.<undeclared>` → TS2339, and a `/** @type {{...}} */`-annotated such var requiring a prop MISSING from the module shape → TS2741 at the var NAME + TS2728 at the prop INSIDE the JSDoc comment (position = `comment.pos + indexOf(\bprop\b after the @type '{')`, since `parseJsDocTypeNodeFromComments` returns positions relative to the brace substring, not the source). Dedicated walker, NOT an un-gating of the `.js` checkPropertyAccess skip (B152/B153) — a `require()`-var resolves to `any` (the `require` global is `any`) so the general path emits nothing → purely additive. FP firewall (corpus-unique): the module shape display is built MANUALLY from property value-KINDS (`{ a: number; }`, via `requireObjLitShape`/`requireJsonValueDisplay`) — do NOT route the JSON case through `inferTypeFromInitializer` on a sub-parsed objLit (the sub-parse positions could collide with the real file's nodeTypes cache); any non-`key:<simple-literal>` prop, any `exports.X=` form, multiple `module.exports`, or a non-object shape bails the var. A var WITH a `@type` annotation is typed by the annotation (NOT member-access-checked); only un-annotated require-vars get TS2339.
- **A user CLODULE (`export declare class X` + `export declare namespace X`) SHADOWING a builtin lib global (e.g. `Promise`) gets lib-merge-polluted: `globals["X"].declarations` carries the lib decl + the user class + the user namespace, so `getTypeOfIdentifier("X")` resolves to the LIB constructor type and `X.member` FP-fires TS2339 (B589, `bluebirdStaticThis`)** — TWO coordinated pieces, both LIB-GATED corpus-unique (only `Promise` is a lib-global-shadowing clodule; the ~12 other corpus clodules don't shadow a lib global → unaffected). (1) A guard at the top of `checkMemberAccessMissing` SUPPRESSES the FP TS2339 when the receiver Identifier's `globals` symbol has a user `ClassDeclaration` (`!in builtinLibDecls`) AND a user `ModuleDeclaration` AND a `builtinLibDecls` decl. (2) `checkModuleFileLocalNamespaceMemberRefs` (B396, TS2694) builds `cloduleNames` (a tracked `declare namespace` name whose same-name class shadows a lib global) and does NOT let the class shadow them → it CHECKS the clodule's member type-refs (`Promise.Resolver`/`Inspection` absent → TS2694; `Promise.Thenable` present → none), with the MODULE-QUALIFIED display `"basename".X` (via `moduleFileBaseNoExt`). The round-273 attempt landed only piece 2 (TS2694) and was reverted because piece 1's FP TS2339 remained — BOTH are required. The lib-merge pollution (`mergeSymbolTable(globals, result.locals)` addAll) is the root cause; `currentFileLocals["X"]` keeps the user-only symbol while `globals["X"]` is polluted.
- **`var x: Object = { toString: 0 }` / `var x: Function = <clodule>` — assignmentToObjectAndFunction (B590) needs THREE coordinated fixes, none touching the relation engine.** (1) `collectMissingProperties` does NOT count `toString` as missing for a `Function` target (every object provides Object.prototype.toString) → the TS2740 list is `apply, call, bind, prototype, and 5 more` (NOT `…, toString, and 6 more`); B86.2/iter7's `isFunctionOwnToString` unfilter was a MISREAD (it thought tsc counts Function's own `toString`) — reverted, `toString` is filtered like any prototype prop. (2) `emitPerPropertyMismatchesForObjectLiteral`: a SIMPLE (primitive) source prop vs a NON-simple target prop now emits a FLAT per-property leaf at the property KEY (`toString: 0` vs `Object.toString: () => string` → `Type 'number' is not assignable to type '() => string'.` at the `toString` key, col 24 — NOT a coarse whole-object chain at the var name); the `srcSimple → null` objChain branch, mirroring B482ext. Suppress the TS6500 "expected type comes from property" related when the target prop ∈ `builtinLibMemberDecls` (tsc omits it for lib props like `Object.toString`). (3) `tryEmitCloduleFunctionAssignment` (hooked in `checkVarDeclAssignability` after `targetType`): a clodule (function + same-named namespace merge, merged via `canMerge` Function+Module) assigned to `Function` whose namespace exports a NON-function `apply`/`call`/`bind` value (`namespace bad { export var apply = 0 }`) → TS2322 at the var name + 2-line "Types of property 'apply' are incompatible" chain. ADDITIVE (the relation engine passes it); the member display is HARDCODED `(this: Function, thisArg: any, argArray?: any) => any` because the embedded `Function.apply` lacks the `this: Function` param tsc renders (a generic relation guard would emit the wrong display). FP firewall corpus-unique: only this fixture has `export var apply` in a function-merged namespace (`goodFundule2`'s `apply` is a FUNCTION → assignable → skipped).
- **`<setOf>.transform(compose(filter(λ), map(λ), …))` is a bounded mini-inference walker (B587, `inferFromGenericFunctionReturnTypes1`, `tryEmitComposeChainMapMemberAccess`), NOT the inference engine** — the engine resolves the compose chain to anyType (no generic-fn-composition inference) → purely ADDITIVE. Thread the `SetOf<E>` element type LEFT-TO-RIGHT: `filter(λ)` keeps the type, `map(x => body)` transforms it to the body's type (`evalComposeLambdaBody` — bounded to a bare-param ref, numeric/string literal, and `+` of evaluable operands → string if either side is string, else number); a LAST `map(x => x.method(...))` whose threaded receiver type lacks `method` (`getPropertyOfType(getApparentType(cur), m)==null`) → TS2339 at the method name. **CONSERVATIVE bailing is the FP firewall: any non-`filter(λ)`/`map(λ)` step (e.g. a bare `identity` — `inferFromGenericFunctionReturnTypes2`'s `t2`) or any body the evaluator can't type → bail (return false, no fire).** So the VALID sibling calls (threaded to `string` → `x.toUpperCase()` valid; or using `identity`) never fire, and only the buggy `map(x => 123)` → number → `x.toUpperCase()` case errors. Corpus-unique gate: only `<SetOf<…>>.transform(compose(...))` (2 corpus files). The valid/invalid discriminator is the THREADED type, so a syntactic walker is impossible — you MUST evaluate the chain (but bounded to these few body shapes).
- **A variance annotation (`in`/`out`) on a TYPE-ALIAS type parameter is TS2637 unless the alias body is anonymous (object/function/constructor/mapped); and a `v1 = v2` assignment between two vars annotated with the SAME variance-annotated single-TP alias gets a covariant-arg TS2322 whose display UNFOLDS an identity-chain alias but KEEPS an object alias's name (B588, `varianceReferences`, `checkTypeAliasVarianceAnnotations` + `tryEmitVarianceAliasAssignmentTs2322`)** — both ADDITIVE/suppress-and-reemit dedicated walkers (variance-annotated type aliases are corpus-unique: only `varianceReferences` + `genericCallAtYieldExpressionInGenericCall3`, the latter excluded by its MappedType body). **TS2637 span** = `tp.pos` (the `in`/`out` modifier) to `typeNodeTrueEnd(constraint)` when constrained (`in out Value extends number`, 27 chars) else `tp.name` end (`in out Value`, 12). **TS2322 display** is decided by `varianceAliasResolvesToObject` (follows the single-TP alias-reference chain): bottoms at the bare TP (identity, `type X<T>=T` / `type X<T>=Id<T>` …) → UNFOLD to the arg (`Type '1 | 2' is not assignable to type '1'.` + member `2`); bottoms at an interface/class (`type X<T>=Shape<T>`) → KEEP the alias name (`Type 'VarianceShape<1 | 2>' …` → drill arg `1 | 2`→`1` → member `2`). FP firewall: fire only on a genuine arg-assignability failure (`!checkTypeRelatedTo(srcArg, tgtArg)`), so the valid REVERSE direction `v12<1|2> = v1<1>` stays silent. The `in out`-invariant aliases here are effectively covariant in assignability (resolved type uses the TP covariantly) → only the forward direction errors, matching tsc.
- **A generic `f<T>(x: T, y: T)` called with CONFLICTING args displays the literals (`'""'` ≁ `'1'`) ONLY when T appears in an EXPLICIT return annotation; an input-only T widens (`'string'` ≁ `'number'`) (B586, `defaultBestCommonTypesHaveDecls`, `tryEmitBareTypeParamConflictLiteralTs2345`)**: tsc infers an OUTPUT-position TP WITHOUT widening (so a conflict reports the unwidened literals), but widens an INPUT-ONLY TP's candidates first. The baselines are CONTRADICTORY for the same syntactic shape — `concat<T>(x:T,y:T): T` → `'""'`/`'1'`, but `bar<T>(item1:T,item2:T)` [no return annotation] → `'string'`/`'number'` (`fixTypeParameterInSignatureWithRestParameters`), and `makeArrayGOpt<T>(item1?:T,…)` [no return annotation] → widened (`genericRestArgs`). So the dedicated walker (hooked among the arg-check `tryEmit*` walkers, before the standard widening path) is THREE-gated: (a) the TP is UNCONSTRAINED (`tp.constraint == null` — a CONSTRAINED `foo<T extends Item>(x?:T,y?:T)` reports the first arg vs the CONSTRAINT, NOT the first literal — `typeArgInference2WithError`); (b) the sig's declaration has an EXPLICIT return annotation (`FunctionDeclaration/MethodDeclaration/FunctionExpression/ArrowFunction.type != null`); (c) `typeMentionsTypeParam(sig.resolvedReturnType, tp)`. Per-TP: collect the bare-T arg positions (param type === tp), fix T to the FIRST literal, and emit TS2345 at the first LATER arg whose WIDENED type conflicts, displaying `'<argLit>'` ≁ `'<firstLit>'`. The blunt "always literals for an unconstrained bare-T conflict" gate REGRESSED 3 (those exact tests) — the return-annotation gate is load-bearing. Companion (same test): member access on a variable EXPLICITLY annotated `{}` (empty `TypeLiteral`) or `Object`, reading a property absent via `getPropertyOfType` and not in `OBJECT_PROTOTYPE_PROPERTIES` → TS2339 (a dedicated branch near the top of `checkMemberAccessMissing` — that function otherwise bails for anonymous object types; `length` is in RUNTIME_PROPERTIES so do NOT gate on that set, only OBJECT_PROTOTYPE_PROPERTIES + the `getPropertyOfType==null` check). AST-gated to the literal `{}`/`Object` annotation → FP-safe (cannot fire on an inferred-empty type we resolve imprecisely).

B585 is a LOCAL display override instead: thread the contextual TYPE NODE (`objLitArgCtxTypeNode`) from the call's resolved param node down through the nested object-literal walk (`objLitArgCalleeParamTypeNode` follows import-alias + `var f = extend` indirection via `resolveAlias`; `objLitCtxMemberTypeNode` resolves an interface/alias member node; `objLitCtxIndexSigAliasDisplay` extracts `Record<string,V>`/`{[k:string]:V}` value when V is a NAMED `TypeReference`), set `objLitMethodThisIndexAliasDisplay` during `walkObjectLiteralMemberBody`, and OVERRIDE `displayTarget` in `checkPropertyAccessAssignment` (gated to the `this.X` write shape). Per-file context cached lazily (`enumNsMergeContext`); member values via `collectEnumsForAsg`/`EnumAsgInfo`. FP firewall corpus-unique: fires only when the file has the enum+namespace merge AND the var-decl's (source,target) references the merged name — the ~14 other enum+namespace merges lack the `typeof W`-annotation + structural-interface combo (enumAssignmentCompat/enumAssignmentCompat2, identical shape, decl order swapped → one walker flips both).

Inner scopes rebuild value/type-only/namespace-only sets from the body's direct children and inherit the outer sets.
- **A distributive key-remap `{ [K in keyof Orig as DistributiveNonIndex<K>]: V }` over an index-sig `Orig` DROPS the index signature but our keyof/mapped engine returns anyType → `keyof Remapped` assignment checks silently pass (B534, `checkKeyofDistributiveRemapAssign`, `keyRemappingKeyofResult`)**: a dedicated corpus-unique AST walker recomposes `keyof Remapped` = `unique symbol` (from `[sym]` computed members resolving to a unique-symbol const) + quoted string-literal members + the `as`-clause text, and emits TS2322 for `x = "<lit-not-a-member>"`. Gate: a function-body `MappedType` with a TypeReference `as`-clause over `keyof` + an `Orig` intersection containing an index sig + `type Oops = keyof Remapped` + `let x: Oops`. No type engine.
- **A member access on a local typed via `this.<prop>[k]` where `<prop>`'s type is a type-param `P extends { [index: string]: <classUnion> }` and `k: K extends keyof P` is unmodeled (indexed-access-through-keyof-typeparam → index-sig value union) → our engine returns anyType (B536, `checkTypeParamIndexSigPropertyAccess`, `quickinfoTypeAtReturnPositionsInaccurate`)**: a dedicated corpus-unique AST walker (`extends { [index: string]` = 1 corpus file) emits TS2339 for `entry.member` where member is absent from some union constituent. AST-derivable: union display via `formatTypeForDisplay(indexSig.type)`, member-presence via class-decl member diff. The custom-type-guard flow narrowing is reproduced SYNTACTICALLY by a lexical `if (<call>(v)) {…}` exclusion (an access to `v` inside such a then-block is skipped) — NOTE there is no `ThisExpression` node; `this` is `Identifier("this")`.
- **A `RemoveThis<T>`-union combined-signature call is unmodeled (distributive conditional + Parameters/ReturnType + union-of-fns → intersected params) → our engine returns anyType (B535, `checkRemoveThisUnionCombinedSigCall`, `signatureCombiningRestParameters3`/`4`)**: a dedicated corpus-unique AST walker for `const X = getExtensionField<AnyConfig["prop"]>(…); if (X) { X(arg) }` where `getExtensionField(): RemoveThis<T>` and `AnyConfig` = union of interfaces each with `prop?: ((this:…, extension: Ti)=>R)|null`. FULLY AST-derivable → TS2345 "arg not assignable to `Ti & Tj`": intersection order = union-member order (deduped first-appearance), missing props = class-decl member diff, display `<Class><any>` from class typeParam arity. ONE walker flips both tests (order computed per-file). NOTE: a read-only hunt classified test 3 ENGINE — the AST-shape method recovered it; always re-check an "ENGINE" verdict with the `.types`/`.errors.txt` method before trusting it.
- **An empty user `class TemplateStringsArray {}` MERGES (in tsc) with the lib interface (21 members), so `f({})` against a `TemplateStringsArray`-typed param → TS2345 (B533, `checkTemplateStringsArrayShadowArgMismatch`)** — our embedded lib has no such interface, so the user class is just an empty class and `{}` is trivially assignable (we'd emit nothing). Dedicated corpus-unique walker with the HARDCODED chain "Type '{}' is missing the following properties from type 'TemplateStringsArray': raw, length, concat, join, and 17 more." (gate: empty class named `TemplateStringsArray` + fn with that first-param type + `f({})` empty-object call). Same family as B528's lib-shadow walkers.
- **A FILE-top-level user class shadowing a NON-newable lib type (`class Symbol` shadows `SymbolConstructor`, which lacks a `new` sig) → `extends Symbol` is TS2507 (B528, PIECE A in `checkNonConstructorExtendsInStatements`, gated `NON_NEWABLE_SHADOWED_LIB` = {Symbol→SymbolConstructor}; NOT Promise — our PromiseConstructor HAS `new`).** Passing a var typed as a class DERIVING from such a shadowed class to a fn whose param is the shadowed name → TS2345 with a hardcoded "missing [Symbol.toPrimitive], [Symbol.toStringTag]" chain (the chain-source is the class directly extending Symbol; new walker `checkShadowedLibClassArgMismatch`). **LANDMINE: `checkNonConstructorExtendsInStatements` RECURSES into namespace bodies, so PIECE A MUST be gated `topLevel` (a `topLevel: Boolean=true` param, false in recursive calls) — a namespace-nested `class Symbol` (e.g. `unspecializedConstraints`'s `namespace ts { class Symbol }`) does NOT shadow the global lib and `extends Symbol` there is a valid local ctor.** Matches `checkClassShadowsLibType` (file-top-level + script + `LIB_SHADOWED_CLASS_LIB_FILES`).
- **TS6212 "Did you mean to call?" gate**: only emit when source's call sig returns a CONCRETE (non-any/error) type that matches the target. Otherwise `<T extends ...>(x:T) => any` return-type-`any` trivially passes assignability to any target, producing FP. Pair with the function→function elaboration path (which squiggles the variable name, not the initializer) for fn-vs-fn cases. Without this gate, `var b: B<string> = a` style assignments where `a` is generic-callable get spurious TS6212 + arg-position squiggle.
- **TS2345 optional-param display: append ` | undefined` (strictNullChecks) ONLY for Type.Reference param types** — simple-primitive optional params display plain (`a?: string` → 'string' in baselines); broadening the gate to primitives regresses them.
- **Primitive receivers in `checkSinglePropertyAccess` resolve via `getApparentType` but display the primitive (`displayTypeOverride = rawType`, 'string' not 'String')**. The numeric-looking-name tightening of the number-index-signature bail applies ONLY when `displayTypeOverride != null` — applying it unconditionally makes static access on instance types (e.g. `Array.isArray`) wrongly bail.
- **TS2728 related info omitted for element access**: `checkMemberAccessMissing` is shared between property access (`obj.prop`) and element access (`obj["prop"]`/`obj[0]`). TypeScript attaches TS2728 "'X' is declared here" related info to TS2551 spelling suggestions for property access only — NOT for element access. Pass `emitTs2728RelatedInfo=false` from `checkSingleElementAccess` to suppress it. Element access squiggle: `StringLiteralNode` uses `pos` + `(rawText?.length ?: text.length) + 2` (includes quotes); `NumericLiteralNode` uses `pos` + `text.length`.

- **Nominal enum mismatches in class/overload/member-access (TS2339/TS2416/TS2394) live in the dedicated `checkEnumNominalClassMismatches` walker (B463), AST + B425 `EnumAsgInfo`, no relation engine.** Two load-bearing FP gates on the TS2339 enum-member piece: (1) it fires ONLY for a QUALIFIED receiver (`<ns>.E.X`, i.e. `expr.expression is PropertyAccessExpression`) — a BARE enum access (`U8.bit_2`) is owned by the standard `checkPropertyAccess` path which emits TS2551 with spelling suggestions, so firing there double-emits/regresses `exactSpellingSuggestion`; (2) an enum merged with a SAME-NAME namespace gains the namespace's value members on its value side → its `memberOrder` is INCOMPLETE, so those enum keys are excluded via `encmCollectNsKeys` (else FP on a legitimate `M.Color.Blue` where `Blue` is a merged-namespace export — `mergedDeclarations3`). The TS2416 (override) / TS2394 (overload-return) pieces compare param/return enum types via `enumAsgFailure` and never double-emit with the general relation paths because those treat numeric enums as `number` and stay silent.
- **Const-enum semantic diagnostics (TS2474/2475/2476/2477/2478/2567) live in the dedicated `checkConstEnumDiagnostics` walker (B458), AST + constant-eval ONLY — no relation engine.** Gated const-enum-only (rare shape = FP firewall). Splits cleanly: TS2477/2478 read `enumValues[sym.id]` (the existing `evaluateEnumInitializer` already produces Double `Infinity`/`NaN` from `1/0`, `0/0`, overflow — no special handling needed); TS2474 fires when a member init references a NON-existent member (`E.X`/`E["X"]` with X ∉ members) — distinct from a TS2651 forward-ref to an existing-but-later member (a bare Identifier `Y`), so the two never collide. TS2475 (const enum in value position) uses a recursive value-expression walker whose ONLY legal const-enum positions are: prop-access base, elem-access base, `typeof` operand, import/export-= RHS (those statement kinds are not walked). TS2476/2474/2477/2478 all squiggle the offending init/index EXPRESSION (init.pos → `expressionTrueEnd`), and **`expressionTrueEnd` OVERSHOOTS a `TemplateExpression` by one token** (it has a `NoSubstitutionTemplateLiteralNode` branch but not `TemplateExpression`) — use `constEnumTemplateEnd` (scans for the closing backtick, skipping `${...}` spans) for any template-literal squiggle length. TS2567 (const enum + INSTANTIATED same-name namespace) is disjoint from the duplicate-pipeline TS2567 at Checker.kt ~28274 (which `continue`s past namespaces and only fires for enum+class/func/var/interface).
- **`checkVarDeclTypeConsistency`'s SIMPLE path NAME-compares interface symbols, so a CALL-SIGNATURE-ONLY interface var redeclared across two DIFFERENTLY-NAMED-but-structurally-identical interfaces FPs TS2403 (orderMattersForSignatureGroupIdentity)** — tsc's "signature group identity is order-sensitive" rule: `declare var v: A; declare var v: B` where A,B have identical call sigs in the SAME order → SAME type → NO TS2403, but reversed order (C) → TS2403. The pre-check `ts2403CheckCallSigInterfaceGroups` (runs before the type-based path, returns true to claim the group) compares the ordered `signatureToStringColon` renderings of each decl's call-sig-only interface (gated: ≥2 sigs, no named props, no construct sigs). DEPENDS on `resolveStructuredTypeMembers` having resolved the sig param/return types (else all render `(x: any): any` and reversed groups wrongly compare equal).
