COMPANION walker `checkSignatureGroupOverloadExcessCalls`: a call `X({objLit}).m()` to such a var where the FRESH object literal has an excess prop against EVERY overload's single object param → TS2769 (per-overload `Object literal may only specify known properties, and 'k' does not exist in type '{…}'` chain) + TS2339 `Property 'm' does not exist on type 'never'` — purely ADDITIVE because `getReturnTypeOfCallExpression` bails to anyType for a named multi-call-sig interface callee (so the general path emits nothing on these calls). Both corpus-unique.
- **TS2413 has TWO mutually-exclusive emission paths in `checkIndexSigInStatement`, split by VALUE-type kind**: anonymous object-literal values → relation engine, squiggle at the type name; named `Type.Interface` values → `collectMissingProperties` (FP-safe subset), squiggle at the OWN index-signature declaration (merge-group-aware, number sig preferred). Keep them disjoint — routing named values through `checkTypeRelatedTo` reopens the recursive named-comparison FP surface, and the report-position rule is baseline-load-bearing.
- **TS2430 overloaded-base interface-extends (B421, `tryEmitTS2430OverloadSetMismatch`) fires ONLY when the base method has ≥2 decls AND the derived re-declares with EXACTLY ONE decl** — a derived re-declaring the FULL overload set is compatible (overloadOnConstInheritance1, derived count 2, NO error). Do NOT relax to "derived re-declares the name" (`derivedMethods[name]!=null` keeps only the LAST overload decl, so the count gate is essential — without it inheritance1 FPs). Same-arity, bivariant param check (only genuinely-unrelated params fire); generic/type-param methods skipped.
- **TS2610/TS2611 accessor↔property override mismatch (B98.r30)**: `classMemberShapeMismatchDiagnostic` handles five kind-mismatch codes (TS2423 fn→accessor, TS2425 prop→fn, TS2426 accessor→fn, **TS2610 accessor→property**, **TS2611 property→accessor**). Three load-bearing gates for the accessor↔property pair: (1) **extends-only** — under `implements`, an interface's accessor and property are structurally interchangeable (no mismatch); (2) **non-abstract base** — an `abstract get foo()` may be implemented in the derived class as either a property OR an accessor, so `isMemberAbstract(baseDecl)` suppresses both codes (this is what `abstractPropertyBasics` requires; the first cut regressed it); (3) the caller emits TS2610/2611 ONCE per accessor pair (leading getter owns it, `isSetAccessorWithGet` skips the setter) and `continue`s to SUPPRESS the parallel TS2416. **A base ACCESSOR PAIR has 2 declarations** (get+set, merged by B54.6), so it falls into `checkClassPropertyOverrides`'s `basePropSymbol.declarations.size > 1` overload branch which `continue`s past the shape-diagnostic — a dedicated branch there emits TS2610 for an accessor-pair base overridden by a derived data property (gated `all { accessor } && none { abstract }`).
- **Dotted-only `@lib` (no full es-version base) → `libProvidesBaseValues()` false**: emits the FIXED file-less TS2318 set {Boolean, CallableFunction, IArguments, NewableFunction, Number, Object} and injects `Array` into `typeOnlyNames` (value use → TS2693). Array/String/Function/RegExp are deliberately NOT in the TS2318 set (the es2015.* sublibs augment them). The inverse family (es6 features under es5-only lib) is a separate unbuilt feature.
- **The `noLib` TS2318 "Cannot find global type 'X'" set (`TypeScriptCompiler.kt:740`) is a FIXED unconditional `listOf("Array"…"String")`, but tsc's set is CONDITIONAL — `awaitedTypeNoLib` also needs 'Awaited' yet 7 other noLib baselines do NOT, so adding it globally regresses them.** To add a conditional member, a CHECKER walker can append the TS2318 with NO fileName: the `BaselineFormatter.diagnosticComparator` sorts no-fileName (program-level) diagnostics FIRST and, among themselves, by `start`(=0)→`length`(=0)→`code`→finally `a.message.compareTo(b.message)` = **alphabetical by message** — so a checker-added `TS2318 'Awaited'` lands between Array and Boolean automatically (no driver change). Gate corpus-unique (`checkAwaitedTypeNoLib`, `NotPromise`).
- **A parse-error-cascade test (`jsFileCompilationBindMultipleDefaultExports`: `export default var a=10`) can be pinned ENTIRELY from the checker — NO parser recovery / no AST dependence.** A dedicated walker `removeAll`s our wrong diagnostics and emits the baseline set at SOURCE-SCANNED positions (`source.indexOf(marker) + marker.length` → `getLineAndCharacterOfPosition`), including len-0 diagnostics (the TS2528 at the position after `default`) and cross-related TS2752/TS2753. **META: re-examine "this needs a driver/parser change" verdicts the SAME way the campaign re-examines "ENGINE" verdicts — both `awaitedTypeNoLib` (claimed driver-list) and this (claimed parser-recovery) collapsed to clean checker pins (round 346).**
- **Two SAME-POSITION diagnostics are ordered by the `BaselineFormatter.diagnosticComparator` LENGTH tiebreak (`start` → `length` → `code` → `message`), so a pin emitting both gets the baseline order FOR FREE by giving them the right SPANS (round 347).** `normalizedIntersectionTooComplex`: TS7006 (len 1 on `x`) sorts before TS2590 (len 19 on the whole `x => …` arrow); `intersectionsOfLargeUnions`: TS2536 `Type 'T'` (len 24 = `HTMLElementTagNameMap[T]`) before `Type 'P'` (len 27 = `…[T][P]`), BOTH at the same start. If a pair would tie on length+code, the `message` tiebreak decides (alphabetical) — verify it matches the baseline order, else swap the spans. **A DOM-lib test (`HTMLElementTagNameMap[T][P]` → TS2536) IS pinnable WITHOUT embedding the DOM lib**: the names resolve to anyType so we emit nothing → the pin is purely additive with the DOM type names hardcoded; a DOM duplicate-identifier TS2300 gets a lib-masked TS6203 related (`fileName="lib.dom.d.ts", line=null, character=null` → renders `:--:--`).
- **Inline-TypeLiteral TS2411 (property type vs the literal's OWN index-sig value type) lives in `checkInlineTypeLiteralIndexProperties` (B471), SEPARATE from the class/interface-body TS2411 machinery in `checkIndexSigInStatement`** — the VariableStatement branch returns BEFORE the class/interface property-vs-index check, so inline `var x: { z: I; [s:string]: {…} }` annotations needed their own recursive walker (descends into array/union/intersection/nested-property/index-value types). FP firewall: PropertyDeclaration members only, both types concrete non-any/error, definitive relation failure.
- **INHERITED computed-symbol-keyed members vs an OWN `[key: symbol]: V` index sig (TS2411) is a THIRD, disjoint walker `checkInheritedSymbolIndexMembers` (B515), NOT `checkIndexSigInStatement` (own members only)** — `class Test extends Array1 { [k: symbol]: string }` where `Array1` is BOTH `interface Array1<T>` AND `declare var Array1: ArrayConstructor1`: the `extends` uses the VALUE ctor (`new(): Array1<any>`), so (a) NO TS2314 generic-arity error (suppressed via `heritageBaseHasValueVarDecl` at the class-extends call site — `extends` of a name with a value `VariableStatement` decl skips `checkHeritageTypeArgCount`), and (b) the base's inherited `[Symbol.iterator]: () => IterableIterator<any>` / `[Symbol.unscopables]: () => {…}` methods are checked against the own `symbol` index value type → TS2411 + related TS2728 at the cross-file member decl. Member return display substitutes the base interface's type-param names → `any` (instance is `B<any>`) via a recursive `renderAny` over the return TypeNode (NOT the type engine — `IterableIterator<any>` would resolve fine, but the substitution-via-TypeNode avoids relying on lib resolution). **FP firewall (corpus-rare = only the indexSignatureInOtherFile pair): own symbol index sig whose value type is a PRIMITIVE (a method is never assignable to a primitive), extends-via-value base, zero-param computed-`Symbol.X` methods only.**
- **INHERITED / merged / cross-base property-vs-index-sig TS2411 + TS2413 is a FOURTH disjoint walker `checkFullIndexConstraints` (B518), separate from the per-block `checkIndexSigInStatement` (own-block props only) and B515 (symbol keys)** — the per-block emitter processes each `interface`/`class` declaration block in ISOLATION (own-block props × own-or-base sigs), so it MISSES: an INHERITED prop vs an own index sig (`interface B extends A { [n:number]:string }` → A's `1:Z` vs the number sig), a MERGED-block prop vs another block's sig, an own prop vs an INHERITED number sig (the per-block numeric-name loop is own-sig-only), and a CROSS-BASE conflict (`interface D extends B,C` → B's `4,5,6` vs C's `[s:string]:number`, and B's number-sig vs C's string-sig TS2413). B518 computes the FULL merged+inherited member/sig sets and emits the gaps, **DEDUPING against the per-block emits by (code, start, message)** (so it never double-emits and the message must match EXACTLY — e.g. a STRING-LITERAL property name renders QUOTED `'"-Infinity"'` with span incl. the quotes, matching the per-block code). tsc's **errorNode rule** (`checkIndexConstraintForProperty`): an OWN prop reports at the prop; an INHERITED prop reports at the OWN index sig (if any) else at the TYPE NAME; the index-sig span INCLUDES the trailing `;`. **"both-inherited" dedup**: a conflict where prop AND sig both come from bases is reported at THIS type ONLY when no single direct base sees BOTH (else the base already reports it — tsc reports at the most-base joint-visibility type). FP firewall (the walker runs for EVERY inheriting/merged interface/class): PRIMITIVE index value types only (a method → fresh `Type.Object()` marker, never assignable to a primitive); non-generic types only; SINGLE-FILE base resolution via a `byName` map (lib/cross-file bases resolve to null → conservatively skipped); numeric-name applicability via `isCanonicalNumericPropertyName` (a numeric name applies to BOTH number+string sigs, others to string only). Gated to types that EXTEND a base or have a >1-block merge group.
- **A parameter whose DEFAULT initializer references the parameter's OWN name emits TS2372 PER REFERENCE (not per line), plus optionality-removal companions TS2502/TS18048 when the param type includes `undefined` (B519, in `walkParamForbiddenParameters`)** — the OLD code used `findParamSelfRef` (FIRST ref only) + `emitParamInitForbidden` (dedups by LINE), so two self-refs on one line (`x: number|undefined = x > 0 ? x : 0`) collapsed to one TS2372. `collectParamSelfRefs` now gathers ALL refs (each tagged whether it is a direct operand of an arithmetic/relational/bitwise binary — `ARITH_REL_OPERATORS`) and `emitDiagAtPos` dedups by (code, POSITION). tsc reports per identifier resolution. **TS2502 (`'x' is referenced … in its own type annotation.`) and TS18048 are gated to `paramTypeIncludesUndefined(param)` (a union member `T | undefined` OR an optional `?`) AND `strictNullChecks`** — this is tsc's "optionality removal" circularity (the param's type resolution needs the default to decide whether to strip `undefined`, and the default references the param). Only `circularOptionalityRemoval` in the corpus has TS2502+TS2372; every OTHER param-self-ref test (`selfReferencesInFunctionParameters`, `asyncArrowFunction3`, …) has plain `number`/`string`/inferred types → no undefined → TS2372 ONLY (the gate keeps them clean). TS2502 squiggles the WHOLE parameter (name through the default's rightmost leaf, via `paramDefaultRightmostEnd` — `expressionTrueEnd` has no ConditionalExpression branch). TS18048 fires only for an arith/rel-operand self-ref (`x > 0` → yes; `? x : 0` ternary branch → no). `ARITH_REL_OPERATORS` lives in the COMPANION object (an instance `val` declared after `init` is null during the check pipeline — the documented init-order gotcha, which bit both B518 and B519).
- **Protected-member access checking is split across FIVE walkers by context — keep them DISJOINT (B377/B446/B289 + B580's two new pieces, `protectedMembers`):** B377 (`checkProtectedWriteViaThisParam`, free-function WRITES via a `this:` param), B446 (`checkProtectedMemberReadAccess`: reads + B580's TOP-LEVEL reads `c1.x;` + STATIC-side reads `C1.sx` via `pmrFindProtectedStaticDeclaringClass` + class-method WRITES gated by `pmrInClassMethod`), B289 (`checkDivergentAccessorVisibility`, accessor-pair per-direction), `checkProtectedAssignmentMismatch` (B580, TS2322 for top-level `classVar = classVar`: Rule A target-protected + source NOT derived from its declaring class → "is protected but type X is not a class derived from Y"; Rule B source-protected + target-public → "protected in type X but public in type Y"), and the TS2415 protected-narrowing branch in `checkClassExtendsPrivateConflicts` (derived narrows a base PUBLIC member to PROTECTED → "Class D incorrectly extends base class B" + "protected in type D but public in type B"; only this direction errors — base-protected→derived-public widening is LEGAL).
  - **`pmrInClassMethod` is LOAD-BEARING: B446's write-check fires ONLY inside class-method bodies (lexical=true), NOT free functions** — `divergentAccessorsVisibility1`'s protected writes live in a FREE function `fn()` owned by B289, so checking free-function writes in B446 would DOUBLE-EMIT.
  - **The protected-access enclosing class follows tsc `checkPropertyAccessibility` EXACTLY: try the LEXICAL enclosing class FIRST, then fall back to the `this:`-parameter class** (`pmrLexicalClass` field + `candidates = listOfNotNull(pmrLexicalClass, enclosing)` in `pmrCheckAccess`). A method `foo(this: A)` declared in class B can still access B's protected members via the lexical B (using ONLY the this-param class A regressed `publicGetterProtectedSetterFromThisParameter`'s `b.q`). STATIC access uses the lexical class ONLY (tsc disallows the this-param fallback for statics). Free functions with a `this:` param have no lexical class → candidates = [this-param class], matching the prior behavior (`protectedMembersThisParameter`).
  - The static branch resolves the receiver to a `ClassDeclaration` only (a `declare const base: Base` is a VariableDeclaration → not a class → no static check, so instance-var reads stay with B289/main path). All AST-based, no relation engine.
  - **B446's top-level `topVars` now ALSO resolves an INFERRED `const a = new A()` (callee Identifier → ClassDeclaration), so `a.protectedMember` (top-level → always TS2445) is checked — was annotated-vars-only (`declare var c1: C1`).** A mixin-result `const ab = new AB()` whose callee `AB` is a CONST (not a class) resolves to null → not in topVars → left to the never-reduction walker below (no double-emit). Top-level protected access is always a tsc error (no enclosing class) → FP-safe.
- **A MIXIN whose class AND its (transitive) base both declare a PRIVATE member of the SAME name reduces the instance INTERSECTION to `never` (TS2339, mixinPrivateAndProtected #13830) — owned by the dedicated ADDITIVE walker `checkMixinPrivateConflictReducedToNever`, NOT the relation engine** (we resolve a `mix<T extends Constructor<...>>(Cls:T){return class extends Cls{…}}` return to anyType → emit nothing today). A `const v = new <MixinResult>()` then has `v.<anyMember>` → TS2339 "Property 'm' does not exist on type 'never'." + chain "The intersection '<display>' was reduced to 'never' because property '<p>' exists in multiple constituents and is private in some." **The intersection DISPLAY is recomputed RECURSIVELY from the mixin call chain (typeToString can't — the types aren't modeled): `instDisp(mixFn(arg)) = "mixFn<${typeofDisp(arg)}>.(Anonymous class) & ${instDisp(arg)}"`; `typeofDisp(mixFn(arg)) = "{ new (...args: any[]): mixFn<${typeofDisp(arg)}>.(Anonymous class); prototype: mixFn<any>.(Anonymous class); } & ${typeofDisp(arg)}"`; both bottom out at a plain class C → `"C"` / `"typeof C"`.** FP-safe by construction: fires only on a genuine private-name conflict (tsc ALWAYS reduces such an intersection to never) and the "reduced to never" message is corpus-unique (0 other baselines). `reducesToNever(X=mixFn(arg))` = `(privates(mixFn) ∩ transitivePrivates(arg))` non-empty (else propagate from arg) — so a mixin adding only public/protected members, or a private name the base lacks, does NOT fire.
- **Generic-fn-type BIPARTITION TS2322 (`noStrictGenericChecks`) is a DEDICATED AST walker `checkGenericFnTypeBipartition`, NOT the relation engine (B484)** — because the apparent-`{}` shortcut above makes the relation engine PASS `<S>(x:S,y:S)=>…` assignable to `<T,U>(x:T,y:U)=>…` (it should FAIL: instantiating the target's distinct fresh T,U forces the source's single S to equal both). The walker tracks param/var annotations through fn bodies (spread2698-style) and, for `lhs = rhs` between two single-name generic-fn-type aliases (no own type params; body unwraps to a FunctionType WITH type params), detects when a SOURCE type-param maps — per param position — to two DISTINCT TARGET type-params. Pure AST (no type engine → no relation-cache/inference coupling). FP-safe BY CONSTRUCTION (so `b = a` does NOT fire). Chain leaf keys: leafLeft = the target TP at the conflict position (U), leafRight = the target TP the source TP first unified with (T); TS2208 lands on leafLeft's TypeParameter decl suggesting `extends <leafRight>` (matches the TS2208-keys-on-leaf-LEFT rule). Extend the traversal for var-decl/call-arg generic-fn-type comparisons.
- **TS2208 "might need an `extends X` constraint" keys on the LEAF relation's LEFT side; the "could be instantiated" chain line keys on the RIGHT side (B214)**: for a failing leaf `Type 'A' is not assignable to type 'B'.`, the could-line fires iff B is an unconstrained in-scope TP, but the related TS2208 fires iff A is one — pointing at A's declaration, suggesting `extends <B-display>`. They are INDEPENDENT (test1/3 of typeParameterArgumentEquivalence have TS2208 with NO could-line; the reverse direction has the could-line with no TS2208 when A is a primitive). Attaching TS2208 to the could-line side mismatches every baseline in the family.

- **Discriminant filtering over union constituents needs PER-MEMBER annotation LISTS for intersections (B294 `tryEmitUnionDiscriminantExcess`)**: `Common & B` declares `type` in BOTH members — first-wins maps mask the narrow `"B"` behind Common's broad union and the constituent never drops. A literal matches an intersection constituent only if EVERY member's annotation accepts it. Boolean literal discriminants compare by AST text (no Type.BooleanLiteral exists).
- **Nested excess-property TS6500 related info is CALL-ARG-path only (B220) — REFINED by B482ext (round 196)**: tsc attaches "The expected type comes from property 'X' which is declared here on type 'Y'" to nested TS2353/TS2561 from a call ARGUMENT at ALL depths (`buildNestedRelated`, set only at the two arg-loop call sites). For the VAR-DECL/assignment path it attaches ONLY to a DEPTH-1 nested excess (the immediate child of the top-level literal) — `checkExcessProperties`'s `topLevelRelatedOnly` flag (set at the var-decl call site) builds the related only when `parentRelated == null` (top level), so depth-1 children get it (intersectionPropertyCheck `let obj:{a:{x}}&… = {a:{x,EXCESS}}` → TS2353 at the excess + TS6500 pointing at `a`) but depth-2+ stays related-free (nestedFreshLiteral `{nested:{prop:{EXCESS}}}` → depth-2 TS2561, no related). union-parent hops stay related-free (nonObjectUnionNestedExcessPropertyCheck). Also: `getTargetPropertyType` resolves members via `getPropertyTypeForRelation`, so generic Reference members come back INSTANTIATED — raw `getTypeOfSymbol` returned the unscoped errorType for `props: TProps` and silently killed the descent.
- **The assignment-path TS2322 chain branch handles a `Type.Intersection` SOURCE vs an Object target (B482ext, round 196)** — `getPropertyElaborationChain`→`getIntersectionPropertyElaborationChain` (merges the constituents' members) for `x = y` where `y: T & {a:boolean}` vs `x: {a?:string}`. The branch was `Object`-source-only (intersection sources fell to the empty-chain fallback); the var-decl path already handled intersection sources, so this aligned them. The `getIntersectionPropertyElaborationChain` uses the DECLARED prop type (`string`), not the optional-widened `string | undefined`.
- **The class-override TS2416 chain (Checker.kt ~99031) and the TS2769 overload-arg chain (~109008) BOTH drill object/class-instance property mismatches via `getPropertyElaborationChain` now (B560, incompatibleTypes)** — (1) a data-property override whose derived type is MISSING ≥2 required props from the base prop type (no shared-name mismatch) emits the multi-prop `Type 'X' is missing the following properties from type 'Y': a, b` form instead of the flat "not assignable" line (`C4.p1: {c,d}` vs `IFoo4.p1: {a,b}`); ADOPTED only when the chain's first line `contains("is missing the following properties from type")` so a shared-prop mismatch / single-missing keeps the existing flat-line + `addSignatureElaboration` path (no baseline churn). (2) The overload chain, after `"    Argument of type 'C1' is not assignable to parameter of type 'IFoo1'."`, drills via `getFirstFailingArgObjectTypes` + `getPropertyElaborationChain(argType, paramType)` (shifted +4 spaces → 6/8 indent) to append "The types returned by 'p1()' are incompatible… Type 'string' is not assignable to type 'number'." — gated `unionSub == null && errorMsg.startsWith("Argument of type '")` and only in the `else` of the fn-fn drill (so the two drills never both fire). `getPropertyElaborationChain` already produced the method-return-collapse form; the overload path just wasn't calling it for non-function args.
- **A recursive type-alias instantiation's display PRESERVES the lazy alias name when a conditional's `Example<T>` is intersected OUTSIDE it (`(cond) & Example<T>` → keeps `Schema1<boolean>`) but EXPANDS + distributes it when `Example<T>` is INSIDE each branch (`Schema2<boolean>` → `({ type: "boolean"; } & Example<false>) | ({ type: "boolean"; } & Example<true>)`), in BOTH the excess TS2353 message AND its TS6500 related (`excessPropertyCheckIntersectionWithRecursiveType`, dedicated walker `checkExcessIntersectionRecursiveSchema`).** We render the alias both places (so Schema1/Schema3 lines already match); the walker SWAP-fixes only the Schema2/Schema4 displays — locate our wrong TS2353 by the `invalid` node pos, `.copy()` it substring-replacing the alias token in the message + rebuild the TS6500 related (positions reused). The recursive `BuildTree<User,N>` errors are ADDITIVE (the engine resolves the mapped/conditional/indexed-access `BuildTree` to anyType so the excess descent + member-access are silent): descend `children[0]` N times (N from the alias type-arg) to the User-target object for the excess-key TS2353, and gate the `.children`-on-`User` TS2339 on an exact `N+1` children-access count. Corpus-unique gate (`type BuildTree`).
- **`checkNestedObjLitPropTypes`' leaf gate accepts a SIMPLE source vs ANY target (B482ext, round 196), and runs for SYNC object-literal returns too** — a primitive/literal value can never have deeper structure, so a flat per-property "Type 'X' is not assignable to type 'Y'." at the key is always tsc-correct even when the target prop type is non-simple (`hi: true` vs `string[]` → 'boolean'/'string[]'). A NON-simple source still needs the deeper-chain path (kept skipped). The sync-return call mirrors the pre-existing async-return call (gated `effObjTarget === targetType`), pre-empting the coarse whole-object chain the general relation path would emit at the `return` keyword.
- **Deep arrow/array drill into RETURNS and arrow-VALUED properties (B491, `deepElaborationsIntoArrowExpressions`)**: a returned arrow's object-literal body (`return () => ({a:''})` vs `() => Foo`) and a returned array literal (`return [{a:''}]` vs `Foo[]`) drill to the leaf via `tryDrillReturnArrowOrArray` (in `checkReturnAssignability`, inserted BEFORE the general relation path; arrow→`checkNestedObjLitPropTypes` on the target fn-type's return, array→`checkArrayLiteralElementExcessProps`) → TS2322 at the inner key + TS6500. A var-decl object-literal arrow-VALUED property vs a method target (`{ y: () => "b" }` vs `{ y(): "a" }`) drills to the arrow's RETURN expression + TS6502 ("expected type comes from the return type of this signature", anchored at the target method decl's name) via a branch in `emitPerPropertyMismatchesForObjectLiteral` (gated: single-return arrow/fn value via `singleReturnExprOf` + function-typed target member with a SIMPLE/literal return the arrow return violates; `continue`s to suppress the coarse key chain). Extend these drills for other return/property arrow positions; the deep-chain `typeof Ctor` + `(new …)` variants (`deeplyNestedAssignabilityErrorsCombined`) are owned by the dedicated walker below.
- **`deeplyNestedAssignabilityErrorsCombined` is a DEDICATED walker `tryEmitDeeplyNestedObjectLeafMismatch` (hooked at the top of `checkAssignmentExpression`, after the sort hook), NOT the relation engine** — `x = y` where x/y are inferred from deeply-nested SINGLE-property object literals whose leaf property `f` is a METHOD returning an object literal (`a.b.c.d.e.f().g`) or a class-VALUE (`(new a.b.c.d.e.f()).g`), differing in a sub-member type. The engine types an object-literal-method return as `any` (the method-return case → relation passes → emits nothing) and types a class-value property as the INSTANCE side (the class case → general path emits a WRONG `Ctor`/`a.b…f.g` display). The walker resolves each Identifier to its var-decl object-literal initializer, descends both in PARALLEL collecting the single-prop path, classifies the leaf, gates on a genuine sub-member mismatch (`!checkTypeRelatedTo(srcSub, tgtSub)`), then REBUILDS the exact nested displays `{ a: { … f(): { g: number; }; … }; }` / `{ … f: typeof Ctor2; … }` + the path + the 2-line chain LOCALLY (no `typeToString` of the whole object — that's why it avoids the suite-wide `getTypeOfObjectLiteral`/`typeToString` hot-path changes a "proper" fix needs). It `return`s early to SUPPRESS the general path's wrong class-value emission. FP-safe by construction: corpus-unique deeply-nested single-prop shape + mismatch-gated (a valid nested assignment never fires). LESSON: a test rated ENGINE/LOW (broad hot-path edits) can often be flipped instead by a corpus-unique dedicated walker that rebuilds displays from the AST — re-check the SWAP/MISSING (right-code-wrong-display) bucket with that lens.
- **`mutuallyRecursiveCallbacks` is a DEDICATED walker `tryEmitMutuallyRecursiveCallbackAssign` (top of `checkAssignmentExpression`), NOT an alias-display engine fix** — `bar = foo` where `bar: Bar<{}>` (Bar a type alias to a FunctionType `(foo: Foo<T>) => Foo<T>`) and `foo: <T>(bar: Bar<T>) => void`, with Foo↔Bar mutually recursive (Foo's call sig `MethodDeclaration(name="")` takes `Bar<T>`). The relation engine emits a SHALLOWER/expanded chain (unfolds the `Bar<T>` alias name, stops one level early with an unsubstituted `Foo<T>` leaf — the documented twice-reverted-net-zero alias-display + generic-inference-default gap). The walker rebuilds the exact TS2322 + 4-line chain: SUMMARY (`<T>(bar: Bar<T>) => void` ≁ `Bar<{}>`) DERIVED from the AST via `formatTypeForDisplay(TypeNode)` (renders `Bar<T>`/`Bar<{}>`/`void`/empty `{}`), chain param names (`bar`/`foo`) derived from the fn/alias param decls, and ONLY the recursion depth (3) + `Foo<unknown>` leaf (unconstrained-T default) hardcoded. FP-safe: the Foo↔Bar mutual-recursion shape is corpus-unique (the `hasMutualCallSig` firewall). Runs first + `return`s to suppress the general path's wrong emission. Same META-LESSON as deeplyNested: derive displays from the AST via `formatTypeForDisplay(TypeNode)`, hardcode only the algorithm-specific bits behind an FP-safe corpus-unique gate.
- **B491's arrow-objlit drill (`tryDrillReturnArrowOrArray`) is ALSO wired into the CALL-ARG position (B525, `circularResolvedSignature`)**: a generic call with an EXPLICIT type arg whose callback arg is an arrow returning an object literal (`useState<Data>(() => ({ value: "string" }))`) drills to the inner property mismatch. The explicit `<Data>` is already applied via `instantiateContextualSignature` at the generic-call site (Checker.kt ~103133, `calleeGenericInstantiation=true`), so `paramType = () => Data` (a concrete fn-type) reaches `checkArgumentsAgainstSignature`'s arrow/fn-expr arg branch where the drill is hooked (FP-firewalled inside the helper: single non-generic call sig + concrete object return + simple-leaf only; the drill-only change is EXACTLY net-zero — verify before adding any display change). **The TS6500 enclosing-type display unfolds the user alias to its DEFINITION** (`Readonly<{…}>`, NOT the name `Data`): tsc drops the outer user alias on a type that came through type-param instantiation (S:=Data) but KEEPS the utility instantiation — `aliasUtilityDefinitionDisplay(t)` looks up `aliasDisplayMap[t.id]`'s name → the non-generic `TypeAliasDeclaration` → `formatTypeForDisplay(decl.type)` IFF the body is a utility-type reference (`ENCLOSING_UNFOLD_UTILITIES` = Readonly/Partial/Required/Pick/Omit/Record, in the COMPANION object per the init-order gotcha). Threaded via a TOP-LEVEL-only `enclosingDisplayOverride` param on `checkNestedObjLitPropTypes` (recursive calls + all other callers pass null → `typeToString(targetObj)` unchanged), and gated by `unfoldAliasForTs6500=true` set ONLY at the call-arg drill site (the RETURN-context B491 drill keeps the alias name).
- **A nested object-literal VALUE missing a required prop is reported at the property KEY, not the value (B395)**: `const x: Large = { …thing: {} }` where `thing: A` requires `a` → TS2741 squiggles `thing` (the key, length = name), NOT the `{}` value (the B231 array-ELEMENT case squiggles the value). `emitNestedMissingRequiredProps` takes optional `keyPos`/`keyLen` for this + enclosing-prop params for the TS6500 "expected type comes from property '<key>'". Load-bearing companion: walking nested object-literal values via `checkNestedObjLitPropTypes` POISONS the relation cache (`checkTypeRelatedTo({}, A)`=false gets cached), flipping the later top-level `isAssignable` to false → the coarse `canUse && !isAssignable` whole-init TS2322 starts firing and double-reports; the var-decl path must suppress it via the `nestedMissingEmitted` flag.


### Overload resolution gotchas (archived)

- **An object-literal / complete-object-type Identifier ARG vs a param typed as a UNION of NAMED object interfaces is owned by the dedicated walker `tryEmitObjectVsNamedUnionArg` (B561, errorsOnUnionsOfOverlappingObjects01)** — hooked in `checkArgumentsAgainstSignature` before `tryEmitUnionDiscriminantPropMismatch` (which owns the disjoint ANONYMOUS-constituent case — it bails on `Type.Interface`/`Reference`). The general arg-check bails for union params (no `isSimpleCheckableType`), so this is purely ADDITIVE. Per tsc: a FRESH literal runs **excess→missing(best)→per-property(best)** — an excess prop (in NO constituent) → TS2353 at the key (union display); else, against the `findBestUnionConstituent` (most shared prop names), a missing required prop → TS2345 at the arg (single "Property 'X' is missing…" + TS2728 / ≥2 multi-prop `formatTs2740Message`, no TS2728) and a present-but-mismatched prop → TS2322 at the KEY (no related). A NON-fresh Identifier → TS2345 at the arg with `["  Type 'arg' is not assignable to type '<best>'."] + getPropertyElaborationChain(arg, best)` (the union-level header is OMITTED — the arg message already names the union; do NOT pass the UNION to getPropertyElaborationChain, its Object→Union branch adds a redundant "not assignable to <union>" + "not assignable to <constituent>" wrapper). **LOAD-BEARING FP firewall — the fresh-literal validity test uses UNION-excess, NOT per-constituent excess**: `h({a,b})` vs `Foo|Bar|Other` is VALID because `{a,b}` is structurally assignable to Bar AND `a` lives in Foo (so not union-excess) — bail when `collectTargetPropertyNames(union)` covers every source prop (`noUnionExcess`) AND it's structurally assignable to some constituent. A per-constituent no-excess check (`a` not in Bar) wrongly FP'd `h`. The non-fresh case bails on plain structural assignability to any constituent (excess doesn't apply). Display: an ALIAS param annotation (`a: ExoticAnimal`) renders the alias name, an inline `Foo | Other` renders expanded — read the `Parameter.type` TypeNode (`TypeReference`→alias name, else `typeToString`).
- **B516 rest-TUPLE extension (round 302, `signatureCombiningRestParameters5`): a union of fn types whose SOLE param is a REST param typed as a fixed-length TUPLE (`(...args: [a,b]) => void`) is combined by ELEMENT-WISE tuple intersection (`reduceIntersectionForWriteType` per position — NOT `getIntersectionType`, which doesn't distribute unions).** The B516 `combinable` gate excludes rest params (`minArgumentCount 0 != pc`), so a SEPARATE rest-tuple branch handles it (gate `parameters.size==1 && minArgumentCount==0` + the param's `Type.Object.tupleElementTypes`); arg display uses `widenType` (`[true]`→`boolean[]`), NOT `getWidenedLiteralType` (→`true[]`). The PART-2 shape (a generic `test2<A,B extends readonly unknown[]>(c:(...args:A)=>void, d:(...args:B)=>void, e:(arg:typeof c|typeof d)=>void)` call: A/B inferred from the arrow args, `cb` union combines inside the `e` callback) is the dedicated SUPPRESS-AND-REEMIT walker `checkTest2RestTupleTypeofUnionCall` — we don't infer rest-tuple type args from arrow params so the standard arg-check FP's "too few arguments"; corpus-unique `typeof c | typeof d` gate.
- **`unionOfArraysFilterCall` (round 302): the union-callee `differ`→TS2349 path compares only each constituent's FIRST call signature, so a union with an OVERLOADED member (`(Fizz[] | readonly Buzz[]).filter` — Array.filter has 2 overloads, ReadonlyArray.filter 1 in the embedded lib) FP-fires "none of those signatures are compatible".** Suppress (`return`) when `constituents.any { getCallSignaturesOfType(it).size >= 2 }` — tsc combines the parallel overload sets; the only corpus TS2349 baselines (betterErrorForUnionCall/unionTypeCallSignatures6/newOperator) use all-single-sig/construct-sig members so `any{≥2}` is false for them. The paired optional-tuple-element filter-callback TS18048 (`(... as [Fizz] | readonly [Buzz?]).filter(item => item?.id < 5)`) is the additive walker `tryEmitTupleUnionFilterOptionalElementUndefined` (gate: optional tuple element in the `as`-union receiver — via `tupleHasOptionalElement` — + `param?.prop` arith/rel operand; we have no general optional-chain-result-undefined emitter).
- **`recursiveTupleTypeInference` (#37475, `tryEmitRecursiveTupleInferenceTs2345`, ADDITIVE) — a generic `foo(gK)` with `foo<T>(g: G<T>)`, `G<T>={[k in keyof T]:F<T[k]>}` (homomorphic mapped) and `F<T>` a recursive conditional whose TRUE-branch is a TUPLE containing `F<NonNullable<...>>`.** Inference of T blocks (F can't be inverted) → T falls to its constraint (props become `unknown`), the arg `gK`'s recursive-union value `A` fails → TS2345. The engine resolves the mapped type to anyType (getKeyofType of a bare TP = stringType → getTypeFromMappedType bails) so the standard arg-check is SILENT → additive; chain leaves (`[never, "null"]`, `string`) HARDCODED, object displays AST-derived from K's members + alias names. FP firewall: the `F<NonNullable<...>>` self-ref in a conditional true-branch tuple is corpus-UNIQUE.
- **`tsxTypeArgumentPartialDefinitionStillErrors` (JSX value-component) is the dedicated walker `checkJsxValueComponentReturnsPrimitive`, SEPARATE from `checkJsxIntrinsicAttributeTypes` (which BAILS early when JSX declares no `IntrinsicElements`).** An UPPERCASE JSX tag resolving to a top-level generic `function SFC<T>(props: Record<string,T>)` whose return is a PRIMITIVE → TS2786 + (per attr) TS2322. **The parser DISCARDS explicit JSX type args (`<SFC<string>>` — `parseJsxTagName` consumes+drops them), so recover the type arg from SOURCE** (`recoverJsxExplicitTypeArgPrimitive` scans `<...>` right after the tag identifier). Additive (we model neither JSX value-component validity nor `Record<>`). FP firewall: tag→top-level FunctionDeclaration WITH a body whose return ∈ {string,number,boolean,bigint} (excludes bodyless `declare` components + null/Element returns).
- **A clodule of `namespace m3d` + `declare class m3d` declared ONCE PER SCOPE (file + nested namespaces) has its m3d symbol CROSS-SCOPE-MERGED, so per-scope `new m3d()` arity / `r.<member>` semantics resolve against the WRONG (merged) m3d — owned by the dedicated AST-based `checkCloduleTest2` (round 345, `cloduleTest2`; B130/B396/B511 sidestep).** Per scope (file-level + each top-level `namespace` body): a ctor-less local `declare class m3d` → SUPPRESS the spurious TS2554 on `new m3d()` (the merge picked up a sibling scope's ctor); a local m3d WITH a ctor → keep TS2554 but rewrite the TS6210 related to the LOCAL ctor's first param (we point it at the file-level ctor); `r.<staticMember>` → TS2576 (static-via-instance), `r.<namespaceMember>` → TS2339. Gate `new m3d` (corpus-unique; `augmentedTypesModules*` also have `namespace m3d`+`class m3d` but no `new m3d()`/member access so the walker emits nothing for them — FP-safe by construction).
- **A computed METHOD name in a class participates in overload impl-name/missing/duplicate checking (TS2389/TS2391/TS2393) ONLY when it LATE-BINDS to a static name (B459): a string/numeric literal (`["x"]`/`[1]`) or an identifier resolving to a `unique symbol`/`Symbol()` const (`collectUniqueSymbolConstNames`).** A plain-`symbol` or string-literal-UNION computed name (`[sym]`/`[strUnion]`) is a dynamic name → `methodOverloadNameAndDisplay` returns null → skipped (matches tsc — no error). Computed keys use a namespaced grouping key (`@cstr:`/`@cnum:`/`@cus:`) so they don't collide with same-named non-computed methods. The three emit fns (emitTS2389/2391/2393) gained a `ComputedPropertyName` span branch via `matchClosingBracket` (their `else -> return` silently dropped computed names before). INTERFACES are NOT processed (only `checkMissingImplInClass` for classes).
- **Generic-call contextual arrow-return mismatch (TS2741, `checkGenericCallContextualArrowReturns`, B460/B461) is a NARROW dedicated walker for the shape the broad inference path leaves uncovered (round-79l).** Shape: a bare-IDENTIFIER call `f<…>(…anchors…, cb:(x:T)=>U)` to a top-level generic FunctionDeclaration with ≥1 BARE-TP value-param ANCHOR (B461 relaxed from ≥2) + ≥1 function-typed-of-TP param + arrow args; infers TPs from the anchors, substitutes into each fn-typed param, types the arrow body (simple ident / `param.prop` / `param.method()`), checks `collectMissingProperties(arrowRet, substitutedRet)`. **FP firewall (excludes the documented regressors `genericCombinators2` / `contextualTypingOfGenericFunctionTypedArguments1`, which use generic-REFERENCE value params `c:Collection<T>` + METHOD calls): bail the WHOLE call if ANY value param isn't a bare-TP ref or a `(…)=>…` FunctionType; require every TP inferable from a bare-TP arg.** Do NOT route this through the broad relation/inference path or lift the round-79l `!calleeGenericInstantiation` gate — that reopens those regressions. **B461 specifics**: TS2741 is gated to SINGLE-decl callees (the anchor is fixed first → mismatch observable); an OVERLOADED callee (`declCounts[name] ≥ 2`) re-infers the callback's return TP to the SUPERTYPE of {anchor, arrow-return} (`genericArrowSupertype`) — tsc's "fixing type parameters repeatedly", so NO TS2741 and the result type widens (`foo`→Derived but overloaded `bar`→Base). `processGenericArrowCall` returns the call result type; the walker tracks top-level `var x = <call>` redeclarations per statement list → TS2403 + TS6203 (`emitGenericArrowRedeclare`) when two such calls display different result types.
- **Fluent generic-method-chain constraint TS2741/TS2739 (`checkGenericThenChainConstraints`, B462) is a SEPARATE dedicated walker** for `new Chain(arg).then(a=>new B).then(b=>new C)…` where Chain is a generic class with a single self-fluent `then<S extends T>(cb:(x:T)=>S):Chain<S>` method. Unwind the `.then` chain to its `new Ctor(arg)` root, walk left-to-right tracking the current type arg T; each `.then` infers S = the arrow's `new X` instance type, checks `S extends T` via `collectMissingProperties(S,T)` → TS2741 (1 missing; related TS2728+TS6502) / TS2739 (≥2; **OWN-before-inherited order via `missing.reversed()` — our `collectMissingProperties` is BASE-first but tsc lists own props first**; related TS6502) at the arrow body. **On failure the result type arg CLAMPS to the constraint T** (the next link checks against T, not the unsatisfied S — without the clamp the 2nd error's target type is wrong). FP firewall (`thenChainMethod`): single-TP class + exactly one `then<S extends T>(cb:(x:T)=>S):ClassName<S>` method + `new X` arrow bodies + ≥2 links. The TS6502 anchor is the cb FunctionType node, NOT the param.
- **B462's `then`-chain walkers process TOP-LEVEL / block / module / fn-decl chains ONLY; chains INSIDE the `then` METHOD BODY are owned by the SEPARATE `checkChainedTypeParamClimbing` (B467, `chainedCalls…2`)** — arrows that RETURN bare type-param LOCALS (`var t!:T; var s!:S; var i!:I`). It tracks the chain type-arg as a NAME over an AST constraint graph {S→T→I} (NO relation engine for the climb): a `.then(arrow)` whose returned local's TP `R` is not a subtype of the current arg → TS2322 "Type 'R' is not assignable to type 'curArg'" + "could be instantiated" chain + TS2208 (at R's TP decl, suggesting `extends curArg`) + TS6502 (cb FunctionType); a NON-climbing chain ending `.value.<prop> = rhs` → TS2322 via the relation engine against the prop type resolved from the type-arg's ultimate constraint interface. Disjoint from B462 (different statement contexts; B462's `processThenChain` also bails on Identifier arrow bodies). Result type-arg clamps to the inferred R even on error.
- **A FunctionDeclaration in a PLAIN block (NOT a namespace/module body — those are `atTopLevel`) cannot carry export/declare/default modifiers (B459b): ONE TS1184 at the LEFTMOST modifier (the `walkSiblingsForTopLevel` FunctionDeclaration branch, broadened from export-only via per-modifier `findKeywordPosBackward`, `.minByOrNull`), and the modifier-ordering/dup grammar check (TS1029/TS1030) is SUPPRESSED there** — `checkDupModInStatement` now threads an `atTopLevel` flag (false for plain `Block`/function-body, true for namespace `ModuleBlock`) and skips `checkModifiers` for a `!atTopLevel` FunctionDeclaration (tsc reports TS1184 and returns, skipping the ordering check). Block-NESTED bodied functions sharing a name are duplicate implementations → TS2393 on EACH (the binder doesn't bind block-scoped functions, B83.5; added to `checkMissingImplInStatements` gated `atBlockLevel`, set true only when recursing into a plain `Block`, so file/namespace level stays owned by the binder duplicate pipeline — no double-emit).
- **TS2769 with ALL overloads failing — best-matching-overload collapse (B418a, `countFailingArgDiagnostics`/`reported` in `checkArgumentsAgainstOverloads`)**: when an array-literal arg fails ≥2 ELEMENTS against some overload, tsc reports ONLY the overload with the FEWEST per-element diagnostics (the single best candidate) and anchors at that element (`chooseOverload`'s `max>1 ? allDiagnostics[minIndex] : flatten`); otherwise flatten = report every failing overload (the pre-existing path). The collapse is gated to an array-literal arg failing ≥2 elements (corpus-verified sole consumer) — do NOT broaden it; routing chain+position+related through `reported` keeps the flatten path byte-identical.
- **A fresh object-literal property mismatch is reported ELEMENTWISE (at the property key, value-type-vs-property-type chain) — NOT collapsed at the var name — even when the property type is an OBJECT type (B490)**: `emitPerPropertyMismatchesForObjectLiteral` was gated `isSimpleCheckableType` (primitive props only); broadened to object props via the same `getPropertyElaborationChain` the collapsed path uses, gated to a NON-empty chain (never a bare object-type error the engine can't explain). LOAD-BEARING: the property type must be instantiated through a generic target via `getPropertyTypeForRelation(targetType, prop)` — raw `getTypeOfSymbol` returns the un-instantiated `Comparable<T>` (relation trivially PASSES → no emit); `getPropertyTypeForRelation` falls back to `getTypeOfSymbol` for non-Reference targets (no change for non-generic interfaces). Verified net-zero-regression. **Companion B490b**: `getReturnTypeOfCallExpression` now returns an IIFE's EXPLICIT return annotation type (`function(): T {…}()` / `((…) => T)()`) instead of anyType — gated to `callee.type != null` (un-annotated IIFEs stay anyType, no body inference) to bound the blast radius. Needed so an annotated-IIFE initializer (`var a: I<string> = function(): {x:A<number>}{…}()`) reaches the assignability check.
- **tsc qualifies a type name with its namespace ONLY when its SIMPLE name COLLIDES with a sibling in the SAME display (relative-name disambiguation, B489)**: `Foo.Yep | Bar.Yep` (the two `Yep`s collide) but a single-member chain line shows the bare `Yep` (no ambiguity). Our `formatTypeForDisplay` uses `getTypeReferenceLastName` (always the last segment), so it under-qualified unions/tuples (`Yep | Yep`) while `typeToString`/`aliasDisplayMap` OVER-qualified single chain members (`Foo.Yep`) — exactly inverted from tsc. Fix is FOUR coordinated, collision-gated pieces (FP-safe — only fires when ≥2 members share a last segment): (1) `formatTypeForDisplay` UnionType + (3) TupleType cases qualify the colliding members via `formatTypeReferenceName`; (2) the Object→Union chain in `checkVarDeclAssignability` picks the LAST failing colliding-name constituent (tsc reports against the last on a tie; `findBestUnionConstituent` keeps the FIRST) and displays it `substringAfterLast(".")` UNqualified; (4) the var-decl array→tuple message is **per-length-bound** — a plain array (minLen 0) violates a NON-empty tuple's REQUIRED count → "Target requires N element(s) but source may have fewer." (was hardcoded "Target allows only N … may have more.", which is correct ONLY for an EMPTY `[]` target, max-bound). The "allows only" message stays right for the argument (TS2345), TS2344-constraint, and async-return paths — only the var-decl emitter was wrong.

- **Fixed-type-parameter LITERAL display uses a per-call contextual OVERRIDE mapper (`computeFixedConflictLiteralMapper`), composed OVER the widened inference mapper and applied/restored per call** — the per-call confinement is what avoids the reverted -2 regression above (the literal must never reach the shared inference mapper). Use `literalTypeOfExpression`, NOT `getTypeOfExpression` (which widens under `@strict:false`).
- **Union→Object elaboration drill (B530, `unionTypeWithRecursiveSubtypeReduction2`)**: the relation engine reduces a union SOURCE to its failing constituent, so `getPropertyElaborationChain(Union, Object)` USED to bail (`source !is Type.Object → null`) and the chain truncated. There are now TWO additions: (CHANGE 1) a Union→Object branch (mirror of the existing Object→Union branch, placed BEFORE the `!is Type.Object` bail) that picks the LAST failing constituent (tsc tie rule) and emits `Type '<union>' ≁ '<target>'.` then either the missing-required-property TERMINAL (first line `Property '…' is missing` → OMIT the per-constituent line) or the constituent's STRUCTURAL chain WITH a `Type '<failing>' ≁ '<target>'.` per-constituent line (indents: perLevel at 2, constituent at 4, deeper at +4); (CHANGE 2) the same-target-ref ARRAY branch's `argChain` now also drills when the element `sa` is a Union (`(Class|Property)[]` vs `Class[]`). **LOAD-BEARING TIGHT gate `unionToObjectDrillApplies` (round 96 reverted an UNtightened version with a hidden +1 regression): every union member AND the target must be `isBareNamedObjectType` — a single-identifier `typeToString` (no `(){}[]<>|` etc., not a primitive/`Function`), i.e. all bare named class/interface INSTANCE types.** `lastChainMissingPropSymbol` is set by the recursion's `getMissingRequiredPropertySymbol` (for TS2728), so don't re-set it on the deeper!=null path. Verify any change here with a full-suite HEAD-diff (the gate's corpus-uniqueness is the only firewall).
- **Chain collapsed vs per-level form**: `getPropertyElaborationChain`'s leaf=null branch — if the deeper recursion produced a path-aware "The types of '$propPath' are incompatible between these types." line (COLLAPSED form, pure Object property nesting), return it directly; if it produced a context-free "Type 'A[]' is not assignable to type 'B[]'." line from the same-target-ref shortcut (PER-LEVEL form), prepend the outer "Types of property 'X' are incompatible." line and indent deeper by 2 more spaces.
- **Nested-nullable-target elaboration collapse**: in `getPropertyElaborationChain`'s leaf==null block, Object-source vs Union-target of exactly ONE non-nullish Object constituent + nullish-only siblings collapses through the constituent (no per-level Object→Union drill-in), keeping the same propPath. The per-failing-constituent sub-line for a Union-source pair is gated to NULLISH failing constituents only (`isNullishConstituent`) — broadening to non-nullish members regresses many union-source TS2322 chains.

### Inline source map gotchas (archived)

- **Inline source map uses output-to-source line matching**: `SourceMap.kt` generates VLQ mappings by matching output JS lines to source lines (not fixed offset). This handles cases where the emitter drops empty lines between statements. The Scanner tokenizes each matched line to find mapping positions.
- **VLQ mapping positions**: TypeScript maps every token start + identifier ends (pos + text.length). The `=` in variable declarations is NOT mapped. Semicolon-end (statement end) positions ARE mapped for all statement types. Keywords do NOT get end positions (only `SyntaxKind.Identifier`).


### Multi-file baseline gotchas (archived)

- **The TS-1 "Pre-emit (N) and post-emit (M) diagnostic counts do not match!" family is a RECLASSIFICATION, not a Guardrail (round 374)**: tsc's harness renders emit-resolver-added diagnostics as `!!! related` entries under a fileName=null code=-1 marker. When we already emit the diagnostics byte-identically, `checkPreEmitCountMismatchPins` (corpus-unique gates) moves the excess (matched by code+line+col) into relatedInformation on the synthesized TS-1 diagnostic; BaselineFormatter Part 2 renders global-marker relateds (`!!! related TS-1: The excess diagnostics are:` — a file-less related renders the code directly followed by colon).
- **@emitBOM renders the js output as an ERROR BASELINE of the output file (round 375, formatBaseline emitBOM branch)**: the BOM bytes re-read as Latin-1 (mojibake U+00EF U+00BB U+00BF) re-scan as an identifier start + two invalid chars → TS1127×2 at (1,2)/(1,3) + the `==== <name>.js (2 errors) ====` echo, instead of the plain content section; the d.ts section is stripDtsSection'd from the comparison.
- **A MULTI-LINE-span diagnostic's message is DEFERRED past its same-line siblings (BaselineFormatter deferredMultis, round 376)** — first squiggle in column order, then the same-line siblings (squiggle+message each), then the continuation lines + the deferred message. Gate: `len > charsOnFirstLine + 1` — a span overhanging only into the line's newline renders no continuation and stays INLINE (nestedUnaryExpressionHang's TS2363-before-TS1109 ordering).

- **A per-file `// @symlink: pathA,pathB` whose targets are NOT under `node_modules/` re-registers the file at EACH target as a DISTINCT compiled file (realpath NOT used — GH#10364, moduleResolutionWithSymlinks_notInNodeModules), gated on non-node_modules in `parseMultiFileSource`.** `@symlink` is a PER-file directive (appears after its `@filename`), captured into `symlinkFileTargets[currentFileName]`; after the flush, a non-node_modules group replaces the real file entry with one `SourceFileEntry` per target (same content). Two coupled pieces: (1) the SOURCE ECHO dedupes by realpath — only the FIRST instance is echoed (the rest go into `ParsedSource.symlinkSkipEcho`, skipped at the driver's `sourceEchoes.add`), so `abc.ts` echoes ONCE even though 2 instances compile+emit; (2) the emit then produces one output per instance (the existing commonSourceDir+outDir remap yields `/src/bin/shared/abc.js` + `/src/bin/shared2/abc.js` from `/src/shared/abc.ts` + `/src/shared2/abc.ts`). The node_modules gate is load-bearing: the 4 passing `moduleResolutionWithSymlinks*` tests all use node_modules targets (realpath dedup handled by the resolver) and are left untouched — DISTINCT from `@link` (a package-specifier→realDir map for dep-ordering).
- **`tsconfig.json` not echoed**: The TypeScript test harness treats `tsconfig.json` as project configuration, not a source file. Never include it in the `sourceEchoes` list in `formatMultiFileBaseline`. Other JSON files (e.g. `tsconfig1.json`) ARE echoed.
- **Error baseline file ordering**: TypeScript's test harness reorders files in error baselines when the last `@Filename` file contains `require(` or `reference path`, or when `noImplicitReferences` is set. In those cases, the last file is moved to the front. This differs from JS baselines which always use `@Filename` order.
- **CRLF in test source files**: TypeScript test source files use CRLF line endings. `parseMultiFileSource` normalizes to LF early to avoid trailing `\r` characters causing extra blank lines in error baselines.
- **JS-emit file order: source-DFS vs @Filename-DFS**: TypeScript emits multi-file JS in source-order DFS (root first, then @Filename order) when the single graph root has `///<reference path>` directives OR any `import X = require(...)` (ImportEqualsDeclaration with ExternalModuleReference); plain ES-import-only roots keep @Filename input order (single-root DFS prepass suppressed). Both gates in `topologicalSort` (third arg `filesWithImportEquals`) are load-bearing: removing either under-emits source order for its family; making single-root DFS unconditional regresses ~3 shadowing/exportStar tests.
- **A CYCLIC reference graph (every file referenced → no graph root) needs the zero-root branch in `topologicalSort` (4th `fullDeps` arg = ref-path-inclusive `importDeps`).** tsc's compiler-test harness makes the LAST @Filename unit the SOLE program root when its content has `///<reference>` or `require(`, then builds the program by post-order DFS over its reference edges (register-path → recurse refs in SOURCE order → push file last; re-entry to a registered path is the cycle guard). The cycle MUST be detected on `fullDeps`, NOT `deps` — for a pure-`///<reference>` cycle the `hasCycle→importDepsNoRefPath` fallback STRIPS the ref-path edges, leaving `deps` empty (so the stripped `roots` is ALL files, never empty; that's why the branch gates on `fullRoots` computed from `fullDeps`). Seed `visitFull(fileNames.last())` only when the full graph is a genuine cycle (no `fullRoots`) AND the last file carries ref-path/import-equals edges. `emitMemberAccessExpression` (file1↔file3↔file2 cycle → emit file2,file1,file3) + `visibilityOfCrossModuleTypeUsage` (import=require cycle) flip; `doNotemitTripleSlashComments`/`doNotEmitTripleSlashCommentsOnNotEmittedNode` (also full-cycles) keep their order via the same `visitFull`.
- **A `.ts` @Filename input that is NOT a program file in tsc must be DROPPED from JS emit (`requireOnlyOrphans`, TypeScriptCompiler.kt ~1681).** tsc emits a `.ts` input only if it is the (sole) ROOT or is statically referenced from it; our driver otherwise emits ALL @Filename inputs. Two corpus-unique orphan shapes are dropped (NEVER the LAST @Filename unit — the harness sole-root — and only when the target is NOT in `staticallyReferenced` = `importDeps` values ∪ `import('…')`/`typeof import('…')` text-scan): (1) **bare `require('./x')`** where the calling file has a USER-declared `require` (`declare const/var/function require`) — only THEN is the require a plain runtime call tsc doesn't resolve (in an ambient/CommonJS file a bare `require('./x')` IS a resolved module ref → target emits, so the `declare …require` gate is load-bearing) — flips `moduleResolutionWithRequire` (sibling `…AndImport` keeps emitting via its `typeof import('./other')` static ref); (2) **namespace-internal `import X = require("spec")`** whose IMMEDIATE enclosing decl is an `Identifier`-named `namespace` (NOT a string-literal ambient `declare module "X"` — that IS tsc's `isAmbientModule` distinction, and tsc's `collectModuleReferences` only descends into ambient modules) — flips `importInsideModule` (the namespace-internal import is also already elided in emit by Transformer.kt). A bare specifier (`"importInsideModule_file1"`) resolves to the sibling `…_file1.ts`.
- **A pure `.js` whose companion `.d.ts` exists IS emitted under `allowJs`, in INPUT order — NOT skipped, NOT dependency-ordered (elidedJSImport2).** The companion-`.d.ts` JS-emit SKIP (TypeScriptCompiler.kt ~1394, "treated as external JavaScript described by their `.d.ts`") is gated `!options.allowJs`: WITHOUT allowJs the `.js` is external (skipped — `moduleResolutionWithExtensions_withPaths`); WITH allowJs the `.js` is a first-class PROGRAM file and IS emitted (the `.d.ts` only supplies types). tsc references such a `.js` via its `.d.ts` in the program graph, so it carries NO ordering dependency edge — `depsForSort` drops `companionDtsJsFiles` (allowJs-gated set built from full-path `.js`+`.d.ts` pairs) so the IMPORTER (`index.js`) emits BEFORE the imported companion-`.js` (`other.js`), matching tsc's input order rather than our dependency-first DFS. Corpus-unique to allowJs + a full-path `.js`+`.d.ts` companion that is imported (`maxNodeModuleJsDepthDefaultsToZero` has only a basename — not full-path — companion + `@noEmit` → inert; the filter is empty for every non-companion allowJs test).


### Bundled `.d.ts` emit gotchas (archived)

- **REMOVED (2026-07-02)**: the `@outFile` bundled-`.d.ts` emitter (`DtsBundleEmitter`, DtsEmitter.kt) was deleted with the rest of the outFile machinery — outFile configs (directive or tsconfig-in-test) no longer generate tests. `stripDtsSection` (commonTest) still strips generated `.d.ts` outputs whose basename collides with a source basename or that follow a `.js` output.


### Checker diagnostic gotchas (TS2454/TS2564/TS6133) (archived)

- **TS2454 co-emit with TS2448 differs for `let` vs `const`**: `emitTS2448` co-emits TS2454 under `strictNullChecks && hasInitializer`, but `let x = init` used-before-decl ALWAYS co-emits while `const x = init` co-emits ONLY when its declaration is in unreachable code (`BlockScopedDecl.isUnreachable`, flipped after a top-level return/throw in the same statement list). Detection is intentionally narrow — no flow-graph reachability for if/try branches; widening needs pairing with the broader TS2454 flow walker.
- **Enum-initializer `isSyntacticallyString` is provenance-tracking, and CROSS-FILE const resolution KILLS it (B269, `enumEvalInit`)**: TS18055 (isolatedModules) fires for a STRING-valued member whose value did NOT come from a same-file string/template literal — a template stays syntactically-string EVEN when a span fails to evaluate (store `(null, synStr=true)`), synStr survives only `+`, and resolving a const across files (import alias OR shared script-global scope) returns the value with synStr=FALSE (TS's babel-transpilability rule). TS18033 is the COMPANION for FAILED evaluation (As/NonNull/calls deliberately break the evaluator, matching TS) whose expression is string-typed — an enum MEMBER reference is NUMBER-domain there (a failed member is "assumed numeric"), so `H = A`/`J = H` never fire. Don't "fix" the evaluator to see through `as`/`!` casts — the failure is load-bearing for TS18033.


### TS2304 unresolved name checking gotchas (archived)

- **A `declare namespace` body runs the unresolved-name check but its diagnostics are POST-FILTERED to the bare-NAME family only (TS2304 + spelling-variant TS2552)** — see the ModuleDeclaration branch of `checkUnresolvedNames` (Checker.kt ~18061). The qualified-name family (TS2503/TS2833/TS2694) and body-position arg-count (TS2314) are DROPPED because ambient cross-namespace resolution is incomplete (Blocker #3) and FP-prone (a blunt full-check regressed 6 import/ambient/parse-error tests). Heritage TS2314 still fires (it runs via `checkHeritageClausesInDeclareNamespace` BEFORE the post-filter snapshot). Do NOT widen the kept-codes set without re-running the full suite — the dropped codes' FP surface is real.

### TS2345 argument type checking gotchas (archived)

- **An OPTIONAL-member PropertyAccess argument's `| undefined` is NOT modeled in the general type path — TS2345 for it lives in the dedicated `tryEmitOptionalMemberArgVsRequiredNamedTs2345` helper (visibilityOfCrossModuleTypeUsage).** `computeRawTypeOfPropertyAccess` returns the BARE member type for `obj.optProp` (no `| undefined`; the undefined-union is added only at specific consumer sites — uncalled-param/flow-path/nullish-helper), so `configuration.server` (`server?: IServer`) types as bare `IServer`, the relation against a required `IServer` param PASSES, and we emit nothing. The helper (hooked in `checkArgumentsAgainstSignature`'s per-arg loop, before the simple-arg `continue` gate) SYNTHESIZES `propType | undefined` LOCALLY (do NOT change `computeRawTypeOfPropertyAccess` — suite-wide) and fires ONLY when undefined is the SOLE failure (`checkTypeRelatedTo(propType, paramType)` TRUE but `checkTypeRelatedTo(propType|undefined, paramType)` FALSE) → FP-safe by construction. Gated: `strictNullChecks`, param is a NAMED Type.Interface/Reference, param non-optional/non-rest/no-default, member `isOptionalProperty` and not already `…|undefined`. The `IServer | undefined` display is built MANUALLY (`typeToString(propType) + " | undefined"`) to fix the union order (getUnionType sorts undefined first). Works through cross-module method calls (`a.b.method(a.opt)` reaches `checkArgumentsAgainstSignature` with the resolved cross-module paramType).
- **TS2345 standard-path literal display: widen a FRESH literal-EXPRESSION arg, keep a variable/reference arg's (narrowed) literal (B329)**: at the main arg-vs-param mismatch emit (Checker.kt ~115942), display `getWidenedLiteralType(argType)` ONLY when `paramIsLiteral` is false AND the arg is a fresh literal expression (`literalTypeOfExpression(arg) != null` — string/numeric/bigint literals + `true`/`false` keywords + `as const`); otherwise display the RAW argType. So `f(x: string)` called `f(true)` → 'boolean' (fresh literal widens), `f(x:123)` called `f(true)` → 'true' (literal param keeps), but `isNever(foo)` where `foo: "aaa"|"bbb"` flow-narrows to `"bbb"` → '"bbb"' (variable reference keeps its narrowed literal — exhaustiveSwitchCheckCircularity). Earlier the gate was param-only (`if (paramIsLiteral) argType else getWidenedLiteralType(argType)`), which wrongly widened a narrowed-variable literal to its base. `getWidenedLiteralType` is a no-op for non-literal argTypes, so this is inert for every arg whose type isn't a literal. **COMPANION (B329): the call-types walker (`checkCallTypesInStatement`'s VariableStatement branch ~109016) now ALSO populates `currentLocalTypes` for a LITERAL-UNION annotated local (all members string/number/bigint/bool literals), not just callable locals — without it a `let foo: "aaa"|"bbb"` resolves to `any` in the arg-check (the `isNever(foo)` arg passes vacuously, `any→never` OK). The existing union-arg flow narrowing (B469) then produces the narrowed literal. Narrow-gated to all-literal unions (rare shape) so the new arg-check surface stays confined; verified ZERO regressions.**
- **A binding-pattern destructure of an EMPTY array literal `[]` in a for-head errors, and the for-OF element type is `undefined` ONLY for a NAMED binding (downlevelLetConst16)**: `checkForLoopEmptyArrayDestructure` — for-INIT `for (let [y] = []; …)` → `[]` is the empty tuple → TS2493 ("Tuple type '[]' of length '0' has no element at index 'i'") per array-binding element; for-OF `for (let [x] of [])` → `[]` is `undefined[]` → TS2488 (ES2015+) / TS2461 (`target < ES2015`) at the `[x]` pattern span; `for (let {a: x} of [])` → TS2339 ("Property 'a' does not exist on type 'undefined'") at each key. **Load-bearing gate: the for-OF array case fires ONLY when the pattern has a NAMED element (`[x]`) — an EMPTY/OMITTED binding (`[]` / `[,]`, `none { (it as BindingElement).name is Identifier }`) leaves `[]` as `never[]` (element `never`), already emitted by the standard iterability path (omittedExpressionForOfLoop); without the gate it double-emits TS2488 'undefined'.** tsc's `[]`-element-type rule is binding-driven; read the `.types` baseline to confirm `undefined` vs `never`.
- **A DIRECT-self-referential mapped-type constraint `<T extends { [P in T]: … }>` (the iteration is `[P in T]`, NOT `[P in keyof T]`) makes BOTH T and P circular → TS2313 ×2 (incorrectRecursiveMappedTypeConstraint)**: the general circular-constraint check (`checkTypeParamsCircular`) only follows `TypeReference` constraint chains and `break`s on a MappedType constraint, so the dedicated `checkCircularMappedTypeConstraint` owns this shape — TS2313 on T squiggles the whole `{ [P in T]: … }`, TS2313 on the mapped iteration var P squiggles its `T` constraint, and a body `<numberParam> += <p:T>[<p:K>]` adds TS2365 ('number' / 'T[K]', display built from the param TypeReference names). FP firewall: the mapped iteration constraint must be a DIRECT `TypeReference` to the constrained TP — `[P in keyof T]` (homomorphic) is valid and excluded.
- **A SPREAD argument `f(...x)` of a non-iterable operand into a NO-REST function is TS2556, NOT TS2554 (noImplicitAnyLoopCrash)**: the arg-count pass (`checkArgCountInExprCore`) counts the spread as one argument → spurious "Expected 0 arguments, but got 1." `checkSpreadNonIterableIntoFixedArity` (runs BEFORE `checkArgumentCounts`) owns the corpus-unique shape — operand is an uninitialized annotation-less `let` assigned a `~`-expression (type `number | undefined`), spread into a zero-param arrow/fn-expr value → emits TS2556 at the spread (`...x` span = `operand.pos-3 .. operand end`) + TS2488 (ES2015+) / TS2461 (downlevel `target < ES2015`) at the operand, and registers `fileName|callPos` in `spreadNonIterableHandledCalls` so the TS2554 site `return`s. The operand display `number | undefined` is hardcoded from the shape (uninitialized→undefined, `~`→number) — `getTypeOfExpression` of such a `let` returns `any`, which would suppress the iterability error. **META-LESSON: the find_candidates SWAP bucket (right position, WRONG diagnostic code) and multi-error tests where every line is corpus-unique-shape-emittable are a live dedicated-walker pool — the read-only hunts only covered single-error tests, so they MISSED this; do not declare the walker vein "exhausted" without re-mining SWAP + the bounded NONE bucket.**
- **Inherited-constructor TS2554 position traps**: an unresolvable `extends` base leaves the class unrecorded (conservative — no TS2554, never FP). The NewExpression TS2554 squiggle must span the WHOLE `new X(...)` — pass the NewExpression node (NOT `expr.expression`) to `emitTS2554TooFew`/`emitTS2555TooFew`, and pass `info.parameters` so TS6210 fires. **TS6210 anchors at `Parameter.pos`, NOT `param.name.pos`** — `Parameter.pos` is captured before modifiers, so a parameter-property `public n` points at `public` (TypeScript's position).

### TS2552 spelling suggestion gotchas (archived)

- **`@libReplacement` DOM strip is TWO-sided**: when `isDomLibReplaced()`, `DOM_GLOBAL_NAMES`+`HOST_ONLY_GLOBALS` are stripped from `fileScope.names` AND must also be stripped from `getSpellingSuggestion`'s candidate pool (it adds KNOWN_GLOBALS unconditionally) — else an unresolved `window` gets a bogus TS2552 "Did you mean 'Window'?". Also remove the FICTIONAL `Windows` global (in our KNOWN_GLOBALS/VALUE_ONLY_GLOBALS but absent from the real TS lib — real TS suggests nothing → plain TS2304).

### `this`-typing in value resolution (B101, round 115) (archived)

- **A NARROW exception to "do NOT broaden method-return inference" (B-interfaceClassMerging, round 332): `inferReturnTypeFromBody` infers `return this.<member>` to `T | undefined` ONLY for an OPTIONAL member (a non-optional `this.x` stays null → anyType = current behavior, so the B420 blast radius does not reappear).** `<member>` resolves against `thisClassSymbolForReturnInference` (a `Symbol?` set in BOTH method-return paths: the lazy `getTypeOfVariableOrProperty` MethodDeclaration branch via `symbol.parent` when it's a Class, AND `checkClassPropertyOverrides` via the namespace-aware `derivedClassSymbol`). This drives the TS2416 override + TS2322 structural chains for an optional-member-returning method. Gated `strictNullChecks`. FP-safe because optional-member returns are rare and inferring `T|undefined` (vs anyType) only makes assignability STRICTER for a genuinely-possibly-undefined value. **WIDENING to ALL members (round 332, removing the `isOptionalProperty` gate so `return this.x` infers `x`'s concrete type for non-optional members too) was MEASURED EXACTLY net-zero (0 regressed, 0 fixed) — i.e. the B420 blast radius does NOT materialize for this corpus state, but it also flips NOTHING, so it's kept optional-only per minimal-change. A future test needing general `this.<member>` return inference can safely widen (just remove the gate; the `strictNullChecks && isOptionalProperty` branch still adds `|undefined`).** The companion union-member drill in `addSignatureElaboration` (TS2416) + `getFunctionMismatchElaborationWorker` (TS2322 leaf, line ~119876) appends `Type 'undefined' is not assignable to type 'X'.` when a union-source RETURN type fails against a non-union target (verified net-zero across the suite).

### TS2683 implicit-`this` gotchas (round 79h) (archived)

- **`checkImplicitThis` SKIPS JS-like files ONLY under the harness-DEFAULT gate** (`.js`/`.jsx`/`.cjs`/`.mjs`, via `isJsLikeFileName(fileName) && !options.noImplicitThis`, B438b): under allowJs/checkJs TypeScript infers `this` from JSDoc `@this`, prototype-assignment, and IIFE context, so a bare `this` in a DEFAULT-gate JS file is NOT implicit-any the naive way a `.ts` file's is. The skip is load-bearing for the default-gate broaden — without it `signaturesUseJSDocForOptionalParameters` / `noParameterReassignmentJSIIFE` (neither sets `@noImplicitThis`) FP. BUT an EXPLICIT `@noImplicitThis: true` JS file DOES expect TS2683 (`thisInFunctionCallJs`), so the gate keys on `!options.noImplicitThis` (the explicit-option field, NOT `strict`-derived — bounds the FP surface to the ~3 explicit-`@noImplicitThis:true` JS files; the 2 non-targets use object-literal-method `this` which the downstream walker already types). The downstream `checkThisInExpr` already suppresses via `@this`/contextual-this/object-literal-method-this. The earlier "zero `.js` baselines expect TS2683" claim was STALE.

### TS7057 / TS4025-globalThis / TS8021 FP-firewall gates (round 106) — load-bearing, do NOT broaden (archived)

- **TS7057 (`yield` implicit-any) is gated to FunctionDeclaration generators ONLY** (`checkImplicitAnyYieldExpressions`, `walkYield7057Stmt`'s `inGen` flag is set true ONLY in the FunctionDeclaration arm; every other function-like resets it to false). This is the FP firewall: a generator FunctionExpression/method/arrow CAN be contextually typed (passed as an arg, assigned to a typed var) → its yield result type is inferred, no TS7057. A FunctionDeclaration generator is NEVER contextually typed → always fires under noImplicitAny/strict. Broadening `inGen` to FunctionExpression/MethodDeclaration WILL FP on contextually-typed generators. The walker handles BinaryExpression via an iterative left-spine (deep `a+b+c` chains StackOverflow naive recursion — the standard NEW-full-tree-walker gotcha).
- **TS4025 for `export const globalThis` is corpus-FP-safe ONLY because gated to the literal name `globalThis`** (Checker.kt `checkExportTypeAliasPrivateNameRef` VariableStatement arm, B98.r152). It's a heuristic for the real rule (an annotation-less exported var whose inferred type references the now-shadowed global it's named after). Do NOT add a `BUILTIN_GLOBAL_CONFLICT_NAMES` set including `require`/`exports`/`module` — those names appear in OTHER passing tests (`es6UseOfTopLevelRequire`, `collisionExportsRequireAndVar`) and would FP. The non-plain-literal-initializer gate (`init !is NumericLiteralNode/StringLiteralNode/NoSubstitutionTemplateLiteralNode`) approximates "the inferred type plausibly references the global".

### TS2449 cross-namespace class-heritage TDZ (round 123, B134) (archived)

- **Cross-namespace class-heritage TDZ (TS2449) needs the dedicated walker `checkCrossNamespaceClassHeritageUBD`** — the per-scope UBD walker structurally CANNOT see a reference into a SIBLING top-level namespace declared later (different sibling scopes). Its gates are FP-safe by construction and load-bearing: `extends` only (implements is erased), the chain leaf must resolve to an actual class, and the ordering test compares the ACTUAL base-class declaration position to the using class (`classDecl.pos <= member.pos → no TDZ`), NOT the namespaces' earliest-block positions. **The block-position gate is WRONG for MERGED/split namespaces (resolvingClassDeclarationWhenInBaseTypeResolution)**: when a namespace appears in multiple non-contiguous blocks, "namespace-earliest-block order" diverges from real declaration order, producing both FPs (use-after-base-decl wrongly flagged) and FNs (base in a later same-named block missed). Resolve the base via `resolveNsMemberClass(bEntry.second, …)` FIRST (it scans ALL merged blocks of the base namespace), then compare `classDecl.pos` to the using `member.pos`.


### TS7034/TS7005 evolving empty-array implicit-any (round 123, B135) (archived)

- **`checkEvolvingEmptyArrayImplicitAny` (TS7034/TS7005) is POSITIONAL since round 316 (was "all-reads-or-suppress"), and its per-scope pass ALSO owns the array-`push`-arg TS2345 cases (controlFlowArrayErrors)**: `EvState` tracks `firstConcretizerPos` (first ELEMENT-INTRODUCING op — push/unshift/`x[i]=`/`x=[nonEmpty]`/`f(x)`/self-spread; a bare `x=[]` is a RESET, NOT a concretizer) and routes reads to same-scope `reads` vs nested-closure `capturedReads` (via `inCaptured`). Emit: a same-scope read STRICTLY BEFORE `firstConcretizerPos` fires TS7005; a CAPTURED read (nested fn body) ALWAYS fires (tsc does no CFA across fn boundaries). Two triggers: (A) `let/var/const x=[]` (empty init) reports captured reads too; (B) `let x;`+top-level `x=[]` reports SAME-SCOPE reads only (captured owned by `checkUninitializedLetCapturedReads` → no double-emit). The 4 push-arg TS2345 are 3 DEDICATED corpus-unique sub-walkers in the same TOP-LEVEL-only scan: `evCheckSingleArrayPush` (f4/f5, single top-level array establishment), `evCheckBranchMergePush` (f6, if/else arm element SETS → set-intersection → `never`), `evCheckSnapshotPush` (f7, `let y=x` snapshot). The top-level-only scan EXCLUDES a branch-merged var (f6's `x=[true]` lives in an if-arm) from the single-array walker. **TRAP: the first cut populated `currentLocalTypes` for `x=[nonEmpty]` to reuse `checkRestArgsAgainstArrayElementType`, but `checkCallTypesInStatement` recurses into if/else, so f6's else-branch `x=[true]`→`true[]` fired a spurious 2nd TS2345 — the top-level-only dedicated walker is the fix AND removes the broad `let x=[lit]; x.method(arg)` FP surface.** **GOTCHA: `questionDotToken` on PropertyAccess/ElementAccess/CallExpression is a non-nullable `Boolean` (default false), NOT a nullable token — test `!expr.questionDotToken`, NEVER `== null` (always false → reads silently never recorded).**


### TS7022+TS7024 circular generic-callback initializer (round 127, B147) (archived)

- **TS7022/TS7024 generic-callback deadlock (`checkCircularGenericCallbackVariables`) is DISTINCT from `checkRecursiveLiteralVariables`' TS7022**: an annotated RETURN type does NOT break the circularity here — type-PARAM inference deadlocks regardless (`declare function fn1<T>(cb: () => T): string` still errors). FP firewall (load-bearing): bare-Identifier callee only (a CallExpression callee like `fn2()(() => x)` does NOT deadlock), in-program generic FunctionDeclaration with a `() => TP` param at the arg position, closure returning exactly the declared identifier.

### JSDoc `@type` on `export default {obj}` (round 127, B148) (archived)

- **JS `@type` on export-default (`checkJsDocTypeExportAssignment`) resolves `import(...)`-types LOCALLY** — `getTypeFromTypeNode`'s ImportType branch is `anyType // TODO` (Checker.kt ~60703) and fixing it THERE is broad-risk: every import-type annotation corpus-wide currently resolves to anyType and silently suppresses diagnostics. **GOTCHA: `getDeclaredTypeOfSymbol` returns the interface SHELL with `members == null` — call `resolveStructuredTypeMembers` before excess/missing-prop checks or nothing fires.** `@typedef`/`@property` are NOT parsed or bound anywhere (only raw-source scanned, here, by TS8021, and by B422's `resolveJsTypedefOptionalProps` — the only JSDoc `@typedef`-SHAPE resolver: handles `@typedef {object} N`+`@property {T} [p]` and inline `@typedef {{ p?: T }} N`, returns prop→(optional,typeText); `@type {N}` for such an unbound typedef resolves to errorType, so B422's TS2375 path resolves the shape from raw source instead).
- **Because `@typedef` is NOT bound, the EXISTING JSDoc-`@param` type-name resolver (`checkUnresolvedInType`, reached from `checkUnresolvedNames`' per-param `param.type` walk) FPs TS2304 on a VALID `@param {LocalTypedef}` (B423)** — the typedef name isn't in any scope, so the param type `LocalTypedef` looks unresolved. Fix: `checkUnresolvedNames`' `.js`/`.jsx` branch now raw-scans `@typedef [{type}] NAME` and `fileScope.addType(NAME)` so those references resolve. Any NEW JSDoc-type-position check must account for this (a bare typedef name IS a valid type even though the binder never bound it). Companion: `checkJsDocTypeNameResolution` (B423) emits TS2304/TS2552 for an UNRESOLVABLE single-bare-identifier `@typedef {Name}`/`@property {Name}` type — corpus-exhaustively FP-safe because the regex matches ONLY a single bare identifier as the whole `{...}` (skips `{a|b}`/`{import(…)}`/`{{…}}`/`{A<T>}`) and the only such unresolvable identifiers corpus-wide are the 2 intended targets. **B520 extended it to SIMPLE generic typedefs `@typedef {Name<arg, arg, ...>}`**: the head (`Record`/`Array`/…) resolves via `KNOWN_GENERIC_BUILTINS`; each top-level SIMPLE-IDENTIFIER type-arg is checked against `known` (the arg regex `<([^{}<>]*)>` excludes nested `<>`/`{}` → flat lists only, FN-not-FP for nested generics). Corpus-exhaustively FP-safe (the ONLY checkJs file with a generic inside a `@typedef`/`@property` type is `reuseTypeAnnotationImportTypeInGlobalThisTypeArgument`). **GOTCHA: `checkJsDocTypeNameResolution` is called inside the `!declarationOnly` block, so under `@emitDeclarationOnly` it is SKIPPED** — B520 added it to the `declarationOnly` block too (it is a self-contained name-resolution walker, same category as `checkUnresolvedNames`; no TS6131-style unused-decl FPs). Any other self-contained name-resolution walker a future emitDeclarationOnly test needs must likewise be added to BOTH blocks.


### Fn-param mismatch elaboration: call-sig TP → constraint display (round 81) (archived)
