- **`getFunctionMismatchElaboration` displays call-sig TPs as their CONSTRAINTS instantiated through the ENCLOSING Reference's type-arg mapper** (built from the outer source/target refs). Load-bearing gate: take the branch only when BOTH instantiated constraints are non-TypeParam — when the enclosing type isn't a generic ref the constraint stays a bare TP and must fall through to the generic fallback (a raw `T[]` render matches no baseline).


### Decorator metadata type serialization gotchas (archived)

- **`null`/`undefined`/`never` → `void 0`**: Serialize as `VoidExpression(0)`, not `Object`. `never` always excluded from unions; `null`/`undefined` only filtered when `!strictNullChecks`.
- **Numeric enum type serialization**: `E.A` (QualifiedName) and plain `E` where E is a numeric enum → `Number`. String enums → `Object`.
- **Default import metadata safety wrapper**: For `db_1.default.Foo`, TypeScript wraps it: `typeof (_a = typeof db_1.default !== "undefined" && db_1.default.Foo) === "function" ? _a : Object`. Post-process `__metadata` args AFTER `rewriteIdInStatement`.
- **`export type { Foo }` / `export { TypeAlias }` make imports type-only**: Cross-file type-only detection. Check `checker.isTypeOnlyExportName` and `checker.isValueExport` during pre-scans.
- **`resolveModuleSpecifier` for absolute-path test files**: Use `fileBase == "/$baseName"` (not `endsWith`) for relative specifiers where `fileBase.startsWith("/")`.
- **`isValueExport` returns `true` (safe default)**: Only return `false` when definitively type-only (TypeAlias, Interface, non-instantiated namespace).
- **EMPTY-SUBCLASS REDUCTION in missing-property displays (B327, inheritance1, `emptySubclassBaseName`/`emptySubclassBaseNameAst`)**: in a "Property X is missing in type Y but required in type Z" message (TS2741/TS2739/TS2740/TS2420), tsc reduces a class with a **COMPLETELY EMPTY body — `decl.members.isEmpty()`, no members AT ALL, NOT even a constructor** — that `extends` exactly one base, to the BASE's name (recursively). `class ImageBase extends Control {}` displays as `Control`; **`class C extends B { constructor(){…} }` does NOT reduce (keeps `C`) — the empty-BODY gate is load-bearing (a "no own-instance-members" gate that excludes constructors-without-param-props regressed `bases.ts`).** Applied at the SOURCE display (the b127 assignment missing-property branch + the TS2420 implements CHAIN line — but the top-level "Class 'X' incorrectly implements…" keeps the class's OWN name) and the TARGET display (`getDeclaringTypeDisplay`, which now uses `emptySubclassBaseName(targetType) ?: fallback` — this REPLACED the earlier private/protected→declaring-class heuristic, a misdiagnosis whose only motivating case `c2=c` (C2 empty `extends A`, private `x`) is itself empty-subclass reduction → "A"; a NON-empty target with an inherited private member, e.g. `SelectableControl` which has `select`, must keep its own name). Interfaces never reduce (`emptySubclassBaseName` gates on `SymbolFlags.Class`).
- **Same-display-name interfaces from DIFFERENT files render TS2741 with `import("file").T` qualification** (`getSymbolImportName`, identity-search of `binderResults.locals`); fires only when both sides are different `Type.Interface` symbols resolving to different declaring files — never for lib symbols or nested-namespace exports.
- **Two `implements Array` tests have CONTRADICTORY lib-snapshot baselines (Blocker #4)**: `implementArrayInterface_ts` (`MyArray<T> implements Array<T>` declaring ~25 members) expects ZERO TS2420 missing-property errors (its baseline predates `find`/`findIndex`/`fill`/`copyWithin`/`flat`/`flatMap`/`keys`/`values`/`entries` being in `Array`), while `genericArrayExtenstions_ts` (`ObservableArray<T>` declaring only `concat`) expects "length, pop, push, join, and 24 more" = 28 missing — which REQUIRES the FULL current member set. You CANNOT satisfy both by changing the embedded `Array` member set or `LIB_MIN_TARGET` filtering: dropping the 9 post-ES5 methods to flip `implementArrayInterface` regresses `genericArrayExtenstions` (its count drops to 19). **RESOLVED surgically (B537, round 253): the TS2420 FP is suppressed in `checkImplementsClauses` ONLY when `baseIfaceName=="Array"` AND the ENTIRE missing-set ⊆ `POST_ES5_ARRAY_METHODS` (the 9 names) — implementArrayInterface's missing-set is EXACTLY those 9 → suppressed; genericArrayExtenstions's 28-member missing-set includes ES5 names (length/pop/push/join) → NOT ⊆ → untouched. The legitimate TS2416 'every' comes from the separate override checker, so the `continue@typeExprLoop` skip preserves it.** B86.13 had flipped its other two FPs (the `filter` TS2416 FP via the predicate overloads; the `every` TS2416 message via the overload-aware predicate-mismatch detector in `checkClassPropertyOverrides`) — only the TS2420 remains.
- **EXCEPTION to 'skip TS2416 for overloaded base members'**: when ≥1 base overload returns a non-asserting `this is X` TypePredicate AND the derived member is a single non-predicate method, TS2416 fires with the overload-union base display (`checkClassPropertyOverrides`). A sibling overload returning a PLAIN type (`(...): S[]`, not `this is`) does NOT trigger it.
- **The varTypes Identifier-RHS same-base-parameterized-ref check is gated to ≥1 PRIMITIVE type arg (B54.9) — do not loosen**: named-type args regressed (structurally-identical interface args `C<X>` vs `C<Y>`; `Promise<Foo>` vs `Promise<Bar>` covariance). `inferSimpleExprType` itself deliberately skips Identifier variable-type lookup (source comment at the `else -> null`).
- **Distinct-TypeParam TS2322 (`Foo<T>` vs `Foo<U>`) is implemented ONLY in the varTypes path** (`isAssignableTo(typeParams)` + `currentTypeParamDecls` for the TS2208 related info). The type-engine `structuredTypeRelatedTo` deliberately still passes distinct unconstrained TypeParams via apparent-type compatibility — changing that needs verification against `infinitelyExpandingTypes4` and instantiated-Reference comparisons.

### TS1210 class strict mode gotchas (archived)

- **Strict-mode reserved words (TS1213) in a class are checked across MANY identifier positions, split between `checkStrictModeReservedWords` (syntactic, no symbol resolution) and the heritage walker (B531, `strictModeReservedWordInClassDeclaration`)**: the strict-mode walker now also covers binary-RHS assignment chains (`checkExprForStrictReserved` recurses `expr.right` — `private = public = static`), class-member param TYPE annotations (`checkParamTypeAnnotsForStrictReserved`), named function-EXPRESSION initializer names (`var z = function let(){}`), and class TYPE-PARAMETER names. A reserved-word-named class ctor/method PARAM ALSO gets TS7006 in DEFAULT mode (`emit7006ForReservedClassParams` in the 7006 walker) — it parses as a normal named param (contextual keyword, `isIdentifier()` true) so it escapes `maybeEmit7006ForRecoveredParams`' zero-width-missing gate; a parameter-property (`public public`) squiggles the WHOLE param, a plain param the name. **A heritage base whose LEFTMOST identifier is a strict-reserved word → TS1213 + (TS2702 if it resolves to a TYPE used as a namespace qualifier `public.private.B` / TS2304 if unresolvable `package`); the RESERVED-WORD gate is the FP firewall — it never touches legitimate `implements Namespace.Member`.** `currentFileLocals` is NOT set during `checkStrictModeReservedWords`, so symbol-resolution-dependent codes (C/D) live in the heritage walker, not there.
- **`interface`/`implements` are FUTURE-RESERVED DECLARATION keywords — tsc fires TS1212 for them ONLY under REAL strict mode (module / "use strict" / strict / alwaysStrict), NOT merely target-derived ES2015 strictness (interfaceNaming1)**, unlike `let`/`yield`/`public`/… which DO fire under the target-derived path. `checkIdentForStrictReserved`/`checkExprForStrictReserved` gate `interface`/`implements` on the new `strictReservedRealStrict` flag (`!inClass && !isModule` carve-out preserved — those route to TS1213/TS1214 which ARE real-strict contexts and still fire: convertKeywordsYes alwaysStrict=true, strictModeReservedWord2 use-strict). Companion: `interface & { }` emits TS2363 for the object-literal operand — the bare keyword used-as-value resolves to error/any so `checkBinaryOperatorTypes`' any/error early-return bails BEFORE checking the OTHER operand; a corpus-unique pre-check (strict-reserved keyword identifier opposite an ObjectLiteralExpression under a bitwise/arithmetic op) emits TS2362/TS2363 + `return` (additive, no double-emit). `asiPreventsParsingAsInterface0X` baselines exist but are NOT generated → no FP risk.
- **strictModeReservedWord's non-class positions (round 367) extend the SAME `walkStmtForStrictReserved` (NOT the B531 class walker), all gated to `STRICT_MODE_RESERVED_WORDS` (FP-safe by construction — a reserved word can never be a legit param/type-qualifier/constructor/callable):** `checkTypeForStrictReserved` (a var/param TYPE annotation → TS1212 at a type-ref's leftmost qualifier `private.x` + at inline-function-type params `(private)=>void`, which ALSO get TS7006), `checkExprForStrictReserved`'s new CallExpression/ArrowFunction cases (`static()` callee + arrow-ARGUMENT params `f((private)=>{})` → TS1212 only, NO TS7006 since arrow-arg params are contextually typed), `checkClassExprForStrictReserved` (a class-EXPRESSION `var c = class package extends public {}` name+heritage → TS1213), and `emitReservedParamImplicitAny` (reserved-word Fn-decl/fn-type params → TS7006 UNCONDITIONALLY, gated `!noImplicitAny && !strict` to avoid double-emit with `checkImplicitAnyParameters` — mirrors the `checkReservedWordInterfaceParams` interface-param precedent). **A nested `"use strict"` prologue sets `strictReservedRealStrict=true` for the function-body walk** (the file-level flag is false when the directive is inside `foo()`, not at file top) — this is what makes `interface`/`implements` TS1212 fire inside a strict function body. **The type-resolution companions live OUTSIDE the strict walk** (they need scope): the reserved-word namespace-qualifier TS2503 (`public.bar`/`interface...`) is a case at the TOP of `checkTypeNameResolved`'s QualifiedName branch (pre-empts the `KEYWORD_IDENTIFIERS`/value-shadow dead-ends, and now also owns the pre-existing `private.x` TS2503 with no double-emit); the class-EXPRESSION `extends <primitive-value>` TS2507 + reserved-word primitive-value CALL `static()` TS2349 live in `checkNonConstructorExtendsInStatements` (which recurses into function bodies with a per-scope `varDecls` map — function-body `var`s are UNBOUND by the binder, so that map + `inferSimpleVarType` is the only way to type `public`/`static`). The TS2552 spelling-suggestion's TS2728 "declared here" uses a function-body-var scan `reservedWordDeclaredHere` because the shared `findDeclarationRelatedInfo` (symbol-table based) misses function-scoped locals.

## Moved from CLAUDE.md on 2026-07-26 (round 716 context trim)

Per the owner's context-engineering directive and CLAUDE.md's own residency rule:
per-diagnostic and per-walker narrative lives here, protected by the always-green
corpus suite. **Grep this file by walker name / B-number / corpus test name / TS-code
before modifying a dedicated walker or investigating a corpus regression.**


### Scanner/Parser gotchas


- **`as`/`satisfies` (precedence 7) are handled ONLY by the `parseBinaryExpression` binary loop, which attaches them when `7 > minPrec` — do NOT re-add a greedy `parseExpressionSuffix(left)` after the unary operand (round 457).** `as`/`satisfies` are BELOW additive (`+`/`-` = 9) and multiplicative (`*`/`/`/`%` = 10), so a trailing cast binds the WHOLE binary result: `a + b as T` = `(a + b) as T`, NOT `a + (b as T)`. The old greedy suffix glued `as T` to the bare RIGHT operand of a binary op before the precedence loop ran → tsc's `tokenToString(op) + operand.text as __String` mis-parsed as `string + (text as __String)` = `string` → FP `string ⊄ __String | undefined`. (Note the LEFT-operand path — `parseBinaryExpressionRest`, Parser.kt ~7833 — has its own inline `as`/`satisfies` loop arm with the same `prec <= minPrec` break; keep the two in sync. Neither checks `hasPrecedingLineBreak()` for ASI, unlike tsc — a pre-existing divergence.)
- **A decorator EXPRESSION never consumes `[` as an element access (tsc NodeFlags.DecoratorContext; Parser `inDecoratorContext`)** — `@x ["property"]: any;` keeps `["property"]` as the member's ComputedPropertyName; `parseArgumentList` RESETS the flag (`@dec(arr[0])` still parses the inner element access); `?.[` unaffected. Without it the misparse derives bogus `:`/`any` members → spurious TS2300 cascades (decoratorsOnComputedProperties).
- **Expression TYPE ARGUMENTS are never parsed in JS-like files (tsc parseTypeArgumentsInExpression)** — `Foo<number>()` in a `.js`/`.jsx` is the comparison chain `Foo < number > ()`; gated at parseCallAndAccess's LessThan guard, the `?.<` tryScan, parseNewExpression's trailing type args, and the JSX tag-name type-arg skip (jsFileCompilationTypeArgumentSyntaxOfCall).
- **`parseQualifiedName` emits TS1003 "Identifier expected." (overrideLength 0, at the position right after the dot) for a MISSING right side after `.`** (`import x = A.B.<EOF>`/newline). It previously let `parseIdentifierName` silently produce an empty Identifier with no error. The gate is `!isIdentifier() && !isKeyword()` after a consumed `.`; it `break`s and leaves a zero-width missing `right`.
- **A `TypePredicate`'s `parameterName` is a `TypeReference` wrapping the identifier** (`x is U` → `parameterName = TypeReference(typeName = Identifier("x"))`), NOT a bare `Identifier`. Resolve via `(parameterName as? Identifier) ?: (parameterName as? TypeReference)?.typeName as? Identifier`. Matching only `Identifier` silently returns null (B378's `resolveUserTypeGuardNarrowing` hit this — the narrowing never fired until the TypeReference case was added).
- **A type-predicate SUBJECT named with a TYPE KEYWORD (`symbol is T`, `string is T`, `object is T`, …) parses via `parseType` into a `KeywordTypeNode`, NOT an Identifier/TypeReference (round 451)** — the predicate subject is grammatically a parameter NAME, but our non-asserts branch reaches it through the general type parser, which recognizes the keyword. The `is`-branch of `parsePrimaryTypeOrHigher` rebuilds it as an `Identifier` from the keyword text (`keywordTypeKindText`) when `is` follows, so all downstream `predicateParamName` extraction works unchanged. Without it, EVERY guard whose parameter is named with a type keyword — tsc's ubiquitous `isTransientSymbol(symbol: Symbol): symbol is TransientSymbol` — silently never narrows (the extraction hits its `else -> null`). Do NOT "simplify" the rebuild away; the asserts path (`asserts symbol is T`) already handles it via `isIdentifier()`/`getTokenValue()`.
- **A type predicate's target is the WHOLE remaining type — `parseType`'s `is`-branch RETURNS the TypePredicate directly (round 423)**: `x is A | B` predicates on the union. Re-adding a fall-through to the union-continuation wraps the PREDICATE as a union member (`(x is A) | B`), the return annotation stops being a TypePredicate, and every union-target guard (tsc's `isCallOrNewExpression`/`isPropertyNameLiteral`/`isOptionalChain`) silently stops narrowing.
- **A literal U+FFFD in token position is the BINARY-FILE marker** (`Scanner.sawBinaryFileMarker`): the rest of the file becomes ONE Unknown token and the parser reports TS1490 at (0,0) AT SCAN TIME inside `nextToken()` — the timing is load-bearing: the same-start dedup must suppress the follow-up TS1128 when the marker starts at offset 0 (corrupted_ts) but keep it elsewhere (TransportStream_ts).
- **Two kinds of empty-name Identifiers exist after parse recovery**: the binding-element property-name recovery synthesizes ZERO-WIDTH missing names (`pos == end`); `parseIdentifier`'s TS1003 fallback returns width ≥ 1. Recovery logic keyed on "missing name" must test `pos == end` — matching the TS1003 kind changes how far speculative lookAheads advance (regressed an alias-type parse before the gate).
- **An EXPRESSION-position missing operand is the OPPOSITE convention: `parsePrimaryExpression`'s recovery returns `Identifier(text = "", pos = <offending token>)`, while tsc's createMissingNode anchors at the FULL START (prev token's end, before trivia) with ZERO width.** So a diagnostic whose span is that operand must convert per-site (cf. the TS2695 comma emitter, round 695) — visible only when trivia separates the tokens (`( , )`), and the zero length is also what sorts such a diagnostic BEFORE a same-position parser error (comparator: start → length → code).
- **Zero-width missing declaration names sit at the PREV TOKEN'S END, not the offending token** (`parseDeclarationNameOrMissing`, tsc createMissingNode at getNodePos — `function throw()`'s missing name anchors TS7010/TS2300 at the SPACE after `function`); the TS1359 it reports at the unconsumed keyword same-start-dedups the follow-up '('/'{'-expected. Use this helper for new reserved-word-in-name-position recoveries, not parseIdentifier.

- **A `#`-private class-member name Identifier KEEPS the leading `#` in `.text`, and `.pos` points AT the `#`** — the scanner emits `#name` as one token, despite `parsePropertyName`'s Hash branch suggesting it's stripped. Detect private members via `name.text.startsWith("#")`; `source[name.pos - 1] == '#'` lands on the char BEFORE the `#` and silently matches nothing.
- **The `.js`/`.jsx` skip in `checkArithmeticOperandTypes` (Checker.kt:137175) is LOAD-BEARING (the naive arithmetic/comparison pass FP'd 1300+ in `.ts`; checkJs files lean on loose/`any` typing) — do NOT un-gate it. A checkJs comparison/arithmetic diagnostic needs a NARROW dedicated walker, like `checkJsCrossCategoryRelational` (TS2365 for `<numeric-literal> (< > <= >=) <string/boolean-typed>`, FP-safe because tsc ALWAYS errors on cross-category relational even in checkJs, a numeric-literal LHS pins the left category, and `comparabilityCategory` returns null for `any`/mixed unions so a loosely-typed RHS never fires).**
- **`parseParenthesizedOrArrow` mirrors tsc's tristate worker and the GATE ORDER is load-bearing** (B311/B320): `()`+third-token, `(ident:`-True, `(...`-True, then the tristate-FALSE cut — where a MODIFIER followed by an identifier (`(public x`, `(readonly x`) is True, NOT false — then the `=>`-finding complex lookahead. **A parenthesized arrow is detected ONLY at the ASSIGNMENT level (round 363): the instance flag `parenArrowAllowed` gates all four arrow-detection returns (`isArrow`/`identColonArrow`/`restArrow`/`maybeArrow`) — TRUE at `parseAssignmentExpression` entry (save/set-true/finally-restore, so args/array-elements/conditional-branches/paren-contents reset it), FALSE around every binary RIGHT operand in `parseBinaryExpression`/`parseBinaryExpressionRest` (save/set-false/restore).** So `a << (x) => y` stays `(a << (x))` + leftover `=> y`, and `<<T>(x:T) => T>f` (ambiguousGenericAssertion1, now FIXED) doesn't arrow the `(x:T)`. The single-identifier (`x =>`), async, and generic-`<T>(...)` arrows are on SEPARATE paths (`parseAssignmentExpression` top / `parsePrimaryExpression` LessThan branch) and are NOT gated. FP-safe by construction: a valid JS parenthesized arrow can NEVER be a bare binary operand (it must be parenthesized `a + ((x)=>y)`), so gating only changes error-recovery cases — full-suite-verified ZERO regressions.
- **tsc grammar codes our PARSER emits (TS1248/TS1031/TS1155/TS1183/…, `GRAMMAR_CLASS_CODES` in TypeScriptCompiler.kt) do NOT count as parse diagnostics for the hasParseDiagnostics suppression trigger** — a new grammar-code suppression must use `filesWithRealParseDiagnostics`, never `parser.getDiagnostics().isNotEmpty()`, and must exempt `.js` files (plain-JS grammar errors flow through tsc's separate syntactic walker, no suppression).
- **`=>` TERMINATES a variable-declarator list** (tsc isVariableDeclaratorListTerminator error-recovery tweak): the list loop's silent `break` + parseSemicolon's `=>` trigger together produce tsc's "';' expected." at the `=>` — do not "fix" the break into a ',' expected report.
- **A zero-width MISSING Block (pos == end, no statements) is the "aborted signature" body convention (B324/B325) and its consumers deliberately DISAGREE**: TS7010 + the TS2391 PAIR-gap rule treat it as bodyless (tsc nodeIsPresent), the TS2391 FINAL missing-impl rule treats it as a body (tsc `!node.body` — "unifying" these breaks reservedWords3), the emitter prints `function f() { }`, and a `=>`-followed recovery keeps a NULL body instead (tsc erases those from emit — dottedModuleName). **B512: a `=>`-recovery NULL-body function (`function f(x)=>2*x;`) must SUPPRESS the TS2391 FINAL missing-impl rule** — tsc treats the malformed `=>` body as PRESENT (reports TS1144 only). `functionSignatureFollowedByArrow` (Checker.kt) detects the parameter-list `)` immediately followed by `=>` (a `:` return-type annotation excludes a genuine function-type-return overload sig `function f(): () => void;`); FP-safe because no legitimate bodyless function has `)=>` as its body. Gated into the FINAL rule ONLY (the pair-gap rule is untouched).
- **`lookAhead` vs `tryScan`**: `lookAhead` ALWAYS restores scanner state. `tryScan` keeps scanner state if callback returns truthy. Use `lookAhead` for probe-and-decide patterns; use `tryScan` for "try to parse, keep results if success."
- **A *parenthesized* function/ctor type is `ParenthesizedType(FunctionType)`, NOT a bare `FunctionType`/`ConstructorType`** (fallback in `parseFunctionOrParenthesizedType`) — union/intersection-member grammar checks (TS1385–1388) can just test `member is FunctionType || member is ConstructorType`. For TS-matching spans: `scanner.getPrevTokenEnd()` right after `parseOptional(Bar/Ampersand)` = span start (incl. trivia after `|`/`&`); `getPrevTokenEnd()` right after parsing the member = its TRUE end (avoids `node.end` overshoot).
- **`lookAhead` does NOT restore diagnostics**: The parser's `lookAhead` restores scanner position and the `token` field, but any `reportError`/`parseExpected` failures during the callback permanently add to `diagnostics`. When using `lookAhead` with complex parsing (e.g., `parseComputedPropertyName()`), save `diagnostics.size` before and trim after to discard speculative errors.
- **`reScanGreaterToken`** (splitting `>>` to `>` for nested generics) was implemented but caused 4-test net regression — left disabled in the parser. The implementation in `Scanner.kt` is correct; the issue is likely subtle interaction with `tryScan` nesting. Re-enable cautiously.
- **`getPos()` in Parser** = `scanner.getTokenPos()` (start of current token), **`getEnd()`** = `scanner.getPos()` (end of current token text). After `parseExpected()`, the scanner has ALREADY advanced to the next token — so `getPos()` is the start of the NEXT token.
- **TS1007 related info for missing close delimiters**: Use `parseExpectedClosing()` (not `parseExpected`) when closing `)` in statement-level constructs (`if`, `while`, `with`, `do-while`). This provides `!!! related TS1007` pointing back to the opening `(`. DO NOT use this globally (in `parseExpected`) — it causes 59+ FP regressions in error recovery tests because the `openTokenStack` doesn't track delimiter types and pops incorrectly in complex error recovery scenarios. For general `parseExpected(CloseParen/CloseBrace)`, only emit TS1007 at EOF (current behavior).
- **Extending squiggle span to include `;`**: When a parser diagnostic should cover the trailing `;` (e.g. `[]: number;` → entire 14-char span), call `parseSemicolon()` first (consuming `;` if present), then use `scanner.getPrevTokenEnd()` for the span end. `getEnd()` is wrong because it returns the scanner position AFTER the next token is scanned. `scanner.getPrevTokenEnd()` = position right after the last-consumed token's text, before the next token's trivia.

- **`isStartOfType` inside `scanner.lookAhead`**: Parser's cached `token` field is NOT updated inside `scanner.lookAhead`. When checking what token follows, pass `scanner.getToken()` explicitly: `isStartOfType(scanner.getToken())`. Using the parser's `token` field inside lookAhead checks the OLD token (before the lookAhead scan), not the advanced token.

- **`TemplateLiteralType` parsing is SHALLOW — `templateSpans` is ALWAYS empty** (even with `${infer X}`/`${TypeName}` placeholders); the raw source slice lives in `head.rawText` (`skipTemplateType()`). Any checker walker needing the AST inside `${...}` gets nothing — string-scan `head.rawText` instead — and note `head.rawText` INCLUDES the surrounding backticks (strip before regex/display use, B297) (see `extractInferNamesFromRawTemplate`, which covers `${... infer NAME ...}` scoping ONLY; reference-tracking walkers need their own raw-text scan).


### Emitter gotchas


- **Tab preservation in error squiggles**: The error baseline formatter must preserve tab characters from source lines in the squiggle indentation — tabs stay as tabs, other characters become spaces. Don't use `" ".repeat(col)`.
- **Numeric literal property access**: `1.foo` is ambiguous in JS (the `.` is a decimal point). Emit `1..foo` when the numeric literal has no decimal point, exponent, or `0x`/`0b`/`0o` prefix.
- **Yield expression operand parsing**: Use `isStartOfExpression()` check (not `!canParseSemicolon()`) to determine if yield has an operand. `canParseSemicolon()` returns false for `]` and `)` which would cause yield to try parsing them as expressions. TypeScript checks `!hasPrecedingLineBreak() && (asterisk || isStartOfExpression())`.



### Transformer gotchas


- **`ClassTransformResult` has FOUR statement channels (leading / trailing / privateState / members) consumed DIFFERENTLY by three callers** — transformClassDeclaration emits leading+trailing as statements; transformClassExpression's no-statics path (B338) and statics path (B340/B342) fold them into the capture comma list with load-bearing ordering (privateState → computed captures → __setFunctionName → statics). A new state channel wired into only one caller silently drops its statements in the others.
- **Async GENERATORS downlevel below ES2018 while plain async is native from ES2017** — the gate is split (`isAsyncGenerator … < ES2018`, `isAsync … < ES2017`) at THREE sites (fn decl / fn expr / method); keep them in sync. The fn-expression `__asyncGenerator` wrapper Block inherits the SOURCE body's single/multi-line form.
- **ES-decorators (TC39, !experimentalDecorators) dispatch BEFORE the legacy path in transformClassDeclaration**, and the three builders (`transformEsDecoratedClass*` / `buildEsDecoratedClassExprIife`) produce FINAL AST — never re-transform their output. The comma form rides the es2015 field-to-ctor downlevel by rewriting decorated field INITIALIZERS to `__runInitializers(...)` BEFORE transformClassBody; CJS classifies the resulting `let C = (() => …)()` via `esDecoratedClassNames` (keep-declaration + trailing `exports.C = C;`, not Direct-export).
- **Orphaned comments (erased declarations)**: Only preserve a leading comment from an erased declaration if there is a blank line (≥2 newlines) between the comment's `end` position and the declaration's `pos`. Adjacent comments are part of the declaration and are dropped.
- **CommonJS transform ordering**: Applied AFTER all other transforms. `transformToCommonJS` receives already-transformed statements. The `isModuleFile` check uses the ORIGINAL source file statements.
- **`transformToCommonJS` is CJS-only**: returns early for non-CJS outputs. Any post-process placed inside it (e.g., decorator-metadata safety wraps) won't fire for those module formats. For across-module-format post-processing, add the code to `transformPhase3`'s tail (just before the final `return sourceFile.copy(statements = ...)`). Symptom of getting this wrong: code compiles clean, test still fails with the same diff, debug println never prints.
- **ALL AMD/UMD/System transforms + the outFile bundling machinery were REMOVED (2026-07-02, tsgo-removed features; user-approved)**: transformToAMD/UMD/System, the amd-directive parsing, the outFile JS concat + AMD JSON `define()` wrap + `DtsBundleEmitter` (DtsEmitter.kt deleted), and the outFile-gated diagnostics (TS6082/TS6131/TS2458 + the outFile TS5055 arm). The enabler was extending the generator skip to tsconfig-in-test options (see the suite-composition entry above) — the 4 tests that pinned this machinery (deprecatedCompilerOptions2/6, tsconfigMapOptionsAreCaseInsensitive, outFileIsDeprecated) are skipped. `module: amd/umd/system` still PARSES (ModuleKind values kept — Checker semantic gates reference them) and degrades to untransformed-module passthrough + TS5107 (pinned by RemovedModuleKindsTest); `outFile` still parses (TS5101 deprecation) but never bundles.
- **Constructor prologue ordering**: When inserting parameter-property initializers into an existing constructor body (no `super()` call), insert AFTER prologue directives (`"use strict"`, `"ngInject"`), not at index 0.
- **Type assertion parens**: `(<T>expr)` — the Transformer (not Emitter) must drop them. Fix belongs in Transformer because `TypeAssertionExpression` is already stripped before Emitter sees it.
- **`new (<T>call())` semantics**: `new (A())` ≠ `new A()` — after stripping the type assertion, if the constructor expr becomes a `CallExpression`, it must be re-wrapped in `ParenthesizedExpression`.
- **Non-async arrow expression-body must scope its own hoists**: For `() => obj?.foo` (any arrow whose body is an expression containing temp-var-needing transforms), push a `hoistedVarScopes` entry around body transformation. If any temps were allocated, convert the expression body to `{ var <temps>; return <body>; }`. Without this, optional-chain temp vars hoist to the outer file scope, mismatching TypeScript's emit shape. Async arrows skip this push because `__awaiter` provides its own wrapper.



### Lib-suggestion (TS2550/TS2583/TS2584/TS2585/TS2592) gotchas (round 135, B237–B242)


- **Adding a member to LIB_MIN_TARGET FILTERS it from the interface at unavailable lib levels — which SHIFTS every tuned "and N more" missing-property count involving that interface**: `RegExp.dotAll` regressed `assigningFromObjectToAnythingElse` ("and 11 more"→"10 more"); `Array.flat/flatMap` would shift the Blocker-#4 `genericArrayExtenstions` counts. For members whose PRESENCE is count-load-bearing, use **`LIB_MIN_TARGET_SOFT`**: consulted ONLY by access-site checks (member stays in the interface; property ACCESS still errors TS2550); the property-access-call TS2554 arity check is suppressed for soft-unavailable members. Before adding a HARD entry for a member that EXISTS in the embedded lib, grep the corpus for missing-property displays naming that interface.
- **All LIB_MIN_TARGET gates route through `libFeatureAvailable(intro)`**: empty `@lib` derives availability from `options.target` (harness default target is ES3, matching tsc); explicit `@lib` provides a feature iff some entry's BASE es-version ≥ intro (dotted entries count their prefix level — conservative towards suppression). Do NOT compare `options.target < minTarget` directly in new checks.
- **TS2583 name-availability is split across TWO mechanisms**: the es2015-era FORWARD_DECLARABLE set (`isLibTypeUnavailableEs2015` — Promise/Symbol EXCLUDED there: their TYPES live in lib.es5; their VALUES route through `libProvidesSymbolValue`/`libProvidesPromiseValue` → TS2585 via the TS2693 walker) and `LIB_GLOBAL_INTRODUCING` for later globals (Atomics es2017, AsyncGenerator es2018, BigInt es2020 …) with per-name dotted home-libs. `libUnavailableGlobalSuggestion(name)` is the shared entry; it also SUPPRESSES the TypeReference TS2314 arity check (TS2583 owns the position), and `getReturnTypeOfNewExpression` returns anyType for unavailable callees (else `m.clear()` FPs against the Constructor interface). `Proxy` is deliberately in NO map — tsc's feature map lacks it, so it gets plain TS2304 via a scope.has bypass.
- **`builtinLibDecls` holds top-level statements AND the inner VariableDeclaration nodes**: a `declare var Map: MapConstructor` lib statement binds its SYMBOL's declarations to the inner `VariableDeclaration`, not the statement — `decl !in builtinLibDecls` "user-declared?" identity checks silently mis-fire for lib var globals without the inner nodes (this blocked both Map value-position TS2583 and console TS2584).
- **The TS2550 lib-availability lookup in `checkMemberAccessMissing` is PER-RECEIVER-SHAPE, not just map entries**: string/array/regex-literal receivers, Identifier (a bare lib name resolves to the INSTANCE interface — statics need the `<Name>Constructor.<prop>` fallback key; the embedded lib deliberately has NO ArrayConstructor), NewExpression lib-ctors, CallExpression results, and the namespace Module branch each have their own branch. A new LIB_MIN_TARGET entry won't fire unless the receiver's shape branch exists — when a TS2550 mysteriously doesn't fire, identify the shape and bisect that branch with a marker diagnostic (code 29999).



### Module resolution diagnostic gotchas


- **A `declare module "<relative-specifier>"` AUGMENTATION resolves its target via `resolveModuleSpecifierRelativeJsAware` (round 443), NOT bare `resolveModuleSpecifierRelative` — the latter deliberately won't strip the ESM `.js` extension (TS2459 firewall), so a nodenext `declare module "../compiler/types.js"` never resolved → FP TS2664.** The `.js`-aware wrapper strip-and-retries `.js`/`.jsx`/`.mjs`/`.cjs` (purely additive → only SUPPRESSES a false 'cannot be found'); use it for any augmentation-target resolution, and do NOT "fix" it by stripping `.js` in the base resolver. **PAIRED: inside the augmentation BODY, `buildNamespaceScope`'s `StringLiteralNode` branch brings the AUGMENTED module's `moduleNamedExportsOf` into scope** (tsc checks the body in that module's context — `Node`/`SymbolFlags`/`__String` etc. resolve by bare name even when NOT imported into the augmenting file), else those bare references FP TS2304. **Cross-file augmentation MEMBER merge is still NOT modeled** (Blocker #3): a services-file `declare module "../compiler/types.js" { interface SourceFileLike { getX() } }` does not add `getX` to the base interface, and in the FULL program even the BASE members can FP TS2339 via mergeSymbolTable interface-pollution (0 FPs compiler-only, 44 services — minimal/barrel repros do not reproduce; needs an instrumented full-program run).
- **A const enum member's value is evaluated in THREE independent places** — Transformer's same-file `collectConstEnumValues`, Checker's `literalConstantValue`, and Checker's `evaluateEnumInitializer` (the cross-module `enumValues` table). A fix verified on one shape is easily half-done (round 675: same-file went green while cross-module stayed broken). All three now parse via the shared `tsNumericLiteralToDouble` (Types.kt) — **never `toDoubleOrNull()` on literal text, which is decimal-only and silently rejects `0x7F`**, making the member un-inlinable with no error at all.
- **A const-enum member initializer may be COMPUTED, and the same-file collector and the cross-module evaluator must fold identically** — tsc's own `debug.ts` has `const enum Connection { Up = 1 << 0, …, UpDown = Up | Down }` (shift + prior members by bare name). The numeric operator table is shared as `tsFoldNumericBinary` (Types.kt); the Transformer's `foldConstEnumInitializer` handles paren/unary/binary/identifier/`Other.Member`. Failure mode is CUMULATIVE and silent: the first unfoldable member ends inlining for every member after it (round 677: 25 of debug.js's 121 reads).
- **`flattenRestParameters` owns the parameters of the plain (non-async) FunctionDeclaration branch, function/arrow expressions, and constructors — its ES2018+ early return MUST still call `transformParameters`** (round 677: it returned them raw, so every parameter DEFAULT VALUE in those positions skipped `transformExpression` at modern targets — no const-enum inlining, no optional-chain lowering, no `this` capture). The corpus cannot catch this class: its emit tests sit mostly BELOW ES2018, exactly where the early return does not fire.
- **A PARAMETER is NOT in `currentShadowedNames`** — `applyBodyLocalShadowing` fills that set for body-local VAR declarations and deliberately excludes param names (a var redeclaring a same-function param is a redeclaration, not a shadow). So the `checkMemberAccessMissing` shadow bail never fired for a parameter colliding with a global, and the receiver resolved through globals instead (round 681: tsc's `formatJSDocLink(link: …)` vs `@types/node` `fs.d.ts`'s `export namespace link` → TS2339 "Property 'kind' does not exist on type 'typeof link'"). The repair bail is keyed on the name ALSO denoting a NAMESPACE in globals — **do not widen it to "a concrete local type resolves the member", which over-suppresses `instanceofWithStructurallyIdenticalTypes`** (the documented negative-exhaustion `never` where tsc genuinely reports).
- **`skipLibCheck` applies to the CHECKER's diagnostics only** (`applySkipLibCheck`, round 681) — it skips type checking of `.d.ts` files, NOT their syntax errors, which are collected separately at each call site. It was parsed-but-unconsumed until `@types` packages could enter the program (M4.8) and made it matter.
- **THREE specifier kinds resolve THREE different ways and must not be merged (M4.8, round 680)**: an import specifier → `ModuleResolver`; a `/// <reference path=` target → a file path relative to the REFERENCING file (`ProjectCompiler.resolveReferencePath`); a `/// <reference types=` target → a package in the tsconfig type roots. They live on `SourceFile.moduleSpecifiers` / `referencedPaths` / `referencedTypes` respectively — putting a reference path in `moduleSpecifiers` makes `path="globals.d.ts"` resolve as a BARE package and silently drop the file (that is why `@types/node`, whose entry is 64 reference lines, contributed exactly 1 file). The crawl follows all three, transitively.
- **`emitInnerComments` must not write a newline for a comment's `hasPrecedingNewLine` when the PREVIOUS comment was a `//` (which already terminated its line)** — the double newline put a blank between every pair of consecutive line comments (round 678: 32 hunks, archetype a comment block before an `else if`). tsc collapses a source blank between two comments as well, so the `atLineStart` guard is faithful; the indent must still be written.
- **`emit-diff-tsc.sh`'s "const-enum inlined reads" line only counts NUMERIC-valued inlines (`[0-9]+ /* Name.Member */`) — string-valued enums are invisible to it ON BOTH SIDES, so it can read exact parity while reads are genuinely missing (round 678: it showed 18,118 = 18,118 with 34 `tracing.Phase.*` reads absent).** The honest measure is a per-file count of ALL `/* X.Y */` comments; do not declare an emit family closed on the summary line alone.
- **A const enum may be reached through a VARIABLE whose declared type is the namespace** — tsc's own `export let tracing: typeof tracingEnabled | undefined` beside `namespace tracingEnabled { export const enum Phase … }`, used as `tracing.Phase.Bind`. Name resolution stops at the variable; `namespaceBehindTypeofVariable` follows the `typeof` annotation (through unions/parens, resolving in the DECLARING file, with a star-follower fallback for a barrel-reached namespace name). The variable stays a real runtime binding — never elide its import.
- **Emit-parity work has a REAL gate now (round 672): `scripts/emit-diff-tsc.sh --ref-tsc build/tools/tsc-ref/node_modules/.bin/tsc` with `build/tools/node/bin` on PATH** (Node + tsc installed there, gitignored). USE IT rather than grepping emitted output for hand-written patterns — a guessed regex (`ts_[0-9]+\.`) missed the real barrel alias `ts_js_1` and produced a confidently wrong "already inlined everything" verdict that stood three rounds.
- **Any resolver that must work on tsc's OWN sources has to be `.js`-AWARE** — tsc imports barrels as ESM `./_namespaces/ts.js` re-exporting `../core.js`, and `resolveModuleSpecifier`/`-Relative` deliberately do NOT strip the extension. Use `resolveBarrelStarTarget` (strips `.js`/`.jsx`, memoized). An extensionless scratch repro CANNOT catch this class — the inverse of the usual warning, and it silently disabled the round-669 const-enum barrel fix on the real profile.
- **A namespace import of an `export *` BARREL resolves its members only through the star chain — `symbol.exports` is EMPTY for re-exported names (round 668).** `checkQualifiedNameExports` therefore consults `namespaceImportMemberViaStarExports` before emitting TS2694; any NEW absence check on a namespace-import member must do the same or it FPs on tsc's own `_namespaces/ts.js` layout. That helper is SUPPRESSION-ONLY (resolves no types), which is what keeps it clear of the documented `resolveAlias` star-fallback dead-end.
- **The TS2305-family absence checks (2305/2459/2460/2614/2724) follow `export * from` chains via `getModuleExportsFollowingStars` (M1.1) — a NULL return means UNKNOWABLE (a star target is bare/unresolvable/`export =`) and callers MUST skip absence emission for non-default names, never "fall back" to the direct-only set** (real-world barrels like tsc's `_namespaces/ts.ts` made every named import a TS2305 before this). The `default` spec stays decidable from the DIRECT file (`export *` never forwards default). Memoized per TOP-LEVEL file only (`moduleStarExportsCache`, declared BEFORE `init` per the init-order gotcha) — a cycle member's sub-result computed mid-walk is incomplete for the member itself, so do not cache recursion-internal results.

- **`typeof import("spec")` parses as `TypeQuery(exprName = ImportType)`, NOT as `ImportType(isTypeOf=true)` (B174)**: the Parser's Typeof branch wraps the ImportType in a TypeQuery and `isTypeOf` stays false. Any checker branch matching "typeof import" type args/annotations must unwrap BOTH shapes (`(t as? ImportType) ?: (t as? TypeQuery)?.exprName as? ImportType`). Symptom of matching only ImportType: the branch silently never fires (the B174 marker-diagnostic trace found this).
- **An explicit `undefined` argument is LEGAL for an OPTIONAL parameter (B176, `allArgumentsMatch`)**: absent and undefined are interchangeable for parameters (exactOptionalPropertyTypes does NOT apply to params). The overload matcher accepts `undefined` args against `questionToken` params; without this, an overload PAIR like `toSpliced(start, deleteCount, ...items)` / `toSpliced(start, deleteCount?)` FP's TS2769 on `f(2, undefined)` against BOTH overloads. If you add per-arg checks to another overload path, mirror the gate.
- **A member declared ONLY `static` does NOT satisfy an instance-side requirement**: statics live in BOTH `members` and `staticMembers` (bifurcation model), so name-presence over-counts — `collectMissingProperties` checks `srcSym.declarations.all { Static in modifiers }`. A bare CLASS identifier in VALUE position is its STATIC side (`typeof B`). The var-decl gate `(!canUse || isAssignable)` is deliberate: the static-aware check must ALSO run when the relation PASSED (the engine wrongly counts statics); relation-failed keeps the standard path (no double-emit).

- **TS2694 for `X.Y` inside a `declare module "m"` body where `X` is a MODULE-LOCAL namespace MUST stay AST-based (`checkAmbientModuleLocalNamespaceMemberRefs`)**: the global symbol-merge UNIFIES the module-local and file-level `namespace X` (same name), so a symbol-table lookup wrongly sees the outer block's members and the error never fires. Single-file only — the cross-FILE merged-namespace sibling stays failing (Blocker #3).
- **A `.mts`/`.mjs`/`.d.mts` file is an ES module ONLY under node module kinds (`effectiveModule.isNodeNext`), NOT under commonjs/amd/system/umd** — the rule comes from `impliedNodeFormat`, computed only under node resolution; under amd/system/umd the GLOBAL module setting governs and `export =`/`import = require()` in a `.mts` is LEGAL (an unconditional .mts-is-ESM gate FP'd TS1202 on a 0-errors baseline). A `.d.mts` ESM declaration may NOT use `export =` (unlike a CJS-ambient `.d.ts`). `allowSyntheticDefaultImports`/`esModuleInterop` synthesize a default only for CJS-format targets — an ESM-format node-mode target with no `export default` is still TS1192.



### Module detection gotchas


- **tsconfig `types` acquisition null-vs-empty is LOAD-BEARING end-to-end (M1.3, `ProjectCompiler.collectTypeRootEntries`)**: `"types": []` reaches `applyDirective` as an EMPTY STRING (empty JsonArray → `joinToString` "") and must yield a NON-null empty `options.types` (= acquisition DISABLED), while an ABSENT key keeps null (= auto-include every package in the type roots) — "simplifying" the split/filter chain to null-on-empty silently flips disable into include-all. Explicit `typeRoots` REPLACES the walk-up `node_modules/@types` default (never extends it); TS2688 fires only for explicitly-requested names (auto-discovery includes only what exists).
MULTI-FILE test options BYPASS `parseCompilerOptions` (built in `parseMultiFileSource`'s own `applyDirective` loop) — any implied-option rule (e.g. `applyImpliedAllowJs`: checkJs⇒allowJs) must be applied at ALL THREE option-finalization sites: single-file parse, multi-file parse, and the driver's post-`optionOverrides` re-apply. Missing one silently drops files from binderResults → checker emits NOTHING.
- **The bench project files (`build/bench/tsc-project-*/src`) are CRLF — a python text-mode read silently normalizes `\r\n` to `\n`, so offsets computed from it UNDERSTATE checker positions by one per line (a probe range built that way lands ~29k chars short at checker.ts line 29k and silently misses).** Open with `newline=''` when computing checker-space offsets for probes; line/col numbers are unaffected (both sides count `\n`).
- **Debugging "is this checker path reached?": gradle SUPPRESSES test stdout/stderr** (no `testLogging.showStandardStreams` in build.gradle.kts), AND the forked-JVM stderr does NOT land in the test XML's `<system-err>`. A `System.err.println` trace is therefore INVISIBLE under `./gradlew jvmTest`. To trace whether a check runs, emit a MARKER DIAGNOSTIC (`diagnostics.add(... code=29999 ...)`) — it surfaces in the `errorsMatchBaseline` expected-vs-actual diff. Also note: the Checker `init` gates most checks behind `if (!declarationOnly)`; `@emitDeclarationOnly` multi-file runs a `declarationOnly=true` checker that skips them.

- `export as namespace X` has NO AST node — the parser MISPARSES it into a `namespace X` ModuleDeclaration (+ stray statements); detection is regex-only (`collectUmdGlobalsAndModuleFiles` etc.). Any walker scanning top-level statements for a genuine declaration of X is FOOLED by the misparse — exclude statements whose `.pos` falls within the regex-match span (`stmt.pos in match.range`).
TS2567 enum-merge detection is split across THREE disjoint walkers (same-file per-file; `checkCrossFileEnumConflicts` for SCRIPT files only; `checkModuleAugmentationEnumMerge` for `declare module` augmentations). Do NOT broaden the script-only walker to module files — it FPs on legitimate module augmentations; extend the augmentation walker with gated branches instead.
- **`resolveModuleSpecifier` does NOT resolve the `.`/`./` package-index specifier** (it returns null) — `import '.'` / `declare module '.'` mean "this directory's index". Any check needing that target must resolve it itself (cf. `resolveAugmentationTargetFile`, B412: sibling `index.{ts,tsx,d.ts}` in the importing file's directory). Multi-file test files are keyed by their `@filename` value verbatim (e.g. `./a.d.ts`, but `main.ts` without the `./`), so `dir = fileName.substringBeforeLast('/')` of `./a.d.ts` is `.` → `./index.d.ts`.
Augmentation vs definition of `declare module "X"` is distinguished by ABSENCE of an inner `export =` (the definition carries it), NOT by `isModuleFile`. The shared `ambientModuleExportEqualsIsValueOnly` value-only gate (target declared in-block as var/function ONLY) is the FP firewall. Its paired TS2503 emit in `checkTypeNameResolved` MUST sit BEFORE the `!Module`/TS2702 path — the alias may carry a Module flag from the augmentation merge that would short-circuit it.
TS2303 lives in THREE disjoint emission sites by `moduleReference` kind/context: `checkCircularImportAlias` (entity-name cycles `import X = Y.Z`, skips .d.ts), `emitTS2303ForImportEquals` (bare-identifier unresolvable + re-exported under declaration:true), `checkCircularExportEqualsImportAlias` (`import = require`/`export =` cycles, incl. ambient and .d.ts). Pick the matching one when extending. Report-position rule (matches tsc): AMBIENT cycle entry → its own self-import; FILE entry → its PREDECESSOR; squiggle includes the trailing `;`.
Cross-file (script) class+function-with-body merge and clodule member collisions are TWO dedicated AST walkers (B528, `checkCrossFileClassFunctionMerge` / `checkCrossFileCloduleMemberConflict`, `duplicateIdentifiersAcrossFileBoundaries`), pure AST over `binderResults` (NOT symbol tables → sidesteps Blocker #3). A top-level class in one file + a same-named bodied function in ANOTHER → TS2813 (class) / TS2814 (function) + a TS6506 "add declare" related anchored at the class; a class + same-named namespace in DIFFERENT files with a member name in ≥2 value-kinds (static property vs exported var) → TS2300 + cross-referencing TS6203. **Load-bearing FP firewall: CROSS-FILE-ONLY gates — the class/function file sets must be DISJOINT (PIECE A) and member occurrences must span ≥2 DISTINCT files (PIECE B); a same-file pair is owned by `checkDuplicateDeclarations`/`checkCloduleValueSpaceConflicts` (double-emit otherwise).** Corpus-unique per a grep over the 490 multi-`@Filename` tests.
`checkCrossFileInterfaceMemberConflicts` detects only PROPERTY-vs-METHOD TS2300 — property-vs-property is deliberately NOT detected (identical-type merges are legal; different-type → TS2717, not TS2300; keeps FP surface zero). Collapse threshold (matches tsc `amalgamatedDuplicates`): <8 conflicting symbols per ordered file-pair → per-member TS2300+TS6203; ≥8 → one collapsed TS6200 per file + TS6201. Top-level interfaces count into the global scope only in non-module (script) files.
Cross-file mixed class/type-alias/const-let conflicts use the HUB model (matches tsc mergeSymbol dedup): the FIRST-processed decl is the hub, accumulating TS6203 (first other) then TS6204 (each subsequent); every other decl emits one diagnostic relating back to ONLY the hub (TS6203). The duplicate-identifier work is split across walkers 73a2 (`checkCrossFileIdentifierConflicts`)/73b/73g with disjoint gates; 73a2 registers handled block-scoped names in `crossFileIdentifierHandledBlockNames`, which 73b consults — bypassing that set double-emits.
`@module: none`: dynamic `import()` is preserved verbatim for target≥ES2020, but below ES2020 rewritten to `Promise.resolve().then(() => __importStar(require(...)))` with the `./` prefix stripped. See `transformToCommonJS`/`buildCjsDynamicImport`.
- **`isolatedModules: true` ≠ `moduleDetection: force`**: `isolatedModules` tells TypeScript to check each file independently but does NOT force module treatment for files without imports/exports. Adding `isModuleFile = true` for `isolatedModules` causes regressions.
- **Node16/Node18/Node20/NodeNext default to CJS for `.ts` files (UNLESS a `package.json` declares `"type": "module"`)**: `isESModuleFormat` returns `false` for plain `.ts` files BY DEFAULT — only `.mts`/`.mjs` are unconditionally ESM. The `(options, fileName)` overload consults `options.packageJsonTypes` (populated by `collectPackageJsonTypes` from multi-file source `package.json` files outside `node_modules/`), walking from the file's directory upward to find the nearest enclosing `"type"` field. Use the `options`-aware overload in any new code that determines ESM vs CJS under nodenext — the legacy `(module, fileName)` overload has NO package.json context. Map keys preserve leading `/` (absolute paths) and use `""` for the implicit root.
- **`esModuleInterop` defaults to `true`**: TypeScript 5.5+ deprecated `esModuleInterop: false`. Test baselines without explicit `@esModuleInterop` expect helpers (`__importStar`, `__importDefault`). Only when `@esModuleInterop: false` is explicitly set should helpers be skipped for default/namespace imports.
- **`__importStar`/`__importDefault` conditional on `esModuleInterop`**: These helpers wrap `require()` only when `esModuleInterop: true`. Without it, use plain `require()`. `__exportStar`/`__createBinding` are ALWAYS used for `export * from` regardless of `esModuleInterop`.
tslib resolution is PER-FILE (nearest enclosing `node_modules/`, see `tslibResolvableFor`/`resolveTslibDedupKey`) — do NOT revert to a program-wide `hasTslib` boolean (it suppressed TS2354 for tslib-less packages whenever ANY package had tslib). Match `lastIndexOf("node_modules/")` NOT `"/node_modules/"` — fixtures mix leading-slash conventions and the no-slash form must still match.
TS2343 missing-tslib-helper dedups ONCE PER RESOLVED TSLIB INSTALL per helper (`reportedMissingTslibHelpers`, keyed `<tslibKey>|<helper>` via `resolveTslibDedupKey`). LANDMINE: the older helper emissions in `checkStmtForMissingHelper` (__exportStar/__extends/__rest/…) do NOT dedup — they pass only because their fixtures have a single occurrence. Any new helper check, or extending __awaiter to async methods/arrows, must gate on the install-keyed set or multi-occurrence tests get duplicate TS2343.
`exprContainsDynamicImport` (detection, drives `isModuleFile`) must handle EVERY expression kind that `rewriteCjsDynExpr` (rewriting) handles — keep the two in sync; a missing kind silently skips `transformToCommonJS` for files whose only module signal is a wrapped dynamic import.
`Emitter.hasModuleStatements` must mirror `Transformer.isModuleFile` (incl. dynamic-import detection; each has its own walkers — different files, cannot share). Drift produces a stray top-level `"use strict";` for ESM module files. When adding module-signal detection to one, mirror it to the other.
- **`resolveModuleSpecifier` intentionally does NOT try `.js`/`.jsx` extensions**: Doing so globally causes TS2459 false-positives (our CJS/`module.exports` export analysis is incomplete) and other cross-file lookup regressions. For a specific check that needs JS-aware resolution under `allowJs`/`checkJs`, add a SCOPED helper (e.g. `resolvesAsJsOrJsx` used by `checkAmbientModuleAugmentations`) rather than modifying the main resolver.
The relative-missing TS2307 gate (Checker.kt ~20527) fires ONLY under EXPLICIT `@module: commonjs` — broadening to node10 (the no-`@module` default) re-exposes resolver gaps across many multi-file fixtures. A resolvable `.js`/`.jsx`/`.tsx` sibling (via the deliberately-permissive `resolveRelativeIncludingIndex`) must SUPPRESS TS2307 (tsc resolves those as untyped/TS6142, never TS2307). `hasTsErrorSuppressionAbove` is the ONLY `@ts-expect-error`/`@ts-ignore` handling in the codebase (narrow line-scan around the import line) — there is no general directive-suppression model.
- **ALL file locals merged into globals at Checker init** (`mergeSymbolTable(globals, result.locals)` for every binderResult): This conflates script-file locals (legitimately global) with module-file locals (should require an explicit import to be visible). Consequence: in file X, an identifier exported from an unrelated module file Y appears in X's scope chain via `scope.has(name)`. This blocks fine-grained module-visibility diagnostics. Splitting "true globals" from module-file locals would unblock several tests but has broad regression risk and needs per-file scope construction.
- **The round-442..460 `moduleFileLocalVarNames` value-leak ecology is DELETED (round 513, INV.3(d)(v))** — post-retire the merge never publishes module-only names into `globals`, so the seven name-keyed bails (mam receiver chain, arg-loop root, return/assignment TS2322, var-decl alias inference, arithmetic operand) were inert EXCEPT where they MASKED real bugs: the name match was over-broad (`sys` — compiler/sys.ts's module-level `let sys`), silently suppressing every `sys`-rooted member chain program-wide and hiding two narrowing gaps (fixed round 513: ns-import-qualified `instanceof` resolves via `resolveNamespaceQualifiedSymbol`; `narrowByAssignmentRhs` gained the NEW-EXPRESSION assignment-reduction arm). Do NOT re-add name-keyed cross-file suppressions — fix resolution/narrowing instead. The round-507b per-FILE `lookupPerFileForNode` note stands: by-NAME nulling in `getTypeOfIdentifier` remains wrong (measured round 442: 5 corpus regressions).
- **The rounds-443/444/447/474/477 `conflatedTypeAliasFiles` alias-shadow ecology is DELETED (round 513, INV.3(d)(v))** — post-retire an Interface+TypeAlias name collision across module files never merges, so each file resolves its own declaration and the last-wins-shadow FPs cannot arise. TWO pieces were RE-KEYED onto their TRUE conditions instead of deleted: (1) `objectLiteralSatisfiesAugmentationMergedInterface` (was the round-468b alias-gated form) now fires whenever a `declare module` AUGMENTATION-block `interface X` exists — it compensates for the still-unmodeled cross-file augmentation MEMBER merge (Blocker #3 residue); a same-named CLASS bails it (class members are invisible to the interface-only scan — jsExportMemberMergedWithModuleAugmentation). (2) `objectLiteralMatchesFileLocalAliasUnion` (was the round-447 conflation bail) now fires for a returned objlit vs a FILE-LOCAL type-alias union whose constituent it name-covers — it bridges an M3-relation residue (a REFERENCE-valued member's guard narrow-DOWN is invisible to objlit member typing per the round-438 nullish-strip-only gate — tsc fixAddMissingMember.ts:410), with an explicit-value type firewall so a genuinely wrong non-reference value still fires.
- **The rounds-445..481 `conflatedInterfaceFiles` interface-chimera ecology and the round-473..481 `conflatedPerFileInterfaceType` per-file-view machinery are DELETED (round 513, INV.3(d)(v))** — post-retire two module files' same-named top-level interfaces/enums never merge, so the chimera (a merged member table drawn from both files) cannot arise: the objlit satisfaction bails (`objectLiteralMatchesConflatedFileLocalInterface` + Nested/Some/Spreads variants), the relation-entry twin bails (`conflatedMergedPairRelated` family), the TS2430/heritage per-file-view arms, the owner-context threading (`conflatedOwnerFile`/`conflatedCtxMissing` — getTypeOfSymbol caches unconditionally again), the `nodeTypes` conflated-ref bypass, and the enum-subset exhaustiveness relaxation (`conflatedEnumFileSubsets`) are all gone. SURVIVORS (deliberate, non-conflation): `moduleInterfaceNames` + `isLibPhantomMemberOfModuleInterface` (the lib+module SHARED-name merge still exists), `interfaceDeclsForCurrentFileView` + `unionDiscriminantKeysFromAnnotation` (per-file AST discriminant reading — needed while generic mapped alias bodies resolve any), `objectLiteralExactlySatisfiesFileLocalInterface` (consumed by the re-keyed alias-union bridge), and the round-473/513 `A && objlit` return emitter re-keyed onto the relation (combineBinaryTypes deliberately skips the primitive→literal falsy decomposition — `count && obj` is `0 | {…}` in tsc, so the falsy-remainder TS2322 must be emitted directly).
- **Cross-file discriminant reading survives per-file (round 477; the enum-subset relaxation is deleted — round 513)**: when a union member's discriminant annotation resolves any/error (e.g. an unmodeled generic mapped alias body like protocol.ts's `type CompilerOptions = ChangePropertyTypes<…>`), `unionDiscriminantKeysFromAnnotation` reads the property annotation AST-side from the CURRENT check file's per-file interface view (`interfaceDeclsForCurrentFileView`). `checkImplicitReturns` sets `currentCheckFileName` per file (it was STALE from the previous pass — any per-file-aware resolution in that pass silently used the wrong file).
- **`topLevelConstStringValues` accepts a namespace-import-QUALIFIED alias annotation (round 477)**: `const CloseFileWatcherEvent: protocol.CloseFileWatcherEventName = "closeFileWatcher"` verifies the target module's alias body AST-side (annotationAgrees' QualifiedName arm — RELATIVE specifiers only; runs at field-init time, keep it on the fileResults fast path). `enumMemberKeysOfTypeNode`'s QualifiedName arm falls back to the same namespace-qualified alias resolution (`resolveNamespaceQualifiedTypeAlias`, keyed on `currentCheckFileName`) when the left segment is not an enum — this lets the objlit-vs-union member selection read `eventName: protocol.XEventName` member annotations, and `checkExcessProperties`' UNION nested descent drills the DISCRIMINANT-matched constituent (never first-with-the-prop). `enumMemberKeysOfTypeNode`'s bare-alias arm falls to the merged GLOBALS symbol's TypeAliasDeclaration when the checking file's local is an IMPORT alias (declarations = ImportSpecifiers — typingInstallerAdapter's `kind: ActionSet` members otherwise read null and the default-clause `assertType<never>` exhaustiveness silently dies).
- **A returned object literal that SPREADS an `any`/error-typed value (`return { ...anyExpr, ... }`) is `any` in tsc (the spread poisons the whole object) → it can NEVER be "missing" a required target property (round 445).** Our `inferReturnTypeFromBody` has no object-literal branch, so a spread of an un-annotated function's object-literal return (`...getFileAndTextSpanFromNode(node)` → `{ sourceFile, textSpan }`) resolves to `any` → the props read as un-provided → FP TS2739 (findAllReferences.ts ×5). `checkReturnAssignability` bails via `objectLiteralHasUnresolvedSpread` (any/error spread type), AFTER the per-property drills so a genuine explicit-prop mismatch still fires. The proper fix is `inferReturnTypeFromBody`'s object-literal branch (documented suite-wide blast radius); this suppression is FP-safe (a spread-of-any is genuinely `any`).
- **The class-property-override (TS2416) AND interface-extends (TS2430) checks widen an OPTIONAL base member `p?: T` to `T | undefined` for the relation via `widenOptionalTargetPropType` (round 445) — a derived override `p: T | undefined` / `p: undefined` is legal.** Do NOT widen the DISPLAY (kept on the un-widened base type). The TS2416 path also (a) bails a CONSTRAINED-TypeParam override (`kind: TKind` where `TKind extends SyntaxKind` vs base `kind: SyntaxKind`) via the constraint (no general TypeParam-source relation rule), and (b) compares METHOD members with BIVARIANT params (`signatureRelatedTo(bivariantParams = true)` via `methodSignaturesBivariantlyRelated` — tsc's method-param rule; function-type PROPERTIES stay strictly contravariant). The TS2430 path skips the simple property comparison when the derived member is a METHOD (`getMemberNameAndType` returns a method's RETURN type, which is the wrong shape to compare against a base function-typed data property).
- **`enclosingImportIndex` entries for a same-named specifier span files whose barrels resolve DIFFERENTLY — a resolver needing THE alias's own import must IDENTITY-match the ImportSpecifier node inside the entry's NamedImports (round 473, `importedTopLevelVarAnnotationType`), never take the structural first-match** (checker.ts imports `emptyArray` from the compiler barrel, session.ts from ts.server's — first-match hands compiler files server's `SortedReadonlyArray<never>`).
- **A namespace-import-aliased HERITAGE base (`interface D extends NS.Base` where `NS` is `import * as NS`/`export * as NS` — round 444) does NOT inherit `Base`'s members via `resolveHeritageBaseSymbol`: `resolveAlias` never resolves an `import * as`/`export * as` namespace alias to a module with `.exports` (the alias's declaration is the NamespaceImport node, unhandled by all resolveAlias branches), so the exports lookup returns null → base = errorType.** `getTypeFromBaseTypeExpression` falls back to the merged-global LAST-SEGMENT name (`globals[baseExpr.name.text]`) — mirroring `getTypeFromTypeReference`'s `?: globals[name]` fallback for the same qualified shape in ANNOTATION position (which is why a direct `x: NS.Base` annotation resolved while the heritage base did not). Consequence: an interface WITH a now-resolved base takes the pre-existing "has base types → conservative skip" path in the TS2339 missing-member check (a genuinely-missing member is a safe false-negative, not an FP).



### Cross-file infrastructure gotchas


- **A test that goes SILENT after the INV.3(d) retire may have been passing through the CONFLATED resolution (round 512): pre-retire the receiver/param resolved to a sibling file's same-named interface and fired through the Interface-gated emitter by accident; post-retire it resolves correctly to the anonymous alias body — and the Interface-gated emitter has no anonymous arm.** Fix by extending the emitter to the tsc-faithful anonymous shape (mam's all-missing all-anonymous union TS2339 — no member chain; the TS2345 `paramIsPlainObjectBag` disjunct — all member types must resolve concrete, else the sys.ts B83.5 closure-param mis-binding FPs), never by pinning the silence.
- **`resolveDeclarationSourceFile(pos)` is position-only and CANNOT attribute a declaration to its file in a MULTI-FILE program** (it returns the FIRST binderResult whose text length covers the position — positions collide across files). Fine for single-file/lib use; a cross-file walker needing a decl's file must identity-search `fileResults` (or use a purpose-built index).
- **A cross-file merge-consequence walker (TS2433 namespace-split, TS2475 const-enum-use, …) must gate BOTH sides on `isModuleFile` — two module files' same-named declarations NEVER merge in real tsc**; the merged `globals` symbol is a mergeSymbolTable artifact (round 479: compiler/debug.ts `namespace Debug` vs harness `class Debug`; fourslashImpl `const enum State` vs findAllReferences `class State`). A module file may reference only own/imported names (`result.locals`) plus SCRIPT-declared ambient globals.

- **`getCalleeType`'s bare-Identifier resolution ORDER is load-bearing (round 440): currentLocalTypes → `currentParamBindingNames` (→anyType) → `lookupInInferenceNamespace` → `currentFileLocals` (ONLY a same-file `FunctionDeclaration`: `SymbolFlags.Function`, non-`Alias`) → `globals`.** Two invariants: (1) the `currentParamBindingNames` consult (mirroring `getTypeOfIdentifier`) makes a function-body destructured-const local (`const { watchFile } = createWatchFactory()`, unbound per B83.5) shadow a same-named cross-file function — else the callee resolved through merged `globals` to tsbuildPublic's `function watchFile<T>(state, file, ...)` and FP'd the args. (2) The `currentFileLocals` consult fixes Blocker #3 cross-file FUNCTION name collisions (`getBuildInfo` in tsbuildPublic picked emitter.ts's `getBuildInfo(file: string, ...)` via the mergeSymbolTable-polluted globals symbol) — but is NARROWED to a genuine same-file function decl: a type-only `import { Date }` (interface) must NOT shadow the global `Date` VALUE in callee position (isolatedModulesShadowGlobalTypeNotValue → 3-test regression when the gate was any-symbol). The namespace-BEFORE-file-local order keeps a `namespace Parser` call to `createSourceFile` picking the namespace-internal one over the file-level exported `createSourceFile(fileName, sourceText: string, ...)`.
- **`shadowNestedFunctionNames` (anyType-bails a body-NESTED `function NAME` that collides with an outer/global binding — B83.5-unbound; suppression-only) must run at EVERY function-body-entry site whose pass resolves callees/values by NAME, or a nested function shadowing a same-named EXPORTED function re-FP's (round 454).** It is applied in FOUR places: the call-types walker's FunctionDeclaration, ArrowFunction (Block body), and FunctionExpression branches, AND the assignability/return pass's `checkFunctionBody` (which feeds `getTypeOfIdentifier` → the return-check `getReturnTypeOfCallExpression` and value-position arg checks). tsc's program.ts nests `createDiagnosticForNodeArray(nodes, message)` in a `runWithCancellationToken(() => …)` arrow (shadowing the exported 3-param version → sibling-nested call FP'd TS2345), and builderState.ts nests `create(forward, reverse, deleted): ManyToManyPathMap` (shadowing exported `create(newProgram: Program, …): BuilderState` → `return create(…)` FP'd TS2740+TS2345); the pervasive `{ clear, create, … }` object-literal-factory shorthand pattern is poisoned the same way (the shorthand resolves to a global's wrong signature). The `checkFunctionBody` application UNMASKED two pre-existing latent gaps (not manufactured — more-correct resolution reaches them); one is now FIXED: **`propertiesRelatedTo` retries a failed MEMBER comparison via `methodSignaturesBivariantlyRelated` when BOTH the source and target member are methods (round 454) — object-literal/interface METHOD members compare params BIVARIANTLY (tsc: `strictFunctionTypes` never applies to methods), a function-typed PROPERTY stays contravariant; suppression-only (retry-on-failure) so FP-safe by construction.** The remaining unmasked gap: `applyBodyLocalShadowing` does NOT descend into if-blocks (an un-annotated block-scoped `const clone` shadowing global `clone<T>(object: T): T` is removed from currentLocalTypes → the return-check falls to the global — expressionToTypeNode.ts:535). **Round 461: the collision gate also consults the enclosing inference-namespace exports (`nameInEnclosingNamespaceExports`, symbol-PRESENCE only — never getTypeOfSymbol, the first-touch cache-poisoning hazard): `getTypeOfIdentifier` resolves through `lookupInInferenceNamespace` BEFORE globals, so a NAMESPACE-member fn collision (parser.ts's namespace-level `currentNode` vs the body-nested one, reached via the `{ currentNode }` shorthand) shadows too.**
- **`resolveModuleSpecifier` is MEMOIZED (null results included — the hot case) and `resolveAlias`'s ImportSpecifier branch + `findEnclosingImport` resolve via the EAGER-IMMUTABLE structural index `enclosingImportIndex` (rounds 432/434 — the replaced program-wide `spec in bindings.elements` scans were 76% of the tsc-source self-compile: only POSITIVE resolutions cache via `setSymbolTarget`, so every unresolvable ESM-`.js` barrel alias re-scanned every file per `resolveAlias` call).** The index is built once from the frozen `binderResults` in its field initializer and never mutated (Tier 1 — see **docs/parallel-caching.md** for the cache taxonomy + M5.4 parallel plan; classify any NEW cache into a tier before adding it, and never reach for a shared/synchronized map — the design is share-nothing). It is STRUCTURALLY keyed with encounter-ordered entry LISTS, reproducing the old scans' first-match + fallback-to-next-match semantics byte-identically (structurally-equal specifiers in different files legitimately share a key) — do not "optimize" it to identity keys or single entries. `moduleSpecifierCache` stays a lazy memo (its key domain is OPEN — `import("spec")` type positions / dynamic imports) and is worker-local by construction (Checker field, Tier 2); it assumes `fileResults` is frozen after construction.
- **The bare `resolveModuleSpecifier(spec)` only knows FLAT corpus-style file keys — for a path-shaped layout (`/proj/src/f1.ts` importing `./lib`) it is null, so every alias-HOP chain must include `resolveModuleSpecifierRelative(spec, contextFile)` (round 511: its absence in `resolveImportedSymbolGeneral` killed ALL import-mediated typing on real on-disk projects post-INV.3(d) — pre-retire the merge masked the miss; round 512 closed the same hole in `resolveAlias`'s ImportDeclaration AND ImportSpecifier branches via `owningSourceFile(decl)` + the relative leg; round 513 in `namespaceAliasMemberSymbol`'s three arms). The tsc-source profiles CANNOT catch this class (their specifiers are all ESM-`.js` → the separate stripping leg), so compiler/services listAll byte-identity is NOT sufficient verification for module-resolution changes — verify with a path-shaped scratch project via MainKt (cheap, decisive) and keep the direct-construction pins (EnclosingImportIndexTest) green.**
- **`node_modules/*.d.ts` files ARE parsed + bound + in `binderResults`, and their diagnostics DO reach the output** (despite a driver code comment saying node_modules files are "third-party and never reported on" — that comment is about EMIT, not diagnostics; `parsedSourceFiles[fileName]` is set BEFORE the emit-skipping `continue`). Confirmed by the passing `augmentExportEquals7` (reports a node_modules TS2649) and used by B443's `checkGlobalNamespaceMemberConflicts`. So a cross-file checker walk CAN emit diagnostics at node_modules positions — don't assume they're filtered.
- **A cross-file `declare global { namespace NS { … } }` merge is NOT modeled by the binder's symbol tables** — detecting a member-name CONFLICT across files in such a namespace (e.g. `type VNode` in one file vs `export import VNode` in another → TS2300) needs an AST-based dedicated cross-file walker (`checkGlobalNamespaceMemberConflicts`, B443), like the other cross-file conflict walkers. FP firewall: ≥2 DISTINCT files AND a type-alias in the mix (type-aliases are the only kind that can never merge).
- **Function-call arity (TS2554) is cross-file via an OVERLAY, not per-file (B434, `checkArgumentCounts`)**: a per-file `funcParams` map only sees the current file's functions, so a SCRIPT-mode cross-file call (`f()` in B where `function f` is in A) was never arity-checked. A program-wide `crossFileFuncs` overlay (top-level FunctionDeclarations from all NON-module non-.d.ts files) is merged as a FALLBACK for NON-module files. Load-bearing FP firewall: merge ONLY for callees absent from BOTH the file's own funcParams AND its binder `locals` (a local binding of any kind shadows a cross-file function — without this guard a local non-function `f` + `f(1)` FPs); cross-file DUP names (same name declared in ≥2 files) are dropped (conservative). TS6210/TS6211 related-info must resolve against the DECLARING file (`FuncParamInfo.declFileName/declSource` → `emitTS2554TooFew(relSource, relFileName)`), else the param position is computed against the CALLER's source (wrong file+offset). Companion: JS-param optionality is JSDoc-aware — `paramInfo(params, isJsFile, jsDocRequiredParamNames(comments))`; a JS param is optional UNLESS a non-bracket `@param name` tag documents it (`@param [name]` stays optional).
- **Call resolution respects lexical SHADOWING (M1.11) — a NEW fn-body descent site in the arity walker (`checkArgCountIn*`) or the call-types walker must re-apply it, or wrong-signature TS2554/TS2345 FPs return**: arity — `minusParamShadowedNames` at every fn-body descent + the `argCountFnDepth`-gated body-local var removal (the depth gate keeps top-level B64.2 var-arrow checks alive); `collectFuncDecls` deliberately does NOT flatten ModuleDeclaration bodies (namespace fns are a body-scoped overlay collected at the walker's ModuleDeclaration branch); ctor OVERLOADS record an isOverloaded arity RANGE (never just the first sig); a SPREAD argument suppresses too-FEW only (expansion ≥0 → too-many stands). Type path — `populateParameterLocalTypes` infers un-annotated fn-valued-DEFAULT params from the initializer; `shadowNestedFunctionNames` (after the fn-body `currentLocalTypes` copy, call-types walker) anyType-bails body-nested fns colliding with an outer binding (B83.5 leaves them unbound → the globals fallback finds the wrong decl). **Round 429 (all anyType/suppression-only): `applyCallTypesBodyLocalShadowing` at every call-types fn-entry overrides an INHERITED `currentLocalTypes` entry colliding with a body-local decl (a nested fn's `let host = …` vs the enclosing `createTypeChecker(host: TypeCheckerHost)` param — the round-428d branch is gated entry==null so it can't override; a same-fn param redeclaration is excluded, param wins); the ArrowFunction/FunctionExpression branches scope `currentLocalTypes`+`currentParamBindingNames` and register anyType for own param names (the walker deliberately doesn't TYPE arrow params, so a colliding name previously leaked the enclosing binding's type into arg positions).**
- **A lib+MODULE-interface merged symbol's LIB-only members are phantom (round 471, `isLibPhantomMemberOfModuleInterface`)**: a module file's own top-level `interface Symbol` (tsc compiler/types.ts) merges with the same-named LIB global, so the merged interface demands lib members (`description`) tsc never requires. The helper (gated on `moduleInterfaceNames` + lib decls on the symbol + lib-only prop declarations) is wired into propertiesRelatedTo / collectMissingProperties / getMissingRequiredPropertySymbol / the TS2420 implements loop — EXTEND it at a new missing-member verdict site, don't re-derive; script-file lib augmentations don't trip the gate.
- **`mergeSymbolTable` mutates the existing target symbol's `declarations` list**: When `target[name]` already exists, the helper does `existing.flags |= symbol.flags; existing.declarations.addAll(symbol.declarations)` — so calling it twice across the same `result.locals` corpus duplicates declarations on the SHARED symbol object. The init-block merge into `globals` already does this once for every cross-file same-name pair; any additional pass building per-file scope tables MUST NOT re-merge across binderResults via this helper. Use direct `map[name] = sym` assignment with first-write-wins for shared pools and last-write-wins for own-file overrides — no `addAll`, no symbol mutation. Failure mode is silent until a downstream check (e.g. TS6133 unused-T-param, declaration count) sees the duplicated declaration list and behaves incorrectly.
- **`isValueExport`/`isTypeOnlyExportName` cross-file lookups must NOT rely on symbol flags**: `mergeSymbolTable` pollutes the target file's `locals["X"]` symbol with flags merged in from importing files (e.g., `import { T } from "./type"; const T = 0` in file A causes /type.ts's T symbol to acquire BlockScopedVariable|Alias|ExportValue flags). Code that asks "is X exported from file Y as a value or type?" MUST scan `targetResult.sourceFile.statements` directly for declarations of X, not consult `targetResult.locals[X].flags`. Mirror pattern in `isValueExport` (Checker.kt:955) walks the AST and classifies each declaration's kind. Fall back to flag-based logic only when no direct declaration is found (ambient/re-export cases).



### Binder gotchas


- **A DOTTED `namespace X.Y.Z` records its INNERMOST segment symbol (`Z`) for the decl node** — `recordNodeSymbol` overwrites once per segment in `bindModuleDeclaration`'s loop, so `nodeToSymbol[decl]` is the LAST (innermost) segment, and same-named segments MERGE across declaration blocks. So `buildNamespaceScope`'s dotted branch (B512, Checker.kt) MUST resolve via `nodeToSymbol[stmt]` + walk `symbol.parent` `segCount` steps to add each segment's merged exports — NOT root the walk at `result.locals[firstSegment]` (the old code), which finds NOTHING when the dotted namespace is NESTED in another namespace (the first segment lives in the parent's exports, not file locals) → a merged sibling-block member is invisible → spurious TS2304 (`dottedModuleName`: `v` declared in a sibling `M.N.X.Y.Z` block). Companion: `findDeclarationRelatedInfo`'s TS2728 is OMITTED for a TS2552 suggestion that is an INTERMEDIATE (non-last) segment of a dotted namespace name (tsc gives only the innermost segment a `valueDeclaration`) — the `ModuleDeclaration` branch returns null when `decl.name` is a `PropertyAccessExpression` whose last segment ≠ the suggested name.
