Too-FEW is safe unguarded (a simplified lib sig never RAISES minArgumentCount). The multi-sig overload-arity branch (TS2554 with cross-overload range, before checkArgumentsAgainstOverloads) has the same lib gate; its too-few TS6210 points at params[argCount] of the overload with the SMALLEST minArgumentCount (overload1's 17:11 'n').
- **`undefined!`/`null!` is `never` in BOTH expression-typing systems (B282)**: `getTypeOfExpression`'s NonNullExpression branch (nullish intrinsic → neverType) AND the string-based `inferSimpleExprType` (inner "undefined"/"null" → null = check-skipped). Fixing only one leaves `return undefined!` FP'ing TS2322 through the OTHER path (the varTypes-based return check at the `return` keyword was the actual emitter — checkReturnAssignability's string fallback).



### Checker gotchas


- **An UNCONSTRAINED type parameter has NO apparent members — not even Object.prototype's (B481c)**: `value.hasOwnProperty(...)` on a param typed `T | { data: T }` is TS2339 (the `{data:T}` member has hasOwnProperty via the object→prototype rule, the bare `T` member does NOT, so the union access fails on `T`). The standard checkPropertyAccess path suppresses this (`hasOwnProperty` ∈ OBJECT_PROTOTYPE_PROPERTIES), so it's a dedicated walker `checkTypeParamUnionMemberAccess` gated to a SINGLE-return body (no narrowing guard can interfere) + a `TP | object` param + unconstrained/constraint-lacks-prop TP. To extend beyond single-return bodies you MUST consult flow narrowing (a narrowed access removes the TP member → no error).
- **`narrowByCallPredicate` infers a GENERIC type-guard's type params structurally (B481a)**: a predicate target that is a fn type-param (`isPlainResponse<T>(value: T | {data:T}): value is T`) resolves to errorType at the call site (T not in scope) — `collectPredicateTpBindings` matches the predicate-position param annotation against the actual narrowed type, binds the TPs, and pushes them into `currentTypeAliasArgs` (consulted at the top of TypeReference resolution) to resolve the target. Without it the false branch is never narrowed → FP TS2339.
- **A `this is X` guard METHOD narrows the CALL RECEIVER, not an argument (round 444)** — the tsc `Type`/`Symbol`/`Node` public-API guards (`type.isUnion(): this is UnionType`, `isIntersection`, `isLiteral`, …, added by a `declare module` augmentation). `narrowByCallPredicate` handles it via TWO coupled pieces: (1) the `this` subject of a `this is X` predicate parses as a **ThisType** node, NOT an Identifier — `predicateParamName` extraction maps `is ThisType -> "this"` (matching only Identifier/TypeReference silently bailed); (2) for `predicateParamName == "this"` the narrowed reference is the method-call RECEIVER (`callee.expression` of a PropertyAccess callee, computed up front so it participates in the P0 fast-path), narrowed via the existing single-type/union logic. **FP gate (RELAXED round 470): a `this is X` method guard narrows a UNION receiver only when EVERY non-nullish constituent resolves the method to the SAME declaration** — tsc narrows a union-receiver method-guard only when EVERY constituent has a matching predicate (typePredicatesInUnion3: `Type1 | Type2` where `Type2.predicate(): boolean` is NOT a guard, still bails), but a mid-walk branch join (`guard() && cond && read`) unions the negative arm's BASE with the positive arm's SUBTYPE, both inheriting the guard — the shared-decl test lets that narrow. PAIRED (round 470): `resolvePropertyMethodDecl` resolves a nullish-containing union receiver through its sole non-nullish member (`type: Type | undefined` — the guard never resolved before), and `resolvedCallReturnTypeForFlow` must bail on an optional-chained CALLEE (`factory?.make(t)`) — previously masked because the nullable receiver never resolved the method.
- **An OVERLOADED contextual fn-type (≥2 call signatures) provides NO single contextual signature in non-strict mode, so arrow params stay `any` (B476)**: `applyContextualParameterTypes` (Checker.kt) picks the SINGLE arity-applicable call signature, or BAILS when 0 or ≥2 are applicable (`!options.strict` — tsc only intersects overloads under strictFunctionTypes, which we don't model). Typing the params from `ctxSigs[0]` instead FPs TS2322 (`(x:string,y:number)=>any` not assignable to the overloaded target — the any-param arrow IS assignable). The paired default-mode TS7006 (`walkVarFn7006Expr` → `maybeEmit7006ForOverloadedAssignTarget`) fires for an assignment-RHS arrow when the target provides NO single contextual signature: the target is `any` (an untyped `let`/`var` — B478) OR a fn-type with ≠1 arity-applicable sig. Non-fn OBJECT targets stay conservative (no emit). Both default-mode-gated.
- **The fn-expr/arrow-param contextual-typing gate is DUPLICATED across the var-decl path AND the class-property path — keep them in sync (classPropertyErrorOnNameOnly)**: the VariableStatement branch of `checkImplicitAnyInStatements` computes `ctxFn = arrowInit && decl.type != null` to suppress TS7006 on a fn-expr/arrow initializer's params, but the `PropertyDeclaration` branch of `checkImplicitAnyInClassElement` did NOT — so `prop: FnType = function(x){}` FP'd TS7006 on `x` while the identical `const v: FnType = function(x){}` did not. The class-prop branch now mirrors `ctxFn`. **Companion dedicated walker `checkFnTypeSwitchReturnMismatch`/`tryEmitFnTypeSwitchReturnMismatch` (TS2322 at the DECL NAME)**: a var/class-prop annotated with a non-generic fn-type ALIAS `(arg:U)=>string|number|boolean` whose initializer is a single-param fn-expr/arrow with a body that is EXACTLY one `switch(param){ case <numLit>: return <lit> }` (no `default`) UNDER-covering the param's numeric-literal union → inferred return includes `undefined` → TS2322 with a 3-line chain. AST-ONLY (reads the alias's FunctionType + the param-union's UnionType nodes — no type-engine assignability), strictNullChecks-gated, corpus-unique shape so it never double-emits with the standard assignability path (which doesn't infer a switch-fallthrough return type). The top-line return display keeps the literal union (`"1" | … | undefined`); the chain widens it (`string | undefined`).
- **The arithmetic walker contextually types object-literal arrow params from the assignment target's INDEX SIGNATURE (B475)**: `checkArithmeticInExpr` sets the LHS type as `contextualType` for an ObjectLiteralExpression `=` RHS, propagates each property's contextual value type (named prop OR index sig, via `lookupPropertyTypeForCtx` — `Type.Interface` IS a `Type.Object` so interface index sigs resolve), and `applyContextualParamTypesForArrow` fills `currentLocalTypes` for unannotated arrow params so `x = { s: t => t*t }` (`[s:string]:(s:string)=>number`) types `t` as `string` → TS2362/TS2363. The body is walked with `contextualType` CLEARED (nested object literals must not inherit the fn type).
- **The JSDoc `@param`-type parser bridge (`applyJSDocParamPrimitiveTypes`) is applied to FunctionDeclaration / MethodDeclaration / Constructor / FunctionExpression params ONLY — NEVER ArrowFunction params (B437c)**: an arrow is parsed as an expression before the `const f = arrow`'s leading JSDoc is associated, so the arrow parser has no `comments` to apply. Consequence: an arrow's JSDoc-documented params (`const f = (value) => …` with `@param {T} value`) stay `type == null`, so the implicit-any walker (`checkImplicitAnyInStatements`) FPs TS7006 even though tsc never flags a documented param. Suppress it checker-side (the B437c gate in the VariableStatement branch: JS file + arrow/fn-expr init + `jsDocDocumentedParamNames` covers EVERY param). The bridge handles `{primitive}`, single-`{Ident}` (real positions, for TS2314/2304), and `{...primitive}` rest (B437a → `ArrayType(prim)` + `dotDotDotToken`); complex types (unions/fn-types/generics) bail to null.
- **Enum-to-enum VARIABLE assignability (TS2322, B425, `checkEnumToEnumAssignments`) is NOMINAL via a dedicated walker — NOT the relation engine** (enum types don't flow through it for this). Rule (derived from `enumAssignmentCompat3`): two enums relate iff they share a SIMPLE name AND every source member is present in the target with an EQUAL value; a const enum relates only to itself; the chain reports the FIRST failing source member ("Property 'X' is missing in type 'TGT'." vs "Each declaration of 'E.X' differs in its value, where 'V_tgt' was expected but 'V_src' was given."). Type names are qualified `Namespace.E` ONLY when src and tgt share a simple name (relative-name disambiguation; `Nope` vs `E` → simple). DISJOINT from `checkEnumLiteralAssignments` (literal RHS) + `checkNamespaceEnumUnionAssignments` (union annotations). String/un-evaluable enums → `values=null` → conservatively assignable (FN, no FP). Remaining enum tests need broad features (typeof-Enum value-position object typing; string-enums+function-params+nested-scope shadowing+`import("f")` cross-file display; enum-relation wired into TS2416/TS2394).
- **Object-literal SPREAD support (B426)**: `getTypeOfObjectLiteral` now merges a spread's GUARANTEED props (via `spreadGuaranteedProps`: single object → required props; UNION → props required in EVERY NON-NULLISH constituent — spreading undefined/null is a no-op so nullish members are filtered, which is why `{id, ...(find()??{label})}` no longer FP's "label missing" WITHOUT any `??`-type change). Merge is ADDITIVE (only adds props not already explicit). **Round 481: a spread whose type resolves any/error makes `getTypeOfObjectLiteral` return `anyType` for the WHOLE literal (tsc: spread-of-any poisons the object) — so a NESTED member value `{ ...unresolvable, prop }` types as any and no per-member relation can FP; the older per-site `objectLiteralHasUnresolvedSpread` bails remain as guards for non-getTypeOfObjectLiteral paths.** TS2783/TS2785 (explicit prop overwritten by a later guaranteeing spread) lives in the separate full-tree walker `checkSpreadPropertyOverrides` — FP firewall: the spread must GUARANTEE the prop (`{y}|{y,x}` and optional `{a,b?}` do NOT fire); squiggle = whole property assignment, related at the spread `...` (`expression.pos - 3`).
- **The B426 spread merge COPIES each guaranteed member into a FRESH literal-owned `Symbol`, never sharing the spread SOURCE's member symbol by reference (round 449)** — a `{ ...src, prop: override }` where `prop` overrides a spread member hits `getTypeOfObjectLiteral`'s `existing != null` branch, which writes `symbolTypes[existing.id]` (and the getter/setter branches MUTATE `existing.declarations`); sharing `src`'s member symbol there corrupts `src`'s member type cache GLOBALLY (the refactor `{ ...info, actions: [...] }` overwrote `ApplicableRefactorInfo.actions`'s cached type with a contextually-inferred `U[]` → every relation against the interface FP-fired TS2322). The copy carries the source member's resolved type + declarations so reads/optionality/positions are byte-unchanged. Reverting the copy to `members[pn] = psym` re-opens the corruption (pinned by SpreadOverrideMemberCorruptionTest, whose invariant test FAILS without the copy). Reproduces only WHOLE-PROGRAM (the `U[]` needs the generic-inference context) — instrument `symbolTypes` writes, not a minimal repro.
- **Ternary-condition TS2872/TS2873 lives in the LEGACY `checkAlwaysTruthyInExpr` ConditionalExpression branch, NOT the np (B277 nullish-predicate) walker** — adding conditional-condition truthiness on the np side DOUBLE-emits (aliasUsageInOrExpression). Extend the legacy branch.

- **The `.js` skips in `checkPropertyAccess` AND `checkCallExpressionTypes` are LOAD-BEARING — do NOT remove them (B152/B152b/B153, round 128 reverted experiments)**: a triage agent will suggest "just remove the `.js`/`.jsx` skip, the downstream handles JS correctly" (for TS2339 / TS2345 on `@checkJs` files). This is the recurring JS-FLIP_NOW over-optimism trap. **Measured: removing the `checkPropertyAccess` skip (gated on `options.checkJs`) flipped 1 (`jsCheckObjectDefineThisNoCrash`) but REGRESSED 5** (`checkIndexConstraintOfJavascriptClassExpression`, `checkSuperCallBeforeThisAccessing9`, `classFieldSuperNotAccessibleJs`, `jsdocReferenceGlobalTypeInCommonJs`, `unusedTypeParameters_templateTag2`) — our JS property-access modeling (expando-`this`, `module.exports`, prototype, JSDoc) is too incomplete, so it FP's net-negative. The comment "property access patterns differ" is correct. Same applies to the `checkCallExpressionTypes` JS skip (`noParameterReassignmentJSIIFE`). A JS-only TS2339/TS2345 needs a NARROW dedicated walker (like B150's object-literal-method path), NOT un-gating the whole walker.
- **Object-literal method/accessor body assignment-checking (`walkObjectLiteralMemberBody`, `this` bound to the object-literal type) is JS-GATED**: in .ts an un-annotated object-method `this` is TS2683, NOT the object type — do not un-gate. Class methods stay safe via fall-through (`getTypeOfExpression(this)` is anyType there, B101).
- **tsc's noImplicitAny element-access rule (TS7052/TS7053/TS2339, `tryEmitNoImplicitAnyIndexAccess`): WRITE-context accesses WIDEN the receiver** (checkElementAccessExpression), so a fresh-literal WRITE takes the get/set-suggestion path while a READ takes the TS2339 branch; ENUM receivers must stay excluded (the TS7015/TS2339-typeof branches own them) and spelling-suggestion hits defer to the pre-existing TS2551 paths.
- **The EMPTY-OBJECT any/string-key TS7053 branch (the SEPARATE emitter at Checker.kt ~113696, `receiver[anyKey]` where receiver is a no-members/no-index `Type.Object`) must ALSO exclude an ENUM-OBJECT receiver (round 407)** — a numeric enum in value position resolves to an empty `Type.Object`, and `NumericEnum[key]` is a valid REVERSE mapping (tsc emits no TS7053; tsc's own `ModuleResolutionKind[moduleResolution]`). Gate on `recvType.symbol?.flags?.hasAny(SymbolFlags.Enum)` OR the receiver identifier resolving (`currentFileLocals ?: globals`) to an enum. This is a DIFFERENT emitter from `tryEmitNoImplicitAnyIndexAccess` (which already had the exclusion) — both need it.
- **`getTypeOfExpression(NonNullExpression)` deliberately does NOT strip nullish from a UNION operand IN GENERAL (`(T | undefined)!` stays the union, only a pure-nullish operand → never)** — a full nullish-strip is CORRECT + tsc-faithful (measured net −17 self-compile at round 407) but UNMASKS incomplete M3 gaps: an object-literal-vs-interface assignability (`const program: Program = {…}` whose member `x!` type becomes precise) and generic-inference FPs surface. Land the FULL strip WITH those M3 (object-literal structural comparison / generic inference) fixes, not before. **EXCEPTION (round 439): a `<call>()!` whose union return is ALL-CONCRETE (no un-inferred type param — `typeContainsUnresolvedTypeParam`) DOES strip nullish** (via `narrowByExcludingNullUndefined`) — `getParseTreeNode(x, guard)!` → `AccessorDeclaration`. This is gated to a CALL operand precisely to avoid the two documented hazards: property-access `.x!` (the object-literal case) and a TP-carrying call return (the generic-inference case) both keep the un-stripped union. The exception exists because round 439's predicate-overload RETURN inference (`tryInferPredicateOverloadReturn`) made such calls return a CONCRETE `X | undefined` (previously a foreign-`T | undefined` suppressed by the round-431 gate), which would otherwise FP against a non-nullish target. **EXTENDED (round 456): a bare-IDENTIFIER `x!` (a local/param/module-var typed `T | undefined`) ALSO strips under the same all-concrete gate** — `writer = _writer!` / `currentFlow = preSwitchCaseFlow!` FP'd `X | undefined ⊄ X`. Safe because a bare Identifier is a simple reference, so it cannot expose the `.x!` object-literal member-precision hazard. **Round 479: PROPERTY-ACCESS `.x!` now strips too under the same all-concrete gate** (`start: diagnostic.start!` — the M3 machinery landed since round 456 covers the historical hazard; verified strictly-removals on all 8 profiles). Compiler 131 → 127, services 229 → 223, corpus green. A future FULL strip (property-access included) still waits on the M3 object-literal/generic-inference fixes.
- **Nullish/void operands & `typeof undefined` under non-strict (widenedTypes)**: tsc's `TypeFlags.Primitive` INCLUDES Null/Undefined/Void, so `isPrimitiveInstanceofLhs` counts them → `null instanceof X` is TS2358; a `null`/`undefined` LITERAL operand of `in` is TS18050 (`checkNullUndefinedInExpr`'s `InKeyword` branch, ungated); `for (x in null)` is NOT an error (`simpleRhsNonObjectDisplay` must NOT flag null/undefined — runtime no-op, legal all modes); and `getTypeFromTypeQuery` resolves `typeof undefined` to `anyType` under non-strict (the Undefined type widens to `any` when `!strictNullChecks`), NOT `undefinedType`. A future "always undefinedType"/"always TS2407"/"primitives exclude nullish" tidy-up reverts widenedTypes.
- **Element-access WRITE checking lives in `checkElementAccessSetterWrite`** (Type.Reference receivers bail entirely — the B89.1 generic-setter caveat). Separately, varTypes stores `let s: A & B` as `"&A & B"`: `isAssignableTo`'s TOP gate (`startsWith("&") → true`) makes such entries behave like ABSENT ones for every pre-existing consumer — any new `&`-consumer must KEEP that gate, or every isAssignableTo call seeing an intersection string goes spuriously strict.
- **The relation engine (`objectTypeRelatedTo`) does NOT check source props against the target's index-sig VALUE type** — that lives in TWO disjoint PRE-checks gated to `isAssignable` (no double TS2322): `checkLiteralValuesAgainstIndexSignatures` (literal-syntax sources) and `checkObjectTypeAgainstIndexSignatures` (non-literal anonymous sources). The latter's firewall (both sides anonymous Type.Object, ≥1 PRIMITIVE side in the failing pair) guards the named-vs-named 39+-regression gate — don't loosen. Numeric element access falls back to the STRING index sig when no number sig exists (`obj[1]` is `obj["1"]`).
- **JSON modules are NOT bound** (the driver skips `.json` files before binding) — `resolveModuleSpecifier` cannot resolve a `.json` specifier; raw content reaches the checker ONLY via `jsonModuleContents`, populated at the single multi-file `Checker(...)` construction site in TypeScriptCompiler.kt. **`CheckerPool` is UNUSED (unreferenced in the driver)** — don't plumb new fields there. No JSON→type modeling exists; typing populated JSON means parsing the content yourself.
- **The binder flat-merges ALL namespace-block members into the merged symbol's `exports` regardless of which BLOCK was exported** — so `isNameExportedFromNamespace` returns true even for members declared only in a NON-EXPORTED block, which TS does NOT expose externally (TS2339). Visibility checks must key on the BLOCK's export modifier, not the merged exports (cf. `moduleBlockExports` branch in `checkMemberAccessMissing`, B130).
- **The 3 general TS2395 walkers (Checker.kt ~20378/21639/21942) cover VALUE-space declaration groups only** — import-equals + type-alias same-name merges (TS2440 conflict; TS2395 mixed-export) live in `checkImportConflictsWithLocal`. Extend THAT walker for other import-equals + X merge-consistency combos.
- **A named import of a TYPE-ONLY export (interface/type-alias, possibly reached through an `export *` barrel) declaration-merges with a local VALUE-only declaration (function / value const) of the same name → NO TS2440** — the import binds the TYPE, the local binds the VALUE, disjoint spaces (tsc's own src/compiler `import { Node } from './_namespaces/ts.js'` + local `function Node` AST-constructor). `checkImportConflictsWithLocal`'s named-specifier loop gates on `importedNameIsTypeOnlyThroughBarrel(sourceName, spec, fromFile)` — a CONSERVATIVE `export *`-following resolver (`isExportedNameTypeOnly` inspects only DIRECT exports, so it misses barrel-re-exported names; use `resolveBarrelStarTarget` for the `.js` specifier since `resolveModuleSpecifier` won't strip `.js`) — AND `localAlias in valueOnlyLocalNames` (function / value var, MINUS `typeSideNames` = class/enum/interface/typealias/namespace, which have a type side that DOES conflict). FN-safe: any uncertainty (unresolvable star, `export { } from`/local `export { }` of uncertain kind, `export =`, depth blow-out, or a value found anywhere) → false → keeps firing. **LOAD-BEARING: the suppression is gated `!options.isolatedModules && !options.verbatimModuleSyntax` — those modes DO error on the type-import + value-local merge (TS2865 / TS1484 / TS2440 via the var-conflict + case-3 paths BELOW the guard), so a blanket `continue` regressed `isolatedModulesSketchyAliasLocalMerge` / `isolatedModulesExportDeclarationType` (the guard skips those emitters).** **The barrel closure is the WHOLE program (a barrel `export *`s everything), so the per-(barrel,name) result MUST be memoized (`barrelTypeOnlyMemo`, declared BEFORE `init` per the init-order gotcha — `checkImportConflictsWithLocal` runs during init) and the recursion must share ONE `visited` set (never fresh-per-re-export) — an un-memoized/fresh-visited walk hangs.**
- **Calls to a FUNCTION-TYPED PARAMETER are invisible to the main TS2558/arg-check paths** (callee resolved via `locals`/`globals` — a parameter is in neither): the dedicated full-tree walker is `checkFnTypedParamCalls` (also covers type-param args and generic-class-instance method calls via `FnParamCtx`) — extend IT for param-callee generic-call checks; the main TS2558 walker still can't see param callees. Its FP firewall is load-bearing: receiver must be an ANNOTATED class-instance var and the method a SINGLE non-overloaded `MethodDeclaration` — the type-engine approach (B89.2) regressed `typeAssertionToGenericFunctionType`. Args' declared tps come from the walker's own `tpVarRefs` map, NOT `getTypeOfExpression` (errorType in this pass — no type-param scope).
- **The string-based `this.prop = value` write path (`checkAssignmentExpression` ~88528, taken when `varTypes["this.$prop"]` is set) is OPTIONALITY-BLIND — `varTypes` stores the bare type-name string (`resolveSimpleTypeName`), dropping the `?`** — so `this.optionalProp = undefined` (a legal write to `p?: T`, non-eOPT) FP-fired TS2322 (round 448; services.ts SymbolObject/NodeObject ctor field resets). The `thisPropertyIsOptional(propName)` helper (consulting `currentClassForThis`'s OWN members) gates a bail for a bare-`undefined` RHS. An array-typed / explicit-`| undefined` optional already passed the lenient string relation. A new nullish-RHS check on this path must mirror the bail (the type-engine `checkPropertyAccessAssignment` at ~89644 already widens the optional target).
- **TWO assignment-expression dispatchers exist**: `checkTypeAssignabilityInStatements` (top-level/block loop — handles VariableStatement INLINE) and `checkTypeAssignabilityInStmt` (if/for/while/try BODIES). They are SEPARATE `when` blocks — any new expression-shape check (e.g. assignment nested in a var-decl initializer) must be added to BOTH, or it silently misses one context.
- **Opening named→named missing-property TS2741 safely: gate it `!canUseTypeEngine(...)` as a FALLBACK (B127)**: the B87.4 missing-property block (`checkAssignmentExpression`, Checker.kt:~58361) was class-instance-source-only because `canUseTypeEngine` BLOCKS named→named structural comparison (the 39+-regression gate). To extend it to PLAIN interface→interface (`a = b` where both are pure property-bag interfaces), gate the new branch on `!canUseTypeEngine(sourceType, tt)` so it fires ONLY when the canUse path is blocked — this makes it a fallback that NEVER preempts the working canUse path (which already emits the correct TS2741, e.g. `errorWithSameNameType`'s cross-file `import("b").F`). Two further load-bearing gates: exclude `sourceType.symbol?.name == "Object"` (the TS2696 "Object assignable to very few types" special-case owns Object-source — without this, `assigningFromObjectToAnythingElse` regresses TS2696→TS2740), and require pure property bags (no `stringIndexInfo`/`numberIndexInfo`, no `callSignatures` on either side) so name-presence IS the assignability criterion. A broad `bothPlainInterfaces` gate WITHOUT `!canUse` regresses exactly those two tests — that's the firewall test.
- **The construct-sig / call-sig MISMATCH elaboration branches (`getNonConstructibleElaboration` / `getCallableMismatchElaboration`) must be kept in sync across ALL THREE assignability sites (B112)**: `var Person: new () => T = function(){}` (var-decl), `Person = function(){}` (assignment, 17.111 ~57950), and `function f(): {new():T} { return function(){} }` (return, B106 ~57397) each need their OWN copy of the construct-sig-mismatch branch, because `canUseTypeEngine` BAILS when the source lacks construct sigs while the target requires them — so the standard relation path never fires and these emit NOTHING without a dedicated pre-gate branch. B112 added the var-decl copy (`checkVarDeclAssignability`, right after `sourceType` is computed, before the `canUseRaw` gate). Same gate at all three: target is `Type.Object` with non-empty `constructSignatures` + not class-instance; source is `Type.Object` that is callable (callSignatures) or property-only, with EMPTY constructSignatures. FP-safe (`getNonConstructibleElaboration` is non-null only for a genuine mismatch). When a future elaboration branch flips a test in one assignability site, grep the other two for the same gap — they drift out of sync.
- **Return-type inference helpers diverge**: `inferReturnTypeFromFunctionExpressionBody` (FunctionExpression single-return only) infers widened object-literal returns; the SHARED `inferReturnTypeFromBody` (FunctionDeclaration/method/accessor/arrow) has NO ObjectLiteralExpression branch → silently anyType. Adding it there is suite-wide blast radius — full-suite verify. **B486 ALIGNED the method-return divergence between the two symbol-type paths**: `getTypeOfVariableOrProperty`'s MethodDeclaration branch now infers a VALUE-returning body's return via `inferReturnTypeFromBody` (was anyType), matching `getTypeOfMemberDecl`/`buildMethodType` — so the STRUCTURAL relation path (`getTypeOfSymbol`→`getPropertyTypeForRelation`) agrees with the DIRECT override check on a method's return type. LOAD-BEARING gate: only VALUE-returning bodies (`bodyHasReturnValue`) infer — a no-return body keeps the `anyType` default, NOT `void` (B420 measured `()=>void` vs `()=>any` as -5: getAndSetNotIdenticalType2/3, inheritanceMemberAccessorOverriding*). `inferReturnTypeFromBody` also gained an `is AsExpression` arm (`return x as T` → T). Both net-zero-verified. The override cluster's deep TS2416 elaboration CHAINS are the engine gap: the `fn` function-type top-line union order (`number | string` annotation order vs our normalized `string | number` — tsc is internally inconsistent: relation lines DO normalize, so no single union order matches both positions) and the `n` union-vs-union "The types returned by 'fn()' are incompatible between these types." elaboration (`addSignatureElaboration` only handles function-type pairs, not union members). **`baseClassImprovedMismatchErrors` itself now FLIPS via the dedicated message-fix walker `checkBaseClassImprovedMismatch` (round 345): it `.copy()`s the 4 TS2416 messageChains directly — drilling the `n`-property chain into the `fn()`-return incompatibility (6 lines) and keeping the `fn`-property fn-type line's annotation-order union `() => number | string` — gated corpus-unique on `return 10 as number | string` (1 file). LANDMINE: a TS2416-REWRITING walker MUST be hooked AFTER `checkPropertyOverride()` in the init pipeline (a too-early hook sees no TS2416 → silent no-op). `incompatibleTypes`/`deeplyNested` are still owned by B560's general drill.**
- **The import=require module-namespace TS2339 branch in `checkMemberAccessMissing` (B114) has a load-bearing firewall**: target must resolve to a `.ts`/`.tsx` FILE (`.js`/CJS excluded — incomplete `module.exports` tracking would FP) and have NO `export * from` re-export. Do NOT broaden. The `import * as foo` namespace-import shape is NOT handled there (different AST, see resolveAlias gap entry).
- **`Pick`/`Omit` are MATERIALIZED by `materializeMemberSetUtility`, wired when `symbol == null` (embedded lib — a user `type Omit<…>` shadow wins) OR when the resolved symbol is a LIB-ONLY declaration of the utility name (real libs — round 393, `isBuiltinUtilityAlias`).** The embedded lib does NOT declare these aliases (they resolve to null → the `symbol==null` dispatch), but the REAL lib does (`type Omit<T,K> = Pick<T, Exclude<keyof T,K>>`), so under `useRealLibs` the generic alias-substitution path would expand the real definition — the non-homomorphic mapped type `{ [P in K]: T[P] }` — and DROP the optional/readonly modifiers (our `getTypeFromMappedType` doesn't yet treat `[P in K extends keyof T]` as homomorphic → M3.3). `isBuiltinUtilityAlias(name, symbol)` (name ∈ Omit/Pick/Readonly/Parameters/ConstructorParameters/ReturnType, `symbol.declarations.all { it in builtinLibDecls }`) routes a lib-only utility through the same materializers so both paths agree (the materializers reuse the source property Symbols → modifiers preserved). Gates are load-bearing — do NOT loosen: T must be a concrete `Type.Object` (NOT Reference — generic instantiations have lazy members), no index sigs, K a string-literal(-union) key set. `Partial`/`Required` CHANGE modifiers, so source-symbol reuse won't work — they need a side-channel flag (cf. Readonly's `mappedReadonlyMemberIds`); they are NOT in `isBuiltinUtilityAlias` (still evaluated via the mapped-type path).
- **A source MISSING required target properties defers the construct-/call-sig mismatch TS2322 to the missing-property path (TS2739/TS2740) — tsc reports a structural relation failure ONCE (round 393, `targetHasRequiredPropAbsentFromSource`).** `redefineArray` (`Array = fn`): under real libs `ArrayConstructor` has a construct sig, so the assignment path's 17.111 construct-sig branch + the general `canUse && !isAssignable` block both fired a TS2322 alongside B444's TS2739 (embedded ArrayConstructor had no construct sig → never fired). Both are now guarded by `!targetHasRequiredPropAbsentFromSource(source, tt)`. **KEY: `collectMissingProperties`/`getMissingRequiredPropertySymbol` BAIL (return empty/null) when `source.members` is null — a bare-function source (`callSignatures` only) has null members, so they can't detect the missing props; `targetHasRequiredPropAbsentFromSource` treats null members as "has nothing" so every required target prop is missing.** The guard fires ONLY when props are genuinely missing, so a source satisfying all named props but failing only the signature still reports the coarse TS2322 (a construct-only interface `{new():X}` with no named props → no missing → TS2322 stands).
- **`Readonly<T>` materialization copies member symbols and records ids in `mappedReadonlyMemberIds`** — the readonly modifier comes from the WRAPPER, not the declaration, so `isReadonlySymbol` checks that set BEFORE the declaration-modifier scan (field declared before `init` — Kotlin init-order). **M1.10: user mapped types register the SAME channels in `getTypeFromMappedType`'s member synthesis — a plain `readonly` token → `mappedReadonlyMemberIds`, the `-readonly` form (`Mutable<T>`; `MappedType.readonlyMinus`, the parser must capture the sign) → `mappedMutableMemberIds`, which ALL readonly predicates consult FIRST (a homomorphic member carries its SOURCE decl, whose `readonly` modifier would otherwise leak through the strip).** Same load-bearing gates as Pick/Omit. The TS2540 `this.X` readonly-write check via `currentClassForThis` covers INHERITED members (always read-only) AND, since B510, OWN members: a get-only accessor (no setter) is read-only EVERYWHERE incl. the ctor; an OWN `readonly` DATA property may be written ONLY directly in the ctor body — `currentThisMemberIsCtorDirect` (set in `checkConstAssignmentInStatement`'s ClassDeclaration handler) is the gate, FALSE in methods / property initializers / nested non-IIFE arrows + function-expressions, TRUE through an immediately-invoked ARROW IIFE (mirrors tsc `getControlFlowContainer`, transparent to IIFEs via `immediatelyInvokedArrowCallee`; a function-expression body additionally nulls `currentClassForThis` since it rebinds `this`). The type-based readonly path uses `isReadonlyAccessOrModifier` (get-only accessors count, e.g. object-literal `{ get a() {} }`). `emitTS2542IfReadonlyIndexElementWrite` covers `xx["foo"]=v`/`yy[1]=v` writes through a readonly string/number index sig (numeric key → number index, fallback string), GATED to skip Array/ReadonlyArray receivers (owned by B435's `tryEmitReadonlyArrayElementWrite` — else double-emit TS2542). typeToString renders anon-object index sigs in DECLARATION-position order. Mixin note: `class C extends (X as new () => T)` resolves the base instance type from the ConstructorType's RETURN (`getTypeFromBaseTypeExpression`).
- **if/while/do CONDITIONS are only checked via the whole-expression predicates `isAlwaysFalsyExpr`/`isAlwaysTruthyExpr`** — `checkAlwaysTruthyInExpr` never walks a condition's SUB-expressions. A truthiness-position check nested inside a condition needs its own condition scan (cf. `scanForNotFalsy`, wired into if/else-if/while/do conditions).
- **`.d.ts` files skip the ENTIRE `checkDuplicateIdentifiers` pipeline** (TS2300/TS2395/TS2567/TS2434) — ambient merge rules differ; broad un-skip FPs. A duplicate/merge-consistency check needed in `.d.ts` must be a SEPARATE narrow walker RESTRICTED to .d.ts (non-.d.ts is covered by the general path → double-emit otherwise); cf. `checkDtsAmbientModuleExportConsistency`.
- **Relational `<`/`<=`/`>`/`>=` comparability is CROSS-category** (`comparabilityCategory` must match on both sides — per-operand `isComparableType` is insufficient: `number < boolean` passes each individually but is TS2365); bigint deliberately maps to null (never emit); TS2365 displays the WIDENED category name. Load-bearing companion: the arithmetic walker's literal-init `currentLocalTypes` recording MUST widen via `getWidenedLiteralType` AND skip `null`/`undefined` initializers (they widen to `any` in non-strict → FP TS2365), with per-file snapshot/restore.
- **Accessor write-type resolution does NOT push the receiver class's type-param scope or apply the `T→arg` mapper** — `set foo(s: T | undefined)` on `Test1<string>` resolves to `string`, not `string | undefined`. So `checkPropertyAccessAssignment`'s nullish-RHS relation path BAILS for accessor properties (`propIsAccessor` flag); don't remove the bail (FPs on generic setters). Proper fix (substitute class type args into the setter annotation) deferred (B54.5 territory).
- **Labeled `break <label>` escapes ALL intermediate loops** (≈ return/throw for fall-through purposes). `containsBreak` (TS7027 termination) counts only UNLABELED breaks at this level. This asymmetry is deliberate — 'simplifying' it breaks reachability tests.
- **The TS2366/TS7030/TS2355 "does the body always return/terminate" analysis (`bodyAlwaysReturns` → `statementAlwaysReturns` → `switchAlwaysReturns`, round 414) is FALL-THROUGH-aware and models three non-obvious terminating shapes — a `return`/`throw` inside them does NOT count as a fall-through exit:** (1) an INFINITE loop (`while(true)`/`for(;;)`/`do..while(true)`) falls through ONLY via a break that exits it (`infiniteLoopFallsThrough` — unlabeled break at the loop level, or a labeled break anywhere inside incl. nested loops via `containsLabeledBreakEscaping`; a plain `return`/`throw` exits the FUNCTION, so it keeps the endpoint unreachable — the OLD `containsBreakOrReturn` wrongly counted returns → ~17 self-compile FPs); (2) a trailing call to a `never`-returning function (`Debug.fail(...)`, `assertNever(x)`) diverges — `callHasNeverReturnAnnotation` resolves the callee via `resolveFlowCalleeDecl` (barrel-aware: follows import aliases + `export *`, handles Identifier AND namespace-member callees) and checks for an explicit `: never` return annotation; (3) a `switch` with a `default` (or exhaustive) clause always-returns iff EACH clause — following FALL-THROUGH — reaches return/throw before escaping (a reverse walk in `switchAlwaysReturns`; a non-empty case completing normally inherits the NEXT clause's guarantee; a `break`-out is checked FIRST since it escapes even after a later return). **Pattern C2 (round 415): an EXHAUSTIVE `switch` with NO `default` is terminating (tsc narrows the discriminant to `never` after all cases). `isExhaustiveEnumSwitch` lands the FP-SAFE subset — an ENUM / enum-member-union / call-return-enum discriminant (a precise enum value set): it claims exhaustive ONLY when every enum member is provably covered (each case an `Enum.Member`/`undefined`/`null`; the discriminant resolving to a precise enum or `enum | undefined` via a param's type ANNOTATION `enumSwitchKeysFromTypeNode` — bare enum → all members, type alias = union of enum members → recurse, `Enum.Member` → that one — OR the resolved expression type `enumSwitchKeysFromType` — pure enum object → all members, `enum | undefined` union → members + nullish marker). ANY uncertainty returns null/false → the TS2366 STANDS (no false negative — the FP-safety contract, since `.errors.txt` disabled = weak corpus gate). Reuses the round-411 barrel-aware enum helpers. REMAINING ~12: `.kind` discriminated-union switches (the discriminant is a property of a union of interfaces/TypeLiterals, needing type-alias-chain + intersection + inherited/TypeLiteral-`.kind` flattening) — the larger M3.4 slice, correctly bails.** `.errors.txt` tests are disabled, so this analysis is gated only by the full suite + local *TerminationTest.kt (strong negative controls MANDATORY for any suppress-a-diagnostic change here). NOTE this analysis feeds ONLY `checkBodyForImplicitReturn`; TS7027 unreachable-code uses the separate `isDefinitelyTerminating`, so a wrong exhaustive-claim cannot spuriously report post-switch code as unreachable. **Round 451: a `switch (typeof x)` with no default is also exhaustive when it covers the SUBJECT'S actual tags (not just all 8) — `typeofTagsOfType(subject)` maps each union member to its `typeof` string (`typeofSwitchSubjectType` resolves a bare-Identifier subject from its param annotation via `currentFunctionParams`, because `getTypeOfExpression` returns `any` in the CFA pass). FP-safe: `typeofTagsOfType` returns null (→ TS2366 STANDS) on ANY uncertain constituent (any/unknown/type-param/enum/intersection — an enum's per-member value kind isn't in the resolved Type). Do NOT add this to the TS7027 `isDefinitelyTerminating` path (its typeof branch must stay all-8-tags — a false-exhaustive there reports post-switch code unreachable).**
- **TS7027 unreachable-code: `isDefinitelyTerminating(TryStatement)` accounts for the FINALLY block (a `try {…} finally { …; return; }` terminates — the finally always runs), AND the walker descends into IIFE bodies (B325, tryCatchFinallyControlFlow).** The reachable-after rule is `finallyTerminates || (tryTerminates && (catch==null || catchTerminates))`. The unreachable-code walker (`checkUnreachableInNestedStatement`) descends into a function-like expression via `checkUnreachableInFunctionLikeExpr`, which unwraps a `CallExpression` callee (through parens) to an arrow/fn-expr → so an IIFE `(() => {…})()` (ExpressionStatement) / `const x = (() => {…})()` (VariableStatement initializer) is now checked. All gated by `@allowUnreachableCode == false`.
- **TS2502 accessor self-ref is SUPPRESSED when a paired same-name accessor has a NON-self-referential annotation** (the pair resolves through the concrete side; pre-pass in `checkAccessorSelfTypeofInTypeLiteralMembers`). Mirror the pre-pass if extending to class bodies with paired accessors.
- **Super-before-`this` (TS17009 `this`/`this.x`, TS17011 `super.x`) is FLOW-BASED (`checkConstructorThisBeforeSuper`+`isPostSuperFlowNode`, B457), a direct port of tsc's `isPostSuperFlowNode`** — NOT the old "first this before first super, then stop" heuristic. `collectThisSuperRefs` gathers every `this` Identifier and the `super` of every `super.<prop>`/`super[<expr>]` in the ctor body, NOT descending into nested function-likes/class bodies (lazy — tsc `getThisContainer(includeArrowFunctions)`) NOR into `super()` call ARGUMENTS (those are owned by the disjoint emitters: `this` in super-args by `findThisInSuperCallArgs`, `super.X` in super-args by the B51.5 emitter, param-init super.X by the TS2336 emitter, `new super()` by the TS2351 emitter — touching any of those risks double-emit). Each ref's `flowGraph.nodeToFlow[nodeKey(node)]` is walked: a `super()` is a `FlowCall` whose `node.expression` is `super`; a `FlowBranchLabel` is post-super only if EVERY antecedent is (memoize by id — exponential otherwise); `FlowLoopLabel` follows antecedent[0]; `FlowUnreachable`→true (silence). The flow builder already models if/else, switch-fallthrough, ternary, and object/array literals, so conditional super() (e.g. `{w: cond ? super() : 0}`) joins correctly.
- **Flow narrowing (`narrowTypeFromFlow`, MIRRORED in `narrowTypeFromFlowFollowLoopEntry` — keep both in sync)** is consulted ONLY by TS2339 (`checkPropertyAccess`), TS2454 (`walkExprForFlowTS2454`), the var-decl assignability path (`checkVarDeclAssignability` ~72491), (B469) the **TS2345 call-arg path** (`checkArgumentsAgainstSignature` narrows Identifier/PropertyAccess Union args via `getNarrowedTypeForReference`), and (round 408) the **TS2349 union-callee "not callable" verdict** (`checkSingleCallExpressionTypes` — before the union callability check, re-narrow an Identifier/PropertyAccess callee via `getNarrowedTypeForReference` and, under an optional call `f?.()`, drop the nullish constituents; gated to a callee that originally HAD a non-callable member so case-(c) all-callable structural-mismatch is untouched; both ops only REMOVE constituents so they can suppress a false positive but never add one), and (round 410) the **plain-assignment RHS** (`checkAssignmentExpression` ~85775, identifier-target branch — narrow an Identifier/PropertyAccess RHS via `getNarrowedTypeForReference` and use the narrowed type only when it is a STRICT improvement that makes the relation pass, for a named object target (Interface/Reference); a preceding user type-guard narrows `y` to a subtype of `x` in `x = y`, e.g. tsc's `node = parent` inside `if (isParenthesizedExpression(parent))` / `target = callee` inside `if (isSuperProperty(callee))`; the var-decl path deliberately DEFERS interface targets, so this is the assignment-path complement; suppression-only + FP-safe), and (round 413) the **RETURN-assignability path** (`checkReturnAssignability` — narrow a returned Identifier/PropertyAccess via `getNarrowedTypeForReference` and substitute the narrowed type only when it is a STRICT improvement that makes the return relate, for a named object target Interface/Reference/Object; tsc's own `toBuilderProgramStateWithDefinedProgram(state){ Debug.assert(isDefined(state)); return state; }` — the returned reference resolved to its wider DECLARED type and FP'd a missing-property TS2739; suppression-only + FP-safe by monotonicity), and (round 438) FOUR symmetric extensions of the same gates: the **assignment-RHS** and **RETURN-assignability** gates now ALSO accept a `Type.Union` target (not just Interface/Reference/Object — a reference guard-narrowed to a subtype of a union member: `currentSourceFile = node` / `return node` where the target is `SourceFile | undefined`); the **call-arg narrow-DOWN** branch (round 428b/429c) now covers a PROPERTY-ACCESS arg (`getExports(node.left)` inside `if (isIdentifier(node.left))` — a PropertyAccess's built-in narrowing only refines UNION receivers, NOT a non-union interface DOWN to a subtype, so it needs the same explicit narrow as a bare Identifier); and an **object-literal property VALUE** narrows in `getTypeOfObjectLiteral` (both the PropertyAssignment-Identifier and ShorthandPropertyAssignment branches — `{ moduleSpecifiers: specs }` where `specs = append(specs, x)` narrowed it), NULLISH-STRIP-gated via `objLitValueNullishStrip` (accept ONLY `X | undefined` → `X`; a narrow-DOWN to an unrelated subtype OR the name-based-flow SHADOWING hazard — an inner `const affected = state.program` under an outer `if (!affected)` over-narrowing to `undefined` — is REJECTED, so the value can only lose a proven-absent nullish member). The empty-object return-path fix is separate (see the checkReturnAssignability empty-object gotcha below). `getTypeOfIdentifier` itself still does NOT consult narrowing — wiring it there would broadcast narrowing through ALL type lookups (high-risk: unrelated tests assume identifier type = declared type, cascading FP emission). FlowLoopLabel returns declaredType (back-edge safe). **B469: `checkCallExpressionTypes` now sets `currentFlowGraph` for the whole call-type walk** — it was null there before, so a `T|null` property-access arg FP-fired TS2345 in the narrowed-to-`T` branch too (`getTypeOfPropertyAccess`'s built-in narrowing is a no-op when `currentFlowGraph` is null). ZERO-regression (narrowing only refines arg types → suppresses FPs, never adds them). Companion `constNullishAnnotationType` (in `narrowByEquality`) resolves a bare Identifier whose declared annotation is exactly the `null`/`undefined` keyword type (a `const myNull: null`) so `x === myNull` narrows like `x === null` (AST-only, FP-safe — only those two keyword annotations qualify).
- **`narrowByTypeOfGuard` filters the `"function"` tag by CALLABILITY (round 408), not TypeFlags** — a function value carries no distinguishing TypeFlags, so the old code returned the union unchanged for `"function"`/`"object"` (→ `typeof f === "function"` narrowed NOTHING, FP TS2349/TS2722). It now keeps union members with call OR construct signatures (removes them on `!== "function"`); any/unknown/error members are kept on BOTH branches, and if the filter keeps nothing or removes nothing `t` is returned unchanged (never narrow to `never` on this too-broad tag). The `"object"` tag is still returned unchanged.
- **An EMPTY array literal assigned to an Array/ReadonlyArray target is contextually typed by the target (`contextualAssignmentRhsType`, round 408): `(x = [])` / `(x ??= [])` has x's array type, NOT the `any[]` a bare `[]` defaults to (B87.6).** Wired into `combineBinaryTypes`' `SyntaxKind.Equals` + the logical-assign ops (`??=`/`||=`/`&&=`). Without it the tsc default-init idiom `(x || (x = [])).push(v)` typed the receiver `T[] | any[]` (tsc: `T[]`), and `.push` on that union resolved to a UNION of two differing call signatures → spurious TS2349. Gated to an empty `[]` RHS + an Array-family Reference target (`[]` is assignable to any array type, so the result is only more precise — FP-safe); every other RHS keeps the plain `getTypeOfExpression(right)`. Does NOT cover a target typed as a UNION (`FlowNode[] | undefined` where the bare member is a union — binder.ts:1375 stays FP) or a call-RHS `??=` (core.ts:2135).
- **The flow walkers' cycle set is `NarrowSeen` (set + add-log, round 433): a FlowBranchLabel walks each antecedent with `mark()`/`popToMark()` on the SHARED instance — exactly equivalent to the old fresh-copy-per-antecedent (only genuinely-added ids are logged, so the pop restores the pre-branch membership).** Any NEW walker recursion that explores SIBLING alternatives must mark/pop around each alternative; linear recursion passes `seen` unmarked (additions persist upward — load-bearing, matches the old shared-set behavior); a new depth-0 entry constructs its own `NarrowSeen` (re-entrant walks must not share logs).
- **`collectReassignedNamesInRange` (Flow.kt, B464) is served from a per-`hi` shared-scan cache (round 433) — its correctness requires the matcher to stay START-INDEPENDENT**: token decisions may read backward context (`getOrNull(i-1)`, unbounded) and forward up to `hi`, but must never depend on where the scan began. A future matcher change that keys on the scan start (e.g. skipping a prefix region) silently breaks every cache-shared sibling closure.
- **The flow walkers are memoized + budgeted (P0 round 385, services-profile hang): `narrowLiveDepth` must stay a checker FIELD, never a parameter** — re-entrant walks (flow-call callee resolution → receiver typing → `getNarrowedTypeForReference` re-entry) share it, and `narrowLiveDepth == 0` IS the outermost-walk test that resets `narrowVisitsLeft`/`narrowWalkDeclCache`. The per-invocation flow-node memo serves an entry ONLY when `depth <= cachedDepth` (unconditional serving over-narrows past the NARROW_MAX_DEPTH truncation; only clean — non-`narrowWalkTruncated` — subtrees are stored). The assert/predicate arg-path pre-checks (bail BEFORE callee resolution) are the services-perf firewall — `narrowByAssertCall` uses path-CONTAINMENT (`argMentionsReferencePath`, M1.5), `narrowByCallPredicate` exact-path; never delete either.
- **`NARROW_MAX_DEPTH` is 2000 (M1.2b round 386 — was 50), aligned with tsc's `flowDepth` guard; do NOT lower it back "to save time": truncation is what COSTS time.** Truncated subtrees are never memo-stored, so the 50-cap made deep-CFG walks recompute everything (self-compile 185.8→68.3 s, −63%, and RSS −325 MB from the lift ALONE; corpus zero-churn-verified 8,861/0/3). Deeper walks also complete more narrowings — self-compile gained 2 TS2345 at utilities.ts:11604/11859 (a narrowing the 50-cap previously hid from an arg-check consumer; tracked in the M1.4 re-measure).
- **Both flow walkers follow LINEAR pass-through antecedents ITERATIVELY (a `while(true)` fast-forward loop, round 413 — tsc `getTypeAtFlowNode`), WITHOUT consuming `NARROW_MAX_DEPTH`; only branch/condition/switch/assertion/discriminant-assignment recursion consumes depth (tsc's `flowDepth`).** A `FlowArrayMutation`, or a `FlowAssignment`/`FlowCall` that does NOT narrow the walked reference, is followed by re-assigning `flowNode` and looping — not recursing. This is what lets a >2000-node straight-line body (builder.ts `createBuilderProgram`) reach a top-of-function assert/guard instead of washing the reference back to its declared type at the depth cap. **The `flowAssignmentMightNarrow`/`flowCallMightNarrow` gates MUST OVER-approximate "narrows" (mirror the target-matching in `narrowByAssignmentRhs` — `flowAssignmentTargetsName` PLUS property-path/`??=`/`||=` targets — and `narrowByAssertCall`'s `argMentionsReferencePath` pre-check): a too-EAGER gate only costs a depth level (correct), but a too-LAX gate iterates PAST a real narrowing and SILENTLY drops it (correctness bug). Both mirrors carry the identical loop — keep in sync. (Round 426b) `flowCallMightNarrow` ADDITIONALLY requires the callee to bear an `asserts` predicate (`flowCalleeMayHaveAssertEffects`, tsc `getEffectsSignature`): EXACT for Identifier callees (map-lookup resolution, provably matches `narrowByAssertCall`'s own bail — iterating past a false is EQUIVALENT, not just safe), conservative-TRUE for PropertyAccess callees (resolving them types the RECEIVER — the round-385 services-hang hazard). With the faithful TS2563 (round 426), a too-eager call gate no longer merely costs a depth level — 2000+ path-mentioning non-assert calls (diagnosticInformationMap.generated.ts's `diag(…, DiagnosticCategory.Error, …)`) TRIP a false TS2563.** Budget/memo/seen/truncation semantics are unchanged (pass-through nodes aren't memo'd — the narrowing-point memos still bound the expensive recomputation; the 1M visit budget bounds the iteration).
- **`narrowByAssignmentRhs`'s non-nullish-RHS branch narrows the DECLARED type, NOT the pre-assignment antecedent (round 416).** An assignment OVERWRITES the reference (tsc `getAssignmentReducedType(declared, rhsType)`), so its post-state is `narrowByExcludingNullUndefined(declaredType)` — independent of the flow-narrowed antecedent. Basing it on `antecedent` (the pre-416 code) FP'd the idiom `if (!x.y) { x.y = new Map() } x.y.method()` for a PROPERTY-PATH target: the then-branch antecedent is `x.y` already narrowed to bare `undefined` by `!x.y`, and `narrowByExcludingNullUndefined` returns a NON-union `undefined` UNCHANGED (nothing to filter — the `t !is Type.Union → return t` early-out), so the then-arm re-adds undefined at the branch join → FP TS18048. `declaredType` is threaded from both callers (`narrowTypeFromFlow` + the FollowLoopEntry mirror). Straight-line assigns are unaffected (antecedent == declaredType there); the LITERAL-RHS branch still filters the antecedent (unchanged — a different mechanism). Do NOT revert to `antecedent` "to be more precise" — it's a shared narrowing path (TS2454/TS18048/TS2339/TS2345) and the declared-type base is what makes a branch-guard join non-nullish. **Round 477 added THREE assignment-REDUCTION arms (round 513 a FOURTH: a NEW-EXPRESSION RHS keeps only declared members the constructed instance relates to — `sys = new System(sys)`, tsc fakesHosts ctor) (the getAssignmentReducedType subset, all BEFORE the non-nullish branch — a both-arms-non-nullish ternary would otherwise reset to the FULL declared union first): `x = typeof x === "tag" ? <objlit> : x` drops the tag's members; a plain objlit RHS drops primitive/nullish members; an array-literal RHS keeps only array-like members (Array/ReadonlyArray refs, tuples, intersections containing one) — the tsc harness fourslash/evaluator reassignment idioms (`expected = typeof expected === "string" ? { name: expected } : expected` / `if (!ts.isArray(expected)) expected = [expected]`).**
- **`resolveNamespaceMemberFnDecl` resolves an `import * as ns` receiver via its own ImportDeclaration + target-file locals + `export *` chain (round 477, memoized `nsImportMemberFnCache`)** — resolveAlias never resolves NamespaceImport aliases (round 444) and the ImportSpecifier-keyed flow resolvers skip them, so `ts.isDocumentRegistryEntry(entry)` guards through the harness `.js` barrel silently never narrowed. The same resolver feeds `initializerCtxTypeForImplicitAny`'s namespace-callee arm (`const compilerHost = ts.createCompilerHostWorker(…)` → the return annotation types the local → `compilerHost.getSourceFile = (fileName, …) => …` arrows get context). Private-member accessibility is LEXICAL: `checkStaticPrivateMemberAccess` accepts an access positionally INSIDE the declaring class declaration (same file) — a function nested in a class method reads the class's static privates legally (fourslash `TestState.nLinesContext`).
- **`asserts` predicates are ACTIVE end-to-end (M1.5, round 386)**: `parseType()` builds `TypePredicate(assertsModifier=true)` (param name = bare `Identifier`; `this` → `Identifier("this")`), an asserts return resolves to VOID (`getTypeFromTypeNode`/`getTypeNodeName`/`resolveSimpleTypeName` all branch on `assertsModifier` — a return-less bodied assert fn draws no TS2355/TS2366/TS7030), and `narrowByAssertCall` narrows FOUR shapes: `asserts x is T` by the resolved target (the predicate-position arg must BE the walked path); `asserts x is NonNullable<T>` as null/undefined exclusion (utility unmodeled → special-cased BEFORE `getTypeFromTypeNode`); `asserts node is Exclude<T, U>` (round 457, tsc's `Debug.assertNotNode`) by REMOVING union members assignable to U, where U is a bare callee type-param bound from the sibling `test` arg's predicate target (`predicateTargetTypeOfGuardExpr` — the same round-424 inference; suppression-only: fires only when a member is removed AND survivors remain; the es2020.ts `flattenChain` compiler site stays FP due to whole-program `Debug`/`OptionalChain` resolution scale, NOT the narrowing — a faithful barrel+guard+loop+intersection repro passes); bare `asserts cond` via `applyConditionNarrowing(t, arg, isTrue=true)` — the arg need only MENTION the path (`Debug.assert(x !== undefined)`), which is why its pre-check is `argMentionsReferencePath` (path-CONTAINMENT, iterative worklist, bails OPEN at 512 visits). `narrowByCallPredicate`'s pre-check stays EXACT-match (its narrowing requires the arg BE the path — don't widen it). `resolveFlowCalleeDecl` also resolves `Ns.fn` namespace-member callees via the namespace symbol's `.exports` (`resolveNamespaceMemberFnDecl` — `Debug.assert` in tsc sources; the namespace receiver types as `any` so `resolvePropertyMethodDecl` misses it). `callHasTypeGuardArg` gates `!assertsModifier` (an asserts callback is not a type guard — also preserves pre-M1.5 behavior when asserts parsed as a bare type).
- **An assert/predicate TARGET that resolves any/error retries via `resolveAssertTargetTypeNode` (round 460)** — member-wise union resolution, lib `Exclude<T, U>` evaluation (keep T's members NOT assignable to U — no union distribution exists in the general conditional evaluation), and FUNCTION-BODY-local alias recovery via `uniqueNestedTypeAliasByName` (the type-alias sibling of `buildNestedFunctionMap`; ambiguous names → null). Wired into `narrowByAssertCall`'s explicit-type-arg branch AND `narrowByCallPredicate`'s target resolution; flow-only — do NOT wire into general type resolution. Companion: `narrowBySwitchClause`'s default clause also exhausts a NON-union subject whose discriminant annotation is an enum-member-union alias fully covered by cases, and a DIRECT enum-typed subject with every member covered (canonical-key space).
- **An IMPORTED type-guard/assert (`import { isDefined } from "./x.js"`) narrows via the FLOW-ONLY `resolveImportedFunctionLikeDecl` (round 409, M3.4) — NOT the general `resolveAlias`.** `resolveFlowCalleeDecl`'s Identifier branch resolved an imported guard to its ImportSpecifier (not a FunctionDeclaration), so it never narrowed. TWO gaps blocked resolution (round 408's naive "call resolveAlias" was inert on the FIRST): (1) `resolveModuleSpecifier` deliberately won't strip the ESM `.js`/.jsx/.mjs/.cjs extension (the TS2459 gotcha), so `resolveAlias` couldn't resolve ANY `.js` import — even a DIRECT one; (2) `targetFile.locals[name]` misses through an `export *` barrel (tsc's `_namespaces/ts.ts`). Fixed with `resolveImportedFunctionLikeDecl` (memoized in `importedGuardDeclCache`, the services-hang firewall) — `.js`-tolerant `resolveAliasJsModuleSpecifier` + `export *`-following `resolveExportedSymbolThroughStars` (the SYMBOL companion to M1.1's name-only `getModuleExportsFollowingStars`). **LOAD-BEARING: do NOT add the `.js`/star fallback to the general `resolveAlias` — a first cut there was corpus-green (0 corpus tests use `.js`/`export *`) but REGRESSED the self-compile 2,618 → 2,915 with a TS2315×466 flood (resolving barrel-imported TYPES exposed our generic-arity/type-resolution M3 gaps). Keep it flow-only.** FP-safe: flow narrowing only removes union constituents. **The barrel-imported NAMESPACE-member case (`Debug.assertIsDefined(x)`) is the sibling `resolveImportedNamespaceSymbol` (memoized in `importedNamespaceSymCache`), consulted by `resolveNamespaceMemberFnDecl` ONLY when the general `resolveAlias` fails to yield a module symbol — same flow-only / no-general-cache-touch discipline. (Dashboard-neutral on the compiler profile: the round-408 `Debug.assertIsDefined` FPs have a root cause deeper than resolution — a fresh repro is queued; but the capability is real for the other profiles.)** COMPANION: the generic guard `isDefined<T>(x: T | undefined): x is T` (tsc's own, pervasive) binds T via naked-type-parameter union inference — `collectPredicateTpBindings`' UnionType branch binds a SINGLE bare-TP union member to the actual constituents no concrete member covers (bare-TP union members were previously skipped as ambiguous → T never bound → no narrowing).
- **A NESTED type-guard function (`function isX(v): v is T` declared INSIDE another function — tsc's `isTupleType`/`isGenericTupleType`/… inside `createTypeChecker`) narrows via `resolveFlowCalleeDecl`'s `uniqueFunctionDeclByName` fallback (round 418, M1.12) — the binder does NOT bind nested-function declarations (B83.5), so the `currentFileLocals?.get ?: globals` callee lookup returns null and the guard NEVER resolved (the single biggest TS2339 sub-family: `x.target` on `Type` after `isTupleType(x)`, ×46 on checker.ts).** The fallback is a program-wide map name → the UNIQUELY-named FunctionDeclaration at any nesting depth (`buildNestedFunctionMap`'s iterative worklist over statement containers / fn-method-accessor bodies / namespace bodies / class members / arrow-and-fn-expr initializers); a colliding name → null → no narrowing (conservative, FP-safe). **TWO consequences the resolver EXPOSES, both handled in-place:** (a) the receiver-narrowing consumers (`computeRawTypeOfPropertyAccess`, the narrowed-to-never TS2339 branch) are ALL gated on `Type.Union`, so a NON-union narrow-DOWN (`Type` → `TupleTypeReference`) never reached the property access — `checkMemberAccessMissing` has a top-of-function suppression: for a pure Identifier/PropertyAccess receiver where flow narrowing yields a STRICT subtype (`narrowed <: raw`, non-union, non-never) that HAS the property, `return` (suppression-only). (b) `narrowByCallPredicate`'s POSITIVE union branch drops a constituent unrelated to the guard target in BOTH directions; an INTERSECTION target `X & {p}` (declarations.ts's `shouldPrintWithInitializer(node): node is CanHaveLiteralInitializer & { initializer: Expression }`) drops EVERY member → collapse to `never` → FP TS2339 — fall back to the antecedent union, gated `narrowed.isEmpty() && targetType is Type.Intersection`. **LOAD-BEARING gate distinction — a POSITIVE-collapse `never` (bug) vs a NEGATIVE-exhaustion `never` (CORRECT): `instanceofWithStructurallyIdenticalTypes` narrows `C1|C2|C3` to `never` via `!isC1 && !isC2` on STRUCTURALLY-IDENTICAL types (the NEGATIVE branch) and tsc EMITS TS2339-on-`never` there.** The intersection-target fallback is POSITIVE-branch-only, and the narrow-DOWN suppression excludes `narrowed === never`, so the negative-exhaustion never survives to the narrowed-to-never TS2339 branch. A blanket never-fix (any positive-empty → union) OR a "raw declared type exposes the property → suppress" guard BOTH over-suppress that corpus test — do not reintroduce either. The residual binder/nodeFactory intersection-arm-UNION FPs (`node.parent` on `PropertyAccessExpression | (ElementAccessExpression & …)`) are a separate M3 gap (union property-access over an intersection-arm member) and were deliberately NOT suppressed.
- **`computeExportedSymbolThroughStars`'s LEAF lookup must gate on the local being genuinely EXPORTED (`name in moduleNamedExportsOf(file)`, round 413) — `export *` re-exports a module's EXPORTS, NEVER a non-re-exported IMPORT alias.** This was the TRUE cause of the builder.ts `Debug.assert(isDefined(state))` TS18048 family (NOT the round-412 depth-truncation red herring — an instrumented run showed ZERO narrowing-walk truncations; the walk simply never resolved `Debug`). tsc's `_namespaces/ts.ts` does `export * from "../core.js"` (core.ts merely IMPORTS `Debug` — a non-exported Alias local) BEFORE `export * from "../debug.js"` (debug.ts DECLARES `export namespace Debug`); the pre-413 leaf returned ANY local named `Debug`, so the star search stopped at core.ts's import alias (flags=Alias, no `.exports`) and never reached debug.ts's namespace → `Debug.assert` never resolved → `resolveNamespaceMemberFnDecl` returned null → the bare-assert narrowing never fired. Fixing the leaf-export gate flipped **self-compile 2,373 → 1,966 (−407; TS2339 614 → 237)** — barrel-imported `Debug.*` + every barrel guard now resolves. FP-safe (flow-only; `resolveExportedSymbolThroughStars` is consulted ONLY by the round-409+ flow-only resolvers — function/namespace/enum). `getModuleNamedExports` covers all the ESM export forms in tsc's source (namespace/function/class/var/interface/type/enum + `export {}` + `export * as`), memoized per-file via `moduleNamedExportsCache`. LESSON: an instrumented "does the walk truncate?" probe (0 truncations) falsified the documented depth diagnosis and redirected to the real resolution gap — always verify a "walk hits the cap" hypothesis by instrumenting the truncation, not by inferring from a file's node count.
- **TS2454 is filtered at the END of init for `flowDisabledRanges` (round 426, faithful TS2563 — tsc's flowAnalysisDisabled emits TS2563 OR TS2454, never both, per CONTAINER)**: a NEW TS2454 emitter must run BEFORE that filter line (just before the `// end if (!declarationOnly)` close), or its diagnostics escape the depth-trip bail on giant real-world files.
- **CFA of `typeof v` in TYPE position (B470, `tryEmitTypeofIndexSigNarrowing`) is a DEDICATED local walker, NOT a change to `getTypeFromTypeQuery`** — three things block the naive route and the walker sidesteps all of them: (a) the binder does NOT bind flow nodes inside type-annotation positions (the `v` identifier inside `typeof v` has no `nodeToFlow` entry), BUT Flow.kt DOES `recordFlow(stmt)` for a `TypeAliasDeclaration` node (look up `getFlowAt(aliasDecl)`, not the typeof node); (b) a non-generic type alias's declared type is CACHED (`declaredTypes`/`nodeTypes`) and resolved EAGERLY without flow, so narrowing inside `getTypeFromTypeQuery` returns the cached un-narrowed union — the walker narrows LOCALLY instead (resolve the var's declared type, `narrowTypeFromFlow` at the alias-decl flow, re-check the literal's property values against the narrowed primitive); (c) a `type C` declared inside a block is BLOCK-SCOPED → NOT bound (B83.5) → `globals`/`currentFileLocals` miss it → resolve via the recursive `findTypeAliasDeclInStatements` AST scan. Currently wired ONLY for the index-signature-value shape (`{ [k:string]: typeof v }`), squiggling the property NAME + related TS6501; extend the same alias-decl-flow pattern for other typeof-in-type-position consumers.
- **B464 flow-into-closures: a captured (closed-over) variable's outer narrowing flows into an ArrowFunction/FunctionExpression body via `FlowStart.outerFlow` (set in `Flow.kt bindFunctionLikeBody` ONLY for those two container kinds nested in another function; `narrowTypeFromFlow`'s FlowStart case + the FollowLoopEntry mirror continue into it via `outerFlowForCapturedName`).** The continuation is GATED — captured root must not be `this`/`super`/`arguments`, not a closure local (`FlowStart.localNames`), and not reassigned at/after the closure (`FlowStart.reassignedAfterNames`). **`reassignedAfterNames` is a source-text scan over the RANGE `[closure.pos, enclosingFn.end)`, NOT "reassigned anywhere in the function"** — tsc's `isPastLastAssignment`: an assignment BEFORE the closure still flows in (the `f4` case: `x="abc"; if(x){ ()=>x.length }` narrows to `string`), only an assignment AT/AFTER the closure withholds (`f5`: `if(x){ ()=>x.length }; x="abc"` keeps `string|undefined`). The "reassigned-anywhere" approximation FPs f4 once a TS18048 emitter exists. The companion `emitTs18048ForClosureCapturedUndefinedReceiver` (TS18048 for `x.prop` on a captured maybe-undefined var) is **deliberately CLOSURE-ONLY** (located via `FlowGraph.closureStarts` range-containment) — there is NO general possibly-undefined property-access emitter (our narrowing has gaps outside closures, so a broad one would FP corpus-wide); scoping to closures, where B464 now flows the enclosing narrowing in, bounds the FP surface to the captured-var family.
- **TS2638 (`in`-RHS "may represent a primitive") for a truthy-narrowed `unknown` (`inKeywordAndUnknown`) uses a SCOPED sentinel, NOT a global narrowing change.** tsc narrows `unknown` truthy to `{}`; the `truthyUnknownType` sentinel (Type.kt) is produced by `narrowByTruthiness` ONLY when the `narrowUnknownToEmptyObject` flag is set — `checkInOperatorRhs` sets it (and lifts `currentFlowGraph` from the dedicated `currentArithmeticFlowGraph` field) around its OWN `narrowTypeFromFlow` walk, so all other narrowing keeps `unknown` unchanged (zero blast radius). A positive `instanceof` of the name demotes the sentinel back to `unknown` in `narrowByInstanceOf` (so `x instanceof Object && 'a' in x` does NOT fire — f1); a declared `{}` param never reaches it. **LOAD-BEARING: the arithmetic pass must expose its flow via `currentArithmeticFlowGraph`, NOT the global `currentFlowGraph` — setting the latter for the whole pass makes `getTypeOfExpression`'s union-receiver path (Checker.kt ~85456) flow-aware and regressed 78 arithmetic/comparison tests.**
- **B539: `checkInRhsPrimitiveTypeParams`' `in`-RHS TS2322/TS2208 walker now ALSO covers ARROW functions with an EXPRESSION body (`=> ( cond ? … )`) — the Block-bodied FunctionDeclaration path is unchanged** — via a recursive `walkExpr` that descends ternary/`&&`/`||`/paren/object-literal/nested-arrow/call with `&&`-chain narrowing (first `in x` fires + narrows x for later `&&` SIBLINGS; `||` and the ternary FALSE-branch do NOT narrow; the ternary TRUE-branch inherits the condition's positive-`in` operands). Two companion ADDITIVE emits live in the same walk, both gated to an UNCONSTRAINED type param (`constraint == null`) of the enclosing generic fn (FP-safe BY CONSTRUCTION — tsc errors on the exact shape, so no passing test carries it un-errored): a param `x: <ownUnconstrainedTP> = undefined` → TS2322 'undefined not assignable to TP' + "could be instantiated…" (whole-param span = `param.pos`..`init.pos+9`); `Object.keys(<unconstrained-TP value>)` → a HARDCODED 2-overload TS2769 (our embedded `Object.keys` is a single `(o: any)` sig, NOT tsc's `(o:{})`/`(o:object)` pair) + 2 related TS2208 (`extends {}` then `extends object`, at the TP decl). Sole consumer: `conditionalTypeDoesntSpinForever` (whose name is a red herring — it needs no TS2589; all 12 errors are these AST-derivable shapes).
- **ES2024 Set set-operations (`setMethods`): the embedded `Set` carries `union`/`intersection`/`difference`/`symmetricDifference` (→`Set<T>`) + `isSubsetOf`/`isSupersetOf`/`isDisjointFrom` (→boolean), all taking the new corpus-unique `interface ReadonlySetLike<T> { keys(): Iterator<T>; has(value: T): boolean; readonly size: number }`.** An EMPTY `[]` arg to such a param → TS2345 via a DEDICATED branch in `checkArgumentsAgainstSignature` (placed BEFORE the Array-literal-vs-Array branch) that HARDCODES the display `'undefined[]'`→`'ReadonlySetLike<unknown>'` + the missing-`has,size` `messageChain` and `continue`s — we type `[]` as `any[]` (B87.6) but tsc types it `undefined[]`, and an array has Array's `keys` but no `has`/`size`. Valid args (Set/Map/`{size,*keys(),has}` object literal) relate to ReadonlySetLike via `@strict:false` param bivariance (the test sets it). Adding the methods was corpus-FP-safe (ReadonlySetLike used nowhere else; no "and N more" Set count shifts). **Round 451: the 7 set-ops are now LIB_MIN_TARGET-gated at `ScriptTarget.ESNext` — they live in `lib.es2025.collection.d.ts` in the pinned tsc, so at a sub-ES2025 lib the `Set` shape lacks them (tsc's own `core.ts` `const set: Set<T> = { has, add, delete, clear, size, forEach, keys, values, entries }` shim relies on that — it FP'd TS2740 while they were unconditional). Gated at ESNext, not ES2024, because our `ScriptTarget` enum has no ES2025 and ESNext is the next level above ES2024; the `setMethods` test's `@target: esnext` keeps them present. If you re-add a Set/ReadonlySet member, decide its lib level the same way (grep the extracted `lib.es20*.collection.d.ts`).
- **B468 refines B464: a captured `var` does NOT get closure narrowing (tsc gives it the DECLARED type), and the TS18048 emitter must RECOVER the captured var's declared type itself.** `FlowStart.enclosingVarDecls` (name→VariableDeclaration, collected in `bindFunctionLikeBody` via `collectEnclosingVarDecls` from the enclosing function body, NOT descending into nested function-likes) drives two things: `outerFlowForCapturedName` returns null when the captured root is a `var` (so `narrowingPastLastAssignment` f13's `var foo:string|undefined; foo=''; ()=>foo.x` keeps `string|undefined` → TS18048), AND `emitTs18048ForClosureCapturedUndefinedReceiver` falls back to `getTypeFromTypeNode(enclosingVarDecls[root].type)` when `getTypeOfExpression(recv)` returns `any` — a function-body `var`/`let`'s declared annotation does NOT resolve across the closure boundary via getTypeOfExpression (a PARAMETER does, which is why B464's f5 param case worked but f13's var case needed the recovery). SEPARATELY, the uninitialized-`let`-captured-reads walker (`checkUninitializedLetCapturedReads`, TS7034/7005) drops captured reads PAST the last UNCONDITIONAL same-scope assignment (`UlState.lastAssignmentPos`, gated `conditionalDepth==0` — a CONDITIONAL `if(cond){x=…}` assignment is NOT definite, so reads past it still fire: `controlFlowNoImplicitAny` f9/f10).
- **`emitTs18048ForClosureCapturedUndefinedReceiver` uses the LOOP-ENTRY-following narrowing variant `getNarrowedTypeForReferenceFollowLoopEntry`, NOT the plain `getNarrowedTypeForReference` (round 416).** The plain variant washes a reference back to its DECLARED type at a loop's FlowLoopLabel (back-edge safety), so a captured var narrowed by a closure-LOCAL guard BEFORE a loop and read INSIDE it (checker.ts:8207 `if (!expandedParams) return; for (…; i < expandedParams.length; …)`; builder.ts:1551 `array` guarded then read in a NESTED-closure loop) FP'd TS18048. The follow-loop-entry variant (already used by the sibling `emitTs18048ForOptionalPropertyAccessReceiver`, B81.1c) follows antecedent[0] so the read sees the pre-loop narrowing. FP-safe: it only ever narrows MORE (suppresses); an un-guarded captured var in a loop still fires. Any NEW closure/loop-scoped possibly-undefined emitter should default to the follow-loop-entry variant.
- **`switch (true) { case <cond>: }` narrowing lives in `narrowBySwitchOnTrue` (B411), dispatched from `narrowBySwitchClause` when the switch expression is the literal `true` Identifier.** The matched `FlowSwitchClause` narrows positively by clause N's case expr (via `applyConditionNarrowing`) and negatively by every CASE clause before N; `default` narrows negatively by every case. Fallthrough is ALREADY modeled by the binder's BranchLabel merges (body entry unions direct-match + prior-body fallthrough) so the `A | B` fallthrough unions are free. **B416 fixed the two gaps that blocked the discriminant siblings**: (1) `bindSwitchStatement` now binds each `case <expr>:` EXPRESSION at `preSwitch` (case expressions all evaluate at the switch HEAD before any body runs), NOT at the prior clause body's fallthrough flow — that flow is unreachable (`never`) after a return/break body and FP'd TS2339-on-never on later case discriminant reads; (2) `narrowByDiscriminantProperty` now narrows a SINGLE discriminated member (non-Union `t`) to `never` when its own literal discriminant contradicts the comparison (gated to a member genuinely carrying the discriminant), so sequential negative narrowing reaches the exhaustive `never`. **B417 closed the cluster** (test3): the relevant narrowing is PAST the switch (`switch (true) { case shape.kind==="circle": return … }` then `if (shape.kind==="circle")`) — `bindSwitchStatement`'s no-default `postSwitch` join now narrows the no-match path by EVERY case-expr-false (chained `newCondition(false, …)` over `preSwitch`) instead of joining bare `preSwitch`, giving tsc's post-switch exhaustiveness (shape → `{square}`); paired with `checkFlowNoOverlapCondition` (next entry). The switch's OWN duplicate case (`case shape.kind==="circle"` again) correctly emits nothing — tsc doesn't narrow case EXPRESSIONS by prior cases, so bare `preSwitch` for case exprs is right.
- **A discriminated union keyed on an ENUM-MEMBER discriminant (`type: Kind.A`) narrows via a DEDICATED AST path, NOT the literal path (round 411)** — enum-member types resolve to `anyType` in our engine (they are NOT modeled as literals; modeling them generally is nominal-enum / B425-risky), so `literalTypeOfExpression(Kind.A)` returns null AND the member's discriminant property `type: Kind.A` resolves to `any`. Both `narrowByDiscriminantProperty` (`s.type === Kind.A`) and `narrowBySwitchClause` (`case Kind.B:`) match on the enum member's canonical key `"${enumSymId}#member"` read from the AST: the union member's DECLARED `PropertyDeclaration.type` annotation (`enumMemberKeysOfTypeNode`) vs the `Enum.Member` on the comparison/case (`enumMemberKeyOfExpr`). **A BARREL-IMPORTED enum (`import { Kind } from "./x.js"`) resolves to an `Alias` (flags=4096) that the general `resolveAlias` CANNOT follow through the ESM `.js` + `export *` barrel — use the FLOW-ONLY `resolveImportedEnumSymbol` (the enum sibling of `resolveImportedNamespaceSymbol`, memoized in `importedEnumSymCache`), never the general resolveAlias (a fallback there regressed the self-compile via a TS2315 flood, round 409).** FP-safe: a member without a resolvable enum-member discriminant is KEPT; a multi-valued discriminant (`type: Kind.A | Kind.B`) survives a single `!==` (keep iff `keys.any { !in caseKeys }`). tsc's UpToDateStatus / TypeMapper / PrivateIdentifierInfo are the corpus-external targets (the single-file corpus never exercises barrel imports). **Round 422 extensions to the same key space — keep both invariants when touching it**: (a) a plain STRING-literal discriminant member (`kind: "untransformed"` mixed into an enum-keyed union, tsc's PrivateIdentifierInfo) carries the DISJOINT key `lit:s:<value>` (`literalDiscriminantKeyOfType`; `enumMemberKeysOfTypeNode`'s LiteralType branch + `narrowBySwitchClause`'s enum path accepting all-convertible literal cases, still gated ≥1 genuine enum key) — string-ONLY and namespace-disjoint BY DESIGN: a string enum member never equals a plain string literal in tsc narrowing, but numeric enums ARE number-comparable, so a NUMERIC literal returns no key — SINCE round 459 `narrowByDiscriminantProperty` instead compares a numeric-literal RHS against the enum's VALUE domain (`enumValues` via `canonicalEnumSymbol`): a member whose enum has NO member value equal to the literal is dropped (tsc's `flowType.flags === 0` → IncompleteType), one WITH a matching value stays conservatively KEPT; (b) an OPTIONAL discriminant access `x?.kind === RHS` (true branch, RHS provably non-nullish — enum member or non-nullish literal, `rhsDefinitelyNonNullishForDiscriminant`) drops nullish members BEFORE the per-member filters and the drop must SURVIVE their bail (members without readable annotations are otherwise kept — including the nullish intrinsics); never applied on the negative branch (`x?.kind !== RHS` is true when x IS undefined).
- **A `default` clause of an EXHAUSTIVE discriminated-union switch narrows the discriminant to `neverType` (round 441) — `narrowBySwitchClause`'s round-425 default-clause branch drops the case-covered members and returns `neverType` (not `null`) when the filtered set is EMPTY.** This is what makes `default: assertNever(x)` / `assertType<never>(x)` type-check (the `never`-param arg-check reads the narrowed `never` via `getNarrowedTypeForReference`). FP-safe by construction: BOTH filter paths only DROP a member with a readable literal/enum `.kind` matching a case (a wide-kind member OR a member without a readable discriminant is KEPT — `filterUnionByEnumDiscriminant` keeps it, `isLiteralKindForDiscriminant` is false for it), so an empty result is a genuine exhaustiveness proof, and a NON-exhaustive switch narrows to the SURVIVING members (the never-param call still errors with the uncovered member). Only fires when the SUBJECT resolves to a `Type.Union`.
