A `switch (node.kind)` where `node`'s DECLARED type is non-union but a preceding `Debug.type<SomeUnion>(node)` / `asType<T>(node)` assert (`asserts value is T`, explicit type arg) casts it to a union DOES work end-to-end (round 441): `narrowByAssertCall` re-types the reference to the union (round-424b explicit-type-arg bind + the non-relating-object → return-target branch), then the switch default exhausts it. **The arg-check CONSUMER coupling is load-bearing (`checkArgumentsAgainstSignature`, ~124781): a NON-union arg to a `never` param is narrowed and the result used ONLY when it PROVES `never` (`n === neverType`) — a partial union (non-exhaustive switch) is NOT used (keeps the declared type → the same TS2345 the pre-narrow path emitted, so no manufactured FP). This relaxes the earlier blanket never-param exclusion, which was correct ONLY before the exhaustive default narrowed to `never`.** The 3 residual self-compile `→never` FPs need the target union to resolve with readable `.kind` members (`Node`/`Expression`-subtype unions like `HasInferredType` whose members are big AST-node interfaces) — a deeper resolution gap.
- **Const-string discriminants (round 473): a `case EventTypesRegistry:` Identifier reads as its string literal via `topLevelConstStringValues`** (program-wide unambiguous top-level const strings; a value-space competitor POISONS the name, type-space aliases don't compete) — wired into narrowBySwitchClause's clause collection + default-exhaustiveness + narrowByDiscriminantProperty; `typeQueryConstStringLiteral` recovers an `eventName: typeof <const>` member annotation that widened to string/any in BOTH discriminant filters (a NEW filter must call it too, or the member is conservatively kept and the narrowing silently dies). The sibling-discriminant TS2339 suppression in checkMemberAccessMissing narrows the BASE identifier and projects the accessed member — the walked path `event.data` is invisible to FlowSwitchClause (subject `event.eventName`).
- **A type-guard `x is C` narrows a union member DOWN to `C` when `C <: member` — `narrowByCallPredicate`'s positive union branch is `m <: c ? m : c <: m ? c : drop` (tsc `getNarrowedType` assumeTrue), round 411.** The old code kept ONLY `m <: c`, so a union whose members are all SUPERTYPES of the guard target (`Expression | PropertyName` narrowed by `is TaggedTemplateExpression`) collapsed to `never` → FP TS2339-on-never. The new mapping only ever KEEPS more than the old filter → FP-safe (can only suppress). The NEGATIVE branch (`!== `, keep `!(m <: c)`) is unchanged. **The SINGLE-type (non-union) branch got the SAME reorder in round 412: `candidate <: t ? candidate : t <: candidate ? t : candidate` — `candidate <: t` checked FIRST (was `t <: candidate` first, keeping the WIDE `t`).** Load-bearing because our relation OVER-accepts `t <: candidate` when `t` has an OPTIONAL prop `p?: T | undefined` and `candidate` (`t`'s declared `extends`-subtype) redefines it REQUIRED `p: T` (both relations hold), so a guard narrowing DOWN kept the wide type and the member stayed possibly-undefined → FP TS18048. Do NOT revert to `t <: candidate` first. **COMPANION (round 412): `emitTs18048ForOptionalPropertyAccessReceiver` narrows the RECEIVER PATH (`state` in `state.program`) via the PROPERTY-ACCESS node's flow (`getFlowAt(narrowExpr)` — the bare receiver Identifier for a CAPTURED var often has NO flow node), because a guard `Debug.assert(isDefinedProgram(state))` narrows `state`, a DIFFERENT path than the `state.program` the reference-path narrowing keys on.** **M3.4-HARD RESIDUAL (don't re-chase — precisely diagnosed round 412): a guard/assert at a function TOP does NOT narrow a use DEEP in a HUGE function (`createBuilderProgram`). Instrumented finding: the flow nodes ARE present (`getFlowAt(state.program)` = FlowCondition/FlowAssignment, non-null), but builder.ts is `cfaTooLarge` (flow graph = 3290 nodes > 2000), so the narrowing walk hits `NARROW_MAX_DEPTH` (2000) before reaching the top-of-function assert FlowCall — `narrowByAssertCall`/`narrowByCallPredicate` never fire for `state`. NOT resolution (barrel `Debug` resolves fine in isolation), NOT a binder flow-node gap. The fix is a smarter/deeper walk for too-large files — but raising `NARROW_MAX_DEPTH` is the round-385 services-HANG P0 risk, so it needs the tsc-shaped budget rebuild (M3.4 "faithful budget consumption"), not a blind bump. This blocks ~15 builder.ts/es2015.ts/module.ts TS18048 + the TS2722 sites.**
- **Round 423 narrowing extensions — union-target guards, aliased conditions, kind-key disjointness (all in the shared condition-narrowing path):** (1) `narrowByCallPredicate`'s union filters consult `typeGuardMemberDisjoint` (round-411 `.kind` key space) BEFORE the relation — enum-member kinds resolve to `any`, so the relation over-accepts and `!isJsxOpeningFragment(node)` collapsed JsxCallLike to `never` (every member "assignable" to the property-poorest fragment); disjoint keys beat the relation in BOTH branches. **Measured dead-ends (each a full self-compile A/B, do NOT re-attempt without a per-site diff): a key-SUBSET ⇒ matched verdict (1,708 → 1,720; brand-intersection targets like `CallChain = CallExpression & {_optionalChainBrand}` share the kind without being matched), and same-SYMBOL union membership (exact no-op — the real-tsc member/target instances aren't symbol-identical). REVERSED at round 425: the tsc-faithful positive-empty → `member & candidate` INTERSECTION fallback (net-negative at round 423) is now LANDED strictly-removals — the round-423 verdict was an ARTIFACT of the enum-key SPLIT (see the round-425 canonical-key entry) feeding wrong disjointness verdicts; lesson: re-measure a dead-end when a root cause upstream of it falls.** (2) ALIASED CONDITIONS (tsc `narrowType` inlineLevel ≤ 5): a bare-Identifier condition resolves to its `const x = <cond>` initializer via `aliasedConditionInitializer` — a flow BACK-WALK through value-preserving nodes only, bailing on loop labels, alias reassignment, or walked-root reassignment. **(round 424) The back-walk follows a closure `FlowStart` (outer flow; B464 captured-name gates applied to BOTH the alias AND the walked root), a `FlowBranchLabel` (every REACHABLE antecedent must independently prove preservation and land on the SAME decl; unreachable antecedents contribute nothing), and `FlowCall`/`FlowArrayMutation` (a call cannot rebind an enclosing let/const), with a per-call node MEMO (a `||`-chain fans out a diamond per term — un-memoized the shared upstream segment re-walks exponentially). TRAP: an assignment EXPRESSION's `FlowAssignment.node` is the whole `BinaryExpression` (Flow.kt `bindAssignmentTarget(expr.left, expr)`) — `flowAssignmentRootName` reads its LHS; without that arm the walk bails at the first assignment-in-condition (`!(oldInfo = oldState!.f(…))`, tsc builder.ts canCopyEmitSignatures).** **MUST stay memoized (`aliasedConditionInitCache`, keyed by start-FlowNode identity — never bare `nodeKey`, the cross-file pos collision) — the uncached first cut ran the self-compile 4×+ slower** (the linear flow iteration visits the same FlowCondition hundreds of times). (3) `checkMemberAccessMissing`'s round-418 narrow-DOWN suppression accepts a narrowed UNION (a union-TARGET guard on a single-type receiver) when EVERY member resolves the property via `resolveMemberPropertyType`; the declared-union receiver stays excluded (the negative-exhaustion `never` corpus pin).
- **Round 424 narrowing extensions (all suppression-only / FP-safe):** (1) the union TS2339 elaboration retries a WASHED plain walk with the loop-entry-following variant (pre-loop guard + read inside a loop, tsc parseResponseFile) — **the "did the walk narrow" gate MUST be STRUCTURAL (member-id sets), never identity: any `&&`/`||` on the path is a 2-antecedent FlowBranchLabel whose union of [declared, declared] MINTS a fresh `Type.Union` (getUnionType does not intern), so `===` misses the wash exactly when a compound condition is present.** (2) `narrowByAssignmentRhs` accepts a CALL RHS whose resolved callee return ANNOTATION classifies non-nullish via the SYNTACTIC `typeNodeDefinitelyNonNullish` (no type resolution in the walker; own-TP references and optional-chained calls bail) — **since round 463 a NULLISH-MIRROR overload cluster (`f(x: T, …): R;` / `f(x: T | undefined, …): R | undefined;` + impl, tsc `instantiateType`) IS claimed via `nullishMirrorOverloadNonNullish`** (first-applicable selection; sound ONLY because the structural shape test rejects any difference beyond threaded nullishness, and only when the FIRST-declared sig is the non-nullish side — tsc matches the nullish-first ordering first for ANY arg); `typeNodeDefinitelyNonNullish`'s TypeReference branch falls back to merged `globals` for a barrel-imported Alias, restricted to interface/class/enum (a TypeAlias through that name-keyed fallback re-opens the round-443 conflation trap). (3) `narrowByCallPredicate` narrows a PREFIX-path guard (`usesWildcardTypes(options): options is CompilerOptions & {types: string[]}` with walked path `options.types`): positive branch only, drop-nullish only, and the tail property must be REQUIRED — **optionality is a SYMBOL attribute (`types?: string[]` resolves to `string[]`), so the type check alone silently passes an optional tail; on an intersection the property is required iff ANY constituent declares it required** (`resolvePrefixTailSegment`). (4) `asserts node is U` with U an INFERRED callee TP (tsc `Debug.assertNode`): U resolves from a type-guard TEST argument's own predicate target first (`predicateTargetTypeOfGuardExpr` — the minimal drop-nullish claim alone TRADES the TS18048 for a TS2339 on surviving union members), falling back to the constraint-chain (`U → T → Node`) drop-nullish claim; `resolveNamespaceMemberFnDecl` PREFERS a TypePredicate-bearing declaration (an overloaded assert's valueDeclaration is the annotation-less IMPL, which made every consumer bail). (5) An OVERWRITE of the walked reference (`narrowByAssignmentRhs`) with an unclassifiable initializer/RHS resets to the PRECISE overwritten type — a DECLARATION to its own annotation/initializer-call return, a plain `=` to its call-RHS return (`resolvedCallReturnTypeForFlow`); `??=`/`||=`/unresolvable keep the antecedent (for `??=` the antecedent IS the base). **NEVER reset to the reader's flat-map declared type: `currentLocalTypes` is block-UNAWARE/first-decl-wins, so it injects an OUTER shadowed binding's type — a blanket declaredType reset was MEASURED at 3 new FPs (builder.ts:1814/destructuring.ts:114/moduleNameResolver:1950); the shadowing-redeclaration-after-falsy-guard shape otherwise collapses to `never` (moduleNameResolver 1924/1931/1950).** (6) `this`-receiver narrowing (the DebugTypeMapper slice): an `asserts value is <TP>` binds the TP from EXPLICIT call type args; an assertion on `any`/`unknown` RE-TYPES the reference (the relation gate trivially passes for `any`); `checkMemberAccessMissing` consults flow narrowing for `this` receivers (`getTypeOfExpression(this)` is deliberately anyType per B101 — the round-418 suppression never applied) and the exhaustive-switch receiver typing recovers an anyType receiver via the same re-type; `buildNestedFunctionMap` resolves a name collision to the UNIQUE TypePredicate-bearing decl (Debug's `type` is sig + annotation-less impl — plain ≥2→ambiguous made the guard invisible; 0 or ≥2 predicate decls stay ambiguous). The single-file repro cleared WITHOUT the collision rule — only the faithful multi-file (barrel + namespace-local overloaded guard) repro exposed it.
- **NEGATIVE type-guard narrowing uses tsc's SUBTYPE relation, where a MISSING source property FAILS an OPTIONAL target property (round 480, `missingVsOptionalProvesNotSubtype`)** — a union member assignable to the guard target only via optional-prop leniency (vfsUtil FileInode vs DirectoryInode) survives `!guard(x)`; wired into BOTH the union filter and the single-type negative return in `narrowByCallPredicate`. A member with NO optional-prop difference still drops (the structurally-identical corpus pin).
- **Round 425 narrowing invariants:** (1) **ANY new `"symId#member"` enum-discriminant key construction MUST route the enum symbol through `canonicalEnumSymbol`** — the SAME enum reaches the key builders as DIFFERENT Symbol instances (the program-global merged instance vs the declaring file's local instance via `currentFileLocals`/the barrel resolver, first-touch-cached by Type.id in `discriminantKindKeysCache`), and a split key space makes `typeGuardMemberDisjoint` catastrophically wrong (all SyntaxKind keys looked pairwise disjoint → every guard-narrowed member dropped → the never×21 cluster). Diagnosed only by stderr-instrumenting the REAL corpus — two rounds of repro-enrichment found nothing (the split needs cross-file first-touch ordering). (2) **A type guard/instanceof with a UNION/candidate target distributes the narrow-DOWN direction PER CANDIDATE** (tsc `getNarrowedType` `mapType(candidate, …)`) — testing `targetUnion <: member` requires every candidate and never holds; same both-directions rule added to `narrowByInstanceOf`'s positive union filter. (4) `typeof x === "object"` classifies an ENUM-flavored Type.Object as NOT-object (enum values are numbers/strings at runtime) and CALLABLE objects as NOT-object (they report "function").
- **A slot-move/probe script that RELOCATES a pass dispatch must match the FULL dispatch text (`pass("checkSpine") { checkSpine() }`), never a substring — the INV.4(d) slot-move comments contain `pass("checkSpine")` verbatim, so a substring match inserts at a COMMENT ~100 passes early (round 555: this invalidated the round-542/543 "giants blocked on first-touch order-sensitivity" bisect and two rounds of (c2)/(c3) audits; at the correct position the coupling set was just two producer hoists).** Producers consumed by a moved pass (relationComplexityHandled, TypeParam .constraint/.default materialization by checkTpListDefaults) must be hoisted with it — grep the moved pass's body for side-set consults first.
- **The checkTypeAssignability AND checkPropertyAccess LEGACY PASSES ARE RETIRED (rounds 585/586)** — every emission of both giants is spine-anchored; the walkers survive as ANCHOR-CALLED leaf machinery only. Do NOT re-add a `pass(...)` dispatch for either (instant double-emission), and note the m3 truncation-mark blocks in the surviving walkers are dead code (only the retired drivers reached marked statements) pending a cleanup sweep.
- **checkSpine's per-file loop sets `currentFileLocals` but NOT `currentCheckFileName` — ANY emission/leaf moved onto the spine must install currentCheckFileName (= spineFileName) in its per-dispatch sandwich or per-file-keyed resolution silently drifts** (round 566: the cta anchor's qualify verdict flip). The cta migration's emission moves use emit-twice-suppress-legacy (the spine anchor emits under frame context; the legacy arm re-runs the leaf for its own maps' interleaved state and truncates its duplicate diagnostics) — a moved arm's anchor must reproduce the legacy scope structure exactly: shared currentLocalTypes across block/namespace boundaries (the leak is LOAD-BEARING — first-decl-wins bails depend on namespace-leaked entries), per-namespace varTypes copies, and the ModuleDeclaration inference-namespace pushes (CtaFrame.nsSymbol).
- **A cta FN-BODY anchor slice (rounds 570c/571) must: thread `frame.typeParams` (never emptySet — the fn's own TPs otherwise read as FOREIGN and the round-431e gate bails genuine TS2322s), and run the ctaFnBodyFrame sandwich under the frame chain's inference-namespace pushes (else shadowNestedFunctionNames' ns-exports consult diverges the frame shadow state).** The spine dispatch gate and the legacy truncation marks share ONE predicate (`ctaM3FnBodyAnchorScope`) — never fork them (a mismatch loses emissions to truncation or double-emits; CtaFnBodyAnchorTest pins exactly-once). **The m3e recording reproduction (round 571) carries three invariants: a recordOnly run's surface is EXACTLY the nested legacy arm (checkVarDeclAssignability + B127 — adding the top-level-only leaves manufactures state legacy doesn't have); narrowing-DISCARD regions (extractNullNarrowing verdicts computed at IfStatement spine enters under frame maps) skip recording; and a recordOnly flow walk that TRIPS must not reportFlowControlError (`ctaM3RecordOnlySuppress`) — the truncation removes the TS2563 but a persisted flowDisabledRanges registration makes the legacy-time walk bail silently (lost TS2563 + mis-filtered TS2454s).** **Round 573b: narrowed then-branches are NARROWING FRAMES (verdict at the If's enter under frame maps; localTypes copy + write + narrowedDeclared entry; a then-Block's narrowing frame REPLACES its statement-position frame — guard the double push). The B136 concreteness discipline has TWO channels now: contextual param typing (round 570) AND the un-annotated var-decl recording (round 573b, `!typeContainsForeignTypeParam(inferred, typeParams)`) — a FOREIGN-TP-carrying type registered into currentLocalTypes makes downstream verdicts flip on cold-vs-warm resolution order; any NEW recording channel needs the same gate.**
- **A fn/class at a BARE statement position (`if (c) function f(){}`) is UNREACHED by the legacy cta giant (`checkTypeAssignabilityInStmt` has NO FunctionDeclaration/ClassDeclaration arm) — `ctaM3FnHop` requires a statement-LIST landing (round 575), else a spine anchor crossing a bare-position fn hop emits where legacy is silent (gate-invisible: the shape is absent from corpus + tsc sources).** The checkFunctionBody BODY-LEVEL walkers (B442 redeclare / B205 FlatArray) anchor at eligible body-Block enters, legacy pair gated by the recorded set keyed on the Block's nodeId; B205 has no internal dedup — it is the sharp double-emit signal. The param-loop emission (16.4ei, round 576) anchors INSIDE the ctaFnBodyFrame sandwich BEFORE param typing with `currentFlowGraph` installed (a null graph loses the round-425 narrowing suppression → over-emission); eligibility is ONE shared predicate (`ctaM3BodyWalkersEligible`). An EXPRESSION-bodied arrow never routes through checkFunctionBody — its param defaults draw nothing on any path (a pin shape trap).
- **Wall-clock A/B runs MUST INTERLEAVE the two binaries (B/A pairs, keep both class dirs), never batch-then-batch — the box drifts within a session (round 493: the SAME binary ran 24.6 s at session start, 26.1–27.1 s two hours later, turning a real 0.8 s WIN into an apparent 0.7 s regression).** Compare medians of interleaved pairs; a suspicious delta re-measures interleaved before any revert/land decision.
- **Flow-aware TS2367 for a discriminant comparison in an if/else-if CONDITION lives in `checkFlowNoOverlapCondition`/`emitFlowNoOverlap` (B417), hooked into BOTH if-statement assignability dispatchers** (`checkTypeAssignabilityInStatements` ~64893 + `checkTypeAssignabilityInStmt` ~66084 — they carry the param scope via `checkFunctionBody`→`currentLocalTypes` and have `currentFlowGraph` set). For `recv.prop === literal` it gets the declared recv type via `getTypeOfExpression`, narrows via `getNarrowedTypeForReference`, and fires TS2367 ONLY when the DECLARED prop's literal set included the literal but the NARROWED set does not (flow-INDUCED no-overlap; statically-impossible comparisons stay owned by the const-literal/typeof TS2367 sites), the narrowed prop is all-literals, and `narrowed !== declared`. Extend `checkFlowNoOverlapCondition` to while/ternary/other comparison positions only when a consumer appears (currently if-conditions only — the sole failing consumer was test3).
- **A FULL union reconstructed by flow narrowing / switch fallthrough does NOT display as its alias unless registered STRUCTURALLY (B416 `unionAliasStructural`)** — `getUnionType` (Checker.kt ~103160) mints a fresh `Type.Union(sorted)` with a new `id` every call (it does NOT intern), so a fallthrough-rebuilt `MyType = A | B | C` has a different id than the alias's union and the id-keyed `aliasDisplayMap` misses → it would display `A | B | C`. `unionAliasStructural` keys non-generic union aliases by their SORTED member-id list → name; consulted at the top of `typeToString`'s Union case. FP firewall (the `allNamed` gate): only register when every member is a NAMED object type — primitive/literal unions (`string | number`) share singleton-ish ids corpus-wide and would over-match inline unions. A strict SUBSET of the alias union (`A | B`) has a different member set → no match → unfolds (matches tsc).
- **Walker broadening direction-of-emission rule (round 36 + round 42 iter5+iter10)**: when broadening a helper or walker's wrapper/expression-kind coverage, classify the helper first: (a) helpers whose result GATES a diagnostic emission (e.g. `isLiteralLikeExpr` gates TS1166/TS1169 — `!isLiteralLikeExpr(expr)` fires; broader = LESS emission; FN risk) vs (b) walkers that DIRECTLY emit per-reached-node (e.g. `checkTypeAsValueInStatement` directly emits TS2693/TS2708 — broader = MORE emission; FP risk) vs (c) pure subtree-visitors that just route control flow without emission semantics (safest). Round 42 iter5 demonstrated (a)-class regression: adding As/Satisfies/NonNull wrappers to `isLiteralLikeExpr` suppressed expected TS1166 on a wrapper-quoted computed property name. Round 42 iter10 demonstrated (b)-class regression: adding loop/switch/try recursion to `checkTypeAsValueInStatement` over-emitted TS2693/TS2708 in newly-reached contexts (-16 net). Class (c) walkers like `walkForOfNonIterableInExpr` (iter9) are safe to broaden freely. **Rule of thumb**: if grep shows the helper's result is consumed via `!helper(...)` for emission, broadening adds FN risk; if the walker has `diagnostics.add(...)` calls directly inside, broadening adds FP risk; if it only forwards to inner walkers with no emission, it's safe.

- **Generic alias instantiation substitutes ALL alias body kinds since round 463 — the historical B50.1 FunctionType/ConstructorType/call-sig-only-TypeLiteral skip is REMOVED** (un-substituted, the body's own `T` stays an unbound `Type.TypeParam` that FAILS the relation against a concrete conforming source — `TransformerFactory<SourceFile | Bundle>` FP'd TS2322 ×3; the old skip's FP hazard is covered by the round-431 foreign-TP source gates at emission sites; measured corpus-green + strictly-removals). The substitution path must stay cache-bypassed (`getTypeFromTypeNode`'s `cacheable` includes `currentTypeAliasArgs == null`); recursion bails at depth 10. **The recursive-alias lazy cycle-break covers TypeLiteral bodies AND deferred-position-only UNION bodies (`unionBodyIsDeferredPositionOnly`)** — a union member that is an INDEXED-ACCESS or MAPPED type FORCES evaluation (`N<T,K> = T | { [P in K]: N<T,K> }[K]` legitimately expects TS2589+TS2615, recursivelyExpandingUnionNoStackoverflow pin), so such bodies keep the eager depth-bail; `WrappedExpression<T>`-style property-position self-refs cycle-break instead.
- **`aliasDisplayMap[Type.id]` registration MUST skip singleton intrinsics** (anyType/unknownType etc. share ids corpus-wide — registering one alias's name on anyType.id corrupts EVERY other any-typed display) **and pure-function-typed results** (callSigs only — TypeScript unfolds those at display time). `typeToStringInProgress` guards recursive alias chains.
- **TS2689 `class extends Interface`**: lib types like `Array`, `Object`, `Promise`, etc. are BOTH `interface X<T> {}` AND `declare const X: XConstructor` in TypeScript's full lib.d.ts. Our embedded lib only includes the interface, so the binder doesn't set `SymbolFlags.Variable`. Without an allowlist, `class C extends Array<T>` emits FP TS2689. `tryClassifyExtendsInterface` (Checker.kt) consults `BUILTIN_LIB_VALUE_INTERFACES` for single-segment lookups. Add new lib types there as they surface. Single-segment only — qualified names like `M.X` fall through to the standard symbol-flag check.
- **`checkPropertyAccessInExpr` contextual typing**: ALWAYS clear `contextualType` before walking any function body (the body must not inherit an outer context), and the FunctionExpression branch must create its own scope SHADOWING outer bindings for un-annotated params — otherwise outer `(s: string)` leaks into inner `function (s) {}`.
- **Shadow detection in `currentLocalTypes` must compare types, not `containsKey`**: the map is populated by BOTH file-level var-decl walks AND function/arrow param scope tracking; "param shadows file var" = cached type differs from the file symbol's rawType (and its widened form). A naive `containsKey` check regresses destructured-param shadowing of file vars.
- **LinkStore pattern for `symbol.target`**: Import alias targets are stored in `CheckerState.symbolTargets` (checker-local side map), NOT on `Symbol.target` directly. Use `getSymbolTarget(symbol)` / `setSymbolTarget(symbol, target)` helpers — never write `symbol.target = X` in checker code. This keeps binder output immutable for parallel checking. The `getSymbolTarget` helper falls back to `symbol.target` for backwards compatibility.
- **TS2322 property elaboration chain**: `getPropertyElaborationChain` (hooked into the four assignability sites) must skip function→function sources (`getFunctionMismatchElaboration` owns them) and union sources (constituent elaboration owns them) — otherwise double-emit. The single-level vs nested-path message wording is baseline-load-bearing (see the format strings in the helper).
- **Relation cache cycle-break invalidation**: When `checkTypeRelatedTo` encounters a cycle (pair already in `relationComparisonStack`), it returns `true` speculatively. The `relationUsedCycleBreak` flag tracks this. Speculative `true` results must NOT be cached — only `false` results and non-cyclic `true` results are safe to cache. Otherwise mutually recursive types (e.g., A↔C, B↔D) get incorrect cached `true` results. `getPropertyElaborationChain` has its own cycle detection via `state.elaborationStack` and prefers leaf (non-Object) mismatches over recursive property paths.
- **`isSymbolTypeOnly` for `declare namespace` with value exports**: A `ModuleInstanceState.NonInstantiated` namespace (from `declare namespace`) is NOT type-only if it has value exports. Check `symbol.exports?.values?.any { it.flags.hasAny(SymbolFlags.Value) }` — if true, the namespace is a runtime value shape even though it has no IIFE. This matters for `export = a` where `a` is a `declare namespace { export const x }`.
- **`node.end` overshoots by one token**: In this AST, `node.end` = `scanner.getPos()` AFTER calling `nextToken()`, so it includes the start/end of the NEXT scanned token — not the true end of the node's text. To compute squiggle length, use `expressionTrueEnd(expr)` which traverses the rightmost leaf: `PropertyAccessExpression → name.pos + name.text.length`, `StringLiteralNode → pos + rawText.length + 2`, `NumericLiteralNode → pos + text.length`, `Identifier → pos + text.length`. Never use `node.end - node.pos` for span length in diagnostics.
- **`ArrowFunction` block body span — prefer `closeBracePos + 1` over `body.end - 1`**: For arrow functions with block bodies, `body.end` is set after the parser advances PAST `}` into the next token's whitespace, so `body.end - 1` lands at the space/trivia AFTER the `}` (one char too far). `Block.closeBracePos + 1` gives the exact position just past `}`. Same pattern as `ObjectLiteralExpression`. Symptom: TS2345/TS2769 squiggle over an arrow-function arg is one char too wide when the source has trailing whitespace between `}` and the next token (e.g. `c.fn(1, (x) => { ... } );`). Applied in `expressionTrueEnd`'s `ArrowFunction` branch with fallback to `body.end - 1` when `closeBracePos < 0` (synthetic blocks).
- **Merged enum autoValue reset**: Each enum declaration block resets auto-increment to 0. `computeEnumSymbolValues` must have `var autoValue = 0.0` INSIDE the loop over declarations, not outside — otherwise merged enums get wrong values (e.g., `Enum1 { A0 = 100 }; Enum1 { A }` → A should be 0, not 101).
- **`resolveAlias` cycle detection**: Import aliases can be circular (import shadowing, re-exports). Must use a `visited: MutableSet<Int>` parameter to prevent StackOverflow.
- **Nested QualifiedName resolution**: `import I = A.B.C.E` creates a QualifiedName where `left` is another QualifiedName, not an Identifier. `resolveAlias` must handle this via `resolveQualifiedName` which recurses.
- **Const enum negative values**: TypeScript does NOT wrap negative const enum values in `ParenthesizedExpression`. The Transformer emits `PrefixUnaryExpression(-, literal)` directly. `parenthesizeForAccess` adds parens only when used as property access base (e.g., `(-1).toString()`).
- **Const enum comment `*/` escaping**: `*/` inside const enum comment labels must be escaped to `*_/` to avoid prematurely closing the `/* ... */` comment.
- **`isolatedModules` and checker const enums**: When `isolatedModules` is true, do NOT use the checker for const enum inlining — the checker has cross-file info that shouldn't be used. Only use local `collectConstEnumValues`.
- **Circular type resolution StackOverflow**: `getDeclaredTypeOfClassOrInterface` must store the type in `declaredTypes` cache BEFORE resolving base types, otherwise `class A extends B` + `class B extends A` causes infinite recursion. Similarly, type alias resolution needs a sentinel cache entry (`errorType`) before resolving the aliased type. A new recursive resolution path needs the same cache-before-recurse / in-progress-sentinel pattern — do NOT wrap call sites in `try-catch(StackOverflowError)` (inline catches were removed 2026-07-02; only the init boundary guard remains).
- **The binder NEVER binds a `ClassExpression`** — class-expression symbols exist only as check-pass-local synthetics (`Symbol(SymbolFlags.Class, "(Anonymous class)")`). Any `when` over `symbol.declarations` for class-like types must mirror `is ClassDeclaration ->` with an `is ClassExpression ->` arm (as `getDeclaredTypeOfClassOrInterface`/`resolveInterfaceMembers`/`resolveBaseTypesLazy` do), or anon-class member/heritage resolution silently regresses to an empty member table.
- **TS2403 uses the dedicated identity comparator `ts2403Identical` (tri-state; UNKNOWN always SUPPRESSES — zero-FP surface), NOT the assignability engine** — routing var-decl consistency through the relation engine reopens the named→named 39+-regression gate. Two traps if extending: the cycle-break must STILL compare per-occurrence resolved args on re-entry (a naive returns-IDENTICAL cycle-break silently masks real differences), and user interfaces merging with embedded-lib ones must filter lib-only members/call-sigs via `builtinLibMemberDecls`.
- **TS2339 false positive prevention**: Check `this.prop` in class bodies AND `ident.prop` for non-this identifiers. For non-this: only check when the identifier is a class/namespace/module (static shapes), or a variable with interface type (not class type — class-typed variables may be narrowed via `instanceof`). Also check symbol's namespace exports for merged class+namespace symbols. Skip classes with base types, .js files, enums, anonymous types, union/intersection types, and RUNTIME_PROPERTIES (prototype, constructor, toString, etc.).
- **Namespace non-exported member access**: The binder puts ALL namespace members in `symbol.exports`, not just exported ones. To check if `M.prop` is valid from outside, use `isNameExportedFromNamespace` which checks `ExportValue` flag and scans `VariableStatement` modifiers (since the binder doesn't set `ExportValue` on variable declarations — the `export` keyword is on the `VariableStatement`, not the `VariableDeclaration`). Key exceptions: `declare namespace` (NamespaceModule) members are implicitly exported without `export` keyword; sub-namespace symbols (Module flag) are always accessible; import aliases should skip the namespace check entirely.
- **TS2416 heritage-base resolution is namespace-aware**: `checkClassPropertyOverrides` resolves `extends`/`implements` base names via the threaded `enclosingNs` stack (innermost-first, before `globals`; qualified bases via `resolveQualifiedValueSymbol`). When extending this walker, thread `enclosingNs` through any new recursion arm or namespace shadowing of heritage bases silently regresses to global resolution.
- **TS2416 FP prevention — overloaded methods**: Skip TS2416 checking for methods that appear multiple times in the class (overloads) or where the base member has multiple declarations. Full overload compatibility checking is not yet implemented.
- **TS2416 method return type inference**: For methods with body but no return type annotation, default to `void` only if the body has no `return expr` statements. If the body returns a value, default to `any` (not `void`) since we can't infer the return type from body analysis. Using `void` unconditionally causes FPs for methods like `foo() { return 1; }`.
- **`getTypeFromTypeNode(TypePredicate)` resolves to `booleanType`** — structural comparison cannot see predicate-vs-boolean mismatches, so TS2416 predicate mismatch needs the dedicated AST-level detection in `checkClassPropertyOverrides` (base return AST is a non-asserts `TypePredicate`, derived's isn't), displayed via `methodSigDisplay`/`methodSigDisplayWithBodyReturn`.
- **`signatureRelatedTo` parameter count**: Must check `source.minArgumentCount > target.parameters.size` — if the source function requires more args than the target provides, they're incompatible. Without this, `(a, b) => void` would be incorrectly assignable to `() => any`.
- **`signatureRelatedTo` substitutes pinned source type params into the RETURN type (17.10d + round 455)**: for a source-generic, target-non-generic sig (`identity<T>(x: T): T` → `GetCanonicalFileName = (fileName: string) => string`), the param loop pins each source bare-TypeParam param to the target's concrete param type (`tpAssignments` by id + `tpAssignByName` — TypeParam interning is per-AST-position (B199), so the return's `T` may carry a different id than the param's, hence the name fallback); the return check THEN instantiates `sourceReturn` with those pins (`T` → `string`) before the covariant compare. Without it the raw TypeParam return FP-rejects against the concrete target return (`identity` → any `A => A` FP'd). Gate: ≥1 source param IS a bare TypeParam (`sourceHasTpInParams`) — correctness preserved (`identity` vs `(x: string) => number` still fails: pinned return `string ⊄ number`). Also fixes the construct-sig variant (`new <TKind extends SyntaxKind>(…): Token<TKind>` → `new (…): Node`).
- **OBJECT_PROTOTYPE_PROPERTIES filter**: Names: `toString`, `toLocaleString`, `valueOf`, `constructor`, `hasOwnProperty`, `isPrototypeOf`, `propertyIsEnumerable`. When the lib `Object` interface resolves to a real Type.Interface (after Variable+Interface merge), these appear in `target.properties`. Every "is this property missing" check needs to skip them — `propertiesRelatedTo`, `collectMissingProperties`, `getMissingRequiredPropertySymbol`, and the TS2344 missing-property loop in `checkConstraintsForTypeArgs`. Otherwise `{a:string}` source fails to satisfy `Object` because `constructor` is "missing".
- **B67.3 refinement: OBJECT_PROTOTYPE_PROPERTIES filter is conditional on source-explicit definition** (`propertiesRelatedTo` only): the filter rationale (prototype provides it) ONLY applies when source doesn't explicitly define the property. When source has its own `toString: 5`, fall through to the type-compatibility check so `{toString: 5}` vs `Object.toString: () => string` emits TS2322 with chain. Gate: `targetName in OBJECT_PROTOTYPE_PROPERTIES && !sourceMembers.containsKey(targetName)`. Other call sites (`collectMissingProperties`, etc.) keep the unconditional filter — they check "is this property missing FROM source", so the source-defined case is impossible there.
- **Primitive→Object via apparent type in `structuredTypeRelatedTo`**: `string` → `Object` is assignable in TypeScript via apparent type (string's apparent type is `String` wrapper, which extends `Object`). Adding the Variable+Interface merge makes Object resolve as a real interface, so `propertiesRelatedTo` is now reachable but rejects `string` (Type.Intrinsic, not Type.Object). Recover by recursing on `getApparentType(source)` when target is `Type.Interface && !is Type.Reference` and source is a primitive — but skip when target is itself a wrapper interface (Number/String/Boolean/Symbol/BigInt) so wrapper-to-mismatched-primitive still emits TS2322. Skip Reference targets too: `Promise<number>` would otherwise let `Number → Promise<number>` pass via Number-extends-Object, hiding real async-return mismatches.
- **`getApparentType` walks the type-param constraint chain ITERATIVELY with a seen-set — do NOT "simplify" it back to `constraint?.let { getApparentType(it) }` (round 685).** An INDIRECT circular constraint (`<T extends U, U extends T>`) then recurses forever: it blew the stack, and the `init` boundary guard turned it into a TS2589 at 0:0 that ABORTED the whole file's checking. Nothing else guards this shape — the general TS2313 walker (`checkTypeParamsCircular`) only catches the DIRECT `<T extends T>` form. A cycle yields `anyType`, exactly like a missing constraint; reporting tsc's TS2313 for the indirect form is still a gap.
- **`getApparentType` does NOT fold Object.prototype's members (toString/valueOf/hasOwnProperty/…) into an object type's apparent members (M2.2/round 394)** — it only maps type-params to constraints and primitives to wrapper interfaces; a `Type.Object`/`Interface`/`Reference` passes through unchanged. So any check that needs an Object.prototype member ON an object type (e.g. TS2790 `delete x.toString`) must fall back to `OBJECT_PROTOTYPE_PROPERTIES` / `getObjectPrototypeMemberType`. This surfaces ONLY under real libs: embedded `Array` (value) resolves to the `Array<any>` INSTANCE (declares its own `toString`) so `getPropertyOfType` finds it, but real-lib `Array` → `ArrayConstructor` (no own `toString`, inherits from Object.prototype) so it's missed. The delete check's fallback fires when the receiver is object-like, the name ∈ `OBJECT_PROTOTYPE_PROPERTIES`, and `propSym == null` (a user type declaring the name optionally still routes through the own-member branch); FP-safe because `delete x.<objProtoMember>` is ALWAYS TS2790 under strictNullChecks (those members are non-optional, present on every object). Folding Object.prototype into `getApparentType` generally is the broad M3 change (touches every relation) — deliberately not done.
- **Async function return-type Promise<T> unwrap**: `async function f(): Promise<number> { return 1 }` is valid — the `1` is implicitly wrapped. `checkReturnAssignability` must consult `inAsyncFunctionBody` (set by `checkFunctionBody` when caller passes `isAsync = ModifierFlag.Async in modifiers`). When in an async body and the declared return type is `Type.Reference(target.symbol.name == "Promise", [T])`, also try assignability against `T` — return success if either passes. Without this, the same Variable+Interface merge that lets Promise resolve as a real interface causes spurious TS2322 on every awaited primitive return.
- **`getTypeOfExpression(AwaitExpression)` unwraps ONE level of `Promise<X>`→`X`**; non-Promise operands pass through, bare/unknown stay anyType (avoids cascade); recursive `Awaited<>`/thenables still TODO. Union-distribution over await was implemented, full-suite-verified net-zero, and REVERTED (no consuming test) — re-add `if (t is Type.Union) getUnionType(t.types.map { unwrapAwaited(it) })` when a union-await test appears.
- **Async-generic-return TS2322 is a NARROW position-specific emission, NOT a relation/scope change (B87.1, round 73)**: For `async function f<T>(...): Promise<T>` returning a value whose awaited type is a UNION containing the bare unconstrained `T` plus other constituents (e.g. `T | Yadda`), TypeScript reports TS2322 "Type 'Yadda | Awaited<T>' is not assignable to type 'T'" + "'T' could be instantiated with an arbitrary type...". This is emitted directly in `checkReturnAssignability` (Checker.kt:~49373) gated on `inAsyncFunctionBody` + `Promise<bareTP>` return annotation (TP ∈ `typeParams`) + `Type.Union` source with ≥1 unconstrained-TypeParam member matching the TP AND ≥1 non-TP non-any/error member. Display = non-TP members first, then TP members wrapped `Awaited<name>`. **DO NOT** "fix this properly" by (A) pushing the function's TPs into `currentTypeParamScope` during return-type-node resolution (would make `Promise<T>` a `Type.Reference` broadly — cascades through every generic return annotation) or (B) making assign-to-bare-TypeParam fail in the relation engine (cascades through every internal generic-function assignment). Those broad changes were deliberately avoided; the narrow emission captures the rule for this shape with a tiny FP-surface. The general (A)/(B) fix remains documented in PLAN-PHASE-4.md B87.1c for any future async-generic test the narrow gate misses.
- **The missing-property SUBSET of named→named comparison is SAFE to open (B87.4, round 73)** — `canUseTypeEngine` gates OFF full named→named (class/interface) structural comparison because of the documented 39+ recursive-type-expansion regressions. BUT `collectMissingProperties` (name-presence: iterate target's required props, check absence in source) does NOT recurse into property types, so it cannot trigger that expansion. `checkAssignmentExpression`'s Identifier-target path (Checker.kt:~50173) emits TS2741/2739 for class-instance-source (`Type.Interface` whose symbol has `SymbolFlags.Class`) → `Type.Interface`-target when required members are absent — added as an additive branch BEFORE the `canUse` gate. It's safe despite class→interface assignments being common because satisfying classes yield empty missing-sets (no emission). DO NOT extend this to full structural relatedness (property-TYPE comparison) for named→named — that re-opens the 39+-regression gate.
- **`formatTypeForDisplay` does NOT render the `readonly` TypeOperator (B87.2, round 73)**: `formatTypeForDisplay(TypeOperator(ReadonlyKeyword, TupleType))` returns `null`, so any diagnostic display for a `readonly [...]` / `readonly T[]` annotation must build it manually as `"readonly " + formatTypeForDisplay(operator.type)`. (Same likely applies to `keyof`/`unique` TypeOperators — check before relying on formatTypeForDisplay for operator types.)
- **`formatTypeForDisplay`'s TypeLiteral case renders members in CANONICAL tsc order (call sigs → construct sigs → index sigs → properties+named-methods), NOT source order (B471)** — tsc reorders anonymous object-type members; preserving source order mismatches the baseline display. Construct sigs render `new (…): T` (space after `new`); an empty object type renders `{}` not `{ ; }`. FP-safe to reorder: a passing test whose TypeLiteral annotation had non-canonical source order would already be failing. SEPARATELY, a bare class identifier in VALUE position displays as `typeof C` (the constructor), not `C` (its instance type) — the var-decl/assignment source-display sites override this when the init is a pure-class identifier.
- **AsExpression (`expr as T`) is visited by `walkTypeAssertionsInExpr` but NOT forwarded to the `onAssertion` callback (B87.2, round 73)**: the shared walker recurses into `AsExpression.expression` but only calls `onAssertion` for `TypeAssertionExpression` (`<T>expr`). So AsExpression casts get no TS2352/erasable checks via that callback. The walker survives ONLY for checkErasableSyntaxOnly (rounds 632/648 moved every flag-gated inline emit to spine anchors). **Since round 630 the spine classifier `spineCoEdge` mirrors this walker's descent arms for the migrated cast-overlap passes, and since round 648 the type-param-cast classifier `spineTcEdge` FOLDS THROUGH `spineCoEdge` for its shared region — any walker-arm change (new expression kind, new descent) must be mirrored in `spineCoEdge` or BOTH passes' reach silently diverges.**
- **Object-literal cast overlap (TS2352, `emitTS2352IfObjectLiteralToInterfaceCast`, B448) uses BIDIRECTIONAL comparability — a one-directional check FPs on EVERY partial/empty cast.** tsc fires TS2352 for `<IFoo>{objLit}` only when NEITHER the literal is comparable to the target NOR the target to the literal. Detecting just "literal not comparable to target" (a missing-required or incompatible-shared prop) is WRONG: an empty `<B>{}` and a props-subsumed partial `<IFoo>{n:1}` (IFoo has more props) are LEGAL because the TARGET side overlaps. The load-bearing gate is `if (checkTypeRelatedTo(target, getTypeOfObjectLiteral(ol), comparableRelation)) return` (suppress when target is comparable to the literal). The first cut regressed contextualTyping/contextualTyping11/m7Bugs (+1/-3 swap) until this gate was added. Any new cast/assignability-overlap check must consider BOTH directions.
- **`isDeeplyNested` heuristic on parallel target stacks**: `relationSourceTargets`/`relationTargetTargets` track `target.id` of every `Type.Reference` source/target on the active comparison stack. When the same target appears 5+ times on its stack, `checkTypeRelatedTo` short-circuits to `true` (cycle break). Catches infinitely-expanding generic comparisons like `A<T> { x: A<()=>T> }` vs `B<T> { x: B<()=>T> }` whose Refs grow unboundedly and never re-occur identically. The same-target arg-shortcut (step a) ALSO defers to structural on re-entry (`countOccurrences > 1`) so the inner cycle-break can fire instead of an eager arg-mismatch `false`.
- **TypeParam vs TypeParam relates via apparent types**: `structuredTypeRelatedTo` returns `true` when both sides are `Type.TypeParam` and either is unconstrained (apparent type `{}`); when both have constraints, recursively compares those. Without this, generic-method signature comparison treats source's `K` and target's `K` (separate fresh params from `resolveGenericPropertyType`) as opaque-and-distinct → spurious TS2322.
- **`getPropertyTypeForRelation` substitution is BUDGETED, not depth-capped (round 552, INV.5(d1))**: `resolveGenericPropertyType` consumes `genericPropInstantiationBudget` (2,000 fresh worker computations per top-level relation, reset at every depth-0 `checkTypeRelatedTo` entry; memo hits free; on trip → raw `getTypeOfSymbol` fallback, the old over-cap behavior). The budget exists because each level of a breadth-exploding comparison mints genuinely NEW `(target, args)` refs (the `(ref.id, propSym.id)` memo never hits — performanceComparisonOfStructurallyIdenticalInterfacesWithGenericSignatures OOMs without it). PAIRED: `tryEmitObjectVsNamedUnionArg` bails on a param union carrying an UN-INFERRED TypeParam — deep substitution makes the whole-union relation fail on the unbound T and tsc's own `flatten<T>` call FPs (the round-431 foreign-TP rationale on the PARAM side); a TP-free gate on the substitution RESULT does NOT work (the relation flips target-side, inside the walk). A NEW emitter whose relation involves un-inferred generic params needs the same param-side gate.
- **`instantiateType` walks anonymous property-bag `Type.Object` ONLY (no symbol, no call/construct sigs); it NEVER walks `callSignatures`/`constructSignatures`** — a function-shaped `Type.Object` (function-typed property or method param on a generic interface) keeps its inner sig's outer-TP references un-substituted, and the failure mode is SILENT (per-property elaboration / TS2345-via-constraint just don't fire). Two per-site remedies: `substituteOuterTypeArgsInGenericFnObject` (in-place mutation; safe because `getTypeFromTypeNode` is uncached under `currentTypeParamScope`) for a DIRECTLY fn-shaped raw type, or **`instantiateTypeFnAware` (round 465, non-mutating — mints fresh objects, preserves sig type params) when the fn type may be NESTED in a union or in a sig's return/params** — used at `resolveGenericPropertyTypeWorker`'s MethodDeclaration RETURN and inside `substituteOuterTypeArgsInSignature` (a method's `((node: T) => T) | undefined` return otherwise kept the raw T — emitter.ts's OrdinalParentheizerRuleSelector). Apply one of the two at any new site with this shape.
- **`currentLocalTypes` is first-decl-wins for annotated `VariableDeclaration`**: when multiple declarations of the same name share an annotation (e.g. `var x: T` + `declare var x: T` in a script file), the FIRST resolved type stays. Each `getTypeFromTypeNode(TypeLiteral)` call produces a FRESH `Type.Object` with FRESH member Symbols, so the SAME `{...}` literal at two source positions produces two distinct member Symbols with distinct declaration positions. Without first-wins, `getTypeOfIdentifier(x)` returns whichever annotation was processed last, and TS6500 / TS2728 "declared here" hints point to the wrong source position. Matches Binder's `valueDeclaration` semantics (only set when `existing.valueDeclaration == null`).

- **Nested generic functions must inherit enclosing type params**: the `checkTypeAssignabilityInStatements` FunctionDeclaration arm passes `outerTypeParams = typeParams` — dropping it makes every bare-TP mismatch check silently never fire inside nested generic fns. Union-constraint rule: a constraint chain reaches a target tp iff ALL union members do (`tpConstraintChainReachesTp`).

- **`checkExcessProperties` reports the FIRST top-level excess property and RETURNS (B560, tsc `hasExcessProperties`)** — tsc's excess check returns `Ternary.False` on the first excess prop of a fresh object literal, so it neither enumerates every excess prop NOR proceeds to the per-property structural check (the recursion into known props' nested literals). So `var o1: { a; b } = { e: 0, f: 0 }` reports only `e`, never `f` (incompatibleTypes). Earlier code emitted every top-level excess + recursed regardless. FP-safe to first-only: NO tsc baseline reports 2+ excess from one fresh literal via this path (the only multi-excess baselines — arrayLiterals, missingAndExcessProperties — are array-of-literals / destructuring-assignment, owned by SEPARATE emitters), so the change can only REMOVE over-emission. The nested-known-prop recursion now runs ONLY when there is NO top-level excess (matching tsc, which never reaches it once an excess is found).
- **TS2344 type-arg constraint satisfaction has NO general TypeParam-source-via-constraint relation rule — the checks (`checkConstraintsForTypeArgs` + the default-validation `checkTpListDefaults`) each carry their OWN constraint-chain bail-outs (round 440-b).** A bare TypeParam arg whose `.constraint` satisfies is handled; ADDITIONALLY (a) a constraint that resolves to `anyType` SATISFIES every target constraint (a literal `extends any` OR — our gap — an enum-member union like `JSDocSyntaxKind = SyntaxKind.A | …` that collapses to `any`: each member type resolves to `any` so the union collapses; a DIRECT `Token<JSDocSyntaxKind>` arg is already skipped by the `argType === anyType` guard, so a `Token<TKind>` TypeParam arg must be too), and (b) a UNION arg/default satisfies when EVERY member does — incl. a TypeParam member whose own constraint relates (`Visitor<TIn extends Node, TOut = TIn | undefined>` vs `Node | undefined`; the whole-union relation misses `TIn <: Node | undefined` for the same missing rule). FP-safe (every union member must genuinely relate). **(c) round 725 added the THIRD arm: an INTERSECTION arg satisfies when a TypeParam CONSTITUENT's constraint does — the real lib's `NonNullable<T> = T & {}` makes every `NonNullable<TIn>` argument an `Intersection[TypeParam, {}]`, so (a)'s bare-TypeParam bail never fires and the constraint is never consulted AT ALL (the already-non-null case fails identically, which is what disproves the obvious "strip the nullish part" reading). An `{}` constituent additionally strips null/undefined/void from that constraint (tsc's non-nullish marker). Non-TypeParam constituents are left to `checkTypeRelatedTo`, which already does "some constituent relates" plus its merged-contradiction guard — a blanket `is Type.Intersection -> continue` passes the target cases and deletes a real diagnostic class; `NonNullableConstraintTest`'s four negative controls pin against it.** The relation engine itself still has NO `source is Type.TypeParam && target !is Type.TypeParam` branch — adding one is a broad change deliberately avoided; extend the per-site bail-outs instead. **MEASURED (round 456, A/B on compiler AND services): the broad rule (`source is Type.TypeParam` → relate its constraint-chain, cycle-guarded for `T extends T`) is NET-ZERO — it fixed exactly one assignment FP (`utilities.ts:12338` `getSynthesizedDeepCloneWithReplacements<T extends Node>`) but INTRODUCED one via OVERLOAD SELECTION: a bare un-inferred `T extends string` (from `getPathFromPathComponents<T extends string>` whose inference our engine leaves un-substituted) now matched the `(path: string)` overload of `ensureTrailingDirectorySeparator` instead of `(path: Path)`, returning `string` → FP `string ⊄ string & {__pathBrand}` at moduleNameResolver.ts:2933. The rule is SOUND (T <: constraint <: target) but overload arg-matching shares `checkTypeRelatedTo`, so it perturbs signature selection wherever inference under-resolves a TypeParam arg. Reverted. If retried, apply it as a per-site bail at the assignment/return TS2322 EMISSION only (not the shared engine), or fix the upstream `getPathFromPathComponents` `readonly T[]`-from-`PathPathComponents` inference first. Round 461 LANDED that per-site variant: `bareTpConstraintRelatesTo` (constraint-chain walk, cycle-guarded, unconstrained-TP → false) bails the ASSIGNMENT TS2322 emission — extend it to other EMISSION sites when this family resurfaces, never the engine.**


### Overload resolution gotchas


- **`literalTypeOfExpression` takes an `arrayCtx` flag (round 471): its ArrayLiteralExpression arm (fresh array literal keeps literal ELEMENT types) fires ONLY when the caller's contextual target is Array/ReadonlyArray (`isArrayLikeReference`)** — an ungated arm regressed assignmentIndexedToPrimitives (`const n4: 0 = [0]` must display `number[]`, not `0[]`: a non-array literal target provides no element context in tsc). A NEW literal-preservation call site whose expression can be an array literal must pass `isArrayLikeReference(target)`.
- **`resolveCallOverload`'s type-based pass treats an UN-INFERRED bare `Type.TypeParam` param as matching ANY arg (round 481)** — tsc infers it; the relation has no concrete-vs-TypeParam rule, so without the lenience a generic overload (Array.reduce's `<U>(cb, initialValue: U)`) was always rejected and the first arity match won. The property-access pass's MULTI-overload contextual branch selects the overload via `resolveCallOverload(strictSelect = true)` (definitive winners only — arity-fallbacks return null) and only diverges from the legacy every-overload-callable heuristic when the winner is NOT `sigs[0]`.
- **Overload arg-MATCHING must keep a LITERAL arg's literal type when the param is a literal — `getTypeOfExpression(StringLiteralNode/NumericLiteralNode)` returns the WIDENED `string`/`number` (Checker.kt ~85874), so a valid `foo("hi")` against an overload param `"hi"` FP-rejects → FP TS2769 (B326, overloadingOnConstants2).** The three overload arg-check helpers (`allArgumentsMatch`, `getFirstArgumentError`, `countFailingArgDiagnostics`) now compute `argType = if (propTypeContainsLiteral(paramType)) (literalTypeOfExpression(arg) ?: getTypeOfExpression(arg)) else getTypeOfExpression(arg)` — gated to literal params so non-literal overloads are byte-identical; the display (`getFirstArgumentError`) likewise keeps the literal (`'"um"'` not `'string'`) when the param is a literal (the established "widen the arg UNLESS the param is a literal" rule).
- **TS2394 overload-impl RETURN compatibility is BIDIRECTIONAL (tsc `isImplementationCompatibleWithOverload`): compatible iff `overloadReturn` assignable to `implReturn` OR `implReturn` assignable to `overloadReturn` (B326).** So `function foo(x:"hi"):D; foo(x:string):C {}` is compatible for the `D` overload (`D extends C` → `D→C` ✓) but NOT for an `E` overload (E↔C unrelated, neither direction) → TS2394. The conservative syntactic `isTypeNodeCompatible` returns true for two class/interface `TypeReference`s (no resolution) → `classReturnImplSatisfiesOverload` (additive, in `isSignatureCompatible`) resolves both non-generic class/interface refs and applies the bidirectional `checkTypeRelatedTo`, conservatively true on any unresolvable/generic/non-class shape. **(round 410) The full tsc rule has a THIRD disjunct FIRST: `targetReturnType === voidType || …` — a `void` OVERLOAD return is compatible with ANY implementation return (the caller ignores the value), so `isSignatureCompatible` SKIPS both the syntactic return compare AND `classReturnImplSatisfiesOverload` when the overload return is a `void` KeywordTypeNode (tsc's own `writeTokenText(…): void;` vs `: number` impl). Constructor overloads have no explicit return annotation → the return check is already skipped, so they're unaffected.**
- **TS2862 ("Type 'T' is generic and can only be indexed for reading.", the B98.r80 `gIdx` walker) fires ONLY when the type param's CONSTRAINT bears a string/symbol index signature (round 410, `constraintHasStringIndexSignature`).** tsc emits it only when a generic index-WRITE would fall back to a string index signature (checker.ts ~19294: `accessFlags & NoIndexSignatures && indexInfo.keyType !== numberType`), so a bare `T extends object` (no index sig) draws NOTHING — tsc's own `assign<T extends object>(t: T){ t[p] = arg[p] }` compiles clean. `constrainedTpNames` (used only by this walker) is gated to constraints that are an inline `{ [s: string]: V }` TypeLiteral, a `Record<K, V>` with a string/symbol-like key, or an intersection of either. A user `TypeReference` to an interface WITH an index signature is a harmless false negative (we can't see through it AST-side; the `cannotIndexGenericWritingError` corpus shapes use `Record`/inline literals).
- **`isDuplicateOverload` compared param-type CLASSES, so two LITERAL-typed params with DIFFERENT values (`(x:"hi")` vs `(x:"bye")`) looked like duplicates and the 2nd overload was wrongly SKIPPED in the TS2394 loop (B326).** Fixed to compare literal VALUES via `literalExpressionEquals` for a `LiteralType`/`LiteralType` param pair (keyword/other-node-class comparisons unchanged).
- **Calling a UNION of function types synthesizes ONE combined signature with INTERSECTED parameter types (tsc `getUnionSignatures`, B516)** — `((x:number)=>string) | ((x:boolean)=>string)` called `f("s")` → combined param `number & boolean` = `never` → TS2345 (`"s"` vs `never`), NOT the "none of those signatures are compatible" TS2349. The combinable gate in the union-callee branch of `checkSingleCallExpressionTypes` (Checker.kt ~102052) requires: every member sig type-parameter-free, SAME non-zero required-only arity (`minArgumentCount == parameters.size`, so no optional/rest/`this`-params — `this` is dropped from `Signature.parameters` at build, so a this-only sig has arity 0 and is excluded), AND ≥1 differing param position. The type-param gate keeps `fnUnion2` (`(<T extends number>(a:T)=>void) | (<T>(a:string)=>void)`, `betterErrorForUnionCall`) and `F3|F4` (`unionTypeCallSignatures6`) on the TS2349 path. DISPLAY: when the combined param is `never`, the arg shows its LITERAL type (`'"s"'`, via `literalTypeOfExpression`), NOT the widened base — the standard widen-arg-unless-param-is-literal rule is overridden for `never`. Arity overflow → `emitTS2554TooMany`; per-arg → manual TS2345 (TS2554 wins alone, no per-arg). **Supporting plumbing (all needed for the generic `T["a"]`/`p:T` cases): (a)** `getIndexedAccessType` now distributes over a UNION object type (`(A|B)["a"]` → `A["a"] | B["a"]`); **(b)** the call walker's FunctionDeclaration branch pushes the function's OWN type params onto `currentTypeParamScope` (mirroring the ClassDeclaration branch) so `p: T`/`var a: T["a"]` resolve through `T`'s constraint; **(c)** `populateParameterLocalTypes` must NOT register a function-typed param whose sig references a Type.TypeParam (gate `isTpReferencingFnParam`) — those calls are owned by `checkFnTypedParamCalls`, and registering them once the pushed TP scope resolves the sig's `T` to a real TypeParam double-emits TS2345 (the prior errorType-param resolution made the gate naturally inert); **(d)** `computeRawTypeOfPropertyAccess`'s union-distribution branch keys on `getApparentType(objectType)` (not `objectType` directly) so a TypeParam-with-union-constraint receiver distributes; **(e)** the call walker's annotated-local population also accepts a `Type.Union` of all-callable members. **B538 (`signatureCombiningRestParameters1`, `checkCombinedRestParamForOfSpreadCall`) is a SEPARATE dedicated AST walker for the variant B516 cannot reach: a union of fn types obtained by iterating `Object.entries(<map>)` where `<map>` is `declare const map: {[K in T1|keyof T2]: (...args: K extends keyof T2 ? T2[K] : []) => unknown}` — the values have a CONDITIONAL REST param, so the combined sig's param collapses to `never` and `fn(...args)` (any) → TS2345 'any'→'never'.** We evaluate neither the mapped-over-conditional type nor Object.entries+for-of destructuring → the callee resolves to `any` → B516's relation-engine path never fires (it needs a real `Type.Union` callee) → the walker is purely ADDITIVE. Do NOT try to route this through B516; extend the dedicated walker. Corpus-unique (only Object.entries+for-of-destructuring file iterating a top-level mapped-const).
- **`callHasTypeGuardArg` detects INLINE arrow/fn-expr guards ONLY; the round-428 multi-sig generic inference additionally gates on the SEPARATE `argIsNamedTypeGuardIdentifier` (an Identifier arg resolving to a `x is T` fn decl) — do NOT fold the two**: B136's concrete-overload swap must keep firing for `.filter(namedGuard)` (its wider concrete return is the established approximation), while the round-428 inference must SKIP named-guard calls (the guard overload's S binds from the PREDICATE, unmodeled). Folding either way regresses the other consumer.
- **Nested-fn call returns resolve ONLY under `inInferenceBodyTyping` (round 466, Blocker #2): `tryNestedFnCallReturnType` types a call to a B83.5-unbound nested fn (unique name, or ambiguous with ALL declarations agreeing on the return), and a BARREL-imported Identifier callee resolves through the flow-only import resolver — both gated to callback-BODY re-typing in `tryInferSingleTypeParamFromArgs`.** The PROGRAM-WIDE version measured net +8 on the compiler profile (giant objlit members / property-write receivers gained concrete types riding M3 relation gaps) — do not un-scope it. The barrel-callee pre-step must stay NON-generic + BODIED + no-explicit-type-args (a generic target's return annotation resolves garbage without the TP scope; a bodyless decl is one OVERLOAD of a cluster — both looser cuts FP'd on `sortAndDeduplicate`). A heterogeneous ARRAY-LITERAL callback body contributes NO candidate (tsc contextually tuple-types it — `map(key => [toFileId(key), toFileIdListId(…)])` vs a tuple-array target).
- **`tryInferSingleTypeParamFromArgs`: an `anyType` arg soft-skips (contributes no candidate) ONLY at `forReturnType=true` sites; the arg-vs-param site keeps the hard `return null`** — its consumers CHECK args against substituted params (could emit, not just suppress). A nullable-union-of-tp param (`nullableUnionOfTpMode`: `T | undefined` mode 1, `T[] | undefined` mode 2) strips nullish members from a UNION arg and soft-skips oddities; the legacy bare-T/`Array<tp>` shapes keep their hard bails.
- **The 5 overload arg-check helpers (`allArgumentsMatch`, `getFirstArgumentError`, `getFirstFailingArgPosition`, `countFailingArgDiagnostics`, `getUnionMemberFailureSubline`) MUST use `restAwareParamType(params, i)`, NOT raw `getTypeOfSymbol(params[i])` (B511)** — a REST param's symbol type is the ARRAY wrapper (`any[]`), so a bare `getTypeOfSymbol` compares an arg against the array type and FP-fires TS2769 ("string not assignable to any[]") for `f("yo")` vs `(...args: any[])`. `restAwareParamType` expands a rest param to its ELEMENT type (mirrors the main `checkArgumentsAgainstSignature` `isRestParam` handling). A NEW overload arg-check helper must use it too — **AND must route the arg type through `overloadNarrowedArgType` (round 422)**: a guard-narrowed union arg (`x ? overloadedFn(x) : undefined`), or a guard-narrowed bare `boolean` vs literal `true`/`false` overload params (our `boolean` is NOT modeled as `true | false`, so the helper narrows a synthetic literal union and accepts only a single-literal result), otherwise fails EVERY overload → FP TS2769. A helper left on the raw type disagrees with the match verdict.
- **A clodule `(declare) namespace M { class C; function C }` does NOT merge in our binder — `canMerge` lacks Class+Function, so the function OVERWRITES the class symbol (last-wins) and `M.C`'s value symbol is the FUNCTION (B511).** Consequences: (a) `new M.C()` must recover the lost class via `findNamespaceMemberClassDecl` (AST-scan the namespace body for the same-named `ClassDeclaration`) — `getReturnTypeOfNewExpression` does this when the qualified value symbol isn't a Class; (b) the duplicate-declaration TS2813/TS2814/TS2300 class+function block must honor the threaded `isAmbientContext` flag (a class/function in a `declare namespace`/.d.ts is implicitly ambient → tsc never emits TS2813 "non-ambient"/TS2814 "with bodies" there).
- **`Type.TypeParam` instances are interned PER AST POSITION, so identity comparison across resolution sites FAILS (B199)**: a signature's `T` (interned at the TypeParameter node) and the SAME `T` inside an annotation like `Iterator<T, boolean>` (resolved through a different path) may be DISTINCT instances — compare by kind + `symbol?.name`, never by `===`, when matching a sig TP against a Reference's resolvedTypeArguments. ALSO: a namespace-local interface sharing an embedded-lib interface's name (e.g. `Underscore.Iterator` vs lib `Iterator`) resolves the REFERENCE's `target.symbol` to the LIB symbol — verify the resolved decl's shape (TP count) and fall back to scanning the file's namespace bodies for the local InterfaceDeclaration before trusting it.
- **Lazily-resolved symbol kinds must store their shell type in `symbolTypes` BEFORE signature resolution (in-progress sentinel, same pattern as the getDeclaredTypeOfClassOrInterface cache-before-bases rule)**: a self-referential annotation (`function foo(): typeof foo`) otherwise recurses to StackOverflowError (historically swallowed silently by inline consumer catches — symptom was zero diagnostics for the whole statement, not a crash; today it would surface via the init boundary guard as TS2589).
- **`tryInferSingleTypeParamFromArgs` MUST publish the WIDENED type into the inference mapper, never a literal (tried, REVERTED -2)**: the mapper feeds `currentLocalTypes`, JS-emit, and contextual `this`-binding; a literal leaks into a shared type cache causing ORDER-DEPENDENT regressions in UNRELATED JS-emit tests (target passes in isolation, fails in the full suite). Literal-vs-widened display for TS's fixed-type-parameter rule must be computed LATE, at the TS2339/TS2345 emission site only — do NOT change what the mapper publishes to fix a display-only mismatch.
- **`typeMentionsTypeParam` does NOT recurse into function-shaped `Type.Object`** — it only detects a TP in a callback's IMMEDIATE param/return position. For NESTED function types (`b: () => (a: T) => void`) use `tparamMentionedInFunctionTypeDeep` (descends call/construct sigs, Type.id cycle-guard).
- **`isSimpleCheckableType` NOT used for overload resolution**: For TS2769, `allArgumentsMatch`/`getFirstArgumentError`/`getFirstFailingArgPosition` check ALL types (including objects, arrays) — not just primitives. The `isSimpleCheckableType` guard is only for single-signature TS2345 checking.
- **Generic type-reference element comparison in `structuredTypeRelatedTo`**: When `source` and `target` are both `Type.Reference` with the **same target interface** (`source.target === target.target`), compare resolved type arguments directly (covariant: each source arg assignable to the matching target arg) instead of full structural comparison. Otherwise `objectTypeRelatedTo` passes trivially because member types with unresolved type params resolve to `errorType` and built-in lib methods resolve to `anyType`. **Skip the shortcut when any target arg is `void`** — the void-return-type rule (a signature returning T is compatible with one returning void) applies transitively when a type arg appears in a covariant return position (e.g. `interface B<T> { x(): T }` — `B<number>` must be assignable to `B<void>` for method-override checks to pass). Falling through to structural comparison preserves the prior trivial-pass behavior for void-arg refs. `getPropertyElaborationChain` emits the matching "  Type 'A' is not assignable to type 'B'." chain line for the first failing arg pair. Cycle detection for self-referential generics (`interface List<T> { next: List<T> }`) is handled by `relationComparisonStack` in `checkTypeRelatedTo`.
- **The same-target covariant shortcut (above) is UNSOUND for a generic interface that uses its TP CONTRAVARIANTLY (B529, `tryEmitGenericInterfaceContravariantArgTs2345`)**: `F<P> { (props: P & {...}): void }` — `F<CP>` is NOT assignable to `F<unknown>` (the call-sig param is contravariant: `unknown & {...}` = `{...}` must be assignable to `CP & {...}`, and `{...}` is not assignable to the bare TP `CP`), but the shortcut PASSES it (CP→unknown covariantly) so the standard arg-loop emits nothing. Fixing the shortcut to do variance analysis would regress the 39+ documented cycle cases, so `quickIntersectionCheckCorrectlyCachesErrors` is a DEDICATED corpus-unique AST-shape PRE-GATE (first in `checkArgumentsAgainstSignature`, fires for non-generic callees) that emits TS2345 + the contravariant chain directly. FP firewall: arg+param both Type.Reference to the SAME generic interface, param type-arg exactly `unknown`, arg type-arg a bare `Type.TypeParam`, the interface call-sig first param is `P & TypeLiteral`. The `{ children?: boolean | undefined; }` display comes from `formatTypeForDisplay(TypeLiteral)` (it renders an optional prop as `?: x | undefined`).
- **Array/ReadonlyArray MUST always use the same-target covariant element shortcut, even under the `isReentry` deferral (round 446) — do NOT let the re-entry structural fallback apply to them.** The `isReentry` guard (defer to structural so a genuine recursive comparison can reach the cycle-break instead of an eager `false`) is keyed on `source.target.id`, so a NESTED `Array<X>` inside `Array<Y>` (`{ actions: ActionInfo[] }[]` vs `ApplicableRefactorInfo[]` — an extremely common shape) counts the inner Array as a re-entry → structural comparison of the two `Array` INTERFACES → `concat`'s contravariant element param `ConcatArray<T>` spuriously FAILS (we have no per-TP variance info) → FP TS2322. Array is covariant (`A ⊄ B ⇒ Array<A> ⊄ Array<B>`) and still terminates via `relationComparisonStack` (identical pair) + `isDeeplyNested` (5+ occurrences), so the eager shortcut is sound. **GATED to TP-FREE target args**: an unbound-TP target (`flatten<T>(…: T[][] | …)`) is an M3.1 generic-inference gap the trivial structural pass currently MASKS — forcing the shortcut there turns the false-negative into an FP (`readonly Diagnostic[] ⊄ T[]`), so keep deferring to structural when `targetArgs` contain an unresolved TP. Non-Array (user recursive) generics keep the deferral unchanged.
- **An array-literal `return [msg, ...args]` against a VARIADIC-TUPLE target (or a union/alias containing one) is matched AST-side, NOT by the relation engine (round 446, `arrayLiteralSatisfiesTupleTarget`).** The engine SKIPS array→tuple and `getTupleType` COLLAPSES the rest slot (per the getTupleType gotcha), so a resolved-Type comparison of `[value]` vs `DiagnosticMessage | [message: DiagnosticMessage, ...args: (string|number)[]]` cannot decide it — both the engine and the "array"-display string fallback FP'd. The helper walks the target NODE (tuple / union / parenthesized / alias, alias bodies resolved through the merged `globals` for imported names — the local symbol is only the ImportSpecifier), parses the tuple into fixed-prefix / one rest / fixed-suffix, and verifies each element against its slot (permissive on unresolvable slots → suppression-only). Hooked into `checkReturnAssignability` (direct returns) + `checkConditionalReturnBranches` (`?:` arms); also covers fixed tuples in a union. Extend to var-decl/argument positions only when a non-return FP surfaces.
- **Generic overload guard**: Skip `checkArgumentsAgainstOverloads` when any signature has type parameters. Without generic type argument inference, parameter types resolve to `errorType` and produce false positive TS2769.
- **MethodDeclaration typeParameters**: Must include type parameters from `md.typeParameters` when creating Signatures for interface method overloads — the generic guard relies on `sig.typeParameters` being populated.
- **TS2793 conditional on implementation match**: Only emit "implementation would have succeeded" when `allArgumentsMatch(args, implSig)` returns true for the implementation signature. Otherwise the implementation may also reject the arguments (e.g., `foo({a:any}[])` still requires property 'a'). In the arity-filtered single-overload path, use the same `allArgumentsMatch` check — don't blindly attach TS2793 to TS2345.
- **TS2793 gates call `allArgumentsMatch(..., bivariantFnParams = true)` under `strictExplicitlyFalse`** (TS treats method params bivariantly when strictFunctionTypes is off, so `(x: 'bye') => number` is accepted by impl param `(x: string) => number`); the overload-resolution match itself MUST keep the default contravariant check or overloads get mis-selected. Bivariant fallback fires only for anonymous Type.Object pairs with non-empty `callSignatures`.
- **Literal type widening in error display**: `getWidenedLiteralType` maps `true`→`boolean`, `false`→`boolean`, string/number/bigint literals→base types. Used in TS2769 error messages but NOT in type checking itself.
- **TS2739 vs TS2740 code selection**: TS2739 for 2-4 missing properties, TS2740 for 5+. Both use the same "Type 'X' is missing the following properties from type 'Y': a, b, ..." message format. TS2741 is for single missing property only.
- **TS1184 accessor guard**: "Modifiers cannot appear here" (TS1184) fires alongside TS1042 only for `MethodDeclaration` in object literals, NOT for `GetAccessor`/`SetAccessor`. TypeScript only emits TS1042 for accessor modifier errors.
- **`collectMissingProperties` returns empty when source.members is null**: Anonymous function types from `() => T` set `Type.Object.callSignatures` only and leave `members = null`. Treating `members ?: emptyMap()` would report every target property as "missing" (e.g. `() => void` → `[string]` → ["0", "length"] → spurious TS2739). Returning empty forces the caller's TS2739/TS2741 gate to fall through to plain TS2322 "Type '() => void' is not assignable to type '[string]'." — kind mismatch, not property-missing. Mirrors `propertiesRelatedTo`'s `source.members ?: return targetProps.isEmpty()` short-circuit.
- **Array-vs-array outer TS2322 suppression for array literals**: Var-decl and assignment paths must skip the outer "Type 'X[]' is not assignable to type 'Y[]'." when `init`/`expr.right is ArrayLiteralExpression` and target is a `Type.Reference` to Array. `checkArrayLiteralElementExcessProps` and related walkers already emit per-element TS2322/TS2353 at the specific offending element; the outer diagnostic would duplicate them.
- **`getTypeFromTypeNode` cache bypass for context-sensitive resolution**: `nodeTypes` cache must be bypassed when ANY context-stack is active that affects type-name resolution (`currentTypeParamScope`, `inferenceNamespaceStack`, …). A type node may resolve to different `Type` values under different contexts, and a stale cache entry (e.g. `errorType` from an earlier eager pass before the context was active) would shadow the correct context-aware resolution. Any new "lazy resolution context stack" added in the future must be checked here too — failure mode is silent (cache hit returns wrong type, downstream type checks pass falsely).
- **Two DISJOINT namespace stacks**: `inferenceNamespaceStack` (type-name resolution) vs `propertyAccessEnclosingNamespaces` (property-access walker) — the walker deliberately does NOT push to `inferenceNamespaceStack` (value-position TS2576 FPs). A type-position resolution inside the property-access walker that needs namespace-local type names must bridge the innermost enclosing-namespace symbol onto `inferenceNamespaceStack` ONLY around that one `getTypeFromTypeNode` call (popped in `finally`; see `populateParameterLocalTypes`) — never for the whole body walk (reintroduces the TS2576 FP). Symptom of the gap: namespace-local-typed params silently resolve to errorType → no TS2339.
- **Lazy variable type inference must thread containing namespace**: Variables declared inside `namespace M { var x = ...; }` have `symbol.parent = M` set by the binder, but `getTypeOfIdentifier`/`getTypeFromTypeReference` don't have an AST-parent walk (no parent pointers in our AST). `getTypeOfVariableOrProperty`'s VariableDeclaration branch must `pushInferenceNamespaceFor(symbol)` before resolving annotations OR initializers so identifier/type-name lookups can consult the namespace's `exports`. Without this, namespace-internal references (e.g., `var inner; export var outer = inner`) silently bail to `anyType`, masking genuine type errors. The push covers both type annotations and initializers because both can reference namespace-local declarations.



### Deprecation diagnostic gotchas


- **TS5101 vs TS5102 vs TS5107**: Three different deprecation codes. TS5107 = target/module/moduleResolution deprecations. TS5101 = outFile/baseUrl/downlevelIteration deprecations (still functioning). TS5102 = removed options (out, charset, keyofStringsOnly, noImplicitUseStrict, etc.) with message "has been removed. Please remove it from your configuration."
- **`const enum` at statement level**: `parseStatement()` must check for `const enum` (not just inside `export`/`declare` contexts) — otherwise `const enum E {}` is misparse as `const` variable named `enum` + expression `E` + block.



### Checker diagnostic gotchas (TS2454/TS2564/TS6133)


- **`strict: false` suppresses TS2454/TS2564**: TypeScript test baselines expect these diagnostics by default, but NOT when `@strict: false` is explicitly set. Use `options.strictExplicitlyFalse` to distinguish default-false from explicit-false.
- **Definite assignment assertion `!`**: `var x!: Type` and class property `x!: Type` skip TS2454/TS2564 checking. Check `exclamationToken` on both `VariableDeclaration` and `PropertyDeclaration`.
- **Ambient contexts**: Skip TS2454/TS2564 in `declare` namespaces, classes, and functions. A class inside a `declare namespace` is ambient even if the class itself doesn't have `declare`.
- **TS6133 non-module files**: Don't check file-level unused declarations in non-module files (no imports/exports). Only check inside namespaces, functions, blocks.
- **TS6133 write-only**: Assignment targets (`x = value`) are NOT reads. Left side of `=` operator is write-only. Compound assignments (`x += 1`) ARE reads.
